Algebraic integrable connections with bounded irregularity
Takuro Mochizuki

Abstract
We study the boundedness of families of algebraic flat connections with bounded irregularity. As an application. we study the boundedness of families of holonomic 𝒟 \mathcal{D} -modules with dominated characteristic cycles. MSC: 14J60, 14F10, 53C07 Keywords: meromorphic flat bundle, 𝒟 \mathcal{D} -modules, meromorphic Lagrangian cover, meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity, boundedness, non-abelian Hodge theory



1
Introduction

1.1
Boundedness

1.1.1
Boundedness for coherent sheaves

We recall the notion of boundedness
for a class of coherent sheaves.
Let
X
be a complex smooth and connected projective variety.
A class
\mathcal{C}
of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
is called bounded
if there exists a complex variety
S
and
a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{S\times X}
-module
\mathcal{E}
flat over
S

with the following property.

•

For any
E\in\mathcal{C}
,
there exists a closed point
s\in S

such that
the restriction of
\mathcal{E}

to
s\times X
is isomorphic to
E
.

For example, we may consider the class
\mathcal{C}_{1}
of
locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
whose rank and Chern classes are fixed.
We may also consider the subclass
\mathcal{C}_{2}\subset\mathcal{C}_{1}
of
semistable sheaves.
Then, it is easy to see that
\mathcal{C}_{1}
is unbounded unless
\dim X=0
.
It is well known that
\mathcal{C}_{2}
is bounded.

This kind of boundedness implies various finiteness properties.
For example,
if a class
\mathcal{C}
is bounded,
there exists
C>0
such that

\dim H^{j}(X,E)<C

for any
E\in\mathcal{C}
and any
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.

1.1.2
Boundedness for meromorphic flat bundles

In this paper,
we are interested in
the boundedness of some families of meromorphic flat bundles.
Though we shall study meromorphic flat bundles
on projective varieties over any algebraically closed field
of characteristic
0
,
we restrict ourselves to the complex case
in this introduction,
and we use the complex analytic language.

Let
H
be a hypersurface of
X
.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module
with an integrable connection
\nabla
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H)
denote the category of
such coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-modules with
an integrable connections.
For positive integers
r
and
N
,
let
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
denote the full subcategory
of
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H)

consisting of

(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H)

satisfying
\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)\leq r

and the following condition.

•

There exists a lattice
\mathcal{L}\subset V

such that

\nabla(\mathcal{L})\subset\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega^{1}(\log H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(NH)
.
Here, a lattice of
V
means a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule
which generates
V
over
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
.

There are two types of boundedness results
for
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
.
First, we have the following boundedness
as meromorphic integrable connections
obtained as a special case of Corollary
6.21
.

Theorem 1.1

There exists a smooth complex variety
\mathcal{S}_{1}

and a coherent torsion-free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\times X}
-module

E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}

with a meromorphic integrable connection

\nabla_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}:E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}\to E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\times X/\mathcal{S}_{1}}(\log(\mathcal{S}_{1}\times H))\bigl(N(\mathcal{S}_{1}\times H)\bigr)

relative to
\mathcal{S}_{1}
such that the following holds.

•

E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}
is a reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\times X}
-module
flat over
\mathcal{S}_{1}
.
For any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
,

E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}|a\times X}
is reflexive.

•

For any
(V,\nabla)\in\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,{\boldsymbol{m}})
,
there exist
s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}

such that

(E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},\nabla_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})_{|s\times X}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)\simeq(V,\nabla)
.

We also have the boundedness as
\mathcal{D}
-modules
obtained as a special case of Theorem
8.3
.
In general,
for any smooth morphism
X_{T}\to T
of smooth complex algebraic varieties,
let
\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}
denote the sheaf of
linear differential operators on
X_{T}
relative to
T
.
For any
\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}
-module
\mathcal{N}
which is flat over
T
,
and for any closed point
u\in T
,
let
\mathcal{N}_{u}
denote the restriction of
\mathcal{N}

to
X_{T}\times_{T}u
.

Theorem 1.2

There exist a complex variety
\mathcal{S}

and a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{S}\times X/\mathcal{S}}
-module
\mathcal{M}

flat over
\mathcal{S}

such that the following holds.

•

For any
(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
,
there exists
s\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{C}})

such that
\mathcal{M}_{s}\simeq(V,\nabla)
.

Remark 1.3

E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}(\ast(\mathcal{S}_{1}\times H))

in Theorem
1.1

is not necessarily
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\times X/\mathcal{S}_{1}}
-coherent,
in general.

As a consequence of Theorem
1.2
,
we obtain the following cohomological boundedness.

Corollary 1.4

There exists a constant
C(X,H,r,N)>0
such that

\dim H^{j}(X,V\otimes\Omega^{\bullet})<C(X,H,r,N)

for any
(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
.

According to
[
11
,
16
]
,
the boundedness of
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
was asked by Esnault and Langer.
Hu and Teyssier
[
11
]

proved the cohomological boundedness
in the case
\dim X\leq 2
.
They obtained much more detailed upper bound for the constant
C(X,H,r,N)
.
In the case
X=\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
Sabbah
[
27
]

studied the finiteness of rigid meromorphic flat bundles
by using the Arinkin-Katz program.

1.1.3
Boundedness for holonomic
\mathcal{D}
-modules
with dominated characteristic cycles

Related with the boundedness as in
Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.2
,
we study the boundedness of
the family of holonomic
\mathcal{D}
-modules
whose characteristic cycles are dominated.

For any holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
M
,
there exists a good filtration
F
.
We obtain the coherent
\mathcal{O}_{T^{\ast}X}
-module
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)^{\sim}
.
The support
\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M)
is a complex Lagrangian cone in
T^{\ast}X
,
and called the characteristic variety.
Let
\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M)=\bigcup_{i}\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M)_{i}

denote the irreducible decomposition.
The characteristic cycle

\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)=\sum_{i}\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)_{i}\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M)_{i}

is a refined invariant,
where
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)_{i}
denotes the multiplicity
of
\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M)_{i}
in
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)^{\sim}
.

Let
\Lambda_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,\ell)
be complex Lagrangian cones
in
T^{\ast}X
.
We obtain a cycle
\Lambda=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}m_{i}\Lambda_{i}
.
We say
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)\leq\Lambda

if
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}a_{i}\Lambda_{i}

with
a_{i}\leq m_{i}
.
Let
\mathcal{C}(X,\Lambda)

denote the family of holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules
M

such that
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)\leq\Lambda
.

Theorem 1.5
(Theorem

8.4
,
Lemma
8.10
)

The family
\mathcal{C}(X,\Lambda)
is bounded
in the sense that
there exists a complex algebraic variety
S

and a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{S\times X/S}
-module

\mathcal{M}
such that the following holds.

•

\mathcal{M}
is flat over
S
,
and

\mathcal{M}_{s}\in\mathcal{C}(X,\Lambda)

for any
s\in S
.

•

For any
M\in\mathcal{C}(X,\Lambda)
,
there exists
s\in S

such that

M\simeq\mathcal{M}_{s}
.

1.2
Good meromorphic flat bundles

Let
X
be a complex projective manifold
with a normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
We recall the notion of good meromorphic flat bundles.

1.2.1
Good set of ramified irregular values

Let
\mathcal{O}_{X}
denote the sheaf of holomorphic functions.
Let
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
denote the sheaf of
meromorphic functions allowing poles along
H
.
The stalks at
P\in H
are denoted by

\mathcal{O}_{X,P}
and
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}
.
The completion of
\mathcal{O}_{X,P}
is denoted by
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,P}
.
We set

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,P}
.
Let
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
be a local coordinate around
P

such that
H=\bigcup_{j=1}^{\ell}\{z_{j}=0\}
around
P
.
For any positive integer
k
,
we set

\mathcal{O}_{X,P}^{(H,k)}=\mathcal{O}_{X,P}[z_{1}^{1/k},\ldots,z_{\ell}^{1/k}]
.
It is independent of the choice of the coordinate system.
We also set

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X,P}=\mathcal{O}^{(H,k)}_{X,P}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,P}
.
We consider

\mathcal{O}^{(H,k)}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}

and

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}

similarly.

We recall a notion of order
for an element

\mathfrak{a}
of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}
.

•

If
\mathfrak{a}
is contained in
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,P}^{(H,k)}
,
we set

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})=(0,\ldots,0)

in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\ell}
.

•

For any
\mathfrak{a}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H.k)}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,P}^{(H,k)}
,
if there exists
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in(\frac{1}{k}{\mathbb{Z}})_{\leq 0}^{\ell}

such that

\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}z_{i}^{-m_{i}}\mathfrak{a}

is an invertible element of

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X,P}
,
we set

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})={\boldsymbol{m}}
.

•

Otherwise,

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})
is not defined.

For example,
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(z_{1}^{-1}z_{2}^{-1})=(-1,-1)
.
But,

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(z_{1}^{-1}z_{2})

and
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(z_{1}^{-1}+z_{2}^{-1})
are not defined.
We can also consider
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})

for any
\mathfrak{a}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{(H,k)}(\ast H)_{P}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X,P}
.

We define the partial order on
(\frac{1}{k}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\ell}

by
{\boldsymbol{m}}\leq{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow\forall i,m_{i}\leq m_{i}^{\prime}
.

Definition 1.6

Let

\mathcal{I}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X,P}

be a finite subset which is invariant under the Galois action.
It is called good,
if the following conditions are satisfied.

•

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})

are defined for any
\mathfrak{a}
in
\mathcal{I}
,
and
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})
are defined
for any
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}
in
\mathcal{I}
.

•

The sets

\{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})\,|\,\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}\}

and

\{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})\,|\,\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{I}\}

are totally ordered
with respect to the partial order
\leq
on

(\frac{1}{k}{\mathbb{Z}})^{\ell}
.

For example,
the set
\{z_{1}^{-1}z_{2}^{-1},z_{1}^{-1},0\}
is good,
but

\{z_{1}^{-1}z_{2}^{-1},z_{1}^{-1},z_{2}^{-1},0\}
is not good.

Remark 1.7

Though the concepts are explained
for finite subsets of

{\mathbb{C}}[\![z_{1}^{1/k},\ldots,z_{\ell}^{1/k},z_{\ell+1},\ldots,z_{n}]\!][z_{1}^{-1},\ldots,z_{\ell}^{-1}]
,
they are obviously generalized to
the context of
finite subsets of

R[\![z_{1}^{1/k},\ldots,z_{\ell}^{1/k},z_{\ell+1},\ldots,z_{n}]\!][z_{1}^{-1},\ldots,z_{\ell}^{-1}]
,
for any ring
R
.

1.2.2
Good meromorphic flat bundles

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module
with an integrable connection.
For
P\in H
,
we set

\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{P}^{(H,k)}=\mathcal{E}_{P}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X,P}
.
We say
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is good at
P

if there exist a good set

\mathcal{I}_{P}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X}(\ast P)/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X,P}

and a decomposition

(\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{(H,k)}_{P},\nabla)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}_{P}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)}\bigl((\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{(H,k)}_{P})_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}\bigr)

such that
each
(\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{(H,k)}_{P})_{\mathfrak{a}}
has a lattice

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{P,\mathfrak{a}}
such that

(\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits)\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{P,\mathfrak{a}}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{P,\mathfrak{a}}\otimes\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log H)
.
Here,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,k)}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}
is a lift of
\mathfrak{a}
.
We say
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is good on
(X,H)

if it is good at any
P\in H
.

Remark 1.8

It is known that

\mathcal{I}_{P}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)\subset\mathcal{O}^{(H,k)}_{X}(\ast P)/\mathcal{O}^{(H,k)}_{X,P}
.

1.2.3
Boundedness of
good meromorphic flat bundles as meromorphic objects

Let
\mathcal{X}_{S}\to S
be a smooth projective morphism of
smooth complex algebraic varieties
such that each fiber is connected.
Let
\mathcal{H}_{S}\subset\mathcal{X}_{S}
be
a relatively normal crossing hypersurface.

For any
s\in S
,
we set
(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s})=(\mathcal{X}_{S},\mathcal{H}_{S})\times_{S}s
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits^{\mathop{\rm good}\nolimits}(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s},r,N)

denote the full subcategory
of
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s},r,N)
,
consisting of good meromorphic flat bundles
contained in
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s},r,N)
.
Let
S({\mathbb{C}})
denote the set of the closed points of
S
.

Theorem 1.9
(Theorem

6.18
)

The family

\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits^{\mathop{\rm good}\nolimits}(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s},r,N)

(s\in S({\mathbb{C}}))

is bounded in the following sense.

•

There exists a smooth complex variety

\mathcal{S}
over
S
.
We set
(\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}}):=(\mathcal{X}_{S},\mathcal{H}_{S})\times_{S}\mathcal{S}
.

•

There exists a locally free

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}}}
-module

\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{S}}

equipped with
a meromorphic integrable relative connection

\nabla_{\mathcal{S}}:\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{S}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}}/\mathcal{S}}(\log\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}})(N\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}})
.

•

For any
s\in S({\mathbb{C}})

and
(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits^{\mathop{\rm good}\nolimits}(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s},r,N)
,
there exists

\widetilde{s}\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{C}})
over
s

such that

(V,\nabla)\simeq\bigl(\mathcal{L}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}}),\nabla\bigr)_{|\widetilde{s}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}}}
.
Moreover,

\mathcal{L}_{|\widetilde{s}\times_{S}\mathcal{X}_{S}}

equals
the good Deligne-Malgrange lattice of

(V,\nabla)
.

Let us mention that
the boundedness in Theorem
1.9

is reduced to a more classical boundedness result
of torsion-free coherent sheaves due to Maruyama

[
20
]
(see Proposition
5.30
)
once we obtain the estimate for
the first and second Chern classes
of a good Deligne-Malgrange lattice
in §
4
.

1.2.4
Resolution of turning points

If
\dim X=1
,
any meromorphic flat bundle is good
by the classical Hukuhara-Levelt-Turrittin theorem.
However, if
\dim X\geq 2
,
there exist meromorphic flat bundles which are not good.
There is the following theorem
which was conjectured by Sabbah
and proved by Kedlaya in
[
14
,
15
]

and the author in
[
23
,
24
]
.
Note that the result of Kedlaya is more general
and available for the complex analytic case
not only for the algebraic case.

Theorem 1.10

There exists a projective morphism

\varphi:X^{\prime}\to X

such that
(i)
H^{\prime}=\varphi^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing,
(ii)
X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus H
,
(iii)
\varphi^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.

For the boundedness of the family of meromorphic flat bundles
which are not necessarily good,
we have to control the resolutions.

1.2.5
Resolutions and boundedness

Let
X
be a smooth projective irreducible complex variety
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
r
and
N
be positive integers.
We have the following more useful boundedness theorem
for
\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,N)

as a special case
of Theorem
6.20
and Theorem
8.2
.

Theorem 1.11

There exist a smooth complex variety
\mathcal{S}
,
a complex variety
\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}
which is smooth projective over
\mathcal{S}

with a morphism
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}:\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{S}\times X
over
\mathcal{S}
,
a hypersurface
\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}}\subset\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}

which is normal crossing relative to
\mathcal{S}
,
a good meromorphic flat bundle
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
on

(\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}},\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}})
relative to
\mathcal{S}
,
and a lattice
\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}}\subset\mathcal{V}

such that the following holds.

•

\rho^{-1}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{S}\times H)=\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}}
,
and
\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}\setminus\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}}\simeq\mathcal{S}\times(X\setminus H)
.

•

For any
(V,\nabla)\in\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,N)
,
there exists
s\in\mathcal{S}

such that

\rho_{s}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)\simeq(\mathcal{V},\nabla)_{s}

and that

\widehat{E}_{s}

is the Deligne-Malgrange lattice of

\rho_{s}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
.

There also exists a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}/\mathcal{S}}
-module

\mathcal{M}
flat over
\mathcal{S}
such that the following holds.

•

\mathcal{M}(\ast\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}})=\mathcal{V}
.

•

For any
(V,\nabla)\in\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,N)
,
there exists
s\in\mathcal{S}

such that

\rho_{s}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)\simeq\mathcal{M}_{s}
.

We obtain Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.2

from Theorem
1.11

by taking the push-forward.
We also apply Theorem
1.11

to study Theorem
1.5
.

1.3
Outline for Theorem
1.11

1.3.1
Meromorphic Lagrangian covers

Let
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

be a closed algebraic subset such that
each irreducible component is dominant and proper over
X\setminus H
.
We also assume that the smooth part of
\Sigma
is Lagrangian.
Such
\Sigma
is called a meromorphic Lagrangian cover.
We introduce
a condition for good behaviour of meromorphic Lagrangian covers
around the boundary.

Definition 1.12

Let
x\in H
.
We say that
\Sigma
is good at
x

if there exist a good set of ramified irregular values

\mathcal{I}(\Sigma,x)\subset\mathcal{O}^{(H,k)}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}/\mathcal{O}^{(H,k)}_{X,x}
,
and a covering
\rho_{x}:X_{x}^{\prime}\to X_{x}
branched along
H\cap X_{x}

such that

\rho_{x}^{\ast}\Sigma=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\Sigma,x)}\bigl(d\mathfrak{a}+\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}\bigr),

where
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}
are logarithmic,
i.e.,
the closure in
T^{\ast}X_{x}^{\prime}(\log H_{x}^{\prime})
is proper over
X_{x}^{\prime}
.
Here,
H_{x}^{\prime}=\rho_{x}^{-1}(H_{x})
.

We say
\Sigma
is good on
(X,H)

if
\Sigma
is good at any
x\in H
.

For positive integers
r
and
N
,
let
\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
denote
the set of meromorphic Lagrangian covers

\Sigma

such that
the closure
\overline{\Sigma}

in
T^{\ast}X(\log H)(NH)
is proper over
X
,
and the degree of the projection
\Sigma\to X\setminus H

is less than
r
.
This family is bounded,
and we can take a resolution in a uniform way.

Proposition 1.13
(Corollary

2.67
)

There exist a complex smooth variety
S
,
a closed subset

\mathcal{Z}\subset S\times T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

flat over
S
,
and a projective morphism

\varphi:\mathcal{X}_{S}\to S\times X

such that the following holds.

•

\mathcal{X}_{S}\to S
is smooth and projective,
and
\mathcal{H}_{S}=\varphi^{-1}(S\times H)

is normal crossing relative to
S
.

•

\mathcal{X}_{S}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{S}\simeq S\times(X\setminus H)
,

•

For any
\Sigma\in\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
,
there exists
s\in S({\mathbb{C}})
such that
\mathcal{Z}\times_{S}s=\Sigma
.
Moreover,

\varphi_{s}^{\ast}(\Sigma)
is good on

(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s})
,
where
\varphi_{s}:\mathcal{X}_{S}\times_{S}s\to X

is induced by
\varphi
.

1.3.2
Wild harmonic bundles

Let
(E,\theta)
be a Higgs bundle on a complex manifold
Y
.
Let
h
be a Hermitian metric of
E
.
We obtain the Chern connection
\nabla_{h}
,
and the adjoint
\theta^{\dagger}_{h}
of
\theta
.
We say
(E,\theta,h)
is a harmonic bundle
if the connection
\mathbb{D}_{h}^{1}=\nabla_{h}+\theta+\theta^{\dagger}_{h}
is flat.
For the Higgs bundle
(E,\theta)
,
we have the corresponding
\mathcal{O}_{T^{\ast}Y}
-module
\widetilde{E}
,
whose supports are finite over
Y
.
The support
\Sigma_{\theta}
is called the spectral cover.
By using Gabber’s theorem
[
4
]
,
if
(E,\theta)
underlies a harmonic bundle
(E,\theta,h)
,

\Sigma_{\theta}
is Lagrangian.

Let
X
be a smooth projective complex variety
with a normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
(E,\theta,h)
be a harmonic bundle on
X\setminus H
.
We say
(E,\theta,h)
is wild on
(X,H)

if
\Sigma_{\theta}
is a meromorphic Lagrangian cover,
i.e.,
\Sigma_{\theta}
is an algebraic subset of

T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)
.
The harmonic bundle is called good on
(X,H)

if
\Sigma_{\theta}
is good on
(X,H)
.

1.3.3
The associated meromorphic objects

From a harmonic bundle on
(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h)
on
X\setminus H
,
we have the flat bundle
(E,\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h})
on
X\setminus H
.
We have the underlying holomorphic vector bundle

\mathcal{E}^{1}=(E,\overline{\partial}_{E}+\theta^{\dagger}_{h})
.
We regard it as an
\mathcal{O}_{X\setminus H}
-module.
Let
\iota:X\setminus H\to X
denote the inclusion.
We obtain the sheaf

\iota_{\ast}(\mathcal{E}^{1})
on
X
.

For any
x\in H
,
let
(X_{x},z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
be a coordinate neighbourhood
such that
X_{x}\cap H=\bigcup_{j=1}^{\ell}\{z_{j}=0\}
.
We obtain an
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}
-module

(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1})_{x}:=\Bigl\{s\in(\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{E}^{1})_{x}\,\Big|\,|s|_{h}=O\Bigl(\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}|z_{i}|^{-C}\Bigr)\,\,\,\exists C>0\Bigr\}.

We obtain an
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module

\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1}\subset\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{E}^{1}

whose stalk at
x
is
(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1})_{x}
.
We recall the following proposition from
[
24
]
.

Proposition 1.14

•

\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1}
is a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module,
and
\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h}
is an integrable connection
of
\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1}
.
Moreover,

(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1},\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h})\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)

if and only if

\Sigma_{\theta}\in\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
.

•

(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h)
is good on
(X,H)

if and only if
(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1},\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h})
is good on
(X,H)
.
We have

\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1},\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h},x)=\bigl\{2\mathfrak{a}\,\big|\,\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\Sigma_{\theta},x)\bigr\}
for any
x\in H
.

We have the following theorem
[
24
]
.

Theorem 1.15

For any semisimple meromorphic flat bundle
(V,\nabla)
on
(X,H)
,
there exists a wild harmonic bundle
(E,\theta,h)
such that

(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1},\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h})\simeq(V,\nabla)
.

Let
(V,\nabla)
be a meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
There exists a Jordan-Hölder filtration,
namely,
each graded piece is simple.
By the above theorem,
we have the harmonic bundle
(E,\theta)

corresponding to
the associated graded meromorphic flat bundle.
We set
\Sigma(V,\nabla):=\Sigma_{\theta}
.

Corollary 1.16

Let
\varphi:X^{\prime}\to X
be a projective morphism
such that
(i)
H^{\prime}=\varphi^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing,
(ii)
\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma(V,\nabla)
is good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.
Then,
\varphi^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
is good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.

Remark 1.17

We shall introduce the notion of
meromorphic Lagrangian irregularities,
which is equivalences
of meromorphic Lagrangian covers.
We shall associate a meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity
with a meromorphic flat bundle.

1.3.4
Outline of a proof of Theorem
1.11

For
(X,H,r,N)
, we have
a smooth complex variety
S

and a projective morphism

\varphi:\mathcal{X}_{S}\to S\times X
over
S

with a normal crossing hypersurface

\mathcal{H}_{S}

as in Proposition
1.13
.
There exists
N^{\prime}>0
such that
the closure of
\mathcal{Z}

in
T^{\ast}(\mathcal{X}_{S}/S)(\log\mathcal{H}_{S})(N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}_{S})

is proper over
\mathcal{X}_{S}
.
There exists

\mathcal{S}\to S

and

(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{S}},\nabla)

as in Theorem
1.9
with
N^{\prime}
.

We have the induced morphism

\varphi_{\mathcal{S}}:\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{S}\times X
,
induced by
\mathcal{X}_{S}\to S\times X
.
We set

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)=\bigl((\varphi_{\mathcal{S}})_{\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{L}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}})\bigr),\nabla\bigr).

By taking a stratification,
we may assume that
it is flat over
\mathcal{S}
.
We may also assume that
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
has a good lattice

\mathcal{L}_{1}
relative to
\mathcal{S}
.

Let
(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
.
We have
\Sigma(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits(X,H,r,N)
.
There exists
s\in S
such that

\varphi_{s}^{\ast}(\Sigma(V,\nabla))
is good on

(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s})=(\mathcal{X}_{S},\mathcal{H}_{S})_{s}
.
It implies

\varphi_{s}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
is good on

(\mathcal{X}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s})
.
There exists
\widetilde{s}\in\mathcal{S}
over
s

such that

(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}}}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}}),\nabla)_{\widetilde{s}}\simeq\varphi_{s}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
.
We can construct a
coherent
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}}/\mathcal{S}}
-submodule

\mathcal{M}
with the desired property
by considering the
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}}/\mathcal{S}}
-module
generated by
\mathcal{L}_{1}(N_{1}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}})
for a sufficiently
large
N_{1}
.

2
Lagrangian meromorphic covers

2.1
Symmetric products of vector spaces

2.1.1
The Chow embedding

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
V
be a finite dimensional
\mathbb{K}
-vector space.
We also regard
V
as a
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
r
be a positive integer.
Let
\mathfrak{S}_{r}
denote the
r
-th symmetric group
acting on

V^{r}=\overbrace{V\times\cdots\times V}^{r}

by permutation of the components.
Let
S^{r}(V)
denote the quotient space,
i.e.,
S^{r}(V)=V^{r}/\mathfrak{S}_{r}
.
The image of
(v_{1},\ldots,v_{r})\in V^{r}
in
S^{r}(V)

is denoted by
\prod_{i=1}^{r}v_{i}
.
Let
\bigl[\prod_{i=1}^{r}v_{i}\bigr]\subset V

denote the subset induced by
the tuple
v_{1},\ldots,v_{r}
.

Let
\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}(\mathbb{K}\oplus V)

denote the vector space obtained as
the
r
-th symmetric tensor product
of
\mathbb{K}\oplus V
.
We also regard it as a
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
\iota:V\to\mathbb{K}\oplus V
be given by

\iota(v)=1\oplus v
.
We obtain the morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

S^{r}(V)\to\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}(\mathbb{K}\oplus V)

defined by

\prod_{i=1}^{r}v_{i}\longmapsto\prod_{i=1}^{r}\iota(v_{i}).

According to the theorem of Chow and van der Wearden
(see
[
5
, §4.1, Theorem 1.1]
),
it is a closed immersion of algebraic
\mathbb{K}
-varieties.

2.1.2
Partitions

For any closed point
\alpha\in S^{r}(V)
,
there exist

\alpha_{i}\in V
and
m_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

(i=1,\ldots,\ell(\alpha))

such that

\alpha_{i}\neq\alpha_{j}

(i\neq j)
,

m_{i}\geq m_{j}

(i\leq j)

and

\alpha=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(\alpha)}\alpha_{i}^{m_{i}}
.
The tuple
(m_{i}\,|\,i=1,\ldots,\ell(\alpha))

is a partition of
r
,
denoted by
{\tt P}(\alpha)
.
The number
\ell(\alpha)

and the partition
{\tt P}(\alpha)

are well defined for
\alpha\in S^{r}(V)
.

2.1.3
Stratification by partitions

Let

{\tt P}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{\ell})

be a partition of
r
,
i.e.,
{\tt P}
is a decreasing sequence of
positive integers
such that
\sum m_{i}=r
.
We set
I_{m}({\tt P})=\{1\leq i\leq\ell\,|\,m_{i}=m\}
.
We obtain the decomposition

\{1,\ldots,\ell\}=\bigsqcup I_{m}({\tt P})

by which
V^{\ell}=\prod V^{I_{m}({\tt P})}
.
Let
\mathfrak{S}_{I_{m}({\tt P})}
denote
the group of automorphisms of the finite subset
I_{m}({\tt P})
,
which is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{S}_{|I_{m}({\tt P})|}
.
The natural
\mathfrak{S}_{I_{m}({\tt P})}

on
V^{I_{m}({\tt P})}

induces
the action of

\prod\mathfrak{S}_{I_{m}({\tt P})}
on
V^{\ell}
.
The quotient is naturally isomorphic to

\prod S^{|I_{m}({\tt P})|}(V)
.

We obtain the finite morphism

\Psi_{{\tt P}}:V^{\ell}\to S^{r}(V)

defined by

\Psi_{{\tt P}}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell})=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}v_{i}^{m_{i}}
.
Let
S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)
denote the image of

\Psi_{{\tt P}}
with the reduced structure.
It is also the image of the induced morphism

\prod S^{|I_{m}({\tt P})|}(V)\to S^{r}(V)
.
Let

(V^{\ell})^{\circ}\subset V^{\ell}

denote the Zariski open subset of

(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell})
such that

v_{i}\neq v_{j}

(i\neq j)
.
Let
S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)^{\circ}
denote the image of

(V^{\ell})^{\circ}
by
\Psi_{{\tt P}}
.
It is a Zariski open subset in

S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)
.
We have

(V^{\ell})^{\circ}/\prod\mathfrak{S}_{I_{m}({\tt P})}\simeq S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)^{\circ}
.

Let
{\tt P}_{1}=(m_{i}\,|\,i=1,\ldots,\ell)
and

{\tt P}_{2}=(n_{j}\,|\,j=1,\ldots,k)
be partitions of
r
.
We say
{\tt P}_{1}\prec{\tt P}_{2}

if the following holds.

•

There exists a surjection

\varphi:\{1,\ldots,k\}\to\{1,\ldots,\ell\}

such that

m_{i}=\sum_{\varphi(j)=i}n_{j}
.

This defines a partial order
\prec
on the set of partitions of
r
.
We have

S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)=\bigsqcup_{{\tt P}\prec{\tt P}^{\prime}}S^{r}_{{\tt P}^{\prime}}(V)^{\circ}
.
In particular,
we obtain the stratification of
S^{r}(V)

by locally Zariski closed subsets

S^{r}(V)=\bigsqcup_{{\tt P}}S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)^{\circ}
.

2.1.4
Universal subsets

For
j=1,\ldots,r
,
we set

\Delta_{0,j}(V)=\bigl\{(v_{0},v_{1},\ldots,v_{r})\,\big|\,v_{0}=v_{j}\bigr\}\subset V\times V^{r}
.
We set

Z(V^{r})=\bigcup_{j=1}^{r}\Delta_{0,j}(V)
.
The
\mathfrak{S}_{r}
-action on
V^{r}

induces an
\mathfrak{S}_{r}
-action
on
Z(V^{r})
.
Let
Z(S^{r}(V))
denote the quotient space.
It is naturally a closed subvariety of

V\times S^{r}(V)
.
We have the naturally defined finite morphism

Z(S^{r}(V))\to S^{r}(V)
.

Let
{\tt P}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{\ell})
be a partition of
r
.
We obtain

Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)^{\circ}):=Z(S^{r}(V))\times_{S^{r}(V)}S_{{\tt P}}^{r}(V)^{\circ},\quad\quad Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)):=Z(S^{r}(V))\times_{S^{r}(V)}S_{{\tt P}}^{r}(V).

Let
Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)^{\circ})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

and
Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V))_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

denote the subvarieties
of
Z(S^{r}(V))
with the reduced structure.
The projection

Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)^{\circ})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)^{\circ}

is a proper etale cover of degree
\ell
.
The projection

Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V))_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V)

is a finite morphism.

2.1.5
Induced finite morphisms

Let
Y
be an irreducible
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a morphism
\phi:Y\to S^{r}(V)
of algebraic
\mathbb{K}
-varieties.
Let
\Sigma(\phi)
denote the fiber product of
\phi

and
Z(S^{r}(V))\to S^{r}(V)
.
The natural morphism

\Sigma(\phi)\to Y
is finite.
For any closed point
y\in Y
,
the set of the closed points of

\Sigma(\phi)\times_{Y}y

is the tuple given by
\phi(y)\in S^{r}(V)
.

There exists a unique partition
{\tt P}(\phi)

such that

\phi(Y)\subset S^{r}_{{\tt P}(\phi)}(V)

and that

U(\phi):=\phi^{-1}(S^{r}_{{\tt P}(\phi)}(V)^{\circ})\neq\emptyset
.
We obtain

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

as the fiber product of

Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(V))_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
and
Y
.
Let
\Sigma(\phi)^{\circ}_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

denote the fiber product of

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
and
U(\phi)
.
The projection
\Sigma(\phi)^{\circ}_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to U(\phi)

is a proper etale cover of degree
\ell
.

2.2
Symmetric products of vector bundles

2.2.1
Symmetric products and locally closed subsets

Let
X
be an irreducible normal algebraic variety over
\mathbb{K}
.
The product bundle
X\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{K}[t]
is
also denoted by
\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be an algebraic vector bundle on
X
.
Let
S^{r}(\mathcal{E})

be the quotient of

\mathcal{E}^{r}=\overbrace{\mathcal{E}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}\mathcal{E}}^{r}

by the natural action of
\mathfrak{S}_{r}
.
As in §
2.1.1
,
there exists the Chow embedding

S^{r}\bigl(\mathcal{E}\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E}\bigr).

We regard
S^{r}(\mathcal{E})

as a Zariski closed subset of

\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E}\bigr)
.

Lemma 2.1

Let
Z\subset X
be a closed subset with
\dim Z\leq\dim X-2
.
Any section
\phi
of
S^{r}(\mathcal{E})
on
X\setminus Z

uniquely extends to a section of
S^{r}(\mathcal{E})
on
X
.

Proof
Let
\widetilde{\phi}
be the induced section of

\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E})
on
X\setminus Z
.
It uniquely extends to a section of

\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E})
on
X
.
Because
S^{r}(\mathcal{E})
is closed in

\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E})
,
it factors through
S^{r}(\mathcal{E})
.

For any partition
{\tt P}
of
r
,
we obtain the closed subvariety

S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})\subset S^{r}(\mathcal{E})

as in §
2.1.3

such that the fiber of
S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})
over
x\in X

is
S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E}_{|x})
.
Similarly,
we obtain the Zariski open subset

S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ}\subset S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})

such that
the fiber of
S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ}
over
x\in X

is
S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E}_{|x})^{\circ}
.

By the construction in §
2.1.4
,
we obtain the Zariski closed subset

Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E}))_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset\mathcal{E}\times_{X}S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})

such that the fiber over
x\in X

is
Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E}_{|x}))_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.
The induced morphism

Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E}))\to S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

is finite.
We have the closed subset

Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset\mathcal{E}\times_{X}S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ}

such that the fiber over
x\in X

is
Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E}_{|x})^{\circ})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.
The induced morphism

Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ}

is proper and etale of degree
\ell
,
where
{\tt P}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{\ell})
.

2.2.2
Finite covers associated with sections

Let
\phi:X\to S^{r}(\mathcal{E})
be any section.
For each closed point
x\in X
,
we obtain the partition
{\tt P}(\phi(x))
of
r

determined by
\phi(x)\in S^{r}(\mathcal{E}_{|x})
.
(See §
2.1.2
.)
The following lemma is obvious
by the stratification in §
2.1.3
.

Lemma 2.2

There uniquely exist a partition
{\tt P}(\phi)
of
r

and a non-empty Zariski open subset
U(\phi)\subset X

such that
(i)
{\tt P}(\phi(x))={\tt P}(\phi)

(x\in U(\phi))
,
(ii)
{\tt P}(\phi)\prec{\tt P}(\phi(x))
and

{\tt P}(\phi)\neq{\tt P}(\phi(x))
for any
x\in X\setminus U(\phi)
.

We set
\ell(\phi)=\ell(\phi(x))

(x\in U(\phi))
.
We obtain

\phi(U(\phi))\subset S^{r}_{{\tt P}(\phi)}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ}
.
Let

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits,U(\phi)}\subset\mathcal{E}_{|U(\phi)}

denote the fiber product of

\phi:U(\phi)\to S^{r}_{{\tt P}(\phi)}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ}

and
Z(S^{r}_{{\tt P}}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to S^{r}_{{\tt P}(\phi)}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ}
.
The projection

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits,U(\phi)}\to U(\phi)

is a proper etale cover of degree
\ell(\phi)
.

Let
\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
denote the closure of

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits,U(\phi)}
in
\mathcal{E}

with the reduced scheme structure.
Note that

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
is finite over
X

because it is contained in
the fiber product of

\phi:X\to S^{r}(\mathcal{E})

and
Z(S^{r}(\mathcal{E}))\to S^{r}(\mathcal{E})
.
The natural isomorphism

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits,U(\phi)}\to U(\phi)\times_{S^{r}_{{\tt P}(\phi)}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ}}Z(S_{{\tt P}(\phi)}(\mathcal{E})^{\circ})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

extends
to a morphism of schemes

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to X\times_{S^{r}_{{\tt P}(\phi)}(\mathcal{E})}Z(S_{{\tt P}(\phi)}^{r}(\mathcal{E}))_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.
It induces a bijection of the closed points.
In particular, the following holds.

Lemma 2.3

For any closed point
x\in X
,
the set of the closed points of

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{X}x

equals

[\phi(x)]\subset\mathcal{E}_{|x}
.

Definition 2.4

A finite cover
Z\to X
is defined to be
a morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties
such that each irreducible component of
Z

is finite and dominant over
X
.
We also say that
Z
is a finite cover over
X
.

Lemma 2.5

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to X
is a finite cover.

Proof
Let

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits,U(\phi)}=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{m}\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits,U(\phi),j}

denote the decomposition into connected components.
Let
\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits,j}
denote the closure of

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits,U(\phi),j}
,
which is irreducible,
and finite and dominant over
X
.
Because
\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits,j}
,
we obtain the claim of the lemma.

2.2.3
Sections associated with finite covers

Let
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{E}
be a closed subset
such that
\Sigma\to X
is a finite cover.
We consider the reduced structure of
\Sigma
.
Note that the properness implies the finiteness.
There exists a Zariski open subset
X_{0}\subset X

such
\Sigma_{0}:=\Sigma_{|X_{0}}\to X_{0}
is finite and etale.
Let
d
denote the degree.

Lemma 2.6

There exists a unique section
\phi_{\Sigma}
of

S^{d}(\mathcal{E})

such that
the set of the closed points of
\Sigma_{|x}

equals
[\phi_{\Sigma}(x)]

for any closed point
x\in X
.

Proof
We set
\Sigma^{d}:=\overbrace{\Sigma\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}\Sigma}^{d}

and
\Sigma_{0}^{d}:=\overbrace{\Sigma_{0}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}\Sigma_{0}}^{d}
.
We consider

A(\Sigma_{0})=\bigl\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\Sigma_{0}^{d}\,\big|\,x_{i}\neq x_{j}\bigr\}.

Let
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
denote the
d
-th symmetric group
acting on
\Sigma^{d}
in a natural way.
It induces the
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on
A(\Sigma_{0})
,
and we have
A(\Sigma_{0})/\mathfrak{S}_{d}\simeq X_{0}
.
Let
\overline{A}(\Sigma)

denote the closure of
A(\Sigma_{0})

in
\Sigma^{d}
.
It is proper over
X
.
Let
Y(\Sigma)
denote the normalization of
\overline{A}(\Sigma)
.
We have the natural
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on
Y(\Sigma)
.
Note that
Y(\Sigma)/\mathfrak{S}_{d}
is normal
and that
X_{0}=A(\Sigma_{0})/\mathfrak{S}_{d}
is naturally
an open subset of
Y(\Sigma)
.
There exists the natural finite morphism

Y(\Sigma)/\mathfrak{S}_{d}\to X

such that
(i) it induces the identity morphism on
X_{0}
,
(ii) it induces the bijection of the closed points.
Hence,
Y(\Sigma)/\mathfrak{S}_{d}\simeq X
.

Let
\pi:Y(\Sigma)\to X
denote the projection.
Let
\varphi_{i}:Y(\Sigma)\to\Sigma
be the morphism
induced by the projection

\Sigma^{d}\to\Sigma
onto the
i
-th component.
It induces a section
s_{i}
of
\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}
.
We obtain the section

\prod_{i=1}^{d}(1\oplus s_{i})

of
\pi^{\ast}\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{d}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E}\bigr)
.
The section is
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-invariant.
Hence, we obtain the section

\widetilde{\phi}_{\Sigma}
of
\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{d}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E}\bigr)

such that

\pi^{\ast}\widetilde{\phi}_{\Sigma}=\prod_{i=1}^{d}(1\oplus s_{i})
.
By the construction,
there is a section
\phi_{\Sigma_{0}}:X_{0}\to S^{d}(\mathcal{E})_{|X_{0}}

such that
\widetilde{\phi}_{\Sigma|X_{0}}
is induced by
\phi_{\Sigma_{0}}
.
Because
S^{d}(\mathcal{E})
is closed in

\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{d}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E})
,
there exists the section
\phi_{\Sigma}

of
S^{d}(\mathcal{E})
which induces
\widetilde{\phi}_{\Sigma}
.
By the construction,
the set of closed points of
\Sigma_{x}

is given by
\phi_{\Sigma}(x)
.
The uniqueness is clear.

Let
Z\subset X
be a closed subset such that

\dim Z\leq\dim X-2
.
Let
\Sigma_{1}\subset\mathcal{E}_{|X\setminus Z}

be a closed subset
such that

\Sigma_{1}\to X\setminus Z
is a finite cover.

Lemma 2.7

The closure
\overline{\Sigma}_{1}
of
\Sigma_{1}
in
\mathcal{E}

is a finite cover over
X
.

Proof
Let
\phi_{\Sigma_{1}}
be a section of
S^{d}(\mathcal{E})_{|X\setminus Z}

induced by
\Sigma_{1}
.
By Lemma
2.1
,
it uniquely extends to
a section
\phi^{\prime}_{\Sigma_{1}}
of
S^{d}(\mathcal{E})
on
X
.
We obtain the closed subset

\Sigma(\phi^{\prime}_{\Sigma_{1}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset S^{d}(\mathcal{E})
.
Because

\Sigma(\phi^{\prime})_{\Sigma_{1},\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

equals
\Sigma_{1}
over
X\setminus Z
,
it is the closure of
\Sigma_{1}
,
and a finite cover over
X
.

2.3
Stratification on the set of sections of symmetric products

2.3.1
The algebraic set of
sections to a closed subset in a vector bundle

Let
Y
be a projective normal irreducible
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
\mathcal{E}_{Y}
be a coherent reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module.
We regard the finite dimensional
\mathbb{K}
-vector space

H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{E}_{Y})
as a
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
Z
be a closed subset of
Y

with
\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits Z\geq 2

such that
(i)
Y\setminus Z
is regular,
(ii)
\mathcal{E}_{Y|Y\setminus Z}
is a locally free sheaf.
We set
Y^{\circ}=Y\setminus Z

and
\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{Y}=\mathcal{E}_{Y|Y\setminus Z}
.
We have

H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{E}_{Y})=H^{0}(Y^{\circ},\mathcal{E}_{Y}^{\circ})
.
We regard
\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{Y}
as a vector bundle on
Y^{\circ}
.
In particular, it is regarded as a
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
Y^{\circ}(\mathbb{K})
denote the set of the
\mathbb{K}
-valued points
of
Y^{\circ}
.

Lemma 2.8

For any Zariski closed subset
C\subset\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{Y}
,
there exists a Zariski closed subset

H^{0}(Y^{\circ},C)\subset H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{E}_{Y})

determined by the following condition.

•

Let
\phi\in H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{E}_{Y})
.
Then,
\phi(Y^{\circ})\subset C
holds
if and only if

\phi\in H^{0}(Y^{\circ},C)
.

Proof
For any
y\in Y^{\circ}(\mathbb{K})
,
we consider the morphism

\Phi_{y}:H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{E}_{Y})\to\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{Y|y}

determined by
\Phi_{y}(\phi)=\phi(y)
.
We obtain the Zariski closed subset

\Phi_{y}^{-1}(C)\subset H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{E}_{Y})
.
Then,

H^{0}(Y^{\circ},C)=\bigcap_{y\in Y^{\circ}(\mathbb{K})}\Phi_{y}^{-1}(C)

has the desired property.

Corollary 2.9

Sections
Y^{\circ}\to C

bijectively correspond to

\mathbb{K}
-valued points of
the
\mathbb{K}
-variety

H^{0}(Y^{\circ},C)
.

Let
Z_{1}
be another closed subset of
Y

with
\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits Z_{1}\geq 2

such that
(i)
Y\setminus Z_{1}
is regular,
(ii)
\mathcal{E}_{Y|Y\setminus Z_{1}}
is a locally free sheaf.
We set
Y^{\circ}_{1}=Y\setminus Z_{1}

and
\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{1}=\mathcal{E}_{Y|Y_{1}^{\circ}}
.
The following lemma is clear.

Lemma 2.10

Let
C\subset\mathcal{E}^{\circ}

and
C_{1}\subset\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\circ}

be closed subsets
such that
C=C_{1}
in
\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\circ}
.
Then, we have

H^{0}(Y^{\circ},C)=H^{0}(Y_{1}^{\circ},C_{1})

in
H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{E})
.

There exists
a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module

\mathcal{G}
with an epimorphism

\mathcal{G}^{\lor}\to\mathcal{E}_{Y}^{\lor}
,
where
\mathcal{F}^{\lor}={\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{O}_{Y})
.
We obtain a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules

\mathcal{E}_{Y}\to\mathcal{G}
.
Let
\overline{C}
denote the closure of
C

in
\mathcal{G}
.

Lemma 2.11

We have
H^{0}(Y^{\circ},C)=H^{0}(Y,\overline{C})
.

2.3.2
Set of sections of symmetric products

Let
X
be a projective normal
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a coherent reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module.
Let
Z
be a closed subset
X
with
\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits Z\geq 2

such that (i)
X^{\circ}=X\setminus Z
is regular,
(ii)
\mathcal{E}^{\circ}=\mathcal{E}_{|X^{\circ}}
is locally free.
From the coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module

\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E})
,
we obtain the reflexive sheaf

(\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{E})^{\lor})^{\lor}

on
X
,
where
\mathcal{F}^{\lor}={\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{O}_{X})
.
We obtain the closed subset

S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ})\subset\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{r}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\circ}}\oplus\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\bigr)
.
We obtain the
\mathbb{K}
-variety

H^{0}(X,S^{r}(\mathcal{E})):=H^{0}(X^{\circ},S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}))

as in §
2.3.1
.
It is independent of the choice of
Z
.

Corollary 2.12

•

Any
\phi\in H^{0}(X,S^{r}(\mathcal{E}))

corresponds to a section

X^{\circ}\to S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ})
,
and hence
we have the associated
closed subset
\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\circ}

such that
(i)
\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

is finite over
X^{\circ}
,
(ii) any irreducible component of
\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

is dominant over
X^{\circ}
.

•

Let
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{E}^{\circ}
be
a closed subset such that
(i)
\Sigma\to X
is finite,
(ii) any irreducible component of
\Sigma
is dominant over
X
.
Let
d\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
be the degree of
\Sigma
over
X
.
Then, there exists a closed point

\phi\in H^{0}(X,S^{d}(\mathcal{E}))

such that
\Sigma=\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.

2.3.3
Decomposition by partitions

Lemma 2.13

Let
T\subset H^{0}(X,S^{r}(\mathcal{E}))

be any non-empty irreducible Zariski closed subset.
Then, there exists a partition
{\tt P}_{T}

and a non-empty Zariski open subset
U\subset T

such that

{\tt P}(\phi)={\tt P}_{T}
for any closed points
\phi\in U
.

Proof
There exists the universal section

\phi_{T}:T\times X^{\circ}\to(T\times X^{\circ})\times_{X^{\circ}}S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ})
.
There exist a non-empty Zariski open subset

U^{\prime}\subset T\times X^{\circ}

and a partition
{\tt P}_{T}

such that

{\tt P}(\phi)={\tt P}_{T}
for any
\phi\in U^{\prime}
.
There exists a Zariski open subset
U
of
T

which is contained in the image of
U^{\prime}\to T
.
The tuple
({\tt P}_{T},U)
satisfies the desired condition.

By using a Noetherian induction,
we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 2.14

There exists a decomposition

H^{0}(X,S^{r}(\mathcal{E}))=\bigsqcup_{i\in\Lambda}T_{i}

by locally Zariski closed irreducible smooth affine subsets such that

{\tt P}(\phi)

(\phi\in T_{i})
are constant.

Remark 2.15

In Corollary
2.14
,
we do not impose
{\tt P}(\phi_{i})\neq{\tt P}(\phi_{j})

for
\phi_{i}\in T_{i}
and
\phi_{j}\in T_{j}
even if
T_{i}\neq T_{j}
.

2.3.4
Refined decomposition

There exists a locally free sheaf
\mathcal{G}
on
X

with an epimorphism
\mathcal{G}^{\lor}\to\mathcal{E}^{\lor}
.
We obtain the monomorphism
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{G}
.
Each
\phi\in H^{0}(X,S^{r}(\mathcal{E}))

induces

\phi_{\mathcal{G}}\in H^{0}(X,S^{r}(\mathcal{G}))
,
and
we obtain the closed subset

\Sigma(\phi_{\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset\mathcal{G}

which is finite over
X
.
It is the closure of

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
,
and we have

\Sigma(\phi)_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}=\Sigma(\phi_{\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\cap\mathcal{G}_{|X^{\circ}}
.

Lemma 2.16

There exist decompositions

T_{i}=\bigsqcup_{j\in\Gamma(i)}T_{i,j}

by locally Zariski closed irreducible smooth affine subsets
such that the following holds for any
i,j
.

•

There exists a closed subscheme

\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset T_{i,j}\times\mathcal{G}

such that

\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to T_{i,j}

is flat,
and that
the fiber over
\phi\in T_{i,j}

equals
\Sigma(\phi_{\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset\mathcal{G}
.

Proof
Let
T_{i}^{\prime}\subset T_{i}
be any non-empty irreducible Zariski closed subset.
Let
R_{i}^{\prime}
denote the ring of algebraic functions on
T_{i}^{\prime}
,
which is finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
be an algebraic closure
of the fractional field of
R_{i}^{\prime}
.
We set
X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}=X\times_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}^{\prime}

on which we obtain the induced vector bundle

\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
.
We have the open subset

X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}=X^{\circ}\times_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}^{\prime}

on which we obtain the induced vector bundle

\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}^{\circ}
.
The universal section

T_{i}^{\prime}\times X^{\circ}\to T_{i}^{\prime}\times S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ})

induces a section
\phi_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
of

S^{r}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}^{\circ})
on
X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
.
We obtain

\Sigma(\phi_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
.

Let
R_{i}^{\prime}\subset R\subset\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
be an extension of rings
such that
R
is regular and finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
\phi_{R}:X^{\circ}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)\to S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ})\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)

denote the induced section.
Then,

\Sigma(\phi_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

is induced by
\Sigma(\phi_{R,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

and the extension
R\to\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
.
By the generic freeness theorem

[
6
, Theorem 6.9.1]
,
we may assume that
\Sigma(\phi_{R,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

is flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
.
It is finite over
X\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
.
By
[
7
, Theorem 12.2.4]
,
we may assume that
for any closed point
y\in\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)

the fiber
\Sigma(\phi_{R,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)}y
is reduced.

For any closed point
y\in\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
,
let
\phi_{y}:X^{\circ}\to S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ})

denote the section induced by
\phi_{R}
.
For any closed point
x\in X^{\circ}
,
the set of the closed points of

\Sigma(\phi_{R,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{X^{\circ}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)}(x,y)

equals

[\phi_{y,\mathcal{G}}(x)]
.
Hence, the closed points of

\Sigma(\phi_{R,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)}y

equals
the closed points of

\Sigma(\phi_{y,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.
Because both

\Sigma(\phi_{R,\mathcal{G}})\times_{\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)}y

and

\Sigma(\phi_{y,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

are equipped with the reduced structure,
we obtain

\Sigma(\phi_{R,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)}y=\Sigma(\phi_{y,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.

There exists a non-empty affine open subset
U^{\prime}
of
T^{\prime}_{i}

contained in the image of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)\to T^{\prime}_{i}
.
By shrinking
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
,
we may assume that
U^{\prime}
equals the image.
We have the universal section

\phi_{U^{\prime}}:U^{\prime}\times X\to U^{\prime}\times S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ})
.
We obtain

\Sigma(\phi_{U^{\prime},\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset U^{\prime}\times\mathcal{G}
.
We may assume that it is flat over
U^{\prime}
.
Because
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)\to U^{\prime}
is smooth,

\Sigma(\phi_{U^{\prime},\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{U^{\prime}}\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)

is reduced,
and hence
we obtain

\Sigma(\phi_{U^{\prime},\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{U^{\prime}}\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)=\Sigma(\phi_{R,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.
For any
\phi\in U^{\prime}
,
there exists
y\in\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
which is mapped to
\phi
.
We have
\phi_{y}=\phi
.
In
\mathcal{G}
,
we have

\Sigma(\phi_{U^{\prime},\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{U^{\prime}}z=\Sigma(\phi_{R,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)}y=\Sigma(\phi_{\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.

By using this consideration with the Noetherian induction,
we can construct desired decompositions.

There exist a Zariski dense open subset

\Sigma(T_{i,j})^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\cap(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\times T_{i,j})

such that
(i)
\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}\to T_{i,j}

is smooth,
(ii) for each
y\in T_{i,j}
,

\Sigma(T_{i,j})^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{T_{i,j}}y

intersects with each irreducible component of

\Sigma(\phi_{y,\mathcal{G}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
,
where
\phi_{y}
denotes the section corresponding to
y
.
Let
\iota_{0}:\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\times T_{i,j}\to\mathcal{G}\times T_{i,j}
,

\iota_{1}:\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to\mathcal{G}\times T_{i,j}

and

\iota_{2}:\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}\to\mathcal{G}\times T_{i,j}

denote the inclusions.

Lemma 2.17

There exist torsion-free sheaves

\mathcal{F}^{k}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})
on

\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

such that the following holds.

•

The restriction of

\mathcal{F}^{k}

to
\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}

equal the sheaves of algebraic relative
k
-forms

\Omega^{k}_{\Sigma(T_{i,j})^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}/T_{i,j}}
.
In particular,
there exist the natural morphisms

\iota_{1\ast}(\mathcal{F}^{k})\to\iota_{2\ast}\Omega^{k}_{\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}/T_{i,j}}
.

•

There exists
a morphism

\iota_{0\ast}\bigl(\Omega^{k}_{(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\times T_{i,j})/T_{i,j}}\bigr)\to\iota_{1\ast}(\mathcal{F}^{k})

such that the induced morphism

\iota_{0\ast}\bigl(\Omega^{k}_{(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\times T_{i,j})/T_{i,j}}\bigr)\to\iota_{2\ast}\Omega^{k}_{\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}/T_{i,j}}

equals
the morphism induced by the pull back of relative
k
-forms.

By refining the decomposition,
we may also assume that

\mathcal{F}^{k}
are flat over
T_{i,j}
.

Proof
We set

\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\circ}=\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\cap(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\times T_{i,j})
.
Let

j_{1}:\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}\to\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\circ}

and

j_{2}:\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\times T_{i,j}

denote the inclusions.
We obtain the morphism
of quasi-coherent sheaves

j_{2}^{\ast}\Omega^{k}_{(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\times T_{i,j})/T_{i,j}}\to j_{1\ast}\Omega^{k}_{\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}}

on

\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\circ}
.
There exists a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}^{k}_{1}

on
\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\circ}

whose restriction to

\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}

equals

\Omega^{k}_{\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}/T_{i,j}}

(see
[
9
, Problem II.5.15]
, for example).
By taking the sum of

\mathcal{F}^{k}_{1}
and
the images of

j_{2}^{\ast}\Omega^{k}_{(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\times T_{i,j})/T_{i,j}}

in

j_{1\ast}\Omega^{k}_{\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}/T_{i,j}}
,
we obtain a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}^{k}_{2}
on

\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}^{\circ}

with a morphism

j_{2}^{\ast}\Omega^{k}_{\mathcal{E}^{\circ}\times T_{i,j}/T_{i,j}}\to\mathcal{F}_{2}^{k}
.
We extend
\mathcal{F}_{2}^{k}
a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}^{k}

on
\Sigma(T_{i,j})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.

2.3.5
Meromorphic
k
-forms on
\mathcal{E}

Let
H
be a hypersurface of
X
.
Let
\tau
be a meromorphic
k
-form on
\mathcal{E}^{\circ}

whose restriction to

\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{|X^{\circ}\setminus H}
is a regular
k
-form.
Let
T_{i,j}(\tau)
denote the set of
the closed points
\phi
of
T_{i,j}

such that
the restriction of
\tau

to
\Sigma(\phi)_{U(\phi),\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
is
0
.

Lemma 2.18

T_{i,j}(\tau)
are Zariski closed subsets of

T_{i,j}
.

Proof
We may assume that
H
is ample
and that
X\setminus X^{\circ}\subset H
.
Let
\pi:\Sigma(T_{i,j})\to X^{\circ}

and
p:\Sigma(T_{i,j})\to T_{i,j}

denote the projections.
Let
\mathcal{F}^{k}
be a torsion-free sheaf
on
\Sigma(T_{i,j})

as in Lemma
2.17
.
Then,
for a sufficiently large integer
N
,

\tau
induces a section

\Phi_{\tau}

of the locally free sheaf

p_{\ast}\Bigl(\mathcal{F}^{k}\otimes\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}(NH)\Bigr)
.
Because
T_{i,j}(\tau)

equals the
0
-set of
\Phi_{\tau}
,
we obtain the claim of the lemma.

2.4
Conditions for meromorphic finite covers

2.4.1
General case

Let
X
be a normal algebraic variety over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
H
be a hypersurface of
X
such that

X\setminus H
is smooth.

Definition 2.19

A meromorphic finite cover over
(X,H)

is a closed subset
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

such that

\Sigma\to X\setminus H
is a finite cover.

For later use,
we also introduce the following condition.

Definition 2.20

A meromorphic finite cover
\Sigma
over
(X,H)

is called Lagrangian
if the smooth part of
\Sigma
is Lagrangian
with respect to the natural symplectic structure of

T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)
.

2.4.2
Formal conditions

We assume that
X
is smooth, and that
H
is normal crossing.
Let
x\in H
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}_{x}
denote the maximal ideal of
the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
.
Let
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
denote the completion of
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}

with respect to
\mathfrak{m}_{x}
.
For any
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-module
\mathcal{M}
,
we set

\mathcal{M}(\ast H):=\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}
.
For any affine morphism
Z\to X
,
let
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}(Z)
denote the ring of
algebraic functions on
Z\times_{X}\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathcal{O}_{X,x})
.
We set

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(Z):=\mathcal{O}_{X,x}(Z)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
.
In particular,
we obtain the
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)
-algebra

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))
.
We have

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}\bigl(T^{\ast}X(\log H)(NH)\bigr)(\ast H)

for any
N\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Definition 2.21

A surjective morphism of

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)
-algebras

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}

is called a formal meromorphic cover at
x

if there exist

N\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

and
a surjection of

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-algebras

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}X(\log H)(NH))\to\mathcal{B}_{N,H}

such that
(i)
\mathcal{B}_{N,H}
is finite over
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
,
(ii)
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{N,H}(\ast H)
,
(iii)
\mathcal{B}_{N,H}
is torsion-free as an
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-module.

Definition 2.22

A formal meromorphic cover

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}

is called logarithmic
if there exists a surjection
of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-algebras

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}X(\log H))\to\mathcal{B}_{0}

such that (i)
\mathcal{B}_{0}
is finite over
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
,
(ii)
\mathcal{B}_{0}(\ast H)=\mathcal{B}

as
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}X(\log H))
-algebras.

Any
\mathfrak{a}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)

induces

d\mathfrak{a}\in(T^{\ast}X(\log H)(NH))_{x}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}

for some
N\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
.
The addition of
d\mathfrak{a}

induces an automorphism of

T^{\ast}X(\log H)(NH)\times_{X}\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x})
.
It induces an automorphism of the
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-algebra

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}X(\log H)(NH))
,
and an automorphism
of the
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)
-algebra

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))
.
The automorphisms of the algebras are denoted by

\kappa^{\ast}_{d\mathfrak{a}}
.

Let

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}

be a formal meromorphic cover at
x
.
We set

\kappa_{d\mathfrak{a}}^{\ast}\mathcal{B}:=\mathcal{B}

as an
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)
-algebra,
and we consider the morphism

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\kappa_{d\mathfrak{a}}^{\ast}\mathcal{B}

obtained as the composition of

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\kappa^{\ast}_{d\mathfrak{a}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}
.

Definition 2.23

A formal meromorphic cover

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}

is unramifiedly good
if there exist
a good set of irregular values

\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{B})\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}

and
the decomposition of

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)
-algebras

\mathcal{B}=\prod_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{B})}\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that the following holds for any
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{B})
.

•

Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)

be a lift of
\mathfrak{a}
.
Then,
\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\mathcal{B}_{\mathfrak{a}}

is logarithmic.

See
§
1.2.1
,
in particular Remark
1.7

for the notion of good set of irregular values.

Let
n=\dim X
.
Let
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
be a generator of
the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{x}
of
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}

such that the ideal sheaf of
H
at
x

is generated by
\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}z_{i}

for some
1\leq\ell\leq n
.
For any
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
we obtain the flat extension of rings

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e,H)}_{X,x}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}[z_{j}^{1/e}\,|\,j=1,\ldots,\ell].

Let
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}

be a formal meromorphic cover.
We obtain
the
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e,H)}_{X,x}
-algebras

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}^{(e,H)}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e,H)}_{X,x},\quad\quad\mathcal{B}^{(e,H)}=\mathcal{B}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e,H)}_{X,x},

and the natural surjective morphism

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}^{(e,H)}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}^{(e,H)}
.
Note that
there exists a morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

f:X^{\prime}\to X
with a point
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
satisfying
f(x^{\prime})=x

such that the following holds.

•

f
induces
an isomorphism

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e,H)}_{X,x}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{\prime},x^{\prime}}
.

•

T^{\ast}X^{\prime}(\log H^{\prime})\simeq f^{\ast}T^{\ast}X(\log H)

and

T^{\ast}X^{\prime}(\log H^{\prime})(eNH^{\prime})\simeq f^{\ast}T^{\ast}X(\log H)(NH)

around
x^{\prime}
.

Hence,

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}^{(e,H)}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}^{(e,H)}

is regarded as a formal meromorphic cover.

Definition 2.24

The formal meromorphic cover

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}
is called good
if there exists
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

such that

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}^{(e,H)}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}^{(e,H)}

is unramifiedly good.

In Definition
2.24
,
the index set

\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{B}^{(e,H)})\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e,H)}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}\big/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e,H)}_{X,x}

is also denoted as
\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{B})
.
It is invariant under the Galois action.
We set

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H)}_{X,x}=\varinjlim\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e,H)}_{X,x}.

If the formal meromorphic cover

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\mathcal{B}
is good,

\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{B})
is naturally regarded as a subset of

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H)}_{X,x}(\ast H)/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H)}_{X,x}
.

2.4.3
Algebraic good set of irregular values

Let
U=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R
be an
n
-dimensional
smooth connected affine
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a normal crossing hypersurface
H_{U}
.
The set of closed points are denoted by
U(\mathbb{K})

and
H_{U}(\mathbb{K})
, respectively.
Let
x\in H_{U}(\mathbb{K})
.
Assume that there exists
an etale morphism

\psi:U\to\mathbb{K}^{n}

such that the following holds.

•

H_{U}=\psi^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{z_{i}=0\}\bigr)

for some
1\leq\ell\leq n
.

•

\psi(x)=(0,\ldots,0)
.

•

\psi^{-1}\{z_{i}=0\}
are connected.

The functions
\psi^{\ast}(z_{j})
are also denoted by
z_{j}
.
We set
R(\ast H_{U}):=H^{0}(U,\mathcal{O}_{U}(\ast H_{U}))
.

Definition 2.25

Such a tuple of functions

(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
is called an etale coordinate system
on
U
.

For any closed point
y\in H_{U}
,
let
\widehat{R}_{y}
denote the completion of
R
at
y
.
Let
\widehat{R}_{y}(\ast H_{U})
denote the formal completion of

R(\ast H_{U})
at
y
.
We have the naturally induced map

R(\ast H_{U})/R\to\widehat{R}_{y}/\widehat{R}_{y}(\ast H_{U})
.

Definition 2.26

A finite subset
\mathcal{I}
of
R(\ast H_{U})/R

is called good if
the induced tuple

\mathcal{I}_{|\widehat{y}}\subset\widehat{R}_{y}(\ast H_{U})/\widehat{R}_{y}

is good for any
y\in H_{U}
.

Lemma 2.27

Let
\mathcal{I}\subset R(\ast H_{U})/R

be a finite subset.
Suppose that

\mathcal{I}_{|\widehat{x}}\subset\widehat{R}_{x}(\ast H_{U})/\widehat{R}_{x}
is good.
Let
Z
be the set of the closed points

y\in H_{U}

such that
\mathcal{I}
is not good in

\widehat{R}_{y}(\ast H_{U})/\widehat{R}_{y}
.
Then,

Z
is a Zariski closed subset
of
H_{U}
,
and
H_{U}\setminus Z
contains
x
.
In particular,
H_{U}\setminus Z
is a Zariski dense
open subset of
H_{U}
.

Proof
Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}\subset R(\ast H_{U})

be a lift of
\mathcal{I}
.
Because
\mathcal{I}
induces
a good set of irregular values at
x
,
we have

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq 0}^{\ell}

and
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq 0}^{\ell}
.
We have

z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})}\mathfrak{a}\in\widehat{R}_{x}

and

z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})}(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})\in\widehat{R}_{x}
.
Let
R_{x}
denote the local ring at
x
.
Because
\widehat{R}_{x}
is faithfully flat over
R_{x}
,

R_{x}(\ast H_{U})/R_{x}\to\widehat{R}_{x}(\ast H_{U})/\widehat{R}_{x}

is injective.
We obtain that

z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})}\mathfrak{a}\in R_{x}

and

z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})}(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})\in R_{x}
.
We obtain that

z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})}\mathfrak{a}

and
z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})}(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})

are contained in the local rings
of the generic points of
H
.
Hence, we obtain that

z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})}\mathfrak{a}

and
z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})}(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})

are contained in
R
.

Let
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})_{i}

(1\leq i\leq\ell)
denote the
i
-th components of

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq 0}^{\ell}
.
We obtain the following closed subset:

Z(z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})}\mathfrak{a}):=\{z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})}\mathfrak{a}=0\}\cap\bigcup_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})_{i}<0}\{z_{i}=0\}.

Similarly,
let
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})_{i}
denote the
i
-th component of

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq 0}^{\ell}
.
We set

Z(z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})}(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})):=\{z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})}(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})=0\}\cap\bigcup_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})_{i}<0}\{z_{i}=0\}.

We obtain

Z(\mathcal{I}):=\bigcup_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}\setminus\{0\}}Z(z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})}\mathfrak{a})\cup\bigcup_{\mathfrak{a}\neq\mathfrak{b}}Z(z^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})}(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})).

Then,

\mathcal{I}
induces a good set of irregular values at
y

if and only if

y\in H_{U}\setminus Z(\mathcal{I})\neq\emptyset
.

2.4.4
Algebraic local conditions

Let

\Sigma_{U}\subset T^{\ast}(U\setminus H_{U})

be a meromorphic finite cover over
(U,H_{U})
.

Definition 2.28

We say that
\Sigma_{U}
is logarithmic on
(U,H_{U})

if the closure of
\Sigma_{U}

in
T^{\ast}U(\log H_{U})
is proper over
U
.

For any
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}
,
let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\in R(\ast H_{U})
be a lift.
We obtain the section

d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}

of
\Omega^{1}_{U}(\log H_{U})(\ast H_{U})
.
We obtain the automorphism

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}
of

T^{\ast}(U\setminus H_{U})

by the addition.
For any closed subset
Z\subset T^{\ast}(U\setminus H_{U})
,
we obtain

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}Z\subset T^{\ast}(U\setminus H_{U})
.

Definition 2.29

\Sigma_{U}
is unramifiedly good on
(U,H_{U})

if there exits
a good finite set
\mathcal{I}(\Sigma_{U})\subset R(\ast H_{U})/R

and a decomposition of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

\Sigma_{U}=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\Sigma_{U})}\Sigma_{U,\mathfrak{a}}

such that

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{U,\mathfrak{a}}

are logarithmic.

For a positive integer
e
,
we set
R^{(e)}=R[z_{i}^{1/e}\,|\,i=1,\ldots,\ell]
.
We obtain

U^{(e)}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R^{(e)}

with the finite morphism

\varphi_{e,H_{U}}:U^{(e)}\to U
.

Definition 2.30

We say that

\Sigma_{U}
is good on
(U,H_{U})

if
\varphi_{e,H_{U}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{U}

is unramifiedly good for some positive integer
e
.
In case,
we obtain

\mathcal{I}(\Sigma_{U}):=\mathcal{I}(\varphi_{e,H_{U}}^{\ast}(\Sigma_{U}))
.

2.4.5
Comparison of the algebraic local condition
and the formal condition

Let
X
be an
n
-dimensional smooth algebraic variety over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
H
be a normal crossing hypersurface of
X
.

An etale coordinate neighbourhood
of
x\in X

is a tuple

(U,x^{\prime},\varphi,\psi)

of a smooth affine
\mathbb{K}
-variety
U
,
a closed point
x^{\prime}\in U
,
an etale morphism
\varphi:(U,x^{\prime})\to(X,x)
,
and an etale morphism

\psi:U\to\mathbb{K}^{n}

satisfying the following conditions.

•

H_{U}:=\varphi^{-1}(H)
equals

\psi^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{z_{i}=0\}\bigr)

for some
1\leq\ell\leq n
.

•

\psi(x^{\prime})=(0,\ldots,0)
.

•

\psi^{-1}(\{z_{i}=0\})

are connected.

Proposition 2.31

A meromorphic finite cover

\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

is formally good at
x\in X

if and only if
there exists an etale neighborhood

(U,x^{\prime},\varphi,\psi)
of
x

such that

\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(U\setminus H_{U})

is good on
(U,H_{U})
.

Proof
The “only if” part is clear.
Suppose that
\Sigma
is formally good at
x
.
We may assume that
X
is affine
and that there exists an etale morphism

\psi:X\to\mathbb{K}^{n}

such that
(i)
H=\psi^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{j=1}^{\ell}\{z_{j}=0\}\bigr)
,
(ii)
\psi(x)=(0,\ldots,0)
,
(iii)
\psi^{-1}(\{z_{j}=0\})
are connected.
We set

R^{(e)}=R[z_{i}^{1/e}\,|\,i=1,\ldots,\ell]

and
X^{(e)}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R^{(e)}
.
We have the ramified cover

\rho_{e}:X^{(e)}\to X
.
We set

H^{(e)}=\rho_{e}^{-1}(H)

and
\Sigma^{(e)}=\rho_{e}^{\ast}\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X^{(e)}\setminus H^{(e)})
.
We have
x^{(e)}\in X^{(e)}

such that
\rho_{e}(x^{(e)})=x
.
Then,

\Sigma^{(e)}
is formally unramifiedly good at
x^{(e)}
.

For any
N\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
we set

\mathcal{E}^{(e)}_{N}:=T^{\ast}X^{(e)}(\log H^{(e)})(NH^{(e)})
.
There exists
N>0
such that
the closure of

\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N}\subset\mathcal{E}^{(e)}_{N}
of
\Sigma^{(e)}

is proper over
X^{(e)}
.
Let
d
be the number of the general fiber of

\Sigma^{(e)}\to X^{(e)}\setminus H^{(e)}
.
We set

A(\Sigma^{(e)}):=\bigl\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\overbrace{\Sigma^{(e)}\times_{X^{(e)}\setminus H^{(e)}}\cdots\times_{X^{(e)}\setminus H^{(e)}}\Sigma^{(e)}}^{d}\,\big|\,x_{i}\neq x_{j}\,\,(i\neq j)\bigr\}.

Let
\overline{A}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})
denote
the closure of
A(\Sigma^{(e)})

in

\overbrace{\mathcal{E}^{(e)}_{N}\times_{X^{(e)}}\cdots\times_{X^{(e)}}\mathcal{E}^{(e)}_{N}}^{d}
.
Let
Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})

denote the normalization of
\overline{A}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})
.
The projection

Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})\to X^{(e)}
is an affine morphism.
Let
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
denote the
d
-th symmetric group.
There exists the natural
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on

Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})
.
We have

Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})/\mathfrak{S}_{d}\simeq X^{(e)}
.
Let
\pi:Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})\to X^{(e)}

denote the projection,
which is a finite morphism.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}^{h}
be a Henselization of
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
.
By
[
21
, Corollary 4.3]
,

\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}:=\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X,x}[z_{i}^{1/e}\,|\,i=1,\ldots,\ell]

is a Henselian ring,
which is a Henselization of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}
.
By
[
21
, Theorem I.4.2]
,
we have the decomposition into local rings:

\mathcal{O}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}(Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N}))\otimes\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}=\bigoplus_{y\in\pi^{-1}(x^{(e)})}\Bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}(Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N}))\otimes\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}\Bigr)_{y}.

By replacing
X
with an etale coordinate neighbourhood of
x
,
we may assume that
there exists the decomposition of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})=\bigsqcup_{y\in\pi^{-1}(x^{(e)})}\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y}

where

\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}_{N})_{y}\times_{X^{(e)}}x^{(e)}=\{y\}
.
Let
G_{y}\subset\mathfrak{S}_{d}
denote the stabilizer.
We have the natural action
G_{y}
on
Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y}
,
and
Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y}/G_{y}\simeq X^{(e)}
.

Let
p_{j}:Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})\to\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N}\subset\mathcal{E}^{(e)}_{N}

denote the morphism induced by the projection onto
the
j
-th component.
It induces a section

s_{j}
of
\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}^{(e)}_{N}

on
Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})
.

Let
y\in\pi^{-1}(x^{(e)})
.
Let
\mathcal{Z}(s_{i}-s_{j})_{y}

(i\neq j)

denote the
0
-set of
s_{i}-s_{j}

in
Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y|X^{(e)}\setminus H^{(e)}}
.
Let
\overline{\mathcal{Z}(s_{i}-s_{j})_{y}}

denote the closure of
\mathcal{Z}(s_{i}-s_{j})_{y}

in
Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y}
.
We consider the equivalence relation
\sim_{y}
on

\{1,\ldots,d\}

defined by the following condition.

•

We have
i\sim_{y}j

if there exists a sequence

i=p(0),p(1),\ldots,p(m)=j

such that

y\in\overline{\mathcal{Z}(s_{p(k)}-s_{p(k-1)})}

for
k=1,\ldots,m
.

Let
S_{y}
denote the quotient set.
We obtain the decomposition

\{1,\ldots,d\}=\bigsqcup_{I\in S_{y}}I
.

Let
\Psi_{y,i}:Y(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y}\to\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N}

be the finite morphism induced by
s_{i}
.
Because
\dim\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi_{y,i}=\dim X
,

(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y,I}:=\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi_{y,i}

is a union of some irreducible components of

\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N}
.
We obtain the decomposition

\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N}=\bigcup_{I\in S_{y}}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y,I}
.
We set

\Sigma^{(e)}_{y,I}:=(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y,I|X^{(e)}\setminus H^{(e)}}
.
By shrinking
X
,
we may assume

\Sigma^{(e)}_{y,I_{1}}\cap\Sigma^{(e)}_{y,I_{2}}=\emptyset
.
We obtain the decomposition of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties:

\Sigma^{(e)}=\bigsqcup_{I\in S_{y}}\Sigma^{(e)}_{y,I}.

Let
A(\Sigma^{(e)})_{y}\subset A(\Sigma^{(e)})

denote the closed subset of

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in A(\Sigma^{(e)})
satisfying the following condition.

•

For any
I\in S_{y}
and
i\in I
,
we have
x_{i}\in\Sigma^{(e)}_{y,I}
.

We obtain the decomposition
of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties:

A(\Sigma^{(e)})=\bigsqcup_{y\in\pi^{-1}(x^{(e)})}A(\Sigma^{(e)})_{y}.

Let
\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})^{\prime}_{y}

denote the normalization of
the closure of

A(\Sigma^{(e)})_{y}

in
\overline{A}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})
.
We obtain the decomposition

\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})=\bigsqcup_{y\in\pi^{-1}(x^{(e)})}\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})^{\prime}_{y}.

Because
y\in\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})
,
we obtain

\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})^{\prime}_{y}=\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y}
.
It implies the following lemma.

Lemma 2.32

For
g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
,
we have

g\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{y}=\overline{Y}(\overline{\Sigma}^{(e)}_{N})_{g(y)}
.
As a result,
we obtain

g(I)=I
for any
g\in G_{y}

and
I\in S_{y}
.

Because
the formal cover

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}(T^{\ast}(X^{(e)}\setminus H^{(e)}))\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}(\Sigma^{(e)})

of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}

is unramifiedly good,
there exists

\mathfrak{a}_{j}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}(\ast H^{(e)})

such that

s_{j}-\pi^{\ast}d\mathfrak{a}_{j}\in\pi^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{H^{(e)},0})\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}
.
Because
\{\mathfrak{a}_{j}\}
is good,
if
i,j\in I
for some
I
,
then we may assume
\mathfrak{a}_{i}=\mathfrak{a}_{j}
.
Hence,
by setting

{\tt Q}_{1}(s_{i})=\frac{1}{|G_{y}|}\sum_{g\in G_{y}}g^{\ast}(s_{i}),

we obtain that

{\tt Q}_{1}(s_{i})-\pi^{\ast}d\mathfrak{a}_{i}\in\pi^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{H^{(e)},0})\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}
.
Because
{\tt Q}_{1}(s_{i})
is
G_{y}
-invariant,
there exists

{\tt Q}(s_{i})\in H^{0}(X^{(e)}\setminus H^{(e)},\Omega^{1}_{X^{(e)}\setminus H^{(e)}})

such that

\pi^{\ast}({\tt Q}(s_{i}))={\tt Q}_{1}(s_{i})
.
We obtain the induced section

[{\tt Q}(s_{i})]

of

\Omega_{X^{(e)}}^{1}(\ast H^{(e)})/\Omega_{X^{(e)}}^{1}(\log H^{(e)})
.
Note that

{\tt Q}(s_{i})-d\mathfrak{a}_{i}\in\Omega_{X^{(e)}}^{1}(\log H^{(e)})_{x^{(e)}}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}
.
Hence, we obtain that

d[{\tt Q}(s_{i})]=0

as a section of

\Omega^{2}_{X^{(e)}}(\ast H^{(e)})/\Omega^{2}_{X^{(e)}}(\log H^{(e)}).

Note that

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{(e)}}(\log H^{(e)})_{x^{(e)}}\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{X^{(e)}}(\ast H^{(e)})_{x^{(e)}}

is a quasi-isomorphism.
Hence,
there exists

\mathfrak{b}_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}(\ast H^{(e)})/\mathcal{O}_{X^{(e)},x^{(e)}}

such that

d\mathfrak{b}_{i}=[{\tt Q}(s_{i})]
.
For each
i,j\in I
,
we have
\mathfrak{b}_{i}=\mathfrak{b}_{j}
.
We set
\mathfrak{b}_{I}:=\mathfrak{b}_{i}

(i\in I)
.
Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{I}\in\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
be a lift of
\mathfrak{b}_{I}
.
By the previous consideration,
we obtain that

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{I}}^{\ast}\Sigma^{(e)}_{I}
is logarithmic.
Thus, we obtain Proposition
2.31
.

2.4.6
Etale locally good meromorphic finite covers

Let
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

be a meromorphic finite cover over
(X,H)
.
For any
x\in X
,
we obtain a local meromorphic finite cover

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\Sigma)

over
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
.

Definition 2.33

•

\Sigma
is called formally good at
x

if
the induced formal cover

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(T^{\ast}(X\setminus H))\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\Sigma)
is good.
In case, let

\mathcal{I}(\Sigma,x)\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H)}_{X,x}(\ast H)/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H)}_{X,x}

denote the set of ramified irregular values
appearing as the index set of the decomposition.
(See
§
2.4.2
.)

•

\Sigma
is called formally good on
(X,H)

if it is formally good at any
x\in H
.
In case,
let

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma)=(\mathcal{I}(\Sigma,x)\,|\,x\in H)
.
denote the tuple of the set of ramified irregular values.

Definition 2.34

\Sigma
is called etale locally good at
x

if
there exists an etale coordinate neighbourhood

(U,x^{\prime},\varphi,\psi)
of
x\in X

such that

\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma
is good on
(U,H_{U})
.
We say that
\Sigma
is etale locally good on
(X,H)

if it is etale locally good at any
x\in H
.

By Proposition
2.31
,
we obtain the following proposition.

Proposition 2.35

\Sigma
is formally good on
(X,H)

if and only if
it is etale locally good on
(X,H)
.
In the case,
there exist a finite etale coordinate cover

\{(U_{i},\varphi_{i},\psi_{i})\,|\,i=1,\ldots,k\}

such that

\varphi_{i}^{\ast}\Sigma
