are
good on
(U_{i},H_{U_{i}})
.

2.4.7
Pull back and push-forward via birational projective morphisms

Let
\varphi:X^{\prime}\to X
be a birational projective morphism
such that
H^{\prime}=\varphi^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing
and that
X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus H
.
We have the natural isomorphism

T^{\ast}(X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime})\simeq T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)
.
For any meromorphic finite cover
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

over
(X,H)
,
we obtain the induced meromorphic finite cover

\varphi^{\ast}(\Sigma)\subset T^{\ast}(X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime})

over
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.
Conversely,
for any meromorphic finite cover

\Sigma^{\prime}\subset T^{\ast}(X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime})

over
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
,
we obtain the induced meromorphic finite cover

\varphi_{\ast}(\Sigma^{\prime})\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

over
(X,H)
.

Proposition 2.36

•

Let

\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

be a formally good meromorphic finite cover over
(X,H)
.
Then,
\varphi^{\ast}(\Sigma)
is formally good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
,
and

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma)=\varphi^{\ast}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma))
holds.

•

Let
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset T^{\ast}(X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime})
be
a formally good meromorphic finite cover over
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.
Suppose that there exists
a tuple of the set of ramified irregular values

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}=(\mathcal{I}_{x}\,|\,x\in H)

such that

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma^{\prime})\subset\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
.
Then,
\varphi_{\ast}(\Sigma^{\prime})\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

is formally good on
(X,H)
,
and we have

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\varphi_{\ast}\Sigma)\subset{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
.

Proof
Let
U=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
be an affine
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a closed point
x
.
Let
\psi:U\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{K}[z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}]
be an etale morphism
such that
\psi(x)=(0,\ldots,0)
,
and that
\{\psi^{\ast}(z_{i})=0\}
are connected.
We set
H=\psi^{-1}(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{z_{i}=0\})
for some

1\leq\ell\leq n
.
Let
U^{\prime}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R^{\prime})
be an affine
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a closed point
x^{\prime}
.
Let
\psi^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{K}[z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}]

be an etale morphism
such that

\psi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=(0,\ldots,0)

and that
\{(\psi^{\prime})^{\ast}z_{i}=0\}
are connected.
We set
H^{\prime}=(\psi^{\prime})^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\{z_{i}=0\}\bigr)
.
We set
\psi_{i}=\psi^{\ast}(z_{i})

and
\psi^{\prime}_{i}=(\psi^{\prime})^{\ast}(z_{i})
.
Let
\varphi:U^{\prime}\to U
be a birational morphism
such that
(i)
\varphi(x^{\prime})=x
,
(ii)
\varphi^{\ast}(\psi_{i})=\prod_{j=1}^{m}(\psi^{\prime}_{j})^{k_{i,j}}\widetilde{g}_{i}

such that
\widetilde{g}_{i}(x^{\prime})\neq 0
.

Let
R^{(e)}=R[\psi_{1}^{1/e},\ldots,\psi_{\ell}^{1/e}]

and
(R^{\prime})^{(e)}=R[(\psi^{\prime}_{1})^{1/e},\ldots,(\psi^{\prime}_{m})^{1/e}]

for any
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
.
Let
U^{(e)}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R^{(e)}

and
(U^{\prime})^{(e)}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R^{\prime})^{(e)}
.
For any
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
there exists
e^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

with the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}(U^{\prime})^{(e^{\prime})}@>{}>{}>U^{(e)}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
U^{\prime}@>{}>{}>U.\end{CD}

A good finite subset of

R^{(e)}(\ast H^{(e)})/R^{(e)}

induces
a good finite subset of

(R^{\prime})^{(e^{\prime})}(\ast(H^{\prime})^{(e^{\prime})})/(R^{\prime})^{(e^{\prime})}
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}\in R^{(e)}(\ast H^{(e)})
.
If
\Sigma=\kappa_{d\mathfrak{a}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{1}
holds
for logarithmic
\Sigma_{1}
,
then

\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma=\kappa_{d\varphi^{\ast}\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\bigl(\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma_{1}\bigr)

holds for logarithmic
\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma_{1}
.
If
\Sigma^{\prime}=\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{1}^{\prime}
holds
for logarithmic
\Sigma_{1}^{\prime}
,
then

\varphi_{\ast}(\Sigma^{\prime})=\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}(\varphi_{\ast}\Sigma_{1}^{\prime})
holds
for logarithmic
\varphi_{\ast}\Sigma^{\prime}_{1}
.
Then, we obtain the claim of
Proposition
2.36

from Proposition
2.35
.

2.5
Family case

2.5.1
Local algebraic conditions

Let
S
be a regular integral domain over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
R
be an
S[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
-algebra
which is finite and etale over
S[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
.
Let
U=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
.
We obtain the normal crossing hypersurface

H=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{x_{i}=0\}
of
U
.
We set
U^{\circ}=U\setminus H
.
Let
T^{\ast}(U^{\circ}/S)
denote the relative cotangent bundle
of
U^{\circ}
over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.
Let
T^{\ast}(U/S)(\log H)

denote the relative logarithmic cotangent bundle
of
(U,H)
over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.

Let
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(U^{\circ}/S)

be a finite cover of
U^{\circ}
,
which is flat over
S
.

Definition 2.37

We say that
\Sigma
is logarithmic relative to
S

if the closure of
\Sigma

in
T^{\ast}(U/S)(\log H)
is proper over
U
.

For any
f\in R(\ast H)
,
we obtain the section
df
of
T^{\ast}(U^{\circ}/S)

by taking the exterior derivative relative to
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.
We obtain the automorphism

\kappa_{df}
of
T^{\ast}(U^{\circ}/S)

by adding
df
.

Definition 2.38

We say that
\Sigma
is unramifiedly good on
(U,H)
relative to
S

if there exist a good set of irregular values

\mathcal{I}\subset R(\ast H)/R

and a decomposition

\Sigma=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that
each
\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

is logarithmic.
Here,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\in R(\ast H)
denotes a lift of
\mathfrak{a}
.

Let
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\widetilde{S})\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
be an etale covering
of affine schemes.
We set
\widetilde{R}=\widetilde{S}\otimes_{S}R

and
\widetilde{U}=\widetilde{R}
.
For
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
we set
\widetilde{R}^{(e)}=\widetilde{R}[x_{1}^{1/e},\ldots,x_{n}^{1/e}]

and
\widetilde{U}^{(e)}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\widetilde{R}^{(e)})
.
We obtain the morphism

\rho_{e}:\widetilde{U}^{(e)}\to U
.
We set
\widetilde{H}^{(e)}=\rho_{e}^{-1}(H)

and
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{(e)}=\rho_{e}^{\ast}(\Sigma)
.

Definition 2.39

We say that
\Sigma
is good on
(U,H)
relative to
S

if there exist
an etale covering of affine schemes
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\widetilde{S})\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

and
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
such that

\rho_{e}^{\ast}(\Sigma)
is
unramifiedly good on
(\widetilde{U}^{(e)},\widetilde{H}^{(e)})
relative to

\widetilde{S}
.

Let
\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
be any algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
S\to\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
be a morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-algebras.
We obtain

(U_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})

from
(U,H,\Sigma)

and
S\to\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
.
The following lemma is clear.

Lemma 2.40

If
\Sigma
is good on
(U,H)
relative to
S
,
then
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
is good on
(U_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})
.

Lemma 2.41

Let
\mathbb{K}_{1}
be an algebraically closed field
which contains
S
.
Suppose that
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
is
good (resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.
Then, there exists an extension
of regular
\mathbb{K}
-algebras
S\subset S_{1}
in
\mathbb{K}_{1}

such that

\Sigma_{S_{1}}
is good
(resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(U_{S_{1}},H_{S_{1}})
relative to
S_{1}
,
where
(U_{S_{1}},H_{S_{1}},\Sigma_{S_{1}})

is induced by
(U,H,\Sigma)
and the extension
S\subset S_{1}
.

Proof
It is enough to consider the case where

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
is unramifiedly good.
We set

R_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=R\otimes_{S}\mathbb{K}_{1}
.
There exist a good set of irregular values

\mathcal{I}\subset R_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})\Big/R_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

and a decomposition

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1},\mathfrak{a}}

such that

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1},\mathfrak{a}}

are logarithmic.
By enlarging
S
in
\mathbb{K}_{1}
,
we may assume that
there exists a finite subset

\mathcal{I}\subset R(\ast H)\Big/R

which induces
\mathcal{I}
.
By Lemma
2.27
,
we may assume that
\mathcal{I}_{S}

is a good set of irregular values
because
\mathcal{I}
is a good set of irregular values.

There exists a decomposition

\Sigma=\bigcup_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1},\mathfrak{a}}=\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}\times_{S}\mathbb{K}_{1}
.
For
\mathfrak{a}\neq\mathfrak{b}
,
because

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1},\mathfrak{a}}\cap\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1},\mathfrak{b}}=\emptyset
,

\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}\cap\Sigma_{\mathfrak{b}}

is not dominant over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.
By enlarging
S
,
we may assume that

\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}\cap\Sigma_{\mathfrak{b}}=\emptyset
.

For
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}
,
the corresponding element of
\mathcal{I}_{S}

is also denoted by
\mathfrak{a}
.
Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\in R(\ast H)
be a lift of
\mathfrak{a}
.
Let us consider

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
Let
\mathbb{P}
be a projective completion of

T^{\ast}(U/S)(\log H)
.
Let
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}
denote
the complement of
T^{\ast}(U/S)(\log H)
in
\mathbb{P}
.
Let
\overline{\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}}

denote the closure of

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}
in
\mathbb{P}
.
Because

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1},\mathfrak{a}}

is logarithmic,

\overline{\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}}\cap\mathbb{P}_{\infty}

is not dominant over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.
By enlarging
S
,
we may assume that

\overline{\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}}\cap\mathbb{P}_{\infty}=\emptyset
.
It implies that

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

is logarithmic.

Let
\Sigma^{(i)}\subset T^{\ast}(U^{\circ}/S)

(i=1,2)

be good covers of
U^{\circ}
.
For any closed point
z
of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
,
we set

(U_{z},H_{z},\Sigma^{(i)}_{z})=(U,H,\Sigma^{(i)})\times_{\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)}z
.
We obtain
good meromorphic covers

\Sigma^{(i)}_{z}
on
(U_{z},H_{z})
,
and the associated tuples

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma^{(i)}_{z})=(\mathcal{I}(\Sigma^{(i)}_{z},x)\,|\,x\in H_{z})
.

Lemma 2.42

There exists a closed subset
Z\subset\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

such that

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma^{(1)}_{z})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma^{(2)}_{z})
holds
if and only if

z\in Z
.

Proof
It is enough to consider the case where
both
\Sigma^{(i)}
are unramifiedly good.
Let
\mathcal{I}^{(i)}\subset R(\ast H)/R

be the index set in Definition
2.38
.
For each
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}^{(i)}

and
z\in\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
,
let
\mathfrak{a}_{z}\in\mathcal{I}^{(i)}_{z}\subset R_{z}(\ast H_{z})/R_{z}

denote the induced element.
It is enough to consider the case
where the hypersurfaces
\{z_{i}=0\}\subset U

are connected,
and there exists a section

\alpha:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)\to\bigcap\{z_{i}=0\}\subset U
.
Let
\widehat{R}_{\alpha}
denote the completion of
R

along the image of
\alpha
.
We obtain the induced subsets

\widehat{\mathcal{I}}^{(i)}_{\alpha}\subset\widehat{R}_{\alpha}(\ast H)/\widehat{R}_{\alpha}
.
For
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\ell}
,
we set
x^{{\boldsymbol{m}}}=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{m_{i}}
.

For
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}^{(i)}
,
we have the expansion

\mathfrak{a}=\sum\mathfrak{a}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}x^{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
,
where
\mathfrak{a}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}=0
for
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{\ell}
.
We may regard
\mathfrak{a}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}

as functions on
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.

For
\mathfrak{a}\in\widehat{\mathcal{I}}^{(1)}_{\alpha}

and
\mathfrak{b}\in\widehat{\mathcal{I}}^{(2)}_{\alpha}
,
we obtain the following closed subsets

Z(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b})=\bigcap_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\{\mathfrak{a}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}=\mathfrak{b}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\}\subset\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.
Then,

\mathfrak{a}_{z}=\mathfrak{b}_{z}
holds
if and only if
z\in Z(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b})
.
We have
\mathfrak{a}_{z}\in\mathcal{I}^{(2)}_{z}

if and only if

z\in\bigcup_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{I}^{(2)}}Z(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b})
.
Hence,

\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{z}\subset\mathcal{I}^{(2)}_{z}

if and only if

z\in\bigcap_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}^{(1)}}\bigcup_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{I}^{(2)}}Z(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b})
.
Similarly,

\mathcal{I}^{(2)}_{z}\subset\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{z}

if and only if

z\in\bigcap_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}^{(2)}}\bigcup_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{I}^{(1)}}Z(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b})
.
Then, we obtain the claim of the lemma.

2.5.2
Families of etale locally good meromorphic finite covers

Let
X
be a
\mathbb{K}
-variety smooth over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.
Let
H\subset X
be a hypersurface of
X

which is normal crossing relative to
S

in the following sense.

•

H
is normal crossing.

•

Let
H=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda}H_{j}

denote the irreducible decomposition.
For any finite subset
I\subset\Lambda
,

H_{I}:=\bigcap_{j\in I}H_{j}

is smooth over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S
.

We also assume that the monodromy of
H
is trivial
in the following sense.

•

Let
H_{I}=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda(I)}H_{I,j}

denote the irreducible decomposition.
Then, any geometric fiber of

H_{I,j}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

is irreducible.

Let
T^{\ast}(X/S)
denote the relative cotangent bundle
of
X
over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.
Let
T^{\ast}(X/S)(\log H)

denote the relative cotangent bundle of
(X,H)
over
S
.

Let
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}((X\setminus H)/S)

be a finite cover of
X\setminus H
,
which is flat over
S
.
Let
x
be any closed point of
H
.
For any etale coordinate neighbourhood

(U,x^{\prime},\varphi,\psi)

of
x
in
X

in the sense of §
2.4.5
,
we set

H_{U}=\varphi^{-1}(H)

and

\Sigma_{U}=\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}((U\setminus H_{U})/S)
.

Definition 2.43

We say that
\Sigma
is etale locally good
(resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
at
x
relative to
S

if there exists an etale coordinate neighbourhood

(U,x^{\prime},\varphi,\psi)
of
x

such that
\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma_{U}

is good
(logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(U,H_{U})
.

Definition 2.44

We say that
\Sigma
is etale locally good
(resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good) on
(X,H)
relative to
S

if it is etale locally good
(logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
at any
x\in X
relative to
S
.

Let
\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
be any algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
S\to\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
be a morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-algebras.
We obtain
(X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})

from
(X,H,\Sigma)
by
S\to\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
.
We obtain the following lemma from Lemma
2.40
.

Lemma 2.45

If
\Sigma
is good
(resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(X,H)
relative to
S
,
then
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
is good
(resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})
.

Lemma 2.46

Let
\mathbb{K}_{1}
be an algebraically closed field
which contains
S
.
Suppose that
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
is
good (resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.
Then, there exists an extension
of regular
\mathbb{K}
-algebras
S\subset S_{1}
in
\mathbb{K}_{1}

such that

\Sigma_{S_{1}}
is good
(resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(X_{S_{1}},H_{S_{1}})
relative to
S_{1}
,
where
(X_{S_{1}},H_{S_{1}},\Sigma_{S_{1}})

is induced by
(X,H,\Sigma)
and the extension
S\subset S_{1}
.

Proof
There exists
a finite tuple
of etale morphisms

\varphi_{j}:U_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}\to X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

with an etale morphism

\psi_{j}:U_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{K}_{1}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]

such that the following holds.

•

X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\varphi_{j}
.

•

H_{U_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}}:=\varphi_{j}^{-1}(H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell(j)}\{\psi_{j}^{\ast}(x_{i})=0\}
.
Moreover,

\{\psi_{j}^{\ast}(x_{i})=0\}
is connected.

•

\Sigma_{U_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}}=\varphi_{j}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

is good on

(U_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j},H_{U_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}})
.

By enlarging
S
,
we may assume that
there exist
a tuple of etale morphisms

\varphi_{S,j}:U_{S,j}\to X_{S}

with an etale morphism

\psi_{S,j}:U_{S,j}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]

which induce
(\varphi_{j},\psi_{j})

(j=1,\ldots,m)
.

We set
H_{U_{S,j}}=\varphi_{S,j}^{-1}(H_{S})

and

U_{S,j}^{\circ}=U_{S,j}\setminus H_{U_{S,j}}
.
We obtain the closed subsets

\Sigma_{U_{S,j}}=\varphi_{S,j}^{\ast}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})

of
T^{\ast}(U_{S,j}^{\circ}/S)
.
According to
[
6
, Theorem 6.9.1]
,
by enlarging
S
,
we may assume that

\Sigma_{U_{S,j}}
are flat over
S
.
According to Lemma
2.41
,
by enlarging
S
,
we may assume that

\Sigma_{U_{S,j}}

are good (resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(U_{S,j},H_{U_{S,j}})
relative to
S
.
Hence, we obtain the claim of Lemma
2.46
.

2.5.3
Families of Lagrangian covers

Let
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}((X\setminus H)/S)

be a finite cover of
X\setminus H
,
which is flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.
Let
\Sigma^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}
denote the smooth part of
\Sigma
.
We also assume the following.

•

Each geometric fiber of
\Sigma\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

is reduced.

•

For any closed point
s
of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
,
any irreducible component of

\Sigma_{S,s}=\Sigma_{S}\times_{\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)}s

has hon-empty intersection
with
\Sigma^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}
.

Definition 2.47

\Sigma
is called Lagrangian if

\Sigma_{S,s}
is Lagrangian
in the sense of Definition
2.20

for any closed point
s
of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.

Let
\omega
denote the relative symplectic structure of

T^{\ast}(X/S)
.
Let
T^{\ast}(\Sigma^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}/S)
denote
the relative cotangent bundle of
\Sigma^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}
over
S
.
We obtain the induced section

\Psi_{\omega}

of
\bigwedge^{2}T^{\ast}(\Sigma^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}/S)

induced by
\omega
.

Lemma 2.48

\Sigma
is Lagrangian if and only if

\Psi_{\omega}=0
.

The following lemmas are clear.

Lemma 2.49

If
\Sigma
is Lagrangian,
then any geometric fiber of
\Sigma_{S}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

is Lagrangian.

Lemma 2.50

Suppose that
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
is Lagrangian
for an injection of
S
to an algebraically closed field
\mathbb{K}_{1}
.
Then,
\Sigma
is Lagrangian.

2.5.4
Comparison of good meromorphic covers

Let
\Sigma^{(i)}\subset T^{\ast}((X\setminus H)/S)

(i=1,2)
be
finite covers of
X\setminus H
which are etale locally good on
(X,H)
.
For each closed point
z
of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
,
we obtain good meromorphic covers
\Sigma^{(i)}_{z}

on
(X_{z},H_{z})
.
We obtain the tuples

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma^{(i)}_{z})
.
We obtain the following proposition from Lemma
2.42
.

Proposition 2.51

There exists a closed subset
Z\subset\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

such that

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma^{(1)}_{z})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma^{(2)}_{z})

if and only if

z\in Z
.

2.6
Models of meromorphic finite covers

2.6.1
Preliminary

Let
\mathbb{K}_{1}
be an algebraically closed field
which contains
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
be
an irreducible projective normal
\mathbb{K}_{1}
-variety.
Let

\boldsymbol{Z}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=(Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}\,|\,j=1,\ldots,m_{1})

be a tuple of closed subsets of
X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

such that

X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}:=X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{1}}Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}

is smooth.
Let
{\boldsymbol{W}}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=(W_{\mathbb{K}_{1},k}\,|\,k=1,\ldots,m_{2})

be a tuple of closed subsets of

T^{\ast}X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}^{\circ}

such that each
W_{\mathbb{K}_{1},k}
is a finite and dominant over

X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.
For each
J\subset\{1,\ldots,m_{1}\}
,
we set

Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},J}=\bigcap_{j\in J}Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}
.
We have the irreducible decompositions

Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},J}=\bigcup_{p\in\Lambda(J,1)}Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},J,p}
.

Let
S\subset\mathbb{K}_{1}

be a regular subring finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}
.

Definition 2.52

A model of
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},\boldsymbol{Z}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},{\boldsymbol{W}}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})

over
S

consists of the data

(X_{S},\boldsymbol{Z}_{S},{\boldsymbol{W}}_{S})
as follows.

•

X_{S}
denotes an irreducible
\mathbb{K}
-variety
flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

such that
(i)
X_{S}
is projective over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
,
(ii) each geometric fiber of
X_{S}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

is normal and irreducible,
(iii)
X_{S}\times\mathbb{K}_{1}=X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.

•

\boldsymbol{Z}_{S}=(Z_{S,j}\,|\,j=1,\ldots,m_{1})
denotes
a tuple of irreducible closed subset
of
X_{S}

such that
(i)
Z_{S,j}
are flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S
,
(ii) each geometric fiber of

Z_{S,j}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

is irreducible and reduced,
(iii)
Z_{S,j}\times\mathbb{K}_{1}=Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}
,
(iv)

X^{\circ}_{S}=X_{S}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{1}}Z_{S,j}

is smooth over
S
.

•

For any non-empty subset
J\subset\{1,\ldots,m_{1}\}
,
we have the decomposition

\bigcap_{j\in J}Z_{S,j}=\bigcup_{p\in\Lambda(J,1)}Z_{S,J,p}
such that
(i)
Z_{S,J,p}
are flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
,
(ii) each geometric fiber of

Z_{S,J,p}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

is irreducible and reduced,
(iii)
Z_{S,J,p}\times\mathbb{K}_{1}=Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},J,p}
.

•

{\boldsymbol{W}}_{S}=(W_{S,k}\,|\,k=1,\ldots,m_{2})
denotes
a tuple of irreducible closed subsets of

T^{\ast}(X_{S}^{\circ}/S)

such that
(i)
W_{S,k}
are flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S
,
(ii) each geometric fiber of

W_{S,k}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

is irreducible and reduced,
(iii)

W_{S,k}\times\mathbb{K}_{1}=W_{\mathbb{K}_{1},k}
,
(iv)
W_{S,k}
are finite and dominant over

X^{\circ}_{S}
.
Here,

T^{\ast}(X_{S}^{\circ}/S)

denotes the relative cotangent bundle of

X^{\circ}_{S}
over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S
.

We shall impose the following additional conditions.

•

Let
I\subset\{1,\ldots,m_{1}\}
be any subset.
If

\bigcap_{j\in I}Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}

is smooth over
\mathbb{K}_{1}
,
then
\bigcap_{j\in I}Z_{S,j}
is smooth over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S
.
(We formally set
Z_{\emptyset}=X
.)

The following lemma is standard.

Lemma 2.53

There exist a regular subring
S\subset\mathbb{K}_{1}

finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}

and a model
(X_{S},\boldsymbol{Z}_{S},{\boldsymbol{W}}_{S})

of
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},\boldsymbol{Z}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},{\boldsymbol{W}}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})

over
S
.

Proof
By taking an embedding

X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
for a large
N
,
we regard

X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
and
Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}

are subvarieties of
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{I}
,
\mathfrak{I}_{j}
,
\mathfrak{I}_{J,p}

denote the homogeneous ideals
of
\mathbb{K}_{1}[y_{0},\ldots,y_{N}]

corresponding to
X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
,
Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}

and
Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},I,p}
,
respectively.
There exists a regular subring
S_{1}\subset\mathbb{K}_{1}

finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}

such that

\mathfrak{I}
,
\mathfrak{I}_{j}

\mathfrak{I}_{J,p}
are induced by
homogeneous ideals

\mathfrak{I}_{S_{1}}
,
\mathfrak{I}_{S_{1},j}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{S_{1},J.p}

of
S_{1}[y_{0},\ldots,y_{N}]
,
respectively.
Let
X_{S_{1}}
,
Z_{S_{1},j}
,
Z_{S_{1},J,p}

denote the corresponding closed subvarieties of
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{S_{1}}
.
We may assume that they are dominant over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S_{1}
.
We may assume that
Z_{S_{1},j}\subset X_{S_{1}}

and
Z_{S_{1},J,p}\subset\bigcap_{j\in J}Z_{S_{1},j}
.
By the generic freeness theorem

[
6
, Theorem 6.9.1]
,
we may assume that

X_{S_{1}}
,
Z_{S_{1},j}

and
Z_{S_{1},J,p}
are flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})
.
By
[
7
, Theorem 12.2.4]
,
we may assume the following.

•

Each geometric fiber
of
X_{S_{1}}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})
is normal and irreducible.
We may also assume that

X^{\circ}_{S_{1}}
is smooth over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})
.

•

Each geometric fiber of

Z_{S_{1},j}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})
is irreducible and reduced.

•

Each geometric fiber of

Z_{S_{1},J,p}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})
is irreducible and reduced.

In particular,

X_{S_{1}}
,

Z_{S_{1},j}

and
Z_{S_{1},J,p}
are irreducible.
We have

\bigcup_{p\in\Lambda(J,1)}Z_{S_{1},J.p}\subset\bigcap_{j\in J}Z_{S_{1},j}
.
Let
Z^{\prime}_{S_{1}}
be an irreducible component of

\bigcap_{j\in J}Z_{S_{1},j}

which is not contained in

\bigcup_{p\in\Lambda(J,1)}Z_{S_{1},J.p}

Because

\bigcup_{p\in\Lambda(J,1)}Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},J,p}=\bigcap_{j\in J}Z_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}
,

Z^{\prime}_{S_{1}}
is not dominant over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})
.
Hence, we may assume that

\bigcup_{p\in\Lambda(J,1)}Z_{S_{1},J.p}=\bigcap_{j\in J}Z_{S_{1},j}
.

Let
U_{S_{1}}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{S_{1}}
be an affine open subset
of
X_{S_{1}}^{\circ}
.
Let
T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1}}/S_{1})

denote the relative cotangent bundle of
U_{S_{1}}
over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S_{1}
.
Let
\widetilde{R}_{S_{1}}
denote the ring of algebraic functions
of
T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1}}/S_{1})
.
We have

T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1}}/S_{1})=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\widetilde{R}_{S_{1}}
.
Let
R_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=R_{S_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{K}_{1}

and
\widetilde{R}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\widetilde{R}_{S_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{K}_{1}
.
We obtain the induced affine open subset

U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

of
X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}^{\circ}
.
We have

T^{\ast}U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\widetilde{R}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.
We have the ideal
\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}_{k}

of
\widetilde{R}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

corresponding to

T^{\ast}U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\cap W_{\mathbb{K}_{1},k}
.
We may assume that
there exists an ideal
\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}_{S_{1},k}
of
\widetilde{R}_{S_{1}}

which induces
\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}_{k}
.
Let
W_{U_{S_{1}},k}\subset T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1}}/S_{1})

denote the corresponding closed subset.
By
[
6
, Theorem 6.9.1]
and

[
7
, Theorem 12.2.4]
,
we may assume the following.

(a1)

W_{U_{S_{1}},k}
is flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S_{1}
.

(a2)

Each geometric fiber of
W_{U_{S_{1}},k}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})

is irreducible and reduced.

(a3)

W_{U_{S_{1}},k}
induces

W_{\mathbb{K}_{1},k}\cap T^{\ast}(U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.

Because
W_{U_{S_{1}},k}
is flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})
,
the following morphism is injective.

\widetilde{R}_{S_{1}}/\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}_{S_{1},k}\to\Bigl(\widetilde{R}_{S_{1}}/\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}_{S_{1},k}\Bigr)\otimes_{S_{1}}\mathbb{K}_{1}=\widetilde{R}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}/\mathfrak{I}^{\prime}_{k}.

Hence, the above conditions
(a1,a2,a3) characterize
W_{U_{S_{1}},k}
.

Let
X_{S_{1}}=\bigcup_{q=1}^{M}U_{S_{1},q}

be a covering by affine open subsets.
We obtain the closed subsets

W_{U_{S_{1},q},k}\subset T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1},q}/S_{1})

(k=1,\ldots,m_{2},\,\,q=1,\ldots,M)
.
Because of the characterization by
the conditions (a1,a2,a3),
we obtain

W_{U_{S_{1},q(1)},k}\cap T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1},q(1)}/S_{1})\cap T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1},q(2)}/S_{1})=W_{U_{S_{1},q(2)},k}\cap T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1},q(1)}/S_{1})\cap T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1},q(2)}/S_{1})

in
T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1},q(1)}/S_{1})\cap T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1},q(2)}/S_{1})
.
Hence, there exist closed subsets

W_{S_{1},k}
such that

W_{S_{1},k}\cap T^{\ast}(U_{S_{1},q}/S_{1})=W_{U_{S_{1},q},k}.

By the construction,
the following holds.

•

W_{S_{1},k}
are flat over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})
.

•

Each geometric fiber of

W_{S_{1},k}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})

is irreducible and reduced.

•

W_{S_{1},k}
induces
W_{\mathbb{K}_{1},k}
.

Clearly,
W_{S_{1},k}\to X^{\circ}_{S_{1}}
is dominant.
We may assume that

W_{S_{1},k}\to X^{\circ}_{S_{1},k}
is proper.
Indeed, let
T^{\ast}(X_{S_{1}}^{\circ}/S_{1})\subset\mathbb{P}

denote the projective completion.
Let
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}
denote the complement of

T^{\ast}(X_{S_{1}}^{\circ}/S_{1})
.
Let
\overline{W}_{S_{1},k}
denote the closure of

W_{S_{1},k}
in
\mathbb{P}
.
Let
T\subset X_{S_{1}}^{\circ}

denote the image of

\overline{W}_{S_{1},k}\cap\mathbb{P}_{\infty}

by the projection
\mathbb{P}\to X_{S_{1}}^{\circ}
.
It is a closed subset.
Because
W_{\mathbb{K}_{1},k}\to X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

is proper,
we obtain that
T\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S_{1})
is not dominant.
Hence, we may assume that
T=\emptyset
,
i.e.,

W_{S_{1},k}\to X^{\circ}_{S_{1}}
is proper.

Let
(X_{S},\boldsymbol{Z}_{S},{\boldsymbol{W}}_{S})
be a model over
S
.
For any closed point
s
of
S
,
by taking the fiber over
s
,
we obtain
the
\mathbb{K}
-variety
X_{S,s}

with the tuple of closed subsets

\boldsymbol{Z}_{S,s}=(Z_{S,s,j}\,|\,j=1,\ldots,m_{1})
of
X_{S,s}

and the tuple of closed subsets

{\boldsymbol{W}}_{S,s}=(W_{S,s,k}\,|\,k=1,\ldots,m_{2})
of

T^{\ast}X^{\circ}_{S,s}
,
where
X^{\circ}_{S,s}=X_{S,s}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{1}}Z_{S,s,j}
.

Lemma 2.54

W_{S,s,k}\to X^{\circ}_{S,s}

is finite and dominant over
X^{\circ}_{S,s}
.

Proof
Because
W_{S,k}\to X^{\circ}_{S}
is proper,
we obtain that

W_{S,s,k}\to X^{\circ}_{S,s}
is proper,
which implies the finiteness.
By using the valuative criterion,
we obtain that the morphism

W_{S,k}\to X^{\circ}_{S}

induces
a surjection of the closed points.
In particular,
W_{S,s,k}\to X^{\circ}_{S,s}
is dominant.

2.6.2
Models for meromorphic covers

Let
X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
be a normal irreducible projective
\mathbb{K}_{1}
-variety
with a hypersurface

H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.
Let

H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{1}}H_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j}

denote the irreducible decomposition.
Let
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\subset T^{\ast}(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})

be a meromorphic finite cover over
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.
Let
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{m_{2}}\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1},k}

denote the irreducible decomposition.
We obtain the tuples

{\boldsymbol{H}}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=(H_{\mathbb{K}_{1},j})

and

{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1},k})
.

Let
S\subset\mathbb{K}_{1}
be
a regular ring finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
(X_{S},{\boldsymbol{H}}_{S},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{S})

be a model of

(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},{\boldsymbol{H}}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.
We obtain the hypersurface

H_{S}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{1}}H_{S,j}
of
X_{S}
,
and the closed subset

\Sigma_{S}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{m_{2}}\Sigma_{S,k}

of
T^{\ast}(X^{\circ}_{S}/S)
,
where

X^{\circ}_{S}=X_{S}\setminus H_{S}
.

Definition 2.55

Such
(X_{S},H_{S},\Sigma_{S})

is called a model of
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},\Sigma)

over
S
.

We obtain the following proposition
by Lemma
2.46
.

Proposition 2.56

Suppose that
X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
is smooth,
that
H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
is simply normal crossing,
and that
\Sigma
is good over
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.
Then,
there exists a regular subring
S\subset\mathbb{K}_{1}

finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}_{1}

and a model
(X_{S},H_{S},\Sigma_{S})
of

(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})

over
S

such that the following holds.

•

X_{S}
is smooth over
S
,
and
H_{S}
is normal crossing relative to
S

with trivial monodromy.
(See
§
2.5.2
.)

•

\Sigma_{S}
is good on
(X_{S},H_{S})
relative to
S

in the sense of Definition
2.44
.

•

If
\Sigma
is Lagrangian,
we can impose the additional condition
that
\Sigma_{S}
is also Lagrangian.

2.7
Change of base fields

2.7.1
Statements

Let
X_{\mathbb{K}}
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a normal crossing hypersurface
H_{\mathbb{K}}
.
Let
H_{\mathbb{K}}=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{\mathbb{K},i}
denote the irreducible decomposition.
For any
I\subset\Lambda
,
we set

H_{\mathbb{K},I}=\bigcap_{i\in I}H_{\mathbb{K},i}

and

H_{\mathbb{K},I}^{\circ}=H_{\mathbb{K},I}\setminus\bigcup_{i\not\in I}H_{\mathbb{K},i}
.

Let
\mathbb{K}\subset\mathbb{K}_{1}
be an extension of
algebraically closed fields of characteristic
0
.
Let
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})

denote the pair of varieties induced by

(X_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}})

and the extension
\mathbb{K}\to\mathbb{K}_{1}
.
The following proposition is obvious.

Proposition 2.57

A meromorphic cover

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}}\subset T^{\ast}(X_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}})

over
(X_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}})

is etale locally good if and only if
the induced meromorphic cover

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}}\times\mathbb{K}_{1}
over
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})

is etale locally good.

Proof
The “only if” part is clear.
Suppose that
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}}\times\mathbb{K}_{1}
is etale locally good.
Let
S\subset\mathbb{K}_{1}
be a regular
\mathbb{K}
-algebra
which is finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
(X_{S},H_{S},\Sigma_{S})

be induced by

(X_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}},\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}})

by
\mathbb{K}\to S
.
According to Lemma
2.46
,
if
S
is sufficiently large,
we may assume that

\Sigma_{S}
is etale locally good.
By taking the fiber over any closed point of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
,
we obtain that

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}}
is etale locally good.

Let

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\subset T^{\ast}(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})

be a formally good meromorphic cover over
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.
Any closed point
x
of
X_{\mathbb{K}}

induces a closed point
x_{1}
of
X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.
We have the natural inclusion

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H_{\mathbb{K}})}_{X_{\mathbb{K}},x}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})\big/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H_{\mathbb{K}})}_{X_{\mathbb{K}},x}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})}_{X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},x_{1}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})}_{X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},x_{1}}.

We shall prove the following proposition in §
2.7.5
.

Proposition 2.58

We assume the following condition.

•

If
H_{\mathbb{K},I}\neq\emptyset
,
any irreducible component of

H_{\mathbb{K},I}^{\circ}

contains a closed point
x

such that

\mathcal{I}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},x_{1})\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H_{\mathbb{K}})}_{X_{\mathbb{K}},x}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})\big/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H_{\mathbb{K}})}_{X_{\mathbb{K}},x}.

Then, there exists

\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}\subset T^{\ast}(X_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}})

such that the following holds.

•

Each irreducible component of
\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}
is
proper and dominant over
X_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}}
.

•

\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}
is good on
(X_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}})
.
In particular,

\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}\times\mathbb{K}_{1}
is good on

(X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.

•

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}\times\mathbb{K}_{1})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.

If
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
is Lagrangian,
we can impose the additional condition that

\Sigma^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}
is Lagrangian.

Remark 2.59

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}}
does not necessarily induce
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.

2.7.2
Injectivity

Let
U_{\mathbb{K}}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{\mathbb{K}}
be
an
n
-dimensional smooth connected affine
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a normal crossing hypersurface
H_{\mathbb{K}}
of
U_{\mathbb{K}}
.
Let
x\in U_{\mathbb{K}}
be any closed point.
Assume that there exists an etale coordinate

\psi_{\mathbb{K}}:U_{\mathbb{K}}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{K}[z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}]

such that
(i)
H_{\mathbb{K}}=\psi_{\mathbb{K}}^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{j=1}^{\ell}\{z_{j}=0\}\bigr)
,
(ii)

\psi_{\mathbb{K}}^{-1}(\{z_{j}=0\})

are connected,
(iii)
\psi_{\mathbb{K}}(x)=(0,\ldots,0)
.
Let
\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}
denote the completion of
R_{\mathbb{K}}
at
x
.

Lemma 2.60

R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/R_{\mathbb{K}}\to\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}

is injective.

Proof
Let
a\in R_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/R_{\mathbb{K},x}

be any element.
It generates the submodule

R_{\mathbb{K},x}a\subset R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/R_{\mathbb{K},x}
.
Because
\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}

is faithfully flat over
R_{\mathbb{K},x}
,
we obtain the injection

0\neq(R_{\mathbb{K},x}a)\otimes\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}\to\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}
.
Hence,

R_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/R_{\mathbb{K},c}\to\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}

is injective.
Let
a\in R_{\mathbb{K},x}\cap R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})
.
There exists
f\in R_{\mathbb{K}}
such that

a\in R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast f)\cap R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})

and that
f\not\in\mathfrak{m}_{x}
.
Because
R_{\mathbb{K}}
is regular,
we have
R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast f)\cap R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast H)=R_{\mathbb{K}}
.

Corollary 2.61

Let
\mathcal{I}_{1},\mathcal{I}_{2}\subset R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/R_{\mathbb{K}}

be good sets of irregular values.
Let
\mathcal{I}_{1|\widehat{x}}
and
\mathcal{I}_{2|\widehat{x}}

denote the induced subsets of

\widehat{R}_{x}(\ast H)/\widehat{R}_{x}
.
If
\mathcal{I}_{1|\widehat{x}}=\mathcal{I}_{2|\widehat{x}}
,
then
\mathcal{I}_{1}=\mathcal{I}_{2}
.

2.7.3
Good sets of irregular values

Let
\mathbb{K}_{1}
be an algebraically closed field which contains
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
and

H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
denote the
\mathbb{K}_{1}
-varieties
induced by
U_{\mathbb{K}}
and
H_{\mathbb{K}}
,
respectively.
Let

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\subset R_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})/R_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

be a good finite subset.
Let
x_{1}
be the closed point of
U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

induced by
x
.

Lemma 2.62

Suppose that

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}|\widehat{x}_{1}}\subset\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K}_{1},x_{1}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K}_{1},x_{1}}

is contained in

\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}
.
Then,

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
is contained in

R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast H)/R_{\mathbb{K}}
.

Proof
Let
S\subset\mathbb{K}_{1}

be a regular subring finitely generated over
\mathbb{K}
.
We set
U_{S}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R_{S}):=U_{\mathbb{K}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S

and
H_{S}:=H_{\mathbb{K}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S
.
We set
R_{S}=R\otimes S
.
If
S
is sufficiently large,
there exists

\mathcal{I}_{S}\subset R_{S}(\ast H_{S})/R_{S}

which induces

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

by the extension
S\to\mathbb{K}_{1}
.
For each closed point
s\in\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S
,
we obtain
\mathcal{I}_{S,s}\subset R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/R_{\mathbb{K}}

as the pull back of
\mathcal{I}_{S}

by
U_{\mathbb{K}}\simeq U_{S}\times s\to U_{S}
.

Let
x_{S}:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{S}
denote the section
induced by
x:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{K}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R_{\mathbb{K}}
.
Let
I\subset R_{S}
denote the ideal corresponding to
the morphism
R_{S}\to S
.
Let
\widehat{R}_{S,I}
denote the completion of
R_{S}

with respect to
I
.
Let
\mathcal{I}_{S|\widehat{x}_{S}}\subset\widehat{R}_{S,I}(\ast H_{S})/\widehat{R}_{S}

denote the tuple induced by

\mathcal{I}_{S}
.
Because

\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}=\mathbb{K}[\![z_{1},\ldots,z_{\ell}]\!],\quad\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K}_{1},x_{1}}=\mathbb{K}_{1}[\![z_{1},\ldots,z_{\ell}]\!],\quad\quad\widehat{R}_{S,x_{S}}=S[\![z_{1},\ldots,z_{\ell}]\!],

\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}\to\widehat{R}_{S,I}(\ast H_{S})/\widehat{R}_{S,I}\to\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K}_{1},x_{1}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K}_{1},x_{1}}

are injective.
Because

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}|\widehat{x}_{1}}\subset\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H)/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}
,
we obtain that

\mathcal{I}_{S|\widehat{x}_{S}}\subset\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H)/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}
.

Let
s
be any closed point of
S
.
By the previous consideration,
we obtain

\mathcal{I}_{S,s|\widehat{x}_{S}}=\mathcal{I}_{S|\widehat{x}_{S}}=\mathcal{I}_{S|\widehat{x}}
.
We obtain

\mathcal{I}_{S,s|\widehat{x}}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}|\widehat{x}_{1}}
,
and hence

\mathcal{I}_{S,s}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.

2.7.4
Unramifiedly good meromorphic finite cover

Let

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\subset T^{\ast}(U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})

be a meromorphic finite cover over
(U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.
Assume that
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}

is unramifiedly good on
(U_{\mathbb{K}_{1}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.
We set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}:=\mathcal{I}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.
(See §
2.4.4
.)
Suppose that

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}|\widehat{x}_{1}}\subset\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}(\ast H)/\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{K},x}
.
By Lemma
2.62
,

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
is induced by
a good set

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}}\subset R_{\mathbb{K}}(\ast H)/R_{\mathbb{K}}
.

Lemma 2.63

There exists
an unramifiedly good meromorphic finite cover

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}}

over
(U_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}})

such that

\mathcal{I}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}})=\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{K}}
.
It implies

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}}\times\mathbb{K}_{1})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.

Proof
Let
S\subset\mathbb{K}_{1}
be a regular subring finitely generated over

\mathbb{K}

such that there exists an unramifiedly good model

\Sigma_{S}\subset T^{\ast}((U_{S}\setminus H_{S})/S)

of
\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
as in Proposition
2.56
.
Let
s\in\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
be any closed point.
We obtain

\Sigma_{S,s}\subset T^{\ast}(U_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}})

which is unramifiedly good
such that

\mathcal{I}(\Sigma_{S,s}\times\mathbb{K}_{1})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.

2.7.5
Proof of Proposition
2.58

Let
(S,X_{S},H_{S},\Sigma_{S})

be a model as in Proposition
2.56
.
For any closed point
s
of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
,
we obtain a good meromorphic finite cover

\Sigma_{S,s}
over
(X_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}})
.
By Lemma
2.63
,
we obtain that

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma_{S,s}\times\mathbb{K}_{1})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}_{1}})
.

2.8
Resolutions of meromorphic Lagrangian covers

2.8.1
The absolute case

Let
X
be a projective normal
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a hypersurface
H

such that
X\setminus H
is smooth.

Proposition 2.64

For any meromorphic Lagrangian cover

\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)
over
(X,H)
,
there exists a birational projective morphism

\rho:X^{\prime}\to X

such that the following holds.

•

X^{\prime}
is smooth,
and

H^{\prime}=\rho^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing.

•

\rho
induces an isomorphism

X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus H
.

•

\rho^{\ast}\Sigma
is etale locally good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.

Proof
It is enough to consider the case where

X
is smooth and
H
is normal crossing.
It is enough to consider the case where

\mathbb{K}
is an algebraic closure of
a field which is finitely generated over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Therefore, it is enough to consider the case
where
\mathbb{K}
is a subfield of
the complex number field
{\mathbb{C}}
.
We set
X_{{\mathbb{C}}}=X\times_{\mathbb{K}}{\mathbb{C}}
,

H_{{\mathbb{C}}}=H\times_{\mathbb{K}}{\mathbb{C}}

and

\Sigma_{{\mathbb{C}}}=\Sigma\times_{\mathbb{K}}{\mathbb{C}}
.
By
[
26
, Theorem 15.2.7]
,
there exists
a projective birational morphism of smooth
{\mathbb{C}}
-varieties

\rho_{{\mathbb{C}}}:X^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to X_{{\mathbb{C}}}

such that
(i)
H^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}}=\rho^{-1}(H_{{\mathbb{C}}})
is normal crossing,
(ii)
\rho_{{\mathbb{C}}}
induces an isomorphism

X^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\setminus H^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\simeq X_{{\mathbb{C}}}\setminus H_{{\mathbb{C}}}
,
(iii)
\Sigma_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\prime}=\rho_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{{\mathbb{C}}}

is formally good on
(X^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}},H^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
.
By Proposition
2.35
,
it is etale locally good on
(X^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}},H^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
.

Let
(S,X^{\prime}_{S},H^{\prime}_{S},\Sigma^{\prime}_{S})
be a model as in
Proposition
2.56
.
We may assume that
there exists a morphism

\rho_{S}:X^{\prime}_{S}\to X\times S
over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits S

which induces
\rho_{{\mathbb{C}}}
.
Let
s
be any closed point of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
.
We obtain a birational projective morphism

\rho_{S,s}:X^{\prime}_{S,s}\to X

such that

H^{\prime}_{S,s}=\rho_{S,s}^{-1}(H)

and

\Sigma^{\prime}_{S,s}=\rho_{S,s}^{\ast}\Sigma
.
By Lemma
2.45
and Lemma
2.49
,
we obtain that

\Sigma^{\prime}_{S,s}
is good on
(X^{\prime}_{S,s},H^{\prime}_{S,s})
.

2.8.2
The family case

Let
X
be a projective normal
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
There exists a closed subset
Z\subset X

with
\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits Z\geq 2

such that
X\setminus Z
is regular.
We set
X^{\circ}=X\setminus Z
.
There exists the reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-coherent sheaf

T^{\ast}X
whose restriction to
X\setminus Z

equals the cotangent bundle
T^{\ast}(X^{\circ})
.
Let
H
be a hypersurface of
X
such that

Z\subset H
.
We set

\mathcal{E}_{H,N}=T^{\ast}X(NH)

and

\mathcal{E}_{H,N}^{\circ}=T^{\ast}X^{\circ}(NH)
.
As in §
2.3.3
,
we obtain the algebraic set

H^{0}(X^{\circ},S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{H,N}))

of sections
X^{\circ}\to S^{r}(\mathcal{E}_{H,N})
.
Let
\mathcal{G}_{H,N}
be a locally free sheaf on
X

with an epimorphism
\mathcal{G}_{H,N}^{\lor}\to\mathcal{E}^{\lor}_{H,N}
.
By Corollary
2.14

and Lemma
2.16
,
there exists a stratification

H^{0}(X^{\circ},S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{N,H}))=\bigsqcup_{i\in\Lambda}T_{i}

by locally Zariski closed subsets
such that the following holds.

•

{\tt P}(\phi)

(\phi\in T_{i})
are constant.

•

There exist closed subschemes

\Sigma(T_{i})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\subset T_{i}\times\mathcal{G}_{H,N}

such that

\Sigma(T_{i})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\to T_{i}
are flat,
and that the fibers over

\phi\in T_{i}
are
\Sigma(\phi_{\mathcal{G}_{H,N}})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}
.

•

There exist torsion-free sheaves
\mathcal{F}^{k}

on

\Sigma(T_{i})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

as in
Lemma
2.17
,
which are flat over
T_{i}
.

Let
T_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}
denote the set of
the closed points
\phi\in T_{i}

such that

\Sigma(\phi)_{U(\phi),\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}\times_{X^{\circ}}(U(\phi)\setminus H)\subset T^{\ast}(U(\phi)\setminus H)

are Lagrangian.
By Lemma
2.18
,
we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 2.65

T_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}
are Zariski closed subsets of
T_{i}
.

Let
\Sigma(T_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

denote the fiber product of
\Sigma(T_{i})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

and
T_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}
over
T_{i}
.

Proposition 2.66

There exists a morphism

\mathbb{K}
-varieties

\psi:\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\to T_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}

and
a projective birational morphism

\rho_{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}}:\widehat{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times X}\to\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times X

such that the following holds.

(i)

\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}
is smooth and affine over
\mathbb{K}
,
and
\psi
induces a surjection of
closed points.

(ii)

\rho_{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}}
induces
an isomorphism

\rho_{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times(X\setminus H))\simeq\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times(X\setminus H)
.

(iii)

The induced morphism

\widehat{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times X}\to\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}

is smooth,
and

\widehat{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times H}=\rho_{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times H)

is a normal crossing hypersurface of

\widehat{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times X}\to\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}

relative to
\widetilde{T}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}

without monodromy.
(See
§
2.5.2
.)

(iv)

Let
\widehat{\Sigma}(T^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}_{i})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

denote the fiber product of

\Sigma(T_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

and

\widehat{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times X}

over

\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times X
.
Then,

\widehat{\Sigma}(T^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}_{i})_{\mathop{\rm red}\nolimits}

is etale locally good
over

\bigl(\widehat{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times X},\widehat{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}\times H}\bigr)
.

Proof
Let
T_{i}^{\prime}\subset T_{i}
be any Zariski closed irreducible subset.
Let
R_{i}^{\prime}
be the ring of algebraic functions on
T_{i}^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
be an algebraic closure of
the fractional field of
R_{i}^{\prime}
.
The universal section

T_{i}^{\prime}\times X^{\circ}\to T_{i}^{\prime}\times S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{H,N})

induces the section

\phi_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}:X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}\to S^{r}(\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{H,N})_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
.

We obtain
the meromorphic Lagrangian cover

\Sigma(\phi_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})\subset T^{\ast}(X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})

over
X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
.
By Proposition
2.64
,
there exists a projective birational morphism

\rho_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}:\widehat{X}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}\to X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}

such that the following holds.

•

\widehat{X}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
is smooth over
\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
,
and

\widehat{H}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}=\rho_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}^{-1}(H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})

is a normal crossing hypersurface of

\widehat{X}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
.

•

\rho_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
induces an isomorphism

\widehat{X}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}\setminus\widehat{H}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}\simeq X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
.

•

\widehat{\Sigma}(\phi_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})=\rho_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}^{\ast}\Sigma(\phi_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})

is etale locally good on

(\widehat{X}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},\widehat{H}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})
.

There exists a model
Let
(S,\widehat{X}_{S},\widehat{H}_{S},\widehat{\Sigma}_{S})

of
(\widehat{X}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},\widehat{H}_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},\widehat{\Sigma}(\phi_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}))

as in Proposition
2.56
.
We may assume that
R_{i}^{\prime}\subset S\subset\mathbb{K}^{\prime}

and that
there exists a morphism

\rho_{S}:\widehat{X}_{S}\to X\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

which induces
\rho_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}
.
The image of the morphism
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)\to T_{i}^{\prime}
contains
a Zariski open subset
U_{i}^{\prime}
.
We may assume that
the image is
U_{i}^{\prime}
,
and that
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)\to U_{i}^{\prime}
is smooth.

By this procedure and a Noetherian induction,
we can construct
\psi
and
\rho_{\widetilde{T}_{i}^{\mathop{\rm Lag}\nolimits}}

as desired.

Corollary 2.67

There exist a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
S
,
a meromorphic Lagrangian cover

\Sigma_{S}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\circ}_{H,N}\times S
,
and a smooth projective variety
\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}
over
\mathcal{S}

with a morphism
\rho:\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{S}\times X

such that the following holds.

•

\widehat{H}_{S}=\rho^{-1}(H\times S)
is normal crossing relative to
S
.

•

\rho^{\ast}\Sigma_{S}
is good on

(\widehat{X}_{S},\widehat{H}_{S})
.

•

For any meromorphic cover
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{E}_{H,N}
,
there exists
a\in S

such that

\Sigma_{S}\times S_{a}=\Sigma
.
Moreover,

\rho_{s}^{\ast}(\Sigma)
is good on

(\widehat{X}_{s},\widehat{H}_{s})
.

2.9
Meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity

2.9.1
Preliminary

Let
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}
be meromorphic Lagrangian covers
on
(X,H)
.
Suppose that
there exists a projective morphism
of smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

\rho:X^{\prime}\to X

such that
(i)
H^{\prime}=\rho^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing,
(ii)
X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus H
,
(iii)
\rho^{\ast}\Sigma_{i}
are
etale locally good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
,
(iv)

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho^{\ast}\Sigma_{1})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho^{\ast}\Sigma_{2})
.

Lemma 2.68

Let
\rho_{1}:X_{1}\to X

be a projective morphism of smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties
such that
(i)
H_{1}=\rho_{1}^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing,
(ii)
X_{1}\setminus H_{1}\simeq X\setminus H
,
(iii)
\rho_{1}^{\ast}\Sigma_{1}
are
etale locally good on
(X_{1},H_{1})
.
Then,
\rho_{1}^{\ast}\Sigma_{2}
is good on
(X_{1},H_{1})
,
and we have

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho_{1}^{\ast}\Sigma_{1})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho_{1}^{\ast}\Sigma_{2})
.

Proof
It follows from Proposition
2.36
.

2.9.2
Meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity

We introduce an equivalence relation
for meromorphic Lagrangian covers.

Definition 2.69

For meromorphic Lagrangian covers
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}
on
(X,H)
,
we say
\Sigma_{1}\sim\Sigma_{2}

if the following holds.

•

There exists a projective morphism
of smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

\rho:X^{\prime}\to X

such that
(i)
H^{\prime}=\rho^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing,
(ii)
X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus H
,
(iii)
\rho^{\ast}\Sigma_{i}
are
etale locally good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
,
(iv)

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho^{\ast}\Sigma_{1})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho^{\ast}\Sigma_{2})
.

An equivalence class is called
a meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity.
An equivalence class of a meromorphic Lagrangian cover
\Sigma

is denoted by
{\boldsymbol{I}}(\Sigma)

and called the irregularity of
\Sigma
.

Remark 2.70

The degrees of
\Sigma_{i}\to X\setminus H

are not necessarily equal
because we do not consider the multiplicity
in this paper.

We obtain the following proposition from
Lemma
2.68
.

Proposition 2.71

Let
\Sigma
be a meromorphic Lagrangian cover over
(X,H)
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{I}}(\Sigma)
denote the irregularity of
\Sigma
.
Let
\rho:X^{\prime}\to X
be any morphism of smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties
such that
(i)
H^{\prime}=\rho^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing,
(ii)
X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus H
,
(iii)
\rho^{\ast}\Sigma
is etale locally good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.
Then, for any
\Sigma_{1}\in{\boldsymbol{I}}(\Sigma)
,

\rho^{\ast}(\Sigma_{1})
is etale locally good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
,
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho^{\ast}\Sigma_{1})={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho^{\ast}\Sigma)
holds.

Definition 2.72

A meromorphic Lagrangian regularity
{\boldsymbol{I}}

is called good (resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(X,H)

if any
\Sigma\in{\boldsymbol{I}}
is
good (resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(X,H)
.
It is equivalent to the condition
that there exists
\Sigma\in{\boldsymbol{I}}

which is
good (resp. logarithmic, unramifiedly good)
on
(X,H)
.

2.9.3
Pull back and push-forward via birational projective morphisms

Let
\varphi:X^{\prime}\to X
be a birational projective morphism
such that
H^{\prime}=\varphi^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing
and that
X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus H
.
We have the natural isomorphism

T^{\ast}(X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime})\simeq T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)
.

Lemma 2.73

•

Let
{\boldsymbol{I}}
be a meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity over
(X,H)
.
For any
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}\in{\boldsymbol{I}}
,
we have
{\boldsymbol{I}}(\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma_{1})={\boldsymbol{I}}(\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma_{2})
.

•

Let
{\boldsymbol{I}}^{\prime}
be a meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity over
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.
For any
\Sigma^{\prime}_{1},\Sigma^{\prime}_{2}\in{\boldsymbol{I}}^{\prime}
,
we have
{\boldsymbol{I}}(\varphi_{\ast}\Sigma_{1}^{\prime})={\boldsymbol{I}}(\varphi_{\ast}\Sigma_{2}^{\prime})
.

Proof
The first claim is clear.
The second claim follows from Proposition
2.36
.

Hence, we define

\varphi^{\ast}({\boldsymbol{I}}):={\boldsymbol{I}}(\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma)

for any
\Sigma\in{\boldsymbol{I}}
.
We also define

\varphi_{\ast}({\boldsymbol{I}}^{\prime}):={\boldsymbol{I}}(\varphi_{\ast}\Sigma^{\prime})

for any
\Sigma^{\prime}\in{\boldsymbol{I}}^{\prime}
.

2.9.4
Comparison

Let
S
,
X
,
H
be as in §
2.5.2
.
Let
\Sigma^{(i)}\subset T^{\ast}((X\setminus H)/S)

(i=1,2)
be
Lagrangian covers of
X\setminus H
which are etale locally good on
(X,H)
.
For each closed point
z
of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
,
we obtain good meromorphic Lagrangian covers
\Sigma^{(i)}_{z}

on
(X_{z},H_{z})
.
We obtain the following proposition from
Proposition
2.51
.

Proposition 2.74

There exists a closed subset
Z\subset\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)

such that

{\boldsymbol{I}}(\Sigma^{(1)}_{z})={\boldsymbol{I}}(\Sigma^{(2)}_{z})

if and only if

z\in Z
.

3
Complex analytic meromorphic flat bundles

3.1
Good meromorphic flat bundles

3.1.1
Formal good meromorphic flat bundles

Let
X
be any
n
-dimensional complex manifold.
Let
H
be a simple normal crossing hypersurface of
X
.
Let
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
denote the completion
of the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}

with respect to the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{x}
.
There exists a parameter system
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
of
\mathfrak{m}_{x}

such that the ideal of
H
at
x
is generated by

\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}
.
For any positive integer
e
,
we set

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}[x_{i}^{1/e}\,|\,i=1,\ldots,\ell]
.
For any
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-module
\mathcal{F}
,
we set

\mathcal{F}(\ast H)=\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}

and

\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{F})=\mathcal{F}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}
.
For any free
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)
-module
\mathcal{F}
,
a lattice of
\mathcal{F}
means
a free
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
-submodule
\mathcal{F}_{0}\subset\mathcal{F}

such that
\mathcal{F}_{0}(\ast H)=\mathcal{F}
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a free
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)
-module of finite rank
with an integrable connection
\nabla:\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}
.
Any
\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{V})
is equipped with
the induced integrable connection
\nabla
.
We recall some definitions.

Definition 3.1

•

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is called regular
if there exists a free
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-module
\mathcal{V}_{0}\subset\mathcal{V}

such that
(i)
\mathcal{V}_{0}(\ast H)=\mathcal{V}
,
(ii)
\nabla(\mathcal{V}_{0})\subset\mathcal{V}_{0}\otimes\Omega^{1}(\log H)
.

•

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is called unramifiedly good
if there exist a good set of irregular values

\mathcal{I}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)/\mathcal{O}_{X,x}

and a decomposition

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}})

(1)

such that

\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}}

are regular.
Here,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}

denote lifts of
\mathfrak{a}
.
We allow
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}=0

in
(
1
)
.
The set

\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{V})=\bigl\{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}\,|\,\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}\neq 0\bigr\}

is well defined.

•

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is called good
if there exists
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

such that

\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{V},\nabla)

is unramifiedly good.
We obtain the set

\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{V})\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}(\ast h)/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}
.

3.1.2
The associated Deligne-Malgrange good filtered bundles
in the formal case

Let
H_{x,i}=\{x_{i}=0\}
.
For any
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-module
\mathcal{F}
,
we set
\mathcal{F}_{|H_{x,i}}=\mathcal{F}/x_{i}\mathcal{F}
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is regular.
For each lattice
\mathcal{V}_{0}\subset\mathcal{V}
such that

\nabla(\mathcal{V}_{0})\subset\mathcal{V}_{0}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log H)
,
we obtain the residue endomorphism

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)
on
\mathcal{V}_{|H_{x,i}}

by taking the residue with respect to
dx_{i}/x_{i}
.

Lemma 3.2

For any
{\boldsymbol{a}}=(a_{i})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
,
there exists a unique lattice

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}

such that the following holds.

•

Any eigenvalue
\alpha

of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)

satisfies

a_{i}-1<-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)\leq a_{i}
.

We obtain the filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(\mathcal{V})=(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(\mathcal{V})\,|\,{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell})

over
\mathcal{V}
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is unramifiedly good
with the decomposition (
1
).
We obtain
the filtered bundles

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}})

over
\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}

by applying the previous construction
to
(\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\mathfrak{a}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}})

which are regular.
We obtain

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V})=\bigoplus\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}).

Let us consider the case

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is good but not necessarily unramifiedly good.
By applying the previous construction
to the unramifiedly good

\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
,
we obtain the filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(\varphi_{e}^{\ast}\mathcal{V})

over
\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{V})
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}
denote the Galois group
of the extension

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
.
We have the natural action of
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}

on
\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{V})
.
It induces the action on
the filtered bundles

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}(\varphi^{\ast}(\mathcal{V}))
.
As the descent,
we obtain the filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V})
over
\mathcal{V}
.

In particular,
\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}(\mathcal{E})

is called the good Deligne-Malgrange lattice.

3.1.3
Good meromorphic flat bundles in the complex analytic case

Let
X
be any complex manifold.
Let
H
be a simple normal crossing hypersurface of
X
.
For each
x\in H
,
there exists a holomorphic coordinate system

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
such that

H=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{x_{i}=0\}
.
We set

\mathcal{O}_{X,x}^{(e)}=\mathcal{O}_{X,x}[x_{i}^{1/e}\,|\,i=1,\ldots,\ell]
.

Let
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
be a meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
For any
x\in X
,
we obtain

\mathcal{V}_{|\widehat{x}}=\mathcal{V}_{x}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}

with the induced integrable connection.
We recall some definitions.

Definition 3.3

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is called regular
(resp. unramifiedly good, good)
if
(\mathcal{V}_{|\widehat{x}},\nabla)

are regular (resp. unramifiedly good, good)
for any
x\in X
.

Proposition 3.4
(
[
25
]
)

If
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is good,
there exists
a filtered bundle
\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V}_{0})

over
\mathcal{V}_{0}

such that

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V}_{0})_{|\widehat{x}}=\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V}_{0|\widehat{x}})
.
In particular,
there exists a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule
\mathcal{V}_{0}

such that
\mathcal{V}_{0|\widehat{x}}

are good Deligne-Malgrange lattice of

\mathcal{V}_{|\widehat{x}}
.

If
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is good,
we obtain the set

\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{V},x)\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}

for any
x\in H

as the index set of the decomposition of

\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{V},\nabla)_{|\widehat{x}}
.
(See Definition
3.1
.)

Proposition 3.5

•

We have

\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{V},x)\subset\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}/\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{X,x}

for any
x\in H
.

•

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\mathcal{V})=(\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{V},x)\,|\,x\in H)

is a good system of ramified irregular values.

Proof
In the unramified case,
it is proved in
[
25
, Proposition 2.19]
.
The ramified case is reduced to the unramified case.

3.2
Meromorphic Lagrangian covers in the complex analytic case

3.2.1
Meromorphic finite covers

Let
X
be a complex manifold.
Let
H
be a complex analytic closed subset of
X
.
We set
X^{\circ}=X\setminus H
.
Let
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}X^{\circ}
be a closed subset.
Let
\mathbb{P}_{T^{\ast}X}
denote
the projective completion of
T^{\ast}X
.

Definition 3.6

\Sigma
is called a meromorphic cover over
(X,H)

if the closure of
\Sigma
in
\mathbb{P}_{T^{\ast}X}

is a complex analytic closed subset.
If the smooth part of
\Sigma
is Lagrangian with respect to
the natural symplectic structure of
T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)
,
it is called a meromorphic Lagrangian cover.

3.2.2
Some conditions in the case where
H
is normal crossing

Let us consider the case where
H
is a normal crossing hypersurface.

Definition 3.7

\Sigma
is called logarithmic
if the closure of
\Sigma
in
T^{\ast}X(\log H)

is proper over
X
.

For any section
\tau
of
T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)
,
let
\kappa_{\tau}
denote the automorphism of

T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

obtained as the addition of
\tau
.

Definition 3.8

•

Let
x\in H
.
We say that
\Sigma
is unramifiedly good at
x

if there exists
a good set of irregular values

\mathcal{I}(\Sigma,x)\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}/\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
,
a neighbourhood
X_{x}
,
and a decomposition

\Sigma_{|X_{x}\setminus H}=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\Sigma,x)}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

is logarithmic on
(X_{x},H\cap X_{x})
.

•

We say that
\Sigma
is unramifiedly good on
(X,H)

if it is unramifiedly good at any
x\in H
.

Let
x\in H
.
There exists a holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood

(X_{x},z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
around
x

such that

H\cap X_{x}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{\ell}\{z_{j}=0\}
.
By the coordinate, we may regard
X_{x}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
.
Let
\varphi_{e}:{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{n}

be defined by

\varphi_{e}(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n})=(\zeta_{1}^{e},\ldots,\zeta_{\ell}^{e},\zeta_{\ell+1},\ldots,\zeta_{n})
.
We set

X_{x}^{(e)}=\varphi_{x,e}^{-1}(X_{x})

and
H_{x}^{(e)}=\varphi_{x,e}^{-1}(H\cap X_{x})
.
The induced map

X_{x}^{(e)}\to X_{x}
is also denoted by
\varphi_{x,e}
.

Definition 3.9

•

\Sigma
is called good at
x

if there exist
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

and
a neighbourhood

X_{x}
of
x

such that
\varphi_{x,e}^{\ast}(\Sigma)

is unramifiedly good
on
(X_{x}^{(e)},H_{x}^{(e)})
.
In this case,
we obtain

\mathcal{I}(\Sigma,x)\subset\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}/\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{X,x}
.

•

\Sigma
is called good on
(X,H)

if it is good at any
x\in H
.
In this case,
we obtain the tuple

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma)=\bigl(\mathcal{I}(\Sigma,x)\,|\,x\in H\bigr)
.

3.2.3
Resolution

Let us consider the case where
H
is not necessarily
a normal crossing hypersurface.
The following proposition is already mentioned in
Proposition
2.64
.

Proposition 3.10
(
[
26
, Theorem 15.2.7]
)

There exists a projective morphism of complex manifolds
\rho:X^{\prime}\to X

such that
(i)
H^{\prime}=\rho^{-1}(H)
is a normal crossing hypersurface,
(ii)
\rho
induces an isomorphism
X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus H
,
(iii)
\rho^{\ast}(\Sigma)
is good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.

Proof
It is enough to consider the case where
H
is a normal crossing hypersurface,
and it is studied in
[
26
, Theorem 15.2.7]
.

3.2.4
Residues

Suppose that
\Sigma
is a good Lagrangian cover.
Let
x\in H
.
Let
\varphi_{x,e}:X^{(e)}_{x}\to X_{x}
be as before.
We have the decomposition

\varphi_{x,e}^{\ast}(\Sigma)=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\Sigma,x)}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that

\kappa_{d\mathfrak{a}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

is logarithmic.
Moreover,
if
X_{x}
is sufficiently small,
there exist

\mathbb{E}(\Sigma,x,\mathfrak{a})\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{\ell}

and a decomposition

\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}=\bigsqcup_{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{E}(\Sigma,x,\mathfrak{a})}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}

such that the following holds.

•

For
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{E}(\Sigma,x,\mathfrak{a})
,
we set

\tau({\boldsymbol{\alpha}})=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\alpha_{i}\varphi_{x,e}^{\ast}(dz_{i}/z_{i})
.
Let

\overline{\kappa_{d\mathfrak{a}+\tau({\boldsymbol{\alpha}})}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}

denote the closure of

\kappa_{d\mathfrak{a}+\tau({\boldsymbol{\alpha}})}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}

in

T^{\ast}X_{x}^{(e)}(\log H_{x}^{(e)})
.
Let
\pi_{x,e}:T^{\ast}X^{(e)}_{x}\to X^{(e)}_{x}

denote the projection.
Then,

\overline{\kappa_{d\mathfrak{a}+\tau({\boldsymbol{\alpha}})}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}\cap\pi_{x,e}^{-1}(H^{(e)}_{x})

is contained in the
0
-section.

3.3
Wild harmonic bundles

3.3.1
Harmonic bundles

Let
(E,\theta)
be a Higgs bundle on a complex manifold
Y
.
Let
h
be a Hermitian metric of
E
.
We obtain the Chern connection
\nabla_{h}
,
and the adjoint
\theta^{\dagger}_{h}
of
\theta
.
If the connection
\mathbb{D}_{h}^{1}=\nabla_{h}+\theta+\theta^{\dagger}_{h}
is flat,

(E,\theta,h)
is called a harmonic bundle.
For the Higgs bundle
(E,\theta)
,
we have the corresponding
\mathcal{O}_{T^{\ast}Y}
-module
\widetilde{E}
,
whose supports are finite over
Y
.
The support
\Sigma_{\theta}
is called the spectral cover.
We recall the following lemma,
which follows from Gabber’s theorem
[
4
]
.

Lemma 3.11

If
(E,\theta)
underlies a harmonic bundle
(E,\theta,h)
,

\Sigma_{\theta}
is Lagrangian.

3.3.2
Wild harmonic bundles and good wild harmonic bundles

Let
X
be a complex manifold
with a closed complex analytic subspace
H
.
Let
(E,\theta,h)
be a harmonic bundle on
X\setminus H
.

Definition 3.12

(E,\theta,h)
is called wild on
(X,H)

if
\Sigma_{\theta}
is a meromorphic cover over
(X,H)
.

Definition 3.13

Suppose that
H
is normal crossing hypersurface.

•

The harmonic bundle
(E,\theta,h)
is called tame
if
\Sigma_{\theta}
is logarithmic.

•

The harmonic bundle
(E,\theta,h)
is called good wild
(resp. unramifiedly good wild) on
(X,H)

if
\Sigma_{\theta}
is good
(resp. unramifiedly good) on
(X,H)
.

Definition 3.14

The harmonic bundle
(E,\theta,h)
is called

\sqrt{-1}{\mathbb{R}}
-wild harmonic bundle
if the following holds.

•

Let
\rho:X^{\prime}\to X
be a resolution for

\Sigma_{\theta}

as in Proposition
3.10
.
Then, for any
x\in H^{\prime}
and

\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\rho^{\ast}\Sigma)
,

\mathbb{E}(\rho^{\ast}\Sigma,x^{\prime},\mathfrak{a})

consists of
tuples of purely imaginary numbers.

3.3.3
The associated meromorphic integrable connections

Let
X
be a complex manifold
with a hypersurface
H
.
Let
\iota:X\setminus H\to X
denote the inclusion.

Let
(E,\theta,h)
be a wild harmonic bundle on
(X,H)
.
From a harmonic bundle on
(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h)
on
X\setminus H
,
we have the flat bundle
(E,\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h})
on
X\setminus H
.
We have the underlying holomorphic vector bundle

\mathcal{E}^{1}=(E,\overline{\partial}_{E}+\theta^{\dagger}_{h})
.
We regard it as an
\mathcal{O}_{X\setminus H}
-module.
We obtain the sheaf

\iota_{\ast}(\mathcal{E}^{1})
on
X
.

For any
x\in H
,
let
X_{x}
be a neighbourhood of
x

with a holomorphic function
f\in\mathcal{O}(X_{x})

such that
H=f^{-1}(0)
.
We obtain an
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}
-module

(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1})_{x}:=\Bigl\{s\in(\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{E}^{1})_{x}\,\Big|\,|s|_{h}=O\Bigl(|f|^{-C}\Bigr)\,\,\,\exists C>0\Bigr\}.

We obtain the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module

\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1}\subset\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{E}^{1}

whose stalk at
x
is
(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1})_{x}
.

Proposition 3.15

•

\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1}
is a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module,
and
\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h}
is an integrable connection
of
\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1}
.

•

Suppose that
H
is normal crossing.
Then,
(E,\theta,h)
is good on
(X,H)

if and only if

(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1},\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h})
is a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
In the case, we have

\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1},\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h},x)=\bigl\{2\mathfrak{a}\,\big|\,\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\Sigma_{\theta},x)\bigr\}
.

3.3.4
The correspondence in the projective case

Suppose that
X
is projective.

Theorem 3.16

For any semisimple meromorphic flat bundle
(V,\nabla)
on
(X,H)
,
there exists a
\sqrt{-1}{\mathbb{R}}
-wild harmonic bundle
(E,\theta,h)
such that

(\mathcal{P}^{h}\mathcal{E}^{1},\mathbb{D}^{1}_{h})\simeq(V,\nabla)
,
which is unique up to isomorphism.

3.4
The associated meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity
in the algebraic case

Let
X
be a normal complex projective variety.
Let
H
be any hypersurface of
X
such that
X\setminus H
is smooth.
Let
(V,\nabla)
be any meromorphic flat connection on
(X,H)
.
There exists a Jordan-Hölder filtration,
namely,
each graded piece is simple.
By Theorem
3.16
,
we have the
\sqrt{-1}{\mathbb{R}}
-wild harmonic bundle
(E,\theta)

corresponding to
the associated graded meromorphic flat bundle.
Let
\Psi_{2}:T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)\to T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

be the automorphism obtained as
v\mapsto 2v

in the fiber direction.
We set

\Sigma(V,\nabla):=\Psi_{2}(\Sigma_{\theta})
.

Corollary 3.17

Let
\varphi:X^{\prime}\to X
be a projective morphism
such that
H^{\prime}=\varphi^{-1}(H)
is normal crossing.
Then,

\varphi^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
is good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})

if and only if

\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma(V,\nabla)
is good on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.
In the case,
we have

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\varphi^{\ast}(V,\nabla))={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\varphi^{\ast}\Sigma(V,\nabla))
.

3.5
Local systems with Stokes structure

3.5.1
Partial orders on the index sets

Let
\varpi:\widetilde{X}(H)\to X
denote the oriented real blow up
along
H
.
Let
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}(H)}
denote the sheaf of
holomorphic functions on
\widetilde{X}(H)
.
For any
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{F}
,
we set

\mathcal{F}(\ast H)=\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\varpi^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X})}\varpi^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H))
.

Let
x\in H
.
We have a holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood

(X_{x};x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
around
x

such that
H\cap X_{x}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{x_{i}=0\}
.
We define

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}_{x}(H_{x})}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}_{x}(H_{x})}[x_{i}^{1/e}\,|\,i=1,\ldots,\ell]
.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{x}\subset\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{X,x}(\ast H)/\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{X,x}

be a good set of ramified irregular values.
By choosing branches
x_{i}^{1/e}

(i=1,\ldots,\ell)
,
we obtain

\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}_{x}(H)}(\ast H)_{x}\big/\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}_{x},x}
.
The image is independent of the choices
of
x^{1/e}_{i}
.

For each

\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}
,
we choose a lift

\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{x}(H)}(\ast H)_{y}
.
We define
\mathfrak{a}\leq_{y}\mathfrak{b}

if there exists a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}_{y}
of
y

in
\widetilde{X}(H)
such that

-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})\leq-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\mathfrak{b})

on
\mathcal{U}_{y}\setminus\varpi^{-1}(H)
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}=(\mathcal{I}_{x}\,|\,x\in H)
be
a good system of ramified irregular values.
If
x^{\prime}
is sufficiently close to
x
,
we have the natural surjection

\mathcal{I}_{x}\to\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
.
If
y^{\prime}\in\varpi^{-1}(x^{\prime})
is sufficiently close to

y\in\varpi^{-1}(x)
,
the map

\varphi_{x^{\prime},x}:\mathcal{I}_{x}\to\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}

preserves the partially orders

\leq_{x}
and
\leq_{x^{\prime}}
,
i.e.,
if
\mathfrak{a}\leq_{y}\mathfrak{b}
,
then
\varphi_{x^{\prime},x}(\mathfrak{a})\leq_{y^{\prime}}\varphi_{x^{\prime},x}(\mathfrak{b})
.

3.5.2
Stokes structures

Let
L
be a local system on
X\setminus H
.
Let
\widetilde{L}
denote the local system on
\widetilde{X}(H)

induced by
L
.
A Stokes structure of
\widetilde{L}

is a tuple of filtrations

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}=\bigl(\mathcal{F}^{y}(\widetilde{L}_{y})\,\big|\,y\in\varpi^{-1}(H)\bigr)

indexed by
the partially ordered set
(\mathcal{I}_{\varpi(y)},\leq_{y})

satisfying the following conditions.

•

There exists a decomposition

\widetilde{L}_{y}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}_{\varpi(y)}}G_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that

\mathcal{F}^{y}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\widetilde{L}_{y})=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{b}\leq_{y}\mathfrak{a}}G_{\mathfrak{b}}
.

•

If
y^{\prime}
is sufficiently close to
y
,
we have

\mathcal{F}^{y^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{b}}(\widetilde{L}_{y^{\prime}})=\sum_{\varphi_{x^{\prime},x}(\mathfrak{a})\leq\mathfrak{b}}\mathcal{F}^{y}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\widetilde{L}_{y})

under the isomorphism

\widetilde{L}_{y}\simeq\widetilde{L}_{y^{\prime}}
.
Here,
x^{\prime}=\varpi(y^{\prime})
and
x=\varpi(y)
.

3.5.3
Deformation (1)

Let
p:X\to B
be a smooth fibration of
complex manifolds.
Let
H
be a normal crossing hypersurface of
X
.
Let
b\in B
.
The fibers over
b
are denoted by
X_{b}
and
H_{b}
.
We assume the following conditions.

•

B
is simply connected.

•

There exists
b_{0}\in B

and a diffeomorphism

(X,H)\simeq B\times(X_{b_{0}},H_{b_{0}})

such that
the restriction to
(X_{b_{0}},H_{b_{0}})

is the identity.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
be a good system of ramified irregular values
on
(X,H)
.
We obtain the induced good system of ramified irregular values

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{b}
on
(X_{b},H_{b})
by the restriction.
We have the natural functor

\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\to\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X_{b},H_{b},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{b}).

(2)

Proposition 3.18

The functor
(
2
)

is an equivalence.

Proof
If
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
is unramified,
it is explained in

[
25
, Theorem 3.9]
.
Let us study the case where
there exists a ramified Galois covering

\varphi:(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})\to(X,H)
over
B

such that

\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
is unramified.
We have the equivalence

\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X^{\prime},H^{\prime},\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\simeq\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X^{\prime}_{b},H^{\prime}_{b},\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{b})
.
It is easy to obtain the equivalence for

\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits
-equivariant objects.
So, we obtain

\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\simeq\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X_{b},H_{b},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{b})
.

Let us study the general case.
Let
x\in H_{b}
.
There exists a diffeomorphism

F:B\times(X_{b},H_{b})\simeq(X,H)
.
We have a
C^{\infty}
-submanifold

F(B\times x)\subset(X,H)
.
There exists an open tubular neighbourhood
N

of
F(B\times x)

such that the following holds.

•

H\cap N=\bigcup_{j=1}^{\ell}H_{j}
,
where
H_{j}
are smooth hypersurfaces of
N
.
Let
H_{j,b}
denote the fiber over
b
.

•

There exists a diffeomorphism

(N,H_{1},\ldots,H_{\ell})\simeq B\times(N_{b},H_{1,b},\ldots,H_{\ell,b})
.

There exists a complex manifold

N^{\prime}

with a ramified Galois covering

\varphi:(N^{\prime},H^{\prime})\to(N,H\cap N)

ramified along each
H_{j}^{\prime}

such that

\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{|N}

is unramified.
By the previous consideration,

\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(N,H\cap N,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{|N})\simeq\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(N_{b},(H\cap N)_{b},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{|N_{b}})
.

Let
(\widetilde{L}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})

(i=1,2)
.
Let
f:(\widetilde{L}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|X_{b}}\to(\widetilde{L}_{2},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|X_{b}}

be a morphism.
Let
X=\bigcup N^{(i)}
be an open covering
such that

(N^{(i)},H\cap N^{(i)})\simeq B\times(N^{(i)}_{b},(H\cap N^{(i)})_{b})

where
N^{(i)}
are as above.
There exist unique morphisms

g^{(i)}:(\widetilde{L}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|N^{(i)}}\to(\widetilde{L}_{2},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|N^{(i)}}

such that

g^{(i)}|_{N^{(i)}_{b}}=f_{|N^{(i)}_{b}}
.
By using the uniqueness,
we obtain
