g^{(i)}_{|N^{(i)}\cap N^{(j)}}=g^{(j)}_{|N^{(i)}\cap N^{(j)}}
.
Hence, we obtain

g:(\widetilde{L}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\to(\widetilde{L}_{2},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})

such that
g_{|X_{b}}=f
.

Let
(\widetilde{L}_{b},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X_{b},H_{b},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.
There exist

(\widetilde{L}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(N^{(i)},(H\cap N^{(i)}),\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}_{N^{(i)}})

such that

(\widetilde{L}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|N^{(i)}_{b}}\simeq(\widetilde{L}_{b},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|N^{(i)}_{b}}
.
By using the fully faithfulness,
we can patch
(\widetilde{L}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})

to construct
(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})

such that
(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|N^{(i)}}\simeq(\widetilde{L}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.
We obtain
(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|X_{b}}\simeq(\widetilde{L}_{b},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.

3.5.4
Deformation (2)

Let
p:X\to B
be a smooth fibration of
complex manifolds.
Let
H
be a normal crossing hypersurface of
X
.
Let
B_{0}\subset B
be a complex submanifold.

•

B
and
B_{0}
are connected,
and
\pi_{1}(B_{0},b_{0})\to\pi_{1}(B,b)

is surjective for any
b_{0}\in B_{0}
.

•

For any
b\in B
,
there exists a neighbourhood
B_{b}\subset B

of
b
such that
there exists a diffeomorphism

(X,H)_{|B_{b}}\simeq B_{b}\times(X_{b},H_{b})
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
be a good system of ramified irregular values
on
(X,H)
.
We obtain the induced good system of ramified irregular values

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{b}
on
(X_{b},H_{b})
by the restriction.

Proposition 3.19

Let
b_{0}\in B
and
Let
(\widetilde{L}_{b_{0}},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X_{b_{0}},H_{b_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{b_{0}})
.
Suppose that there exists a local system

\widetilde{L}
on
\widetilde{X}(\widetilde{H})
such that

\widetilde{L}_{|\widetilde{X}_{b_{0}}(H_{b_{0}})}\simeq\widetilde{L}_{b_{0}}
.
Then, there exists a unique
Stokes structure
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}
of
\widetilde{L}
indexed by
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}

such that

(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|\widetilde{X}_{b_{0}}(H_{b_{0}})}\simeq(\widetilde{L}_{b_{0}},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.

Proof
Let us consider the case
B_{0}=\{b_{0}\}
.
Let
\widetilde{p}:\widetilde{X}(\widetilde{H})\to B
denote the projection.
For any
b\in B
,
let
\gamma:[0,1]\to B
be a path
such that
\gamma(0)=b_{0}
and
\gamma(1)=b

and that
\gamma
is injective.
There exist
a simply connected neighbourhood
W
of
\gamma([0,1])

and a diffeomorphism

(X,H)_{|W}\simeq W\times(X_{b_{0}},H_{b_{0}})
.
By Proposition
3.18
,
there exists the Stokes structure

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}
of
\widetilde{L}_{|\pi^{-1}(W)}

whose restriction to
\widetilde{X}_{b_{0}}(H_{b_{0}})

equals
(\widetilde{L}_{b_{0}},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.
We obtain

(\widetilde{L}_{|\widetilde{X}_{b}(H_{b})},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.
Because
B
is simply connected,
by using Proposition
3.18
,
we can check that
(\widetilde{L}_{|\widetilde{X}_{b}(H_{b})},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})

is independent of the choice of
\gamma
.
For any
b\in B
,
there exist a simply connected neighbourhood
B_{b}

and a diffeomorphism

(X,H)_{|B_{b}}\simeq B_{b}\times(X_{b},H_{b})
.
By Proposition
3.18
,
there exists the Stokes structure

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}
of
\widetilde{L}_{|\pi^{-1}(B_{b})}

whose restriction to

\widetilde{X}_{b}(H_{b})
equals

(\widetilde{L}_{|\widetilde{X}_{b}(H_{b})},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.
For
b,b^{\prime}\in B
,
we can check that
the induced Stokes structures of

\widetilde{L}_{|\pi^{-1}(B_{b}\cap B_{b^{\prime}})}

are the same.
Hence, we obtain the Stokes structure

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}
of
\widetilde{L}

such that

(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|\widetilde{X}_{b_{0}}(H_{b_{0}})}\simeq(\widetilde{L}_{b_{0}},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.

Let us study the general case.
Let
\Psi:B^{\prime}\to B
be a universal covering.
We obtain

X^{\prime}=B^{\prime}\times_{B}X
and

H^{\prime}=B^{\prime}\times_{B}H
.
We obtain the induced good system of ramified irregular values

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{\prime}
over
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.
We have the map

\widetilde{\Psi}:\widetilde{X}^{\prime}(H^{\prime})\to\widetilde{X}(H)
.
We set
\Gamma=\pi_{1}(B,b_{0})
.
We have the natural Galois action of
\Gamma
on
B^{\prime}
.
It lifts to the action on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})

and
\widetilde{X}^{\prime}(H^{\prime})
.

Let
b^{\prime}_{0}\in\Psi^{-1}(b_{0})
.
By the previous consideration,
there exists
a unique Stokes structure
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
of

\Psi^{\ast}(\widetilde{L})

such that

\Psi^{\ast}(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})_{|\widetilde{X}^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}_{0}}(H_{b^{\prime}_{0}})}=(\widetilde{L}_{\widetilde{X}_{b_{0}}(H_{b_{0}})},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}_{|\widetilde{X}_{b_{0}}(H_{b_{0}})}).

Let
B_{0}^{\prime}=B^{\prime}\times_{B}B_{0}
.
Because
\pi_{1}(B_{0},b_{0})\to\pi_{1}(B,b_{0})
is surjective,

B^{\prime}_{0}
is connected.
We set

(X_{B_{0}},H_{B_{0}}):=(X,H)_{|B_{0}}

and

(X^{\prime}_{B_{0}^{\prime}},H^{\prime}_{B_{0}^{\prime}})=(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})_{|B_{0}^{\prime}}
.
We have the induced map

\Psi_{1}:(X^{\prime}_{B_{0}^{\prime}},H^{\prime}_{B_{0}^{\prime}})\to(X_{B_{0}},H_{B_{0}})

and

\widetilde{\Psi}_{1}:\widetilde{X}^{\prime}_{B^{\prime}_{0}}(H^{\prime}_{B_{0}})\to\widetilde{X}_{B_{0}}(H_{B_{0}})
.
We obtain the Stokes structure

\widetilde{\Psi}_{1}^{\ast}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}_{B_{0}})

of

\Psi^{\ast}(\widetilde{L})_{|\widetilde{X}^{\prime}_{B_{0}^{\prime}}(H^{\prime}_{B_{0}^{\prime}})}

which is
\Gamma
-equivariant.
For any
\gamma\in\Gamma
,
the isomorphism

\gamma^{\ast}:\widetilde{\Psi}^{\ast}(\widetilde{L})_{\gamma(b^{\prime}_{0})}\simeq\widetilde{\Psi}^{\ast}(\widetilde{L})_{b^{\prime}_{0}}

is enhanced to the isomorphism of
the local systems with Stokes structure

\gamma^{\ast}:(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\ast}(\widetilde{L}),\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})_{\gamma(b^{\prime}_{0})}\simeq(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\ast}(\widetilde{L}),\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})_{b^{\prime}_{0}}
.
Hence,

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant,
and there exists
a Stokes structure
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}
of

\widetilde{L}
indexed by
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}

such that

\widetilde{\Psi}^{\ast}(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})=(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\ast}(\widetilde{L}),\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
.
Then,
(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})

satisfies the desired condition.

3.6
Riemann-Hilbert-Birkhoff correspondence

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
be a good system of ramified irregular values.
Let
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
denote the category of
good meromorphic flat bundles
(V,\nabla)
on
(X,H)

such that
\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{V},x)\subset\mathcal{I}_{x}
for any
x\in H
.
Let
\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
denote the category of
local systems on
\widetilde{X}(H)

with Stokes structure indexed by
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
.

Let
(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
.
Let
L_{V}
be the local system on
X\setminus H

obtained as the sheaf of flat sections of
(V,\nabla)
.
It induces the local system

\widetilde{L}_{V}
on
\widetilde{X}(H)
.
Let
x\in X
.
There exists a neighbourhood
X_{x}\subset X
of
x

and a ramified Galois covering

\varphi_{x}:(X_{x}^{\prime},H_{x}^{\prime})\to(X_{x},H_{x})

such that

\varphi_{x}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
is unramifiedly good
on
(X_{x}^{\prime},H_{x}^{\prime})
,
and that
\varphi_{x}^{-1}(x)
consists one point
x^{\prime}
.
Let
\varpi^{\prime}:\widetilde{X}^{\prime}_{x}(H^{\prime}_{x})\to\widetilde{X}_{x}

denote the oriented real blow up.
It induces a morphism

\widetilde{\varphi}_{x}:\widetilde{X}_{x}^{\prime}(H_{x}^{\prime})\to\widetilde{X}(H)
.
As explained in
[
25
, §4]
,
we obtain the Stokes structure

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}_{x^{\prime}}=\bigl(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}^{y^{\prime}}(\varphi_{x}^{\ast}(\widetilde{L}_{V})_{y^{\prime}})\,|\,y^{\prime}\in(\varpi^{\prime})^{-1}(H_{x}^{\prime})\bigr)

of
\widetilde{\varphi}_{x}^{\ast}(\widetilde{L}_{V})

associated with
\varphi_{y}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
.
Because
\varphi_{y}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)

is equivariant with respect to the action
of the Galois group
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits
of the covering
\varphi_{x}
,

(\widetilde{\varphi}^{\ast}(\widetilde{L}_{V}),\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}_{x^{\prime}})

is also equivariant with respect to
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits
-action.
We obtain the tuple of the filtrations

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}_{x}=(\mathcal{F}^{y}(\widetilde{L}_{V,y})\,|\,y\in\varpi^{-1}(H_{x}))
.
It is the Stokes structure of
(V,\nabla)_{|X_{x}}
.
By varying
x
,
we obtain
the Stokes structure
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}
of
\widetilde{L}_{V}
.
In this way,
we obtain

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V,\nabla)=(\widetilde{L}_{V},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}).

It is a functor of abelian categories.

Proposition 3.20

The functor
\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits
is an equivalence.

Proof
If
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
is unramified,
it is already explained in
[
25
, §4.2]
.
Let us study the case where there exists
a ramified Galois covering
\varphi:(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})\to(X,H)

such that
\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
is unramified.
Let
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits
denote the Galois group.
For any
(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
,
we obtain

\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits
-equivariant object

\varphi^{\ast}(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X^{\prime},H^{\prime},\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
.
There exits

(V^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X^{\prime},H^{\prime},\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})

such that

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})\simeq\varphi^{\ast}(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.
Because

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits:\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X^{\prime},H^{\prime},\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\simeq\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X^{\prime},H^{\prime},\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
,

(V^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
is
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits
-equivariant,
and there exists
(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})

such that

\varphi^{\ast}(V,,\nabla)\simeq(V^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
.
The
G
-equivariant isomorphism

\varphi^{\ast}\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V,\nabla)\simeq\varphi^{\ast}(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})

induces an isomorphism

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V,\nabla)\simeq(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.
It implies that
\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits
is essentially surjective.
Clearly,
\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits
is faithful.
Let
(V_{i},\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})

with a morphism
f:\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V_{1},\nabla)\to\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V_{2},\nabla)
.
Because

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits:\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X^{\prime},H^{\prime},\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\simeq\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X^{\prime},H^{\prime},\varphi^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
,
we obtain

g^{\prime}:\varphi^{\ast}(V_{1},\nabla)\to\varphi^{\ast}(V_{2},\nabla)

such that

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(g^{\prime})=\varphi^{\ast}(f)
.
We obtain that
g^{\prime}
is
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits
-equivariant,
and hence there exists

g:(V_{1},\nabla)\to(V_{2},\nabla)

such that
\varphi^{\ast}(g)=g^{\prime}
.
We can check that
\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(g)=f
,
i.e.,

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits
is full in this case.

Let us study the general case.
By using the equivalence in the local case above,
we can check that

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits
is fully faithful.
Let
(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X,D,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
.
By the equivalence in the local case above,
there exist a open covering
X=\bigcup\mathcal{U}_{i}

and
(V_{i},\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(\mathcal{U}_{i},H\cap\mathcal{U}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{|\mathcal{U}_{i}})

such that

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V_{i})\simeq(\widetilde{L},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|\varpi^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{i})}
.
By the local equivalence above,
we have the isomorphisms

\psi_{j,i}:\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V_{i})_{|\mathcal{U}_{i}\cap\mathcal{U}_{j}}\simeq\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V_{j})_{|\mathcal{U}_{i}\cap\mathcal{U}_{j}}

which satisfies the cocycle condition.
By patching them,
there exists
(V,\nabla)

such that

(V,\nabla)_{|\mathcal{U}_{i}}=(V_{i},\nabla)
.
Hence,
\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits
is essentially surjective.

Corollary 3.21

In the setting of
§
3.5.3
,
we obtain the equivalence

\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\simeq\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X_{b},H_{b},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{b})
.

Corollary 3.22

Let
X,B,H,B_{0},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}

be as in
§
3.5.4
.
Let
(V_{0},\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X_{B_{0},}H_{B_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{B_{0}})
.
Suppose that the associated local system
L_{V_{0}}

extends to a local system on
X\setminus H
.
Then, there exists
(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})

such that
(V,\nabla)_{|X_{B_{0}}}\simeq(V_{0},\nabla)
.
Such
(V,\nabla)
is unique up to canonical isomorphism.

3.6.1
Restriction to ample hypersurfaces

Let
X
be a smooth complex projective variety with
\dim X\geq 3
.
Let
H
be a simple normal crossing hypersurface
with the irreducible decomposition
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
be a good system of ramified irregular values on
(X,H)
.
Let
Z
be an ample smooth hypersurface of
X
such that
(i)
Z\subsetneq H
,
(ii)
Z\cup H
is also simple normal crossing hypersurface.
Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{Z}
be the good system of ramified irregular values
on
(Z,Z\cap H)
induced by
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
.

By the restriction,
we obtain the functors

\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\to\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(Z,Z\cap H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{Z}),

(3)

\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(X,H,\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\to\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(Z,Z\cap H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{Z}).

(4)

Theorem 3.23

The restriction functors

(
3
)

and
(
4
)

are equivalences.

Proof
For
I\subset\Lambda
,
we set
H_{I}=\bigcap_{i\in I}H_{i}

and
H_{I}^{\circ}=H_{I}\setminus\bigcup_{j\not\in I}(H_{I}\cap H_{j})
.
We also set

H_{[j]}=\bigcup_{|I|=j}H_{I}
.

We have
H^{\circ}_{i,j}\cap Z\neq\emptyset

for any
i,j\in\Lambda
with
i\neq j
.
By
[
8
]
,
the following holds.

•

\pi_{1}(Z\setminus H)\simeq\pi_{1}(X\setminus H)
.

•

\pi_{1}(H_{i}^{\circ}\cap Z)\to\pi_{1}(H_{i}^{\circ})

is onto for any
i\in I
.

Let
(V_{i},\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
.
Suppose there exists

f:(V_{1},\nabla)_{|Z}\to(V_{2},\nabla)_{|Z}
.
We obtain that

(\widetilde{L}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|\widetilde{Z}(H\cap Z)}\to(\widetilde{L}_{2},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})_{|\widetilde{Z}(H\cap Z)}
.
It extends to

L_{1}\to L_{2}

and

\widetilde{L}_{1}\to\widetilde{L}_{2}
.
Moreover,
it is compatible with the Stoke structures
on
\varpi^{-1}(H_{i}^{\circ})
for any
i
.
Hence, there exists

f^{\prime}:(V_{1},\nabla)_{|X\setminus H_{[2]}}\to(V_{2},\nabla)_{|X\setminus H_{[2]}}
.
Because
f^{\prime}
preserves the good Deligne-Malgrange lattices
on
X\setminus H_{[2]}
,
by the Hartogs property,
it extends to a morphism

\widetilde{f}:(V_{1},\nabla)\to(V_{2},\nabla)

such that
\widetilde{f}_{|Z}=f
.
Therefore, the functors are fully faithful.

Let
(\widetilde{L}_{Z},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Loc^{St}}(Z,H\cap Z,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{Z})
.
Because
\pi_{1}(Z\setminus H)\simeq\pi_{1}(X\setminus H)
,
there exists a local system
L
on
X\setminus H

such that
L_{|Z\setminus H}=L_{Z}
.
By Proposition
3.19
,
there exists Stokes structure
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}}

of
\widetilde{L}_{|\varpi^{-1}(X\setminus H_{[2]})}

over
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{|X\setminus H_{[2]}}
.
Because
H^{\circ}_{i,j}\cap Z\neq\emptyset
,
it extends to a Stokes structure
of
\widetilde{L}_{|\varpi^{-1}(X\setminus H_{[3]})}

over
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{|X\setminus H_{[3]}}
.
Hence, there exists

(V^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X\setminus H_{[3]},H\setminus H_{[3]},{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})

such that

\mathop{\rm RHB}\nolimits(V^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})=(\widetilde{L}_{|\varpi^{-1}(X\setminus H_{[3]})},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{F}})
.
By
[
19
,
28
]
,

(V^{\prime},\nabla)
extends to a meromorphic connection

(V,\nabla)
on
(X,H)
.
By using
[
25
, Lemma 2.37, Lemma 2.38]
,
we obtain that
(V,\nabla)\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,D,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})
.

4
Estimate of characteristic numbers in the complex surface case

4.1
Statement

Let
X
be a smooth connected complex projective surface.
Let
H
be a simple normal crossing hypersurface of
X
.
Let
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
be the irreducible decomposition.
Let
[H_{i}]\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{R}})

denote the cohomology classes induced by
H_{i}
.

Let
(V,\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
We obtain the good Deligne-Malgrange filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}V
.
We obtain the locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module

V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}=\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}V
.
For
-1<a\leq 0
,
we obtain the locally free
\mathcal{O}_{H_{i}}
-module

{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})
.
We shall prove the following proposition
in §
4.4

after preliminaries
in §
4.2
and
§
4.3
.

Proposition 4.1

The class
c_{1}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})

is contained in

\Bigl\{\sum_{i\in\Lambda}a_{i}[H_{i}]\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{R}})\,\Big|\,0\leq a_{i}<\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)\Bigr\}.

There exists
C_{1}>0

depending only on
([H_{i}]^{2},\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V))

such that the following holds for any
-1<a\leq 0
:

\left|\deg_{H_{i}}\bigl({}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})\bigr)\right|<C_{1}.

There exists
C_{2}>0

depending only on

\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)

and the intersection numbers

[H_{i}]\cdot[H_{j}]

(i,j\in\Lambda)

such that

|c_{2}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})|<C_{2}.

4.2
Ramified coverings and good system ramified irregular values

4.2.1
Oriented real blow up

Let
X
be a complex surface.
Let
C\subset X
be
a smooth
1
-dimensional compact connected complex submanifold.
We assume the following:

•

The inclusion map
C\to X
is homotopy equivalent.

Let
H
be a hypersurface of
X

such that
(i)
C\not\subset H
,
(ii)
C\cup H
is a simple normal crossing hypersurface,
(iii) each connected component of
H
is isomorphic to a disc.
We set
H_{C}:=C\cap H\subset C
.
Let
\varpi:\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)\to X
denote the oriented real blow up
along
C\cup H
.
Let
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}
denote the sheaf of
holomorphic functions on
\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)
.
We set

\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}(\ast(H\cup C)):=\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}\otimes_{\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varpi^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast(H\cup C)))
.

4.2.2
Some sheaves on the oriented real blow up

Let
\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}

denote the sheaf on
\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)

determined as follows.

•

\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)|X\setminus H}=\mathcal{O}_{X\setminus H}
.

•

For each
x\in C\setminus H
,
there exists a holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood

(X_{x},z_{1},z_{2})

such that
C\cap X_{x}=\{z_{1}=0\}
.
Around
y\in\varpi^{-1}(x)
,
by taking a branch of
z_{1}^{1/e}
,
we set

\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}=\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}[z_{1}^{1/e}]
,
which is independent of the choices of
a coordinate system and a branch.

•

For each
x\in H\setminus C
,
there exists a holomorphic coordinate system

(X_{x},z_{1},z_{2})

such that
H\cap X_{x}=\{z_{2}=0\}
.
Around
y\in\varpi^{-1}(x)
,
by taking a branch of
z_{2}^{1/e}
,
we set

\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}=\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}[z_{2}^{1/e}]
,
which is independent of the choices of
a coordinate system and a branch.

•

For each
x\in H\cap C
,
there exists a holomorphic coordinate system

(X_{x},z_{1},z_{2})

such that
H\cap X_{x}=\{z_{2}=0\}

and
C\cap X_{x}=\{z_{1}=0\}
.
Around
y\in\varpi^{-1}(x)
,
by taking branches of
z_{i}^{1/e}
,
we set

\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}=\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}[z_{1}^{1/e},z_{2}^{1/e}]
,
which is independent of
the choices of a coordinate system
and branches.

We set

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}=\varinjlim\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}
.
We also set

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}(\ast(H\cup C))=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}\otimes_{\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast(H\cup C)).

We obtain the sheaf of sets

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}(\ast(H\cup C))\big/\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}

on
\varpi^{-1}(H\cup C)
.

4.2.3
Local systems induced by
good system of ramified irregular values on
(X,H)

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}=(\mathcal{I}_{x}\,|\,x\in C\cup H)

be a good system of ramified irregular values on
(X,C\cup H)

in the sense of
[
26
, §15.1]
.
For
x\in H
and
y\in\varpi^{-1}(x)
,

\mathcal{I}_{x}
determines the subset

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})_{y}\subset\bigl(\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}(\ast(H\cup C))\big/\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}\bigr)_{y}.

This procedure induces a subsheaf of sets

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}(H\cup C)}(\ast(H\cup C))\big/\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}}(H\cup C)

on
\varpi^{-1}(C\cup H)
.
By restricting to

\varpi^{-1}(C\setminus H)
,
we obtain a local system of sets

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})_{C\setminus H}:=L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})_{|\varpi^{-1}(C\setminus H)}

By restricting to
\varpi^{-1}(C\cap H)
,
we obtain a local system of sets

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})_{C\cap H}:=L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})_{|\varpi^{-1}(C\cap H)}.

4.2.4
Statement

We shall prove the following proposition
in §
4.2.8
after preliminaries.

Proposition 4.2

There exist complex manifolds

X^{(j)}

(j=1,2,3,4)

and holomorphic maps

X^{(4)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi_{4}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X^{(3)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi_{3}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X^{(2)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X^{(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X

such that the following holds.

•

X^{\prime}=\varphi_{1}(X^{(1)})
is
a neighbourhood of
C
in
X
.
The induced map

\varphi_{1}:X^{(1)}\to X^{\prime}

is a proper and finite map.

•

There exists a hypersurface
H^{\prime}
of
X^{\prime}

such that
(i) any connected component of
H^{\prime}
is isomorphic to a disc,
(ii)
H\cap H^{\prime}=\emptyset
,
(iii)
C\cup H^{\prime}\cup H
is normal crossing,
(iv)
\varphi_{1}:X^{(1)}\to X^{\prime}
is a finite proper map
ramified along
\varphi_{1}^{-1}(H\cup H^{\prime})
.
We set

C^{(1)}=\varphi_{1}^{-1}(C)
and

H^{(1)}=\varphi_{1}^{-1}(H)
.

•

\varphi_{2}
is a finite Galois covering map.
We set

C^{(2)}=\varphi_{2}^{-1}(C^{(1)})

and

H^{(2)}=\varphi_{2}^{-1}(H^{(1)})
.

•

\varphi_{3}
is a finite proper map
ramified along
C^{(3)}=\varphi_{3}^{-1}(C^{(2)})
,
and
the induced map

X^{(3)}\setminus C^{(3)}\to X^{(2)}\setminus C^{(2)}

is a cyclic covering map.
We set

H^{(3)}=\varphi_{3}^{-1}(H^{(2)})
.

•

\varphi_{4}
is a finite Galois covering map.
We set

C^{(4)}=\varphi_{4}^{-1}(C^{(3)})

and

H^{(4)}=\varphi_{4}^{-1}(H^{(3)})
.

•

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(4)}
denote the induced
good system of ramified irregular values
on
(X^{(4)},C^{(4)}\cup H^{(4)})
.
Then,
the local systems

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{4})_{C^{(4)}\setminus H^{(4)}}

and

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{4})_{C^{(4)}\cap H^{(4)}}

are globally constant sheaves
on each connected component.

We have the following complement.
Let
G_{3}
denote the Galois group of
\varphi_{3}
,
and let
G_{4}
denote the Galois group of
\varphi_{4}
.
We have the natural actions of
G_{j}
on
X^{(j)}
.
We shall prove the following lemma in
§
4.2.9
.

Lemma 4.3

There exists a natural action of

G_{3}\times G_{4}
on
X^{(4)}

which induces the
G_{3}
-action on
X^{(3)}
.

4.2.5
Preliminary

Let
Y
be a complex manifold.
Let
L
be a holomorphic line bundle on
Y
.

Lemma 4.4

Assume that there exists
\alpha\in H^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}})

such that
c_{1}(L)=m\cdot\alpha

for some integer
m
.
Then, there exists a holomorphic line bundle
L^{1/m}

with an isomorphism

(L^{1/m})^{\otimes m}\simeq L
.

Proof
From the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\ast}\longrightarrow 0
,
we obtain the exact sequence

H^{1}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})\longrightarrow H^{1}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\ast})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{2}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}).

We note that

H^{j}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})
are
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector spaces.
Note that
\psi_{2}(c_{1}(L))=0

in
H^{2}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})
.
Hence,
m\cdot\psi_{2}(\alpha)=0
,
which implies that

\psi_{2}(\alpha)=0
.
Hence, there exists a holomorphic line bundle
L_{0}

such that

c_{1}(L_{0})=\alpha
.
Because

c_{1}(L^{-1}\otimes L_{0}^{m})=0
,
there exists
\beta\in H^{1}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})

such that

\psi_{1}(\beta)=L^{-1}\otimes L_{0}^{m}
.
We set

L^{1/m}:=L_{0}\otimes\psi_{1}(\beta/m)
.
Then, it satisfies

(L^{1/m})^{\otimes m}=L
.

4.2.6
Covering branched along
H\cup H^{\prime}

Let
H^{\prime}
be a hypersurface of
X

such that

H\cap H^{\prime}=\emptyset
and that

H\cup H^{\prime}\cup C
is normal crossing.

Lemma 4.5

Suppose that
each connected component of
H\cup H^{\prime}
is homeomorphic to a disc.
Suppose that

|H\cap C|=|H^{\prime}\cap C|
.
Then,
for any
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
there exists
a complex manifold
X^{\prime}

with a holomorphic map

\varphi_{m}:X^{\prime}\to X

such that the following holds.

•

X^{\prime}\setminus\varphi_{m}^{-1}(H\cup H^{\prime})\to X\setminus(H\cup H^{\prime})

is a cyclic covering of order
m
.

•

\varphi_{m}^{-1}(H\cup H^{\prime})\to H\cup H^{\prime}

is an isomorphism.

Proof
We consider the line bundle

L=\mathcal{O}_{X}(H-H^{\prime})
.
We have
c_{1}(L)=0
in
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
.
There exists a holomorphic line bundle

L^{1/m}

with an isomorphism

(L^{1/m})^{\otimes m}\simeq L
.
There exists the morphism

L^{1/m}\to L

given by
v\longmapsto v^{m}
.
It extends to a morphism

\Phi:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus L^{1/m})\to\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus L)
.
We set

X_{0}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus 0)

and

X_{\infty}=\mathbb{P}(0\oplus L)
.
We also set

X_{0}^{(m)}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus 0)

and

X_{\infty}^{(m)}=\mathbb{P}(0\oplus L^{1/m})
.
Then,

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus L^{1/m})\setminus(X_{0}^{(m)}\cup X_{\infty}^{(m)})\to\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus L)\setminus(X_{0}\cup X_{\infty})

is a cyclic covering of order
m
,
and
\Phi
induces an isomorphism

X_{0}^{(m)}\simeq X_{0}

and

X_{\infty}^{(m)}\simeq X_{\infty}
.

The morphism

\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}(H)

induces

X\to\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus L)
.
Let
X^{\prime}
denote the fiber product of

X
and
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus L^{1/m})

over
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\oplus L)
.
Then, the induced morphism
X^{\prime}\to X

satisfies the desired conditions.

4.2.7
Covering branched along
C

Let
\chi
denote the degree of
the normal bundle of
C
in
X
.

Lemma 4.6

Let
m
be a positive integer
such that
\chi/m
is an integer.
Then,
there exists a complex manifold
X^{\prime}

with a morphism

\varphi_{m}:X^{\prime}\to X

such that
the following holds.

•

X^{\prime}\setminus\varphi_{m}^{-1}(C)\to X\setminus C

is a cyclic covering of order
m
.

•

\varphi_{m}^{-1}(C)\simeq C
.

The Euler class of the normal bundle of
\varphi_{m}^{-1}(C)

in
X^{\prime}
is
\chi/m
.

Proof
We consider the holomorphic line bundle

L=\mathcal{O}_{X}(C)
.
Because
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})\simeq H^{2}(C,{\mathbb{Z}})
,
there exists a
\alpha\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})

such that
c_{1}(L)=m\alpha
.
There exists a holomorphic line bundle
L^{1/m}

with an isomorphism

(L^{1/m})^{\otimes m}\simeq L
.
There exists the natural section
X\to L
.
Then, we can construct
X^{\prime}\to X

as in the previous lemma.

4.2.8
Proof of Proposition
4.2

By shrinking
X
,
we may assume that there exists a hypersurface
H^{\prime}
of
X

such that
(i) each connected component of
H^{\prime}
is isomorphic to disc,
(ii)
H\cap H^{\prime}=\emptyset
,
(iii)
X\cup H\cup H^{\prime}
is normal crossing,
(iv)
|H\cap C|=|H^{\prime}\cap C|
.
We also assume that
C\to X
is homotopy equivalent.
There exists
m_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

such that the following holds.

•

Let
x\in H\setminus C
.
Then,

\mathcal{I}_{x}\subset\mathcal{O}^{(m_{1})}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}/\mathcal{O}^{(m_{1})}_{X,x}
.

Let
\varphi^{(1)}_{m_{1}}:X^{(1)}\to X

be the cyclic covering of order
m_{1}

ramified along
H\cup H^{\prime}
,
as in §
4.2.6
.
We set
C^{(1)}=(\varphi_{m_{1}}^{(1)})^{-1}(C)
,

H^{(1)}=\varphi_{m_{1}}^{-1}(H)

and

H^{\prime(1)}=\varphi_{m_{1}}^{-1}(H^{\prime})
.
We obtain
a good system of ramified irregular values

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(1)}
on
(X^{(1)},C^{(1)}\cup H^{(1)})
.

For each
x\in C^{(1)}\setminus H^{(1)}
,
let
[\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{x}]
denote the quotient set of

\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{x}

by the natural action of the Galois group of
the extension

\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)},x}\to\mathcal{O}^{(e)}_{X^{(1)},x}
.
The tuple

[{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(1)}]=([\mathcal{I}^{(1)}_{x}]\,|\,x\in C^{(1)}\setminus H^{(1)})

gives a local system of finite sets on
C^{(1)}\setminus H^{(1)}
.
Note that the monodromy around any point of
C^{(1)}\cap H^{(1)}

is trivial by the construction of
\varphi_{m_{1}}^{(1)}
.
Hence,
[{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(1)}]
extends to a local system
on
C^{(1)}
,
which is denoted by
[{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(1)}]^{\prime}
.

Let
x_{0}\in C^{(1)}
.
We obtain a homomorphism
\kappa^{(1)}

of
\pi_{1}(C^{(1)},x_{0})

to the group of automorphisms of
the finite set
[{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(1)}]^{\prime}_{x_{0}}
.
Let
\varphi^{(2)}:X^{(2)}\to X^{(1)}

denote the finite Galois covering map
corresponding to
the normal subgroup
\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits(\kappa^{(1)})

of
\pi_{1}(X^{(1)},x_{0})=\pi_{1}(C^{(1)},x_{0})
.
We set

C^{(2)}=(\varphi^{(2)})^{-1}(C^{(1)})

and

H^{(2)}=(\varphi^{(2)})^{-1}(H^{(1)})
.
Note that

(\varphi_{|C^{(2)}})^{-1}([{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(1)}]^{\prime})

is constant.

Let
\chi
be the Euler number of the normal bundle of

C^{(2)}
in
X^{(2)}
.
There exists
e_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

such that the following holds.

•

Let
x\in C^{(2)}\setminus H^{(2)}
.
Then,

\mathcal{I}^{(2)}_{x}\subset\mathcal{O}^{(e_{2})}_{X^{(2)}}(\ast C^{(2)})_{x}/\mathcal{O}^{(e_{2})}_{X^{(2)},x}
.

We set
m_{2}=|\chi|
if
\chi\neq 0

or
m_{2}=e_{2}
if
\chi=0
.
Let
\varphi^{(3)}_{m_{2}}:X^{(3)}\to X^{(2)}

be the cyclic covering of order
m_{2}

ramified along
C^{(2)}

as in §
4.2.7
.
We set
C^{(3)}=(\varphi_{m_{2}}^{(3)})^{-1}(C^{(2)})
,

H^{(3)}=(\varphi^{(2)}_{m_{2}})^{-1}(H^{(2)})
.
We obtain
a good system of ramified irregular values

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)}
on
(X^{(3)},C^{(3)}\cup H^{(3)})
.

Let

\varpi^{(3)}_{0}:\widetilde{X}^{(3)}(C^{(3)})\to X^{(3)}

denote the oriented real blow up
of
X^{(3)}
along
C^{(3)}
.

Lemma 4.7

The local system of sets

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)})_{C^{(3)}\setminus H^{(3)}}

on
C^{(3)}\setminus H^{(3)}

naturally extends to
a local system of sets

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)})^{\prime}_{C^{(3)}}

on
(\varpi^{(3)}_{0})^{-1}(C^{(3)})
.

Proof
Let
x\in C^{(3)}\cap H^{(3)}
.
Let
\gamma
be any loop around
(\varpi^{(3)}_{0})^{-1}(x)
.
By the construction of
\varphi^{(1)}
,
the monodromy
of
L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)})^{\prime}_{C^{(3)}}

along
\gamma
is trivial.
Then, we obtain the claim of the lemma.

The orbit decompositions of
\mathcal{I}^{(3)}_{x}

(x\in C^{(3)}\setminus H^{(3)})

induce the decomposition

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)})^{\prime}_{C^{(3)}}=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{o}\in\Lambda}L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)})^{\prime}_{C^{(3)},\mathfrak{o}},

where

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)})^{\prime}_{C^{(3)},\mathfrak{o}}

are principal
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell(\mathfrak{o})
-bundles
on
(\varpi^{(3)}_{0})^{-1}(C^{(3)})
.

Lemma 4.8

For any
x\in C^{(3)}\setminus H^{(3)}
,
the monodromy of
L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)})^{\prime}_{C^{(3)}}

along
(\varpi_{0}^{(3)})^{-1}(x)
is trivial.

Proof
If
\chi=0
, it follows from the construction of

\varphi^{(3)}_{e_{2}}
.
If
\chi\neq 0
,
we note that Euler number of
the normal bundle of
C^{(3)}
in
X^{(3)}

is
1
or
-1
.
Hence, the natural morphism

H_{1}\bigl((\varpi_{0}^{(3)})^{-1}(C^{(3)}),{\mathbb{Z}}\bigr)\longrightarrow H_{1}(C^{(3)},{\mathbb{Z}})

is an isomorphism.
Hence, the natural morphism

H^{1}(C^{(3)},{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}})\longrightarrow H^{1}\bigl((\varpi_{0}^{(3)})^{-1}(C^{(3)}),{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\bigr)

is an isomorphism for any
\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
.
Then, we obtain the claim of the lemma.

Let

\varpi^{(3)}_{1}:\widetilde{X}^{(3)}(C^{(3)}\cup H^{(3)})\to X^{(3)}

denote the oriented real blow up
of
X^{(3)}
along
C^{(3)}\cup H^{(3)}
.
We obtain the following lemma
by the construction of
\varphi^{(1)}_{m_{1}}

and Lemma
4.8
.

Lemma 4.9

The local system

L({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)})_{C^{(3)}\cap H^{(3)}}

is constant on each connected component of

(\varpi_{1}^{(3)})^{-1}(C^{(3)}\cap H^{(3)})
.

By Lemma
4.8
,

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}^{(3)}=(\mathcal{I}^{(3)}_{x}\,|\,x\in C^{(3)}\setminus H^{(3)})

determines a local system on
C^{(3)}\setminus H^{(3)}
,
which extends to a local system of finite sets
on
C^{(3)}
.
Let
x_{0}\in C^{(3)}
.
We have the natural homomorphism
\kappa^{(3)}

of
\pi_{1}(C^{(3)},x_{0})
to the automorphisms of
\mathcal{I}^{(3)}_{x_{0}}
.
Let
X^{(4)}\to X^{(3)}
be the covering space
corresponding to the normal subgroup

\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits(\kappa^{(3)})

of
\pi_{1}(X^{(3)},x_{0})=\pi_{1}(C^{(3)},x_{0})
.
Then, the induced map
X^{(4)}\to X

satisfies the desired conditions.

4.2.9
Proof of Lemma
4.3

Let
g_{X^{(4)}}
be a
G_{4}
-invariant Riemannian metric
of
X^{(4)}

such that

g_{X^{(4)}}(Jv,Jv)=g_{X^{(4)}}(v,v)

for any tangent vector
v
,
where
J
is the automorphism of
TX^{(4)}

induced by the multiplication of
\sqrt{-1}
.
It induces a Riemannian metric
g_{X^{(3)}}
of
X^{(3)}

such that the covering map
\varphi_{4}
is locally isometric.
For
j=3,4
,
let
N^{{\mathbb{R}}}_{C^{(j)}}X^{(j)}
denote the normal bundles
of
C^{(j)}
in
X^{(j)}

obtained as the orthogonal complement of

TC^{(j)}\subset TX^{(j)}_{|C^{(j)}}
.
They are naturally complex vector bundles.
The restriction
\varphi_{4|C^{(4)}}
induces
an isomorphism

N^{{\mathbb{R}}}_{C^{(4)}}X^{(4)}\simeq\varphi_{4|C^{(4)}}^{\ast}\bigl(N^{{\mathbb{R}}}_{C^{(3)}}X^{(3)}\bigr).

(5)

Let
0_{C^{(j)}}
denote the image of the zero section of

N^{{\mathbb{R}}}_{C^{(j)}}X^{(j)}
.

We regard

G_{3}\subset S^{1}=\bigl\{a\in{\mathbb{C}}\,\big|\,|a|=1\bigr\}
.
There exists the natural
G_{3}
-action on

N^{{\mathbb{R}}}_{C^{(4)}}X^{(4)}
.
The isomorphism (
5
)
is
G_{3}
-equivariant.
The
G_{3}
-action on
N^{{\mathbb{R}}}_{C^{(4)}}X^{(4)}

extends to the
G_{3}\times G_{4}
-action on
N^{{\mathbb{R}}}_{C^{(4)}}X^{(4)}
.

There exist
G_{3}
-invariant neighbourhoods

C^{(3)}\subset U^{(3)}\subset X^{(3)}

and

0_{C^{(3)}}\subset V^{(3)}\subset N^{{\mathbb{R}}}_{C^{(3)}}X^{(3)}

such that
the exponential map with respect to
g_{X^{(3)}}

induces a
G_{3}
-equivariant diffeomorphism

V^{(3)}\simeq U^{(3)}
.
We obtain the neighbourhood

0_{C^{(4)}}\subset V^{(4)}\subset N^{{\mathbb{R}}}_{C^{(4)}}X^{(4)}

as the pull back of
V^{(3)}
.
It is
G_{3}\times G_{4}
-invariant.
Let
C^{(4)}\subset U^{(4)}\subset X^{(4)}

denote the neighbourhood obtained as the image of
V^{(4)}

by the exponential map
with respect to
g_{X^{(4)}}
.
We have the
G_{3}\times G_{4}
-action on
U^{(4)}
,
which induces
the
G_{3}
-action on
U^{(3)}=U^{(4)}/G_{4}
.

Note that
the inclusion maps

U^{(4)}\to X^{(4)}
are homotopy equivalent.
For any
g\in G_{3}
,
the map
g:U^{(4)}\simeq U^{(4)}

uniquely extends to
X^{(4)}\simeq X^{(4)}

because

g:U^{(3)}\simeq U^{(3)}
extends to

X^{(3)}\simeq X^{(3)}
,
and
X^{(4)}\to X^{(4)}
is a covering map.
We obtain the
G_{3}
-action on
X^{(4)}
.
Let
g_{3}\in G_{3}
and
g_{4}\in G_{4}
.
Note that
g_{4}^{-1}g_{3}g_{4}=g_{3}
on
U^{(4)}
,
and that
g_{4}^{-1}g_{3}g_{4}
and
g_{3}
induce
the same map on
X^{(4)}
.
Hence, we obtain that

g_{3}^{-1}g_{2}g_{3}=g_{2}
on
X^{(4)}
,
i.e.,
the
G_{3}
-action and
G_{4}
-action are commuting.
We note that
the actions are independent of the choice of
g_{X^{(4)}}
.

4.3
Degree of the graded pieces

4.3.1
Statement

Let
X
,
C
and
H
be as in §
4.2.1
.
Let
(V,\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle
on
(X,H\cup C)

of rank
r
.
We obtain the good Deligne-Malgrange filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(V)
on
(X,C\cup H)

indexed by
{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{H}
.
For
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
by taking the graduation with respect to the filtration along
C
,
we obtain
the locally free
\mathcal{O}_{C}(\ast H_{C})
-module

{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)
.
Moreover,
for
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
{\boldsymbol{b}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{H}
,
we obtain locally free sheaves

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{b}}}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr):={}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}\bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{a,{\boldsymbol{b}}}(V)\bigr)

on
C
.
Thus, we obtain a filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)
on
(C,H_{C})
.
Let
N_{C}X
denote the normal bundle of
C
in
X
.
We shall prove the following proposition in
§
4.3.7
.

Proposition 4.10

We have

\deg\left(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)\right)=a\cdot\deg(N_{C}X)\cdot\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V).

(6)

4.3.2
Refinement to the equivariant case

Suppose that

X
is equipped with an action of a finite cyclic group
G

satisfying the following condition.

•

G\cdot x=x
for any
x\in C
.

•

The
G
-action preserves
any connected component of
H
.

•

(V,\nabla)
is
G
-equivariant.

•

The quotient space
X/G
is a complex manifold
such that
the projection
X\to X/G

is a ramified covering
of complex manifolds.

The Deligne-Malgrange filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(V)
is
G
-equivariant.
Let
\mathop{\rm Irr}\nolimits(G)
denote the set of
the irreducible representations of
G
.
We obtain the decomposition

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)=\bigoplus_{\chi\in\mathop{\rm Irr}\nolimits(G)}\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)_{\chi},

where
we have
g^{\ast}v=\chi(g)v

for any
g\in G
and a section
v
of

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)
.
The following proposition is a refinement of
Proposition
4.10
,
which we shall prove in §
4.3.8
.

Proposition 4.11

We have

\deg\left(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)_{\chi}\right)=a\cdot\deg(N_{C}X)\cdot\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)_{\chi}.

(7)

4.3.3
Global decomposition by irregular values in the special case

Suppose that
X
and
(V,\nabla)
is
G
-equivariant
as in §
4.3.2
.
We study the special case.

Condition 4.12

Suppose that

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
satisfies the following conditions.

•

\mathcal{I}_{x}\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast(H\cup C))_{x}/\mathcal{O}_{X,x}

for any
x\in C\cup H
.
We obtain the local system

L_{C\setminus H}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})=(\mathcal{I}_{x}\,|\,x\in C\setminus H)
.

•

The local system

L_{C\setminus H}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})

is constant.

We set
\Gamma:=H^{0}(C\setminus H,L_{C\setminus H}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}))
.
We have the action of
G
on
\Gamma
by the pull back.
We have the decomposition

(V,\nabla)_{|\widehat{C}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\Gamma}(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}},\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathfrak{a}})

such that the following holds.

•

Let
x
be any point of
C\setminus H
.
Let
X_{x}
be a neighbourhood of
x
,
and we set
C_{x}:=X_{x}\cap C
.
For
\mathfrak{a}\in\Gamma
,
let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}\in\mathcal{O}_{X_{x}}(\ast C_{x})

be a lift of
\mathfrak{a}
.
Then,

\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{P}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}}

are regular singular along
C_{P}
.

•

Let
x\in H\cap C
.
Let
X_{x}
be a neighbourhood of
x
,
and
C_{x}:=X_{x}\cap C
and
H_{x}:=X_{x}\cap H
.
For
\mathfrak{a}\in\Gamma
,
let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}\in\mathcal{O}_{X_{x}}(\ast(C_{x}\cup H_{x}))

be a lift of
\mathfrak{a}
.
Then,
there exists a lattice

\widehat{V}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\subset\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that

(\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}})\bigl(\widehat{V}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\bigr)\subset\widehat{V}^{(0)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X_{x}}(\log C_{x})(\ast H_{x}).

•

For any
g\in G
,
we have

g^{\ast}\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}=\widehat{V}_{g^{\ast}\mathfrak{a}}
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(V)

be the unramifiedly good Deligne-Malgrange filtered bundle of
(V,\nabla)
.
We have the decomposition of filtered bundles

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(V)_{|\widehat{C}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\Gamma}\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}.

We set

V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}=\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}(V)

and
\widehat{V}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\mathfrak{a}}=\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}})
.
We obtain the following locally free sheaf on
C

{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\Gamma}{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\widehat{V}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\mathfrak{a}}).

It is equipped with the endomorphisms
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{C}(\nabla)
.
The eigenvalues
\alpha
are constant on
C
,
and satisfy
\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)=-a
.

For each
\mathfrak{a}\in\Gamma
,
let
G(\mathfrak{a})=\{g\in G\,|\,g^{\ast}\mathfrak{a}=\mathfrak{a}\}
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Irr}\nolimits(G(\mathfrak{a}))

denote the set of irreducible representations of
G(\mathfrak{a})
.
Each
{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\widehat{V}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\mathfrak{a}})

with
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{C}(\nabla)

is
G(\mathfrak{a})
-equivariant.
We obtain the decomposition

{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\widehat{V}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\mathfrak{a}})=\bigoplus_{\chi\in\mathop{\rm Irr}\nolimits(G(\mathfrak{a}))}{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\widehat{V}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\mathfrak{a}})_{\chi},

where
g^{\ast}v=\chi(g)v

for any section
v
of
\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{g},\chi}

and
g\in G(\mathfrak{a})
.
We obtain the generalized eigen decomposition
with respect to
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{C}(\nabla)

{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})_{\chi}=\bigoplus_{\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)=-a}{}^{C}\!\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})_{\chi}.

The nilpotent part of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{C}(\nabla)

induces the weight filtration
W
.
We obtain the locally free
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-modules

{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})_{\chi}=\bigoplus_{\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)=-a}{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})_{\chi}.

We have the induced filtered bundles

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})_{\chi}(\ast H_{C})\Bigr)

on
(C,H_{C})
.

4.3.4
An auxiliary function

We may assume that

H^{0}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C}(-C+H^{\prime}))
has a non-trivial section
\tau

satisfying the following conditions.

•

\tau_{|x}\neq 0
in
\mathcal{O}_{C}(-C+H^{\prime})_{|x}

for any
x\in H^{\prime}\cap C
.

•

\tau
has only simple zeroes.

•

There exists
H^{\prime\prime}

such that
(i)
H^{\prime\prime}\cap C=\tau^{-1}(0)
,
(ii)

H^{\prime\prime}\cap(H^{\prime}\cup H)=\emptyset
,
(iii)
C\cup H\cup H^{\prime}\cup H^{\prime\prime}
is normal crossing.

We set
H^{\prime}\cap C=H^{\prime}_{C}

and
H^{\prime\prime}\cap C=H^{\prime\prime}_{C}
.

Lemma 4.13

\deg(N_{C}X)=|H^{\prime}_{C}|-|H^{\prime\prime}_{C}|
.

Proof
It follows from

N_{C}(X)\simeq\mathcal{O}_{C}(C)\simeq\mathcal{O}_{C}(H^{\prime}_{C}-H^{\prime\prime}_{C})
.

4.3.5
Connections on the graded pieces

Let
x\in C
.
Let
(X_{x},z_{1},z_{2})
be a holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood
as in §
4.2.2
.
There exists a
G
-invariant section
\widetilde{\tau}_{x}

of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(-C+H^{\prime}-H^{\prime\prime})
on
X_{x}

which induces
\tau_{|C_{x}}
.
For any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{C}}
,
we obtain the
G
-equivariant meromorphic flat bundle on

(X_{x},C_{x}\cup H_{x}^{\prime}\cup H_{x}^{\prime\prime})
:

\mathcal{L}_{x,\widetilde{\tau}_{x}}(\alpha)=\Bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X_{x}}(\ast(C_{x}\cup H^{\prime}_{x}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{x})),d+\alpha\widetilde{\tau}_{x}^{-1}d\widetilde{\tau}_{x}\Bigr).

Let
\mathfrak{a}_{x}
be a
G(\mathfrak{a})
-invariant section of

\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast(C\cup H\cup H^{\prime}))
on
X_{x}

which is a lift of
\mathfrak{a}
.
We obtain the
G(\mathfrak{a})
-equivariant meromorphic flat bundle
on
(X_{x},C_{x}\cup H_{x}\cup H_{x}^{\prime})
:

\mathcal{L}_{x}(\mathfrak{a}_{x})=\Bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X_{x}}(\ast(C_{x}\cup H^{\prime}_{x}\cup H_{x})),d+d\mathfrak{a}_{x}\Bigr).

We obtain the
G(\mathfrak{a})
-equivariant meromorphic flat bundle
on
(X_{x},C_{x}\cup H_{x}\cup H_{x}^{\prime}\cup H_{x}^{\prime\prime})
:

\bigl(V_{x}(-\alpha,-\mathfrak{a}_{x}),\nabla^{(-\alpha,-\mathfrak{a}_{x})}\bigr):=(V,\nabla)_{|X_{x}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{x,\widetilde{\tau}_{x}}(\alpha)^{-1}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{x}(\mathfrak{a}_{x})^{-1}.

We obtain the
G(\mathfrak{a})
-equivariant filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl(V_{x}(-\alpha,-\mathfrak{a}_{x})\bigr)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl(V\bigr)_{|X_{x}}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{L}_{x,\widetilde{\tau}_{x}}(\alpha)^{-1}\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{L}_{x}(\mathfrak{a}_{x})^{-1}\bigr).

For
\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
we obtain

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl(\widehat{V_{x}(-\alpha,-\mathfrak{a}_{x})}_{\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a}}\bigr)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{b}}\bigr)_{|X_{x}}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{L}_{x,\widetilde{\tau}_{x}}(\alpha)^{-1}\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{L}_{x}(\mathfrak{a}_{x})^{-1}\bigr).

We set
H_{C,x}=C_{x}\cap H_{x}
,

H^{\prime}_{C,x}=C_{x}\cap H^{\prime}_{x}
,
and

H^{\prime\prime}_{C,x}=C_{x}\cap H^{\prime\prime}_{x}
.
We set
G(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b})=\{g\in G(\mathfrak{a})\,|\,g^{\ast}\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{b}\}

for
\chi\in\mathop{\rm Irr}\nolimits(G(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}))
,
there exists the natural isomorphism of

{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\beta-\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{b+\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\Bigl(\widehat{V_{x}(-\alpha,-\mathfrak{a}_{x})}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a}}\Bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C,x}\cup H_{C,x}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C,x}))\simeq\\
{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\beta}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{b}\Bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\Bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{x}\cup H_{x}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{x})).

(8)

Note that
G(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a})=G(\mathfrak{a})
.
For
(\mathfrak{b},\beta)=(\mathfrak{a},\alpha)
,
we have the induced connection

\nabla(\mathfrak{a},\chi,\tau,\alpha,j)

on

{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{0}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{0}\Bigl(\widehat{V_{x}(-\alpha,-\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}_{x})}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}\Bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{x}\cup H_{x}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{x}))\simeq{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\Bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\Bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{x}\cup H_{x}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{x})).

The connection

\nabla(\mathfrak{a},\chi,\tau,\alpha,j)

is independent of the choice of

\mathfrak{a}_{x}
and
\widetilde{\tau}_{x}
,
i.e.,
it depends only on

\mathfrak{a}
and
\tau
.

By varying
x\in C
,
we obtain
a connection

\nabla(\mathfrak{a},\chi,\tau,\alpha,j)

on
the locally free
\mathcal{O}_{C}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))
-module

{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\Bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\Bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C})),

depending only on
the choice of
\mathfrak{a}
and
\tau
.

4.3.6
Degree in the special case

Lemma 4.14

We have

\deg\left(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))\Bigr)\right)=\\
-(|H^{\prime}_{C}|-|H^{\prime\prime}_{C}|)\times\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)\times\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}.

(9)

Proof
We obtain the filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\prime\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))\Bigr)

with respect to the connection

\nabla(\mathfrak{a},\chi,\tau,\alpha,j)
.
By the general property of
the Deligne-Malgrange filtration,
we have

\deg\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))\Bigr)\right)=0.

(10)

Around
H_{C}
,
we have

\mathcal{P}^{\prime\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))\Bigr)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))\Bigr).

Around
P\in H^{\prime}_{C}
,
we have

\mathcal{P}^{\prime\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))\Bigr)={}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C})),

\mathcal{P}^{\prime\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{<-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))\Bigr)=0.

Around
P\in H^{\prime\prime}_{C}
,
we have

\mathcal{P}^{\prime\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))\Bigr)={}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C})),

\mathcal{P}^{\prime\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{<\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\Bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{W}_{j}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)}\bigl(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigr)_{\chi}(\ast(H^{\prime}_{C}\cup H_{C}\cup H^{\prime\prime}_{C}))\Bigr)=0.

Then, we obtain (
9
)
from (
10
).

Corollary 4.15

If Condition
4.12
is satisfied,
the equality
(
7
)
holds.

Proof
On
{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)
,
any eigenvalue
\alpha
of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{C}(\nabla)

satisfies
\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)=-a
.
We also have

\deg(N_{C}X)=|H^{\prime}_{C}|-|H^{\prime\prime}_{C}|
.
We obtain (
7
)
from (
9
)
by taking the sum with respect to

(\mathfrak{a},\alpha,j)
.

4.3.7
Proof of
Proposition
4.10

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
be a good system of ramified irregular values.
We use the notation in Proposition
4.2

and Lemma
4.3
.
We obtain good meromorphic flat bundle

(V^{(j)},\nabla)
on
(X^{(j)},C^{(j)}\cup H^{(j)})

(j=1,2,3,4)

as the pull back of
(V,\nabla)
.
Note that

(V^{(4)},\nabla)
and
(V^{(3)},\nabla)

are naturally
G_{3}
-equivariant.
Because
(V^{(4)},\nabla)

satisfies Condition
4.12
,
we obtain the following equality
for any
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\chi\in\mathop{\rm Irr}\nolimits(G_{3})
:

\deg\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(4)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(4)})_{\chi}\bigr)\Bigr)=a\deg(N_{C^{(4)}}X^{(4)})\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{(4)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(4)})_{\chi}.

We obtain

\deg\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(3)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(3)})_{\chi}\bigr)\Bigr)=\frac{1}{|G_{4}|}\deg\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(4)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(4)})_{\chi}\bigr)\Bigr)\\
=a\frac{\deg(N_{C^{(4)}}X^{(4)})}{|G_{4}|}\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{(4)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(4)})_{\chi}=a\deg(N_{C^{(3)}}X^{(3)})\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{(3)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(3)})_{\chi}.

(11)

Let
1_{G_{3}}
denote the trivial representation of
G_{3}
.
Let
m_{3}
denote the ramification index of
\varphi_{3}
along
C^{(3)}
.
We have

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(3)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{m_{3}a}(V^{(3)})_{1_{G_{3}}}\bigr)\simeq\varphi_{3|C^{(3)}}^{\ast}\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(2)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(2)})\bigr)\Bigr).

Note that
\varphi_{3|C^{(3)}}=\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{C^{(3)}}
.
We obtain

\deg\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(2)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(2)})\bigr)\Bigr)=\deg\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(3)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{m_{3}a}(V^{(3)})\bigr)_{1_{G_{3}}}\Bigr)\\
=am_{3}\deg(N_{C^{(3)}}X^{(3)})\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{(3)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{m_{3}a}(V^{(3)})=a\deg(N_{C^{(2)}}X^{(2)})\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{(2)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(2)}).

(12)

Let
G_{2}
denote the Galois group of
\varphi_{2}
.
We have

\deg\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(1)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(1)})\bigr)\Bigr)=\frac{1}{|G_{2}|}\deg\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(2)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(2)})\bigr)\Bigr)\\
=a\frac{\deg(N_{C^{(2)}}X^{(2)})}{|G_{2}|}\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{(2)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(2)})=a\deg(N_{C^{(1)}}X^{(1)})\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{(1)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(1)}).

(13)

We have

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(1)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(1)})\bigr)\simeq\varphi_{1|C}^{\ast}\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)\Bigr).

We also have

N_{C^{(1)}}X^{(1)}\simeq\varphi_{1|C^{(1)}}^{\ast}\Bigl(N_{C}X\Bigr)
.
Let
m_{1}
be the degree of
\varphi_{1}
.
By using Lemma
4.16
below,
we obtain

\deg\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)\Bigr)=\frac{1}{m_{1}}\deg\Bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{(1)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(1)})\bigr)\Bigr)\\
=a\frac{\deg(N_{C^{(1)}}X^{(1)})}{m_{1}}\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{(1)}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{(1)})=a\deg(N_{C}X)\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V).

(14)

Thus, we obtain Proposition
4.10
.

Lemma 4.16

Let
\varphi:C_{1}\to C_{2}
be a surjective morphism of compact Riemann surfaces.
Let
D_{2}\subset C_{2}
be a finite subset.
We set
D_{1}:=\varphi^{-1}(D_{2})
.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}V
be a filtered bundle on
(C_{2},D_{2})
.
Let
d(\varphi)
denote the degree of
\varphi
.
Then, we have

\deg(\varphi^{\ast}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}V))=d(\varphi)\cdot\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}V)
.

Proof
Let
U_{2}
be a neighbourhood of
D_{2}
in
C_{2}
.
Let
h
be a Hermitian metric of
V_{|C_{2}\setminus D_{2}}

such that
(i) the Chern connection of
h
is flat on
U_{2}\setminus D_{2}
,
(ii)
h
is adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}V
.
Let
R(h)
denote the curvature of the Chern connection of
h
.
As well known, we have

\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}V)=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\int_{C_{2}\setminus D_{2}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits R(h).

We have the induced metric
\varphi^{-1}(h)

of
\varphi^{\ast}(V)_{|C_{1}\setminus D_{1}}
.
We obtain

\deg(\varphi^{\ast}\mathcal{P}_{\ast}V)=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\int_{C_{1}\setminus D_{1}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits R(\varphi^{\ast}h)=d(\varphi)\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\int_{C_{2}\setminus D_{2}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits R(\varphi^{\ast}h)=d(\varphi)\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}V).

4.3.8
Proof of Proposition
4.11

We set
X^{\prime}=X/G
and
H^{\prime}=H/G
.
Let
\pi:X\to X^{\prime}
denote the quotient map.
We set
C^{\prime}=\pi(C)\subset X^{\prime}
.
There exists a good meromorphic flat bundle
(V^{\prime},\nabla)

on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})

such that
\pi^{\ast}(V^{\prime},\nabla)\simeq(V,\nabla)
.
We have

\pi^{\ast}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}V^{\prime})\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(V)
.
By Proposition
4.10
,
we obtain

\deg\left(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{\prime}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{b}(V^{\prime})\bigr)\right)=b\cdot\deg(N_{C^{\prime}}X^{\prime})\cdot\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{\prime}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{b}(V^{\prime}).

We identify
G={\mathbb{Z}}/m{\mathbb{Z}}
for
a positive integer
m
.
Let
\chi:G\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\ast}
be the character
such that

\chi(1)=\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}(\ell/m))

where
0\leq\ell<m
.
Let
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
We set
b=(a-\ell)/m
.
There exists a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{\prime}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{b}(V^{\prime})\bigr)\simeq\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)_{\chi}\otimes N_{C}(X)^{-\ell}.

Note that

\deg(N_{C^{\prime}}X^{\prime})=m\deg(N_{C}X)
.
We obtain

\deg\left(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)\bigr)\right)=\deg\left(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}\bigl({}^{C^{\prime}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{b}(V^{\prime})\bigr)\right)+\ell\deg(N_{C}X)\\
=b\cdot\deg(N_{C^{\prime}}X^{\prime})\cdot\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{\prime}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{b}(V^{\prime})+\ell\deg(N_{C}X)\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C^{\prime}}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{b}(V^{\prime})\\
=a\deg(N_{C}X)\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{C}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V)_{\chi}.

(15)

Thus, we obtain Proposition
4.11
.

4.4
Proof of Proposition
4.1

Note there exists
0\leq\alpha_{i}<\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)
such that

c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}V)=c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}V)+\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\alpha_{i}[H_{i}].

Because
c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}V)=0
,
we obtain the first claim.
By Proposition
4.10
,
we have

\deg_{H_{i}}\Bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\bigl({}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})\bigr)\Bigr)=a\cdot\deg(N_{H_{i}}X)\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})=a\cdot[H_{i}]^{2}\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}).

Hence, we obtain the second claim.
Because

\int\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}V)=0
,
we obtain

\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}V)-\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{-1<a\leq 0}a\deg_{H_{i}}\bigl({}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}V)\bigr)=\\
-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in\Lambda}\sum_{-1<a\leq 0}a^{2}\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\bigl({}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{a}(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}V)\bigr)\int_{X}[H_{i}]^{2}\\
-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq j\\
P\in H_{i}\cap H_{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{-1<a_{i},a_{j}\leq 0}a_{i}\cdot a_{j}\cdot\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{P}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}_{(a_{i},a_{j})}\bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}(V)\bigr).

(16)

We obtain the third claim.

5
Algebraic meromorphic flat bundles

5.1
Good meromorphic flat bundles

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
X
be any
n
-dimensional smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
H
be a simple normal crossing hypersurface of
X
.
Let
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
denote the completion
of the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}

with respect to the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{x}
.
There exists a parameter system
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
of
\mathfrak{m}_{x}

such that the ideal of
H
at
x
is generated by

\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}
.
For any positive integer
e
,
we set

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}[x_{i}^{1/e}\,|\,i=1,\ldots,\ell]
.
For any
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-module
\mathcal{F}
,
we set

\mathcal{F}(\ast H)=\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}

and

\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{F})=\mathcal{F}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}
.
For any free
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)
-module
\mathcal{F}
,
a lattice of
\mathcal{F}
means
a free
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
-submodule
\mathcal{F}_{0}\subset\mathcal{F}

such that
\mathcal{F}_{0}(\ast H)=\mathcal{F}
.

Let
V
be a free
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module of finite rank
with an integrable connection
\nabla:V\to V\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}
.
We set
V_{|\widehat{x}}=V_{x}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}

which is equipped with the induced integrable connection
\nabla
.
Any
\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(V_{|\widehat{x}})
is equipped with
the induced integrable connection
\nabla
.
We recall some definitions.

Definition 5.1

•

(V,\nabla)
is called regular at
x

if there exists a free
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-module
V_{0}\subset V

such that
(i)
V_{0}(\ast H)=V
,
(ii)
\nabla(V_{0})\subset V_{0}\otimes\Omega^{1}(\log H)
.

•

(V,\nabla)
is called unramifiedly good at
x

if there exist a good set of irregular values

\mathcal{I}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H)/\mathcal{O}_{X,x}

and a decomposition

(V,\nabla)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}(V_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}})

(17)

such that

\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{V_{\mathfrak{a}}}

are regular.
Here,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}

denote lifts of
\mathfrak{a}
.
We allow
V_{\mathfrak{a}}=0

in
(
17
)
.
The set

\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)=\bigl\{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}\,|\,V_{\mathfrak{a}}\neq 0\bigr\}

is well defined.

•

(V,\nabla)
is called good at
x

if there exists
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

such that

\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)

is unramifiedly good.
We obtain the set

\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}(\ast H)/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(e)}_{X,x}
,
which is invariant under
the action of the Galois group
of the extension

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,e)}_{X,x}
.

We say that
(V,\nabla)
is
good (resp. regular, unramifiedly good) on
(X,H)

if
(V,\nabla)_{|\widehat{x}}
is
good (resp. regular, unramifiedly good) at any
x\in H
.

5.1.1
Extension of fields

Let
X
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety with
normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
be an algebraically closed field which contains
\mathbb{K}
.
We set
(X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})=(X,H)\times_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}^{\prime}
.
Let
(V,\nabla)
be a meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
Let
(V_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},\nabla)
denote the induced meromorphic
flat bundle on
(X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})
.

Proposition 5.2

(V,\nabla)
is good if and only if

(V_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},\nabla)
is good.

Proof
Suppose that

(V_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},\nabla)
is good.
Let
x
be any closed point of
X
.
The induced closed point of
X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}

is also denoted by
x
.
Note that

(V_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}|\widehat{x}},\nabla)

is induced by

(V_{|\widehat{x}},\nabla)

and the extension

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}},x}
.
By
[
14
, Lemma 1.6.5, Definition 3.2.1, Theorem 4.4.2]
,

(V_{|\widehat{x}},\nabla)
is good
if and only if

(V_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}|\widehat{x}},\nabla)
is good.
Hence,

(V,\nabla)
is good.

Let us study the converse.
Let
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
be the irreducible decomposition.
For any non-empty
I\subset\Lambda
,
we set

H_{I}=\bigcap_{j\in I}H_{j}

and

H_{I}^{\circ}=H_{I}\setminus\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda\setminus I}H_{j}
.
Let
y
be a closed point of

(H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})_{I}^{\circ}
.
Let
\eta
denote the point of
H_{I}^{\circ}

obtained as the image of
y

by the natural morphism

X_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}}\to X
.
There exist
an affine neighbourhood
U
of
\eta
in
X

with sections
x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)

such that
the ideal of
H\cap U
is generated by

\prod_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}
.
For any
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
let
U^{(e)}
denote the affine
\mathbb{K}
-variety
associated with

\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)[x_{1}^{1/e},\ldots,x_{m}^{1/e}]

with the natural morphism

\varphi_{e}:U^{(e)}\to U
.
There exists
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

such that

\varphi_{e}^{\ast}V

is unramifiedly good at any point of

\varphi_{e}^{-1}(H)
.
Let
R
denote the ring
corresponding to
\varphi_{e}^{-1}(U\cap H_{I})
.
We set
z_{i}=x_{i}^{1/e}

(i=1,\ldots,m)
.
We obtain the locally free

R[\![z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}]\!][z_{1}^{-1},\ldots,z_{m}^{-1}]
-module

V_{I}

with the integrable connection
from
\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
.
By
[
15
, Proposition 4.4.1]

and a remark after
[
14
, Definition 5.1.1]
,
there exists a finite etale extension

R\subset R^{\prime}

such that the following holds.

•

\varphi_{e}^{-1}(y)
is contained
in the image of
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R^{\prime})\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
.

•

There exist a good set of irregular values

\mathcal{I}\subset R^{\prime}[\![z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}]\!][z_{1}^{-1},\ldots,z_{m}^{-1}]\big/R^{\prime}[\![z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}]\!]

and a good decomposition

V_{I}\otimes_{R[\![z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}]\!]}R^{\prime}[\![z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}]\!]=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}(V_{I,\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}})

(18)

such that
\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}

are regular singular.

We obtain the ramified good decomposition
of
(V_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}|\widehat{y}},\nabla)

from (
18
).

5.1.2
Restriction

Let
X
be a smooth complex
\mathbb{K}
-variety with
\dim X\geq 3
.
Let
H
be a simple normal crossing hypersurface
with the irreducible decomposition
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
.
Let
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

be good on
(X,H)
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma)

be a good system of ramified irregular values on
(X,H)
.

Let
Z
be an ample smooth hypersurface of
X
such that
(i)
Z\subsetneq H
,
(ii)
Z\cup H
is also simple normal crossing hypersurface.
Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{Z}
be the good system of ramified irregular values
on
(Z,Z\cap H)
induced by
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
.
By the restriction,
we obtain the functors

\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(X,H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\to\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(Z,Z\cap H,{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}_{Z}),

(19)

We obtain the following proposition
from Proposition
5.2

and Theorem
3.23
.

Theorem 5.3

The restriction functor

(
19
)

is an equivalence.

5.2
Good lattices and the eigenvalues of the residues

5.2.1
Unramifiedly good lattices

Let
X
be a smooth projective
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H

with the irreducible decomposition
H=\bigcup_{k\in\Lambda}H_{k}
.
Let
(V,\nabla)
be an unramifiedly good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
For any
x\in H
,
there exists a decomposition

(V_{|\widehat{x}},\nabla)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)}(V_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}})

such that
\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}

are regular.

Let
E
be a lattice of
(V,\nabla)
,
i.e.,
E
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule of
V

such that
E(\ast H)=V
.
We obtain

E_{|\widehat{x}}\subset V_{|\widehat{x}}
.

Definition 5.4

We say that
E
is unramifiedly good at
x

if

E_{|\widehat{x}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)}(E_{|\widehat{x}}\cap V_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}}),

(20)

and
\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\mathfrak{a}
are logarithmic
with respect to
E_{|\widehat{x}}\cap V_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}}
.
We say that
E
is unramifiedly good on
(X,H)

if it is unramifiedly good at any
x\in H
.

5.2.2
Good lattices

Let
(V,\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
Let
x\in H
.
Let
U=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
be an affine open neighbourhood of
x

with an etale coordinate system
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

such that
U\cap H=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{x_{i}=0\}
.
For
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,

R^{(e)}=R[x_{1}^{1/e},\ldots,x_{\ell}^{1/e}]

and
U^{(e)}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R^{(e)})
.
Let
H^{(e)}_{U}
denote the inverse image of
H
by
U^{(e)}\to X
.
We obtain the unramifiedly good meromorphic flat bundle

(V^{(e)}_{U},\nabla)
on
(U^{(e)},H^{(e)}_{U})

as the pull back of
(V,\nabla)
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits^{(H,e)}_{U}
denote the Galois group of
the ramified covering
U^{(e)}\to U
.

Definition 5.5

Let
E
be a lattice of
V
.
We say that
E
is good at
x

if there exist an etale coordinate neighbourhood

(U,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
of
x
as above
and a
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits^{(H,e)}_{U}
-equivariant
unramifiedly good lattice
E_{U}^{(e)}

of
(V^{(e)}_{U},\nabla)

such that
E_{|U}
is the descent of
E_{U}^{(e)}
.
We say that
E
is good on
(X,H)

if it is good at any
x\in H
.

5.2.3
Decompositions into the regular part and the irregular part

Let
(V,\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
Let
E
be a good lattice.
Let
x\in H_{k}
.
We have the natural map

\rho_{k}:\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,e)}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}\big/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,e)}_{X}(\ast H_{\neq k})_{x}

Let
\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)_{k,0}=\rho_{k}^{-1}(0)
.
We set

(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits,k}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)_{k,0}}(E_{|\widehat{x}}\cap V^{(H,e)}_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}}),\quad\quad(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\not\in\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)_{k,0}}(E_{|\widehat{x}}\cap V^{(H,e)}_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}}).

We obtain the decomposition

E_{|\widehat{x}}=(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits,k}\oplus(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}.

(21)

It is easy to check the following

\nabla(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits,k}\subset(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits,k}\otimes\Bigl(\Omega^{1}(\log H_{k})+\Omega^{1}(\ast H_{\neq k})\Bigr),\quad\quad\nabla(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}\subset(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}\otimes\Omega^{1}(\ast H).

(22)

Let
E_{|\widehat{H}_{k}}
denote the completion of
E
along
H_{k}
,
i.e.,

E_{|\widehat{H}_{k}}=\varprojlim E/E(-mH_{k})
.

Proposition 5.6

There exists the decomposition

E_{|\widehat{H}_{k}}=E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\oplus E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}

(23)

which induces
(
21
)

for any
x\in H_{k}
.
Moreover, we have

\nabla(E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits})\subset E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\otimes\Bigl(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log H_{k})+\Omega^{1}_{X}(\ast H_{\neq k})\Bigr)

and

\nabla(E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits})\subset E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(\ast H)
.

The second claim follows from (
22
).
We shall study the first claim
for the unramifiedly good case
in §
5.2.4
,
and for the general case
in §
5.2.5
,

5.2.4
Proof of Proposition
5.6

in the unramified case

Suppose that
(V,\nabla)
and
E
are unramifiedly good.
Let
x\in H_{k}
.
Let
U\subset X
be a Zariski open neighbourhood of
x

with an etale coordinate system

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

such that
the ideal of
H
at
x
is generated by

\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}

and that the ideal of
H_{k}
at
x
is generated by
x_{1}
.
We set
H_{U,k}=U\cap H_{k}
.
We also assume that
E_{|U}
is
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-free.

We set

\mathcal{A}^{(0)}=H^{0}(H_{U,k},\mathcal{O}_{H_{k}}(\ast\partial H_{k}))
,
where
\partial H_{k}=H_{k}\cap H_{\neq k}
.
We set

\mathcal{R}^{(0)}=\varprojlim\mathcal{A}^{(0)}[x_{1}]/x_{1}^{m}\mathcal{A}^{(0)}[x_{1}]\simeq\varprojlim\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H_{\neq k})(U)/x_{1}^{m}\mathcal{O}^{h}_{X}(\ast H_{\neq k})(U).

We also set

\mathcal{M}^{(0)}=\mathcal{R}^{(0)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U)}E(U)
.
It is equipped with the induced integrable connection

\nabla:\mathcal{M}^{(0)}\to\mathcal{M}^{(0)}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(\ast H)
.
There exists a positive integer
N
such that

x_{1}^{N+1}\nabla_{x_{1}}\mathcal{M}^{(0)}\subset\mathcal{M}^{(0)}
.
We obtain the
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear map

G^{(0)}:\mathcal{M}^{(0)}\to\mathcal{M}^{(0)}
induced by
x_{1}^{N+1}\nabla_{x_{1}}

satisfying

G^{(0)}(fs)=x_{1}^{N+1}\partial_{x_{1}}(f)s+fG^{(0)}(s)

for
f\in\mathcal{R}^{(0)}
and
s\in\mathcal{M}^{(0)}
.
It induces the
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}
-endomorphism
F^{(0)}
of

\mathcal{M}^{(0)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(0)}
.

Lemma 5.7

There exist a finitely generated
Galois extension
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}\subset\mathcal{A}^{(1)}
,
a finite subset

\mathcal{J}_{1}\subset\mathcal{A}^{(1)}

and the decomposition

\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{(0)}}(\mathcal{M}^{(0)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(0)})=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{J}_{1}}\Bigl(\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{(0)}}(\mathcal{M}^{(0)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(0)})\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{c}}

(24)

such that (i)
F^{(0)}-\mathfrak{c}=0
on

\Bigl(\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{(0)}}(\mathcal{M}^{(0)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(0)})\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{c}}
,
(ii) for any two distinct elements
\mathfrak{c}_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{2}\in\mathcal{J}_{1}
,
the difference
\mathfrak{c}_{1}-\mathfrak{c}_{2}
is invertible
in
\mathcal{A}^{(1)}
,
(iii) there exists an open neighbourhood
x\in U^{\prime}\subset H_{k}

such that
