\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathcal{A}^{(1)})\times_{U}U^{\prime}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathcal{A}^{(0)})\times_{U}U^{\prime}
is etale.

Proof
We have the decomposition (
20
)
of
E_{|\widehat{x}}
.
We have the map

\nu:\mathcal{I}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H_{\neq k})\big/x_{1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}(\ast H_{\neq k})=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{H_{k},x}(\ast\partial H_{k})

by considering

\mathfrak{a}\mapsto x_{1}^{N+1}\partial_{x_{1}}\mathfrak{a}
.
Let
\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
denote the image of
\nu
.
For
\mathfrak{c}\in\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
, we set

E_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{c}}=\bigoplus_{\nu(\mathfrak{a})=\mathfrak{c}}(E_{|\widehat{x}}\cap V_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}})
.
We have the decomposition

E_{|\widehat{x}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\in\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{1}}E_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{c}}
,
and

x_{1}^{N+1}\nabla_{x_{1}}E_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{c}}\subset E_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{c}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H_{\neq k})_{x}
.
For two distinct
\mathfrak{c}_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{2}\in\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
,

\mathfrak{c}_{1}-\mathfrak{c}_{2}
is invertible.
Let
\widehat{F}^{(0)}
be the endomorphism of

(E_{|\widehat{x}}/x_{1}E_{|\widehat{x}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{k}}(\ast\partial H_{k})_{x}
.
We have the decomposition

(E_{|\widehat{x}}/x_{1}E_{|\widehat{x}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{k}}(\ast\partial H_{k})_{x}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\in\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{1}}(E_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{c}}/x_{1})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{k}}(\ast\partial H_{k})_{x},

(25)

and

\widehat{F}^{(0)}-\mathfrak{c}=0

on

(E_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{c}}/x_{1})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{H_{k}}(\ast\partial H_{k})_{x}
.

Let
P^{(0)}
denote the characteristic polynomial of
F^{(0)}
.
We fix an algebraically closed field which contains
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}
.
Let
{\mathcal{S}p}(P^{(0)})
denote the set of the solutions of
P^{(0)}
.
Let
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}\subset\mathcal{A}^{(1)}

denote a finitely generated Galois extension such that
(i)
{\mathcal{S}p}(P^{(0)})\subset\mathcal{A}^{(1)}
,
(ii) for any two distinct elements

\mathfrak{c}_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{2}\in{\mathcal{S}p}(P^{(0)})
,

\mathfrak{c}_{1}-\mathfrak{c}_{2}\in\mathcal{A}^{(1)}
.
We obtain the decomposition (
24
),
which is preserved by
F^{(0)}
,
such that

F^{(0)}-\mathfrak{c}
are nilpotent on

\Bigl(\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{(0)}}(\mathcal{M}^{(0)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(0)})\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{c}}
.
By the comparison with (
25
),
we obtain that
the conditions (i) and (iii) are also satisfied.

We set

\mathcal{R}^{(1)}=\varprojlim\mathcal{A}^{(1)}[x_{1}]/x^{m}\mathcal{A}^{(1)}[x_{1}]

and

\mathcal{M}^{(1)}=\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U)}E(U)
.
It is equipped with the induced integrable connection

\nabla:\mathcal{M}^{(1)}\to\mathcal{M}^{(1)}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(\ast H)
.
It is standard to obtain the unique decomposition

\mathcal{M}^{(1)}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{J}_{1}}\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}

such that the following holds.

•

The decomposition is preserved by
G^{(0)}
.

•

\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}=\Bigl(\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{(0)}}(\mathcal{M}^{(0)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(0)})\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{c}}
.

•

x_{1}^{-1}(x_{1}^{N+1}\nabla_{x_{1}}-\mathfrak{c})\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}\subset\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.

•

\nabla(\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c}})\subset\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}\otimes\Omega^{1}(\ast H)
.

Lemma 5.8

Inductively,
for
j=1,\ldots,N
,
we can construct the following tuples.

•

Finitely generated Galois extensions

\mathcal{A}^{(j)}
of
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}
.
We set

\mathcal{R}^{(j)}=\varprojlim\mathcal{A}^{(j)}[x_{1}]/x^{m}\mathcal{A}^{(j)}[x_{1}]

and

\mathcal{M}^{(j)}=\mathcal{A}^{(j)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U)}E(U)
.
It is equipped with the induced integrable connection

\nabla:\mathcal{M}^{(j)}\to\mathcal{M}^{(j)}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(\ast H)
.

•

Finite subsets

\mathcal{J}_{j}\subset\mathcal{R}^{(j)}/x_{1}^{j}\mathcal{R}^{(j)}
,
and decompositions

\mathcal{M}^{(j)}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{J}_{j}}\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.

They satisfy the following conditions.

•

The projection

\mathcal{R}_{x}/x_{1}^{j+1}\mathcal{R}_{x}\to\mathcal{R}_{x}/x_{1}^{j}\mathcal{R}_{x}

induces the surjection

\mathcal{J}_{j}\to\mathcal{J}_{j-1}
.

•

If two distinct elements
\mathfrak{c}_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{2}\in\mathcal{J}_{j}

are mapped to the same element in
\mathcal{J}_{j-1}
,
then

x_{1}^{-j}(\mathfrak{c}_{1}-\mathfrak{c}_{2})

is invertible in

\mathcal{A}^{(j)}
.

•

x_{1}^{-j}(x_{1}^{N+1}\nabla_{x_{1}}-\mathfrak{c})\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}\subset\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.

•

\nabla(\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}})\subset\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}\otimes\Omega^{1}(\ast H)
.

•

There exists an open neighbourhood
x\in U^{\prime}\subset U

such that

\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathcal{A}^{(j)}\times_{U}U^{\prime}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathcal{A}^{(0)}\times_{U}U^{\prime}

is etale.

In particular,
we have

(x_{1}\nabla_{x_{1}}-x_{1}^{-N}\mathfrak{c})\mathcal{M}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{c}}\subset\mathcal{M}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{c}}

for any
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{J}_{N-1}
.

Proof
Suppose we have already constructed

\mathcal{A}^{(j)}
,
\mathcal{J}_{j}
and

\mathcal{M}^{(j)}=\bigoplus\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.
We obtain the
\mathbb{K}
-linear map

G_{\mathfrak{c}}^{(j)}:\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}\to\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}

induced by
x_{1}^{-j}(x_{1}^{N+1}\nabla_{x_{1}}-\mathfrak{c})
.
It satisfies

G_{\mathfrak{c}}^{(j)}(fs)=fG_{\mathfrak{c}}^{(j)}(s)+x_{1}^{N+1-j}\partial_{x_{1}}(f)s

for
f\in\mathcal{R}^{(j)}
and
s\in\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.
It induces the
\mathcal{A}^{(j)}
-endomorphism
F^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}

of
\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.
As in Lemma
5.7
,
there exist a finitely generated
Galois extension
\mathcal{A}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}
of
\mathcal{A}^{(j)}
,
a finite subset

\mathcal{J}_{\mathfrak{c},j+1}\subset\mathcal{A}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}

and the decomposition

\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{(j+1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{(j)}}(\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}})=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathfrak{c},j+1}}\Bigl(\mathcal{A}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{(j)}}(\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}})\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{b}}

(26)

such that (i)
F_{\mathfrak{c}}^{(j)}-\mathfrak{b}=0
on

\Bigl(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{(j+1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{(j)}}(\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}})\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{c}}
,
(ii)for any two distinct elements

\mathfrak{b}_{1},\mathfrak{b}_{2}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathfrak{c},j+1}
,
the difference
\mathfrak{b}_{1}-\mathfrak{b}_{2}
is invertible
in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{(j+1)}
,
(iii) there exists a neighbourhood
x\in U^{\prime}\subset U

such that

\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathcal{A}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}\times_{U}U^{\prime}\to\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathcal{A}^{(0)}\times_{U}U^{\prime}

is etale.
Let
\mathcal{A}^{(j+1)}
be
a finitely generated
Galois extension of
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}
,
which contains all
\mathcal{A}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.
We set

\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}=\mathcal{A}^{(j+1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U)}E(U)
,
equipped with the induced integrable connection

\nabla:\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}\to\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}\otimes\Omega^{1}(\ast H)
.
It is standard to obtain the unique decomposition

\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathfrak{c},j+1}}\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}+x_{1}^{j}\mathfrak{b}}

such that the following holds.

•

The decomposition is preserved by
G^{(j)}
.

•

\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}+x_{1}^{j}\mathfrak{b}}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}+x_{1}^{j}\mathfrak{b}}=\Bigl(\mathcal{A}^{(j+1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{(j)}}(\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{c}})\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{b}}
.

•

x_{1}^{-j}(x_{1}^{N+1}\nabla_{x_{1}}-(\mathfrak{c}+x_{1}^{j+1}\mathfrak{b}))\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c}+x_{1}^{j}\mathfrak{b}}\subset\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c}+x_{1}^{j}\mathfrak{b}}
.

•

\nabla(\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}+x_{1}^{j}\mathfrak{b}})\subset\mathcal{M}^{(j+1)}_{\mathfrak{c}+x_{1}^{j}\mathfrak{b}}\otimes\Omega^{1}(\ast H)
.

We set

\mathcal{J}_{j+1}=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{J}_{j}}\{\mathfrak{c}+x_{1}^{j}\mathfrak{b}\,|\,\mathfrak{b}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathfrak{c},j+1}\}
.
Thus, the inductive construction can proceed.

We set
\mathcal{M}^{(N)\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}=\mathcal{M}^{(N)}_{0}

and
\mathcal{M}^{(N)\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}\neq 0}\mathcal{M}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.
We obtain the decomposition

\mathcal{M}^{(N)}=\mathcal{M}^{(N)\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\oplus\mathcal{M}^{(N)\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}.

(27)

The decomposition is preserved by
\nabla
.

By shrinking
U
,
we may assume that

\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathcal{A}^{(N)})\to(U\cap H_{k})\setminus\partial H_{k}

is finite and etale.
Because the decomposition (
27
)
is invariant under the Galois extension

\mathcal{A}^{(0)}\to\mathcal{A}^{(N)}
,
there exists the decomposition

\mathcal{M}^{(0)}=\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\oplus\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}

(28)

as the descent of (
27
).
We can check the following.

\nabla\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\subset\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\otimes\Bigl(\Omega^{1}(\log H_{k})+\Omega^{1}(\ast H_{\neq k})\Bigr),\quad\quad\nabla\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}\subset\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}\otimes\Omega^{1}(\ast H).

(29)

We can also check that

\sum_{j\geq 0}(x_{1}\nabla_{x_{1}})^{j}(\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits})_{\widehat{y}}=(\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits})_{\widehat{y}}(\ast H_{k}),\quad\sum_{j\geq 0}(x_{1}\nabla_{x_{1}})^{j}(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}=(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}.

(30)

Lemma 5.9

There exists the decomposition

E_{|\widehat{H}_{k}}(U)=(E_{|\widehat{H}_{k}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}(U)\oplus(E_{|\widehat{H}_{k}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}(U)
,
which induces

(
21
)
and

(
28
)
.
It also induces the decompositions

(
21
)

for any
y\in U\cap H_{k}
.

Proof
We set

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{H_{k},x}(\ast\partial H_{k})[\![x_{1}]\!]

which contains
both

\mathcal{R}^{(0)}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
and

\mathcal{O}_{H_{k}}(H_{U,k})[\![x_{1}]\!]
.
There exists the natural isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\otimes E_{|\widehat{y}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{(0)}
.
By the properties (
22
),
(
29
)
and (
30
),
we obtain

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\otimes(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits,k}=\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits},\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\otimes(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}=\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{(0)\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}.

Because

\mathcal{R}^{(0)}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}=\mathcal{O}_{H_{k}}(H_{U,k})[\![x_{1}]\!]

in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
,
we obtain the first claim.
Applying the same argument to
y\in H_{U,k}
,
we obtain the second claim.

Thus, we obtain the decomposition (
23
).

5.2.5
Proof of Proposition
5.6
in the general case

Let
H_{U,k}^{(e)}
denote the inverse image of
H_{k}

by
U^{(e)}\to X
.
We have the
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits^{(H,e)}_{U}
-equivariant decomposition

(E_{U}^{(e)})_{|\widehat{H}_{U,k}^{(e)}}=(E_{U}^{(e)})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}_{\widehat{H}_{U,k}^{(e)}}\oplus(E_{U}^{(e)})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}_{\widehat{H}_{U,k}^{(e)}}.

(31)

Let
H_{U,k}=H_{k}\cap U
.
Because
E_{|U}
is the descent of
E^{(e)}_{U}
,
we obtain the following decomposition
as the descent of (
31
):

(E_{U})_{|\widehat{H}_{U,k}}=(E_{|U})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}_{\widehat{H}_{U,k}}\oplus(E_{|U})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}_{\widehat{H}_{U,k}}.

Then, we obtain
(
21
).

5.2.6
Residues

If
E
is unramifiedly good,
for
k\in\Lambda
such that
x\in H_{k}
,
we obtain the endomorphism

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)
of
(E_{|H_{k}})_{|\widehat{x}}

as the residue of

\bigoplus(\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\mathfrak{a})

along
H_{k}
.

Proposition 5.10

If
E
is unramifiedly good,
there exists the endomorphism
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)
of
E_{|H_{k}}

which induces

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)
of
(E_{|H_{k}})_{\widehat{x}}

for any
x\in H_{k}
.

Proof
We continue to use the notation in the proof of
Proposition
5.6
.
We obtain the endomorphism
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

of
\mathcal{M}^{(N)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(N)}

as

\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{c}}\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{x_{1}}\bigl(x_{1}\nabla_{x_{1}}-x_{1}^{-N}\mathfrak{c}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{M}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{c}}}\bigr)
.
Because it is invariant under the action of the Galois group,
there exists

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)
on
\mathcal{M}^{(0)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(0)}

which induces

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)
on
\mathcal{M}^{(N)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(N)}
.
We obtain the claim of Proposition
5.10

from the following lemma.

Lemma 5.11

There exists the
\mathcal{O}_{H_{k}}(H_{U,k})
-endomorphism
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

of
E(U)/x_{1}E(U)

which induces
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

on
\mathcal{M}^{(0)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(0)}
.

Proof
By using the decomposition (
21
),
we obtain
the
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}/x_{1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-endomorphisms

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{x_{1}}(\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\mathfrak{a})

of
(E_{|\widehat{x}}\cap V_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}})\big/x_{1}(E_{|\widehat{x}}\cap V_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}})
.
We obtain the
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}/x_{1}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
-endomorphisms

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{x_{1}}(\nabla)=\bigoplus\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{x_{1}}(\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\mathfrak{a})

of
E_{|\widehat{x}}/x_{1}E_{|\widehat{x}}
.
By comparing two residue endomorphisms,
we obtain that
there exists the endomorphism
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

on
E_{|H_{k}}(U)

which induces

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

of
\mathcal{M}^{(0)}/x_{1}\mathcal{M}^{(0)}
.
Thus, we obtain Lemma
5.11

and Proposition
5.10
.

Let us consider the case where
E
is good but not necessarily
unramifiedly good.
By (
22
),
we obtain the endomorphism

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

of
(E^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}_{\widehat{H}_{k}|H_{k}})_{|\widehat{x}}
.

Proposition 5.12

Let
E
be good lattice.
We obtain the residue endomorphism

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

on
(E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits})_{|H_{k}}
,
which induces
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

on
(E^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}_{\widehat{H}_{k}|H_{k}})_{|\widehat{x}}

for any
x\in H_{k}
.

Note that the set of the eigenvalues of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)_{x}

are independent of
x\in H_{k}
.

Notation 5.13

Let
{\mathcal{S}p}(E,\nabla,H_{k})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\subset\mathbb{K}

denote the set of the eigenvalues of

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

on
(E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits})_{|H_{k}}
.

5.3
Good
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange filtered bundles

5.3.1
The case
\mathbb{K}\subset{\mathbb{C}}

Let
\mathbb{K}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
be an algebraically closed field.
Let
X
be a smooth projective
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
(V,\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.

Let
(U=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R,\varphi,\psi)

be an etale coordinate system as in §
2.4.5
.
For
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
let
R^{(e)}=R[x_{1}^{1/e},\ldots,x_{\ell}^{1/e}]

and
U^{(e)}=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits R^{(e)}
.
Let
\rho^{(e)}:U^{(e)}\to U
denote the induced morphism.
Let
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}
denote the Galois group of the ramified covering.
For any
e\in(\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits V)!{\mathbb{Z}}
,
we set

(V^{(e)},\nabla)=\rho_{e}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
,
which is
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}
-equivariant
and unramifiedly good
on
(U^{(e)},H^{(e)})
.

By the extension
\mathbb{K}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
,
we obtain the unramifiedly good meromorphic flat bundle

(V^{(e)}_{{\mathbb{C}}},\nabla)

on
(U^{(e)}_{{\mathbb{C}}},H^{(e)}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
.
Let
E
be any unramifiedly good lattice of
(V^{(e)}_{{\mathbb{C}}},\nabla)
.
There exists the residue endomorphisms

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)

(i=1,\ldots,\ell)
of
E_{|H^{(e)}_{{\mathbb{C}},i}}
.
The eigenvalues of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)_{|y}

are independent of
y\in H_{{\mathbb{C}},i}^{(e)}
.

Lemma 5.14

The eigenvalues of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)_{|y}

are contained in
\mathbb{K}
.

Proof
Let
z\in H^{(e)}_{i}\setminus\bigcup_{j\neq i}H^{(e)}_{j}
.
There exists an unramifiedly good lattice

L\subset V_{|\widehat{z}}

for which we have the decomposition

(L,\nabla)_{|\widehat{z}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(V,z)}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}})

such that
\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}

are logarithmic.
We obtain the residues

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}})

of
L_{|z}
.

For any eigenvalue
\alpha
of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)
on
E_{|z}
,
there exists an eigenvalue
\beta
of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)
on
L_{|z}

such that
\alpha-\beta\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Conversely,
for any eigenvalue
\beta
of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)
on
L_{|z}
,
there exists an eigenvalue
\alpha
of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)
on
E_{|z}

such that
\alpha-\beta\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

For any
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
,
there exists the unramifiedly good lattice

\mathcal{P}_{e{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(V^{(e)}_{{\mathbb{C}}})

determined by the following condition.

•

Any eigenvalue
\alpha

of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\nabla)

satisfies

ea_{i}-1<\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\alpha)\leq ea_{i}
.

Because it is
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}
-equivariant,
we obtain the good lattice

\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(V_{{\mathbb{C}}})
of
V_{{\mathbb{C}}|U_{{\mathbb{C}}}}

as the descent of

\mathcal{P}_{e{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(V^{(e)}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
.
We also have the invariance of

\mathcal{P}_{e{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(V^{(e)}_{{\mathbb{C}}})

under the action of the Galois group of
{\mathbb{C}}/\mathbb{K}
,
and hence
there exists a lattice

\mathcal{P}_{e{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(V^{(e)})
of
V^{(e)}

which induces

\mathcal{P}_{e{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(V^{(e)}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
.
We also have the lattice

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}(V)_{U}
of
V_{|U}
.

Let
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
be the irreducible decomposition.
By patching
\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}(V)_{U}
for any
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
,
we obtain the lattices
\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}(V)
.

5.3.2
General case

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
,
which is not necessarily contained in
{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
X
be a smooth projective
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
(V,\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)
.

There exists a subfield
\mathbb{K}_{0}\subset\mathbb{K}

such that
(i)
\mathbb{K}_{0}
is the algebraic closure of a field
finitely generated over
{\mathbb{Q}}
,
(ii)
(X,H)
and
(V,\nabla)
are defined over
\mathbb{K}_{0}
,
i.e.,
there exist
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{0}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{0}})
over
\mathbb{K}_{0}

and
(V_{\mathbb{K}_{0}},\nabla)
on
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{0}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{0}})

such that they induce
(X,H)
and
(V,\nabla)
.

There exists an embedding

\mathbb{K}_{0}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
.
Depending on the embedding,
there exists a filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(V_{\mathbb{K}_{0}})

indexed by
{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
.
It induces a filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(V)

indexed by
{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
.

5.3.3
Pull back

Let
Y
be a smooth projective variety over
\mathbb{K}

with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H_{Y}
.
Let
f:Y\to X
be a morphism such that
f^{-1}(H)=H_{Y}
.
Recall the following.

Lemma 5.15

(V_{Y},\nabla)=f^{\ast}(V,\nabla)

is a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(Y,H_{Y})
.

Proof
Let
y\in Y
be a closed point.
We set
x=f(y)
.
For some
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
there exist

\mathcal{I}(V,x)\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,e)}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,e)}_{X,x}

and a decomposition

(V,\nabla)\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,e)}_{x}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(V,x)}(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}})

such that
each
(\widehat{V}_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}})

has a logarithmic lattice.
There exists
e^{\prime}

such that the morphism

\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\to\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}

induces

\mathcal{O}^{(H,e)}_{X,x}\to\mathcal{O}^{(H_{Y},e^{\prime})}_{Y,y}
.
We obtain

\mathcal{I}(V_{Y},y)\subset\mathcal{O}^{(H_{Y},e^{\prime})}_{Y}(\ast H_{Y})_{y}/\mathcal{O}^{(H_{Y},e^{\prime})}_{Y,y}

and the decomposition

(V_{Y},\nabla)\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H_{Y},e^{\prime})}_{Y,y}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(V,x)}(\widehat{V}_{Y,\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}})

such that
each

(\widehat{V}_{Y,\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}})

has a logarithmic lattice.

Let
H_{Y}=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda_{Y}}H_{Y,i}

denote the irreducible decomposition.
For any
i\in\Lambda
,
let
{\boldsymbol{b}}^{(i)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{\Lambda_{Y}}

be determined by the pull back

f^{\ast}(H_{i})=\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{Y}}b^{(i)}_{j}H_{Y,j}

as the divisor.
The matrix
(b^{(i)}_{j})

determines the map

f^{\ast}:{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda_{Y}}
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\Lambda}=(1,\ldots,1)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
.

Lemma 5.16

For any
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
,
we have

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{f^{\ast}({\boldsymbol{a}}-{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\Lambda})}(f^{\ast}V)\subset f^{\ast}\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}(V)\subset\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{f^{\ast}({\boldsymbol{a}})}(f^{\ast}V)
.

Proof
It is enough to consider the unramified case
and
\mathbb{K}={\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
y\in H_{Y}
and
x=f(y)
.
Let
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})
be an etale coordinate around
y

such that

H_{Y}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}\{y_{i}=0\}
.
Let
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
be an etale coordinate neighbourhood around
x

such that

H=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{x_{i}=0\}
.
Let
\alpha_{i,j}

(1\leq j\leq n,\,\,1\leq i\leq m)

be the non-negative integers determined by

f^{\ast}(dx_{j}/x_{j})=\sum\alpha_{i,j}dy_{i}/y_{i}.

Let
\beta_{i}\in\mathbb{K}

such that
a_{j}-1<-\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\beta_{j})\leq a_{i}
.
We obtain

\sum\alpha_{i,j}(a_{j}-1)<-\sum\alpha_{i,j}\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\beta_{j})\leq\sum\alpha_{i,j}a_{j}.

If
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{<0}^{\Lambda}
,
we have

-\sum\alpha_{i,j}\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\beta_{j})<0
.
If
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 1}^{\Lambda}
,
we have

0<-\sum\alpha_{i,j}\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(\beta_{j})
.

Corollary 5.17

For
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{<0}^{\Lambda}
,
we have

f^{\ast}\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}V\subset\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{<0}(f^{\ast}V)
.
For
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 1}^{\Lambda}
,
we have

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}(f^{\ast}V)\subset f^{\ast}\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}V
.

5.4
The associated meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity

Let
X_{\mathbb{K}}
be a normal projective
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a hypersurface
H_{\mathbb{K}}

such that
X_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}}

is smooth.
Let
(V,\nabla)
be a meromorphic integrable connection on

(X_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}})
,
i.e.,

V
is a coherent reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathbb{K}}}(\ast H_{\mathbb{K}})
-module
with an integrable connection

\nabla:V\to V\otimes\Omega_{X_{\mathbb{K}}}^{1}
.
The following theorem is proved in
[
15
]
.
If
\mathbb{K}={\mathbb{C}}
,
it is also proved in
[
24
]
.

Theorem 5.18

There exists a projective birational morphism

\rho_{\mathbb{K}}:X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}\to X_{\mathbb{K}}

such that
(i)
X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}
is smooth,
(ii)
H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}=\rho_{\mathbb{K}}^{-1}(H_{\mathbb{K}})
is normal crossing,
(iii)
X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}\simeq X_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}}
,
(iv)
\rho_{\mathbb{K}}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
is good
on
(X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}},H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}})
.

Theorem 5.19

There exists the unique irregularity of
a meromorphic Lagrangian cover
{\boldsymbol{I}}(V,\nabla)

such that the following holds.

•

Let
\rho:X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}\to X_{\mathbb{K}}

be a projective morphism of
smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties
such that
(i)
H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}=\rho^{-1}(H_{\mathbb{K}})

is normal crossing,
(ii)
X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}}\simeq X_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}}
.
Then,

\rho^{\ast}(V,\nabla)

is good on
(X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}})

if and only if

\rho^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{I}}(V,\nabla)

is etale locally good on
(X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}},H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}})
.
In case,
we have

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho^{\ast}(V,\nabla))={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho^{\ast}{\boldsymbol{I}}(V,\nabla))
.

Proof
Let us consider the case
where
\mathbb{K}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
.
We obtain
X_{{\mathbb{C}}}
,
H_{{\mathbb{C}}}

and
(V_{{\mathbb{C}}},\nabla)

from
X_{\mathbb{K}}
,
H_{\mathbb{K}}

and
(V_{\mathbb{K}},\nabla)
.
There exists a Lagrangian meromorphic cover

\Sigma(V_{{\mathbb{C}}})\subset T^{\ast}(X_{{\mathbb{C}}}\setminus H_{{\mathbb{C}}})

associated with
(V_{{\mathbb{C}}},\nabla)

as in §
3.4
.
Let

\rho_{\mathbb{K}}:(X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}},H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}})\to(X_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}})

be such that
\rho_{\mathbb{K}}^{\ast}(V_{\mathbb{K}},\nabla)

is good on
(X^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}},H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{K}})
.
Let
\rho_{{\mathbb{C}}}:(X^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}},H^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}})\to(X_{{\mathbb{C}}},H_{{\mathbb{C}}})

be the induced morphism.
By Corollary
3.17
,

\rho_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\ast}(\Sigma(V_{{\mathbb{C}}}))
is good on

(X^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{C}}},H_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\prime})
,
and we have

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\ast}(\Sigma(V_{{\mathbb{C}}})))={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\ast}(V_{{\mathbb{C}}},\nabla))
.
There exists

\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}}\subset T^{\ast}(X_{\mathbb{K}}\setminus H_{\mathbb{K}})

such that

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}\bigl((\rho_{\mathbb{K}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathbb{K}})\times{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\ast}\Sigma(V_{{\mathbb{C}}}))

by Proposition
2.58
.
By Proposition
2.57
,
Corollary
3.17

and Proposition
5.2
,
the claim holds in this case.

In the general case,
there exists an algebraically closed field

\mathbb{K}_{0}
of characteristic
0

such that
(i)
\mathbb{K}_{0}
is an algebraically closure of
a field finitely generated over
{\mathbb{Q}}
,
(ii)
\mathbb{K}_{0}\subset\mathbb{K}
,
(iii) there exists
(X_{\mathbb{K}_{0}},H_{\mathbb{K}_{0}},V_{\mathbb{K}_{0}},\nabla)

which induces
(X_{\mathbb{K}},H_{\mathbb{K}},V_{\mathbb{K}},\nabla)
.
By the previous consideration,
there exists
the irregularity of meromorphic Lagrangian cover

{\boldsymbol{I}}(V_{\mathbb{K}_{0}},\nabla)

associated with
(V_{\mathbb{K}_{0}},\nabla)
.
We set

{\boldsymbol{I}}(V_{\mathbb{K}},\nabla)={\boldsymbol{I}}(V_{\mathbb{K}_{0}},\nabla)\times\mathbb{K}
.
Then,
By Proposition
2.57
,
Corollary
3.17

and Proposition
5.2
,
the claim holds in this case.

5.4.1
Comparison with the pole orders of the connections

Let
X
be a normal
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a hypersurface
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
.
There exists a closed subset
Z\subset H

with
\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits_{X}(Z)\geq 2

such that
(i)
X^{\circ}=X\setminus Z
is smooth,
(ii)
H^{\circ}=H\setminus Z
is a normal crossing hypersurface of
X^{\circ}
.
We also set
H_{i}^{\circ}=H_{i}\setminus Z
.

Let
(V,\nabla)
be a meromorphic integrable connection
on
(X,H)
.
Note that there exists a closed subset

Z\subset Z^{\prime}\subset H

with
\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits_{X}Z^{\prime}\geq 2

such that

(V,\nabla)_{|X\setminus Z^{\prime}}

is good on
(X\setminus Z^{\prime},H\setminus Z^{\prime})
.

Proposition 5.20

For a tuple of non-negative integers

{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{i}\,|\,i\in\Lambda)
,
the following conditions are equivalent.

•

There exists a reflexive lattice
V_{0}\subset V

such that

\nabla(V_{0|X^{\circ}})\subset V_{0|X^{\circ}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X^{\circ}}(\log H^{\circ})(\sum m_{i}H^{\circ}_{i})
.

•

Let
Z\subset Z^{\prime}
be any closed subset of
H

with
\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits_{X}Z^{\prime}\geq 2

such that

(V,\nabla)_{|X\setminus Z^{\prime}}

is good on
(X\setminus Z^{\prime},H\setminus Z^{\prime})
.
Let
V_{0}\subset V
be any coherent reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
such that (i)
V_{0}(\ast H)=V
,
(ii)
V_{0|X\setminus Z^{\prime}}

is a good lattice of

(V,\nabla)_{|X\setminus Z^{\prime}}
.
Then, we have

\nabla(V_{0|X^{\circ}})\subset V_{0|X^{\circ}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X^{\circ}}(\log H^{\circ})(\sum m_{i}H_{i}^{\circ})
.

•

For any
\Sigma\in{\boldsymbol{I}}(V,\nabla)
,
the closure of
\Sigma
in

T^{\ast}X^{\circ}(\log H^{\circ})(\sum m_{i}H_{i}^{\circ})

is proper over
X^{\circ}
.

The conditions are independent of the choice of
Z
.

Proof
The second condition implies the first.
Let us prove that the first condition implies the second.
It is enough to consider the case
\dim X=1
.
Moreover, it is enough to consider formal and unramified case.
Let
V
be a finite dimensional

\mathbb{K}(\!(z)\!)
-vector space
with a connection
\nabla
.
Suppose that there is
a finite subset
\mathcal{I}\subset z^{-1}\mathbb{K}[z^{-1}]

and a decomposition

(V,\nabla)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}(V_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}})

such that
\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\mathfrak{a}
are regular.
Let
E=\bigoplus E_{\mathfrak{a}}
be an unramifiedly good lattice.
Let
N_{1}=\max\{\deg_{z^{-1}}\mathfrak{a}\,|\,\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}\}
.
Take
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}
such that

\deg_{z^{-1}}\mathfrak{a}=N_{1}
.
Let
G
be any lattice of
V
.
There exists
N_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
such that

z\nabla_{\partial_{z}}(G)\subset z^{-N_{2}}G
.
We set
G_{\mathfrak{a}}=G\cap E_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
We have

z\nabla_{\partial_{z}}G_{\mathfrak{a}}\subset z^{-N_{2}}G_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
Then, it is easy to check that

N_{2}\geq N_{1}
.
Thus, we obtain that the second claim implies the first.

Suppose that the second condition is satisfied.
Because

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma)={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(V,\nabla)
on

(X\setminus Z^{\prime},H\setminus Z^{\prime})
,
we obtain that the closure

\overline{\Sigma}
of

\Sigma
in

T^{\ast}(X\setminus Z^{\prime})(\log H^{\circ})(\sum m_{i}H_{i}^{\circ})

is proper over
X\setminus Z^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
2.7
,
the third condition is satisfied.
Conversely, if the third condition is satisfied,

\nabla(V_{0})\subset V_{0}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X^{\circ}}(\log H^{\circ})(\sum m_{i}H_{i}^{\circ})

holds on
X\setminus Z^{\prime}
.
By the Hartogs property,
the second condition is satisfied.

5.5
Universal integrable meromorphic relative connections

5.5.1
Universal meromorphic integrable relative connection of a given bundle

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
be a smooth projective morphism of

\mathbb{K}
-varieties
with relatively very ample line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(1)
.
Let
\mathcal{H}\subset\mathcal{X}
be a hypersurface of
\mathcal{X}

which is flat over
\mathcal{S}
.
For any
\mathbb{K}
-variety
\mathcal{S}_{1}
over
\mathcal{S}
,
we set
\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}_{1}

and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}=\mathcal{H}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}_{1}
.
For any coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
\mathcal{F}
flat over
\mathcal{S}
,
let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}
denotes the pull back of
\mathcal{F}

to
\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}
.

Proposition 5.21

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a locally free

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module of finite rank.
For any positive integer
N
,
there exists a complex variety over
\mathcal{S}_{1}
over
\mathcal{S}

such that the following holds.

•

The locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}}
-module

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}
is equipped with
an integrable meromorphic relative connection

\nabla:\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H})_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}.

•

Let
s\in\mathcal{S}
be any closed point.
Let
\nabla:\mathcal{V}_{s}\to\mathcal{V}_{s}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H})_{s}

be a meromorphic integrable connection.
Then, there exists a closed point

s_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
over
s

such that

(\mathcal{V}_{s},\nabla)\simeq(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},\nabla)_{s_{1}}
,
where

(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},\nabla)_{s_{1}}

denotes the restriction of

(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},\nabla)

to
(\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})_{s_{1}}
.

Proof
By considering a covering by affine open subsets of
\mathcal{S}
,
we may assume that
(i)
\mathcal{S}
is affine,
(ii) there exist mutually distinct smooth hypersurfaces
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,m)
obtained as sections of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\ell)

for some
\ell\geq 1
,
(iii)
\bigcap_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime}=\emptyset
.
We set
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=\bigcup\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime}
.
Let
N^{\prime}
be a sufficiently large positive integer.
Let
p_{\mathcal{S}}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
denote the projection.
We may assume that

R^{j}p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})\Bigr)=0\quad(i=1,\ldots,m),\quad R^{j}p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\Bigr)=0

for any
j>0
,
and

p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\Bigr)

and

p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{i})\Bigr)

(i=1,\ldots,m)

are locally free.
We may also assume that

R^{j}p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\otimes\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(2N\mathcal{H}+2N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\Bigr)=0
for any
j>0
,
and

p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\otimes\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(2N\mathcal{H}+2N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\Bigr)

is locally free.

Lemma 5.22

If
N^{\prime}
is sufficiently large,
there exist meromorphic relative connections

\nabla^{(i)}_{0}:\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}^{1}(N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime}),\quad\nabla^{(0)}_{0}:\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}^{1}(N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}),

which are not necessarily integrable.

Proof
Because
\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime}
is affine,
there exists a sufficiently large
M

such that
\mathcal{V}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})
is a direct summand of

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})^{\oplus M}
.
Let

\pi_{i}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})^{\oplus M}\to\mathcal{V}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})

denote the projection
with respect to a decomposition into a direct sum.
Let

j_{i}:\mathcal{V}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})^{\oplus M}

denote the inclusion.
Let
\nabla^{\prime(i)}_{0}
be the trivial connection
of the trivial bundle

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})^{\oplus M}
.
We obtain a connection

\nabla^{(i)}_{0}=\pi_{i}\circ\nabla^{\prime(i)}_{0}\circ j_{i}

of
\mathcal{V}(\ast\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})
.
Similarly,
we can construct
\nabla^{(0)}_{0}
.

Let
\nabla^{(i)}_{0}
and
\nabla^{(0)}_{0}

be meromorphic relative connections
as in Lemma
5.22
.
We regard

p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}^{1}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\Bigr)

as an affine variety
\mathcal{S}^{(0)}
over
\mathcal{S}
.
There exists the universal section
\Psi^{(0)}
of

\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}

on
\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}
.
By using
\nabla^{(0)}_{0}
and
\Psi^{(0)}
,
we obtain the meromorphic relative connection

\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}.

The following holds.

•

Let
s\in\mathcal{S}
be any closed point.
Let
\nabla:\mathcal{V}_{s}\to\mathcal{V}_{s}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})_{s}

be a meromorphic connection.
Then, there exists

s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}^{(0)}
over
s

such that

(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)\prime}},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)})_{s^{\prime}}\simeq(\mathcal{V}_{s},\nabla)
.

Similarly,
we regard

p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}^{1}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{i})\Bigr)

as an affine variety
\mathcal{S}^{(i)}
over
\mathcal{S}
.
There exists the universal section
\Psi^{(i)}
of

\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)}})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{i})_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)}}

on
\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)}}
.
By using
\nabla^{(i)}_{0}
and
\Psi^{(i)}
,
we obtain the meromorphic relative connection

\widetilde{\nabla}^{(i)}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{i})_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)}}.

The following holds.

•

Let
s\in\mathcal{S}
be any closed point.
Let
\nabla:\mathcal{V}_{s}\to\mathcal{V}_{s}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\prime})_{s}

be a meromorphic connection.
Then,
there exists

s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}^{(i)}

over
s

such that

(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)\prime}},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(i)})_{s^{\prime}}\simeq(\mathcal{V}_{s},\nabla)
.

We obtain the section

\widetilde{\nabla}^{(i)}-\nabla_{0}^{(0)}=(\nabla^{(i)}_{0}-\nabla^{(0)}_{0})+\Psi^{(i)}
of

\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)}})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H}+N\mathcal{H}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)}}
.
We obtain the induced closed embeddings

\mathcal{S}^{(i)}\to\mathcal{S}^{(0)}

such that the pull back of

(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)})

are isomorphic to

(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(i)}},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(i)})
.
Let
p_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}:\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}\to\mathcal{S}^{(0)}

denote the projection.
As the curvature,
we obtain
the section
(\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)})^{2}

of the locally free sheaf

p_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}\ast}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}})\otimes\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(2N\mathcal{H}+2N^{\prime}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}\Bigr).

Let
\mathcal{S}_{1}\subset\mathcal{S}^{(0)}
be the subscheme
as the intersection of

\mathcal{S}^{(i)}

(i=1,\ldots,m)

and the zero of
(\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)})^{2}
.
Let
(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},\nabla)
be
the pull back of

(\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)})
.
Then, it has the desired property.

5.5.2
Appendix: Universal extension

Let
\mathcal{V}_{i}
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules of finite rank
with an integrable meromorphic relative connection

\nabla_{i}:\mathcal{V}_{i}\to\mathcal{V}_{i}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}^{1}(N\mathcal{H}).

Proposition 5.23

There exist a
\mathbb{K}
-variety
\mathcal{S}_{1}
over
\mathcal{S}
,
a locally free

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}}
-module
\mathcal{V}_{3}

equipped with a relative integrable meromorphic connection

\nabla:\mathcal{V}_{3}\to\mathcal{V}_{3}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H})_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}

and the exact sequence of integrable meromorphic connections

0\longrightarrow(\mathcal{V}_{1},\nabla)_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{V}_{3},\nabla)\longrightarrow(\mathcal{V}_{2},\nabla)_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}\longrightarrow 0,

(32)

such that the following holds.

•

Let
s\in\mathcal{S}
.
Let
V
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}
-module
with an integrable meromorphic connection

\nabla:V\to V\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H})_{s}

with an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow(V_{1},\nabla)\longrightarrow(V,\nabla)\longrightarrow(V_{2},\nabla)\longrightarrow 0

(33)

such that

(V_{i},\nabla)\simeq(\mathcal{V}_{i},\nabla)_{s}
.
Then,
there exists

s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
over
s

the pull back of
(
32
)

to
s^{\prime}

is isomorphic to
(
33
)
.

Proof
By setting

\mathcal{C}^{j}={\mathcal{H}om}(\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{1})\otimes\Omega^{j}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(jN\mathcal{H})
,
we obtain the complex

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{X}
.
By taking an affine open covering of
\mathcal{S}
,
we may assume that
\mathcal{S}
is smooth and affine.
We may also assume that there exist
mutually distinct hypersurfaces
\mathcal{H}_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,m)
of
\mathcal{X}

such that
(i)
\mathcal{H}_{i}
are smooth over
\mathcal{S}

and obtained as the zero of
sections of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(\ell)
for some
\ell>0
,
(ii)
\bigcap\mathcal{H}_{i}=\emptyset
.
We set
\mathcal{H}(I)=\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathcal{H}_{i}

for any
I\subset\{1,\ldots,m\}
.
For any non-empty
I
,
we may assume that

R^{k}p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{j}\otimes\mathcal{O}((j+1)\mathcal{H}(I))\bigr)=0

(k>0)
,
and that

p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{j}\otimes\mathcal{O}((j+1)\mathcal{H}(I))\bigr)

are locally free.

We set

U=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}{\mathbb{C}}e_{i}
.
For an ordered subset

I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{p}\}\subset\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
we set

e_{I}=e_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{i_{p}}
.
We set
U_{I}={\mathbb{C}}e_{I}\subset\bigwedge^{|I|}U
.
The multiplication of
e_{i}

induces

\varphi_{i}:U_{I}\to U_{I\sqcup\{i\}}
.
Together with the natural inclusion

p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{j}\otimes\mathcal{O}((j+1)\mathcal{H}(I))\bigr)\to p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{j}\otimes\mathcal{O}((j+1)\mathcal{H}(I\cup\{i\}))\bigr)
,
we obtain

p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{j}\otimes\mathcal{O}((j+1)\mathcal{H}(I))\bigr)\otimes U_{I}\longrightarrow p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{j}\otimes\mathcal{O}((j+1)\mathcal{H}(I\cup\{i\}))\bigr)\otimes U_{I\cup\{i\}}.

We set

\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{j,p}=\bigoplus_{|I|=p}p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{j}\otimes\mathcal{O}((j+1)\mathcal{H}(I))\bigr)\otimes U_{I}.

We obtain

\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{j,p}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{j,p+1}
.
We obtain the morphisms

p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{j}\otimes\mathcal{O}((j+1)\mathcal{H}(I))\bigr)\to p_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{j+1}\otimes\mathcal{O}((j+2)\mathcal{H}(I))\bigr)

induced by the integrable meromorphic relative connections,
and hence

\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{j,p}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{j+1,p}
.
Thus, we obtain a double complex

\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
.
Let
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}

denote the total complex.
We may regard

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{i}
are vector bundles
on
\mathcal{S}_{0}
,
and
we have the morphisms

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{i}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{i+1}
.

We regard
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
as a variety
\mathcal{S}^{(0)}
over
\mathcal{S}
.
Let
\pi_{1}:\mathcal{S}^{(0)}\to\mathcal{S}
denote the projection.
We obtain the section
\Psi
of

\pi_{1}^{\ast}(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{2})
on
\mathcal{S}_{0}

induced by
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{2}
.
Let
\mathcal{S}_{1}\subset\mathcal{S}_{0}

denote the subscheme obtained as
\Psi^{-1}(0)
.

For
i\neq j
,
we have the morphisms

\psi_{i,j}:\mathcal{V}_{2,\mathcal{S}_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{1,\mathcal{S}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{H}_{i}\cup\mathcal{H}_{j})_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}.

For
i
,
we have the morphisms

\phi_{i}:\mathcal{V}_{2,\mathcal{S}_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{1,\mathcal{S}_{1}}\otimes\bigl(\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}}(N\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathcal{O}(2\mathcal{H}_{i})\bigr)_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}.

They satisfy the cocycle condition:

\psi_{i,j}+\psi_{j,k}=\psi_{i,k},\quad\nabla_{1}\circ\psi_{i,j}-\psi_{i,j}\circ\nabla_{2}+\phi_{i}-\phi_{j}=0,\quad\nabla_{1}\circ\phi_{i}+\phi_{i}\circ\nabla_{2}=0.

We obtain the isomorphisms

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}&\psi_{i,j}\\
0&\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{V}_{2}}\end{array}\right):\Bigl((\mathcal{V}_{1,\mathcal{S}_{1}}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2,\mathcal{S}_{1}})_{|(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{j})_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}}\Bigr)_{|(\mathcal{X}\setminus(\mathcal{H}_{i}\cup\mathcal{H}_{j}))_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}}\simeq\Bigl((\mathcal{V}_{1,\mathcal{S}_{1}}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2,\mathcal{S}_{1}})_{|(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{i})_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}}\Bigr)_{|(\mathcal{X}\setminus(\mathcal{H}_{i}\cup\mathcal{H}_{j}))_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}}.

(34)

They satisfy the cocycle conditions.
By patching

(\mathcal{V}_{1,\mathcal{S}_{1}}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2,\mathcal{S}_{1}})_{|(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{j})_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}}

via the isomorphisms (
34
),
we obtain a vector bundle
\mathcal{V}_{3}

on
\mathcal{S}_{1}
.
The integrable meromorphic relative connections
\nabla
of

(\mathcal{V}_{i})_{|\mathcal{S}_{3}\times(X\setminus H_{i})}

and
\phi_{i}
induces
an integrable meromorphic relative connections of

(\mathcal{V}_{1,\mathcal{S}_{1}}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2,\mathcal{S}_{1}})_{|(\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{j})_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}}
.
By patching them,
we obtain
a integrable meromorphic relative connection of
\mathcal{V}_{3}
.
There exists the exact sequence (
32
).
The desired conditions are satisfied by the construction.

5.6
Estimate of maximal slopes in terms of slopes

5.6.1
Slopes

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
X
be an
n
-dimensional smooth connected projective

\mathbb{K}
-variety
with an ample line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)
.
We refer
[
3
]

for the intersection theory and
the characteristic classes of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
in the Chow group of
X
.
We set
\omega=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(1))
.
For any torsion-free coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
V
,
we set

\deg_{\omega}(V)=\int_{X}c_{1}(V)\omega^{n-1}

and

\mu_{\omega}(V)=\deg_{\omega}(V)/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)
.
There exists the Harder-Narasimhan filtration
\mathcal{F}^{HN}
of
V

with respect to the slope
\mu_{\omega}
,
i.e.,
there exists a unique decreasing filtration
\mathcal{F}^{HN}
of
V

indexed by
{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
(i)
\mathcal{F}^{HN}_{a}(V)=0
and
\mathcal{F}^{HN}_{a}(V)=V

for any sufficiently large
a>0
,
(ii)
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}^{HN}}_{a}(V)
are
\mu_{\omega}
-semistable sheaves
with
\mu\bigl(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{{\mathcal{F}}^{HN}}_{a}(V)\bigr)=a
.
We set

\mu_{\omega,\max}(V)=\max\bigl\{a\,\big|\,\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}^{HN}}_{a}(V)\neq 0\bigr\},\quad\mu_{\omega,\min}(V)=\min\bigl\{a\,\big|\,\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}^{HN}}_{a}(V)\neq 0\bigr\}.

Lemma 5.24

Let
V_{i}

(i=1,2)
be torsion-free coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
with a non-zero morphism
\varphi:V_{1}\to V_{2}
.
Suppose that
V_{1}
is
\mu_{\omega}
-semistable.
Then, we obtain
\mu_{\omega}(V_{1})\leq\mu_{\omega,\max}(V_{2})
.

Proof
Let
\mathcal{F}^{HN}(V_{2})
be a Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
V_{2}
.
There exists
b(0)
such that

\varphi(V_{1})\subset\mathcal{F}^{HN}_{b(0)}(V_{2})

and
\varphi(V_{1})\not\subset\mathcal{F}^{HN}_{<b(0)}(V_{2})
.
We obtain a non-zero morphism

\varphi_{1}:V_{1}\to\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{b(0)}(V_{2})
.
Because

V_{1}
and
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{b(0)}(V_{2})

are
\mu_{\omega}
-semistable,
we obtain

\mu_{\omega}(V_{1})\leq\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\varphi_{1})\leq\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{b(0)}(V_{2}))\leq\mu_{\omega,\max}(V_{2}).

Thus, we are done.

Lemma 5.25

Let
V
be a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
with an increasing filtration
F_{j}(V)

(j=0,\ldots,m)

by coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodules
such that
(i)
F_{0}(V)=0
and
F_{m}(V)=V
,
(ii)
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j}(V)
are torsion-free.
Then, we have

\min_{1\leq j\leq m}\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V))\leq\mu_{\omega}(V)\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq m}\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V)).

Proof
If
m=1
, the claim is trivial.
Suppose
m\geq 2
.
Either one of the following holds.

•

\mu_{\omega}(\mathcal{F}_{m-1}(V))=\mu_{\omega}(V)=\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{m}(V))
.

•

\mu_{\omega}(\mathcal{F}_{m-1}(V))\leq\mu_{\omega}(V)\leq\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{m}(V))
.

•

\mu_{\omega}(\mathcal{F}_{m-1}(V))\geq\mu_{\omega}(V)\geq\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{m}(V))
.

Then, we obtain the claim of the lemma by an induction on
m
.

5.6.2
Estimate of maximal slopes in terms of slopes

For a sufficiently large integer
M_{1}
,
there exists a monomorphism

\Omega_{X}^{1}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}(M_{1})^{n}
.
We set
M_{2}=\deg_{\omega}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(M_{1}))
.
Let
H
be a normal crossing hypersurface.
Let
N
be any positive integer.

Lemma 5.26

Let
V_{i}

(i=1,2)

be torsion-free coherent
\mu_{\omega}
-semistable
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
with a non-zero morphism

V_{1}\to V_{2}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(NH)
.
Then,
we obtain

\mu_{\omega}(V_{1})\leq\mu_{\omega}(V_{2})+M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H)

Proof
There exists a non-zero morphism

V_{1}\to V_{2}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(M_{1})
.
Because
V_{1}
and
V_{2}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(M_{1})

are
\mu_{\omega}
-semistable,
we obtain

\mu_{\omega}(V_{1})\leq\mu_{\omega}(V_{2}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(M_{1}))=\mu_{\omega}(V_{2})+M_{2}
.

Proposition 5.27

Let
V
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
equipped with a meromorphic connection

\nabla:V\to V\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(NH)

such that

(V,\nabla)
is irreducible,
i.e.,
there is no saturated
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule
V^{\prime}
of
V

such that
\nabla(V^{\prime})\subset V^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{X}^{1}(NH)

with
0<\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V^{\prime})<\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)
.
Then, we obtain

\mu_{\omega,\max}(V)-\mu_{\omega,\min}(V)\leq\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)\cdot\bigl(M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H)\bigr).

As a result, we obtain

\mu_{\omega,\max}(V)\leq\mu_{\omega}(V)+\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)\cdot\bigl(M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H)\bigr).

Proof
Let
(V,\nabla)
be as in the statement of the proposition.
If
V
is a
\mu_{\omega}
-semisimple sheaf,

\mu_{\omega,\max}(V)-\mu_{\omega,\min}(V)=0
.
Suppose that
V
is not
\mu_{\omega}
-semistable.
We set

V_{a}=\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}^{HN}}_{a}(V)
.
Let
a_{0}>a_{1}>\cdots>a_{m}

be the set

\{a\,|\,V_{a}\neq 0\}
.
We have
a_{0}=\mu_{\omega,\max}(V)

and
a_{m}=\mu_{\omega,\min}(V)
.
We set
i(0)=0
.

Lemma 5.28

There exists
a strictly increasing sequence

i(0)<i(1)<\cdots<i(q)=m

and

k(p)\leq i(p-1)

(p=1,\ldots,q)

such that the following holds:

a_{k(p)}\leq a_{i(p)}+M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H).

(35)

Proof
Because
(V,\nabla)
is irreducible,
the induced morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

\mathcal{F}_{a_{0}}\to(V/\mathcal{F}_{a_{0}})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(NH)

is non-trivial.
There exist
0<i(1)\leq m

and a non-zero morphism

V_{a_{0}}\to V_{a_{i(1)}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(NH)
.
We set

k(1)=0
.
We obtain

a_{k(1)}\leq a_{i(1)}+M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}H.

Suppose we have already constructed

0=i(0)<\cdots<i(s)<m

and
k(p)\leq i(p-1)

(p=1,\ldots,s)

such that (
35
) holds.
Because
(V,\nabla)
is irreducible,
the induced morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

\mathcal{F}_{a_{i(s)}}\to(V/\mathcal{F}_{a_{i(s)}})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(NH)

is non-trivial.
There exist

k(s+1)\leq i(s)<i(s+1)

and a non-zero morphism

V_{a_{k(s+1)}}\to V_{a_{i(s+1)}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(NH)
.
It implies that

a_{k(s+1)}\leq a_{i(s+1)}+M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H)
.
Hence, we obtain the claim of Lemma
5.28

by an induction.

For
p=1,\ldots,q
, we obtain

a_{i(p-1)}\leq a_{k(p)}\leq a_{i(p)}+M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H).

We obtain

a_{0}\leq a_{m}+q(M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H))\leq a_{m}+\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)(M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H))
.
Thus, we obtain Proposition
5.27

in the case that
V
is not
\mu_{\omega}
-semistable.

Corollary 5.29

Let
V
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
with a meromorphic connection

\nabla:V\to V\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(NH)

and an increasing filtration
F_{j}(V)

(j=0,\ldots,m)

by
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodules
such that
(i)
F_{0}(V)=0
and
F_{m}(V)=V
,
(ii)
\nabla(F_{j}(V))\subset F_{j}(V)\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(NH)
,
(iii)
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j}(V):=F_{j}(V)/F_{j-1}(V)

(j=1,\ldots,m)
are locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.
Then, we obtain

\mu_{\omega,\max}(V)\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq m}\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j}(V))+\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)(M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H)).

(36)

If moreover there exist constants
A_{i}

(i=1,2)
such that

A_{1}\leq\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V))\leq A_{2}

(j=1,\ldots,m)
,
we obtain

\mu_{\omega,\max}(V)\leq\mu_{\omega}(V)+\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)(M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H))+A_{2}-A_{1}.

(37)

Proof
By Proposition
5.27
,
we obtain

\mu_{\omega,\max}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j}(V))\leq\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j}(V))+\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j}(V)(M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H)).

(38)

Let
\mathcal{F}^{HN}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j}(V)
denote the Harder-Narasimhan filtration
of
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j}(V)
.
Let
\mathcal{F}^{HN}(V)
denote the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
V
.
We set
a(0)=\mu_{\omega,\max}(V)
.
There exists
j(0)
such that

\mathcal{F}^{HN}_{a(0)}(V)\subset F_{j(0)}(V)

and

\mathcal{F}^{HN}_{a(0)}(V)\not\subset F_{j(0)-1}(V)
.
We obtain the non-zero morphism

\mathcal{F}^{HN}_{a(0)}(V)\to\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j(0)}(V)
.
By Lemma
5.24
,
we obtain

a(0)\leq\mu_{\omega,\max}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{j(0)}(V))
.
Then, we obtain (
36
) from (
38
).
We obtain (
37
)
from (
36
)
and Lemma
5.25
.

5.7
Hilbert polynomials

5.7.1
Hilbert polynomials of torsion-free sheaves

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
X
be an
n
-dimensional projective smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with an ample line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)
.
For any torsion-free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{F}
,
let
P_{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathbb{Q}}[t]
denote the Hilbert polynomial of
\mathcal{F}
,
i.e.,

P_{\mathcal{F}}(m)

equals
the Euler number of
\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(m)

for any
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
i.e.,

P_{\mathcal{F}}(m)=\int_{X}\mathop{\rm td}\nolimits(X)\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits(\mathcal{F})\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits(\mathcal{O}_{X}(m)).

There exist integers
a_{i}(\mathcal{F})

(i=0,\ldots,n)

such that

P_{\mathcal{F}}(m)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}a_{i}(\mathcal{F})\left(\begin{array}[]{c}m+n-1\\
n-i\end{array}\right).

We have
a_{0}(\mathcal{F})=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{F})\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(1))^{n}
.

5.7.2
Maruyama’s boundedness Theorem

Let
\mathcal{X}_{S}\to S
be a smooth projective morphism of
smooth complex algebraic varieties
with a relatively ample line bundle
\mathcal{O}(1)
.
Let
r
be a positive integer,
let
a_{1},a_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let
M>0
.
Let
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{s},r,a_{1},a_{2},M)

denote the category of
torsion-free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}
-sheaves
E

satisfying the following conditions:

\mu_{\max}(E)<\mu(E)+M,\quad\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(E)=r,\quad|a_{1}(E)|\leq a_{1},\quad a_{2}(E)\geq a_{2}.

Proposition 5.30
(Maruyama

[
20
]
)

The family
(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{s},r,a_{1},a_{2},M)\,|\,s\in S({\mathbb{C}}))

is bounded in the following sense.

•

There exists a morphism of smooth complex varieties

\mathcal{S}\to S
.

•

There exists a coherent

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{S}}}
-module
\mathcal{E}

flat over
\mathcal{S}
.

•

For any
b\in S({\mathbb{C}})

and
E\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{s},r,a_{1},a_{2},M)
,
there exists

\widetilde{b}\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{C}})
over
b

such that

\mathcal{E}_{\widetilde{b}}\simeq E
.

Remark 5.31

This was generalized by Langer
to the positive characteristic case.
See
[
18
]
.

5.8
Estimates for some lattices

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
X
be an
n
-dimensional projective smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with an ample line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)
.
We set
\omega=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(1))
.
Let
H=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda}H_{j}

be a simple normal crossing hypersurface of
X
.
By the intersection theory
[
3
]
,
we obtain the following numbers
associated with
(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(1))
:

\int_{X}\omega^{n},\quad\int_{X}c_{1}(X)\omega^{n-1},\quad\int_{X}c_{1}(X)^{2}\omega^{n-2},\quad\int_{X}c_{2}(X)\omega^{n-2},\quad

(39)

We also obtain the following additional numbers
associated with
(X,H,\mathcal{O}_{X}(1))
:

\int_{X}c_{1}(X)[H_{i}]\omega^{n-2},\quad\int_{X}[H_{i}]\omega^{n-1},\quad\int_{X}[H_{i}]\cdot[H_{j}]\omega^{n-2}.

(40)

Proposition 5.32

There exist
a constant
A>0
depending only on

\int_{X}[H_{i}]\omega^{n-1}

(i\in\Lambda)
,
and a constant
C(r)>0

depending on a positive number
r

and the numbers
(
39
,
40
)

such that the following holds.

•

Let
(V,\nabla)
be any good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)

with
\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)\leq r
.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be a filtration of
(V,\nabla)
.
Then, there exists a good lattice
V_{0}
of
V

such that
(i)
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V_{0})
are locally free,
(ii)
0\leq\mu_{\omega}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}V_{0})\leq A
,
(iii)

|a_{1}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V_{0}))|+|a_{2}(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V_{0}))|\leq C(r)
,
(iv)
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V_{0})
generates

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V)
as a
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module,
i.e.,

\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V_{0})=\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V)
.

Proof
Let us consider the case
\mathbb{K}={\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\subset V
denote the good Deligne-Malgrange lattice
of
(V,\nabla)
.
By the Riemann-Roch formula,
we have

P_{V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}}(m)=m^{n}\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)\int_{X}\omega^{n}+m^{n-1}\int_{X}\bigl(c_{1}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})+\mathop{\rm td}\nolimits_{1}(X)\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})\bigr)\omega^{n-1}\\
+m^{n-2}\int_{X}\Bigl(\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})+c_{1}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})\mathop{\rm td}\nolimits_{1}(X)+\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})\mathop{\rm td}\nolimits_{2}(X)\Bigr)\omega^{n-2}+O(m^{n-3}).

(41)

We may assume that
\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)
is very ample.
There exists a smooth surface
Z

obtained as the complete intersection of

(n-2)
-sections of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)

such that

H_{Z}=Z\cap H
is normal crossing in
Z
.
The restriction

(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits},\nabla)_{|Z}

is the good Deligne-Malgrange lattice
of
(V,\nabla)_{|Z}
.
By Proposition
4.1
,
there exists
C_{1}>0

depending only on

\int_{X}[H_{i}]\omega^{n-1}

such that

\left|\int_{X}c_{1}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})\omega^{n-1}\right|\leq\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)C_{1}.

There also exists
C_{2}>0

depending only on

\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)
,

\int_{X}[H_{i}]\omega^{n-1}
,

\int_{X}[H_{i}]c_{1}(X)\omega^{n-2}

and

\int_{X}[H_{i}]\cdot[H_{j}]\omega^{n-2}

such that

\left|\int_{X}c_{1}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})c_{1}(X)\omega^{n-2}\right|+\left|\int_{X}c_{1}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})^{2}\omega^{n-2}\right|+\left|\int_{X}c_{2}(V^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits})\omega^{n-2}\right|\leq C_{2}.

Then, we obtain the desired estimate in the case
\mathbb{K}={\mathbb{C}}
.

Let us study the general case.
It is enough to consider the case

\mathbb{K}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
.
We consider the splitting
\tau:\mathbb{K}/{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{K}

induced by the bijection

\{a\in\mathbb{K}\,|\,0\leq\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits(a)<1\}\simeq\mathbb{K}/{\mathbb{Z}}
.
By
[
15
, Theorem 5.3.4]
,
there exists the good Deligne-Malgrange lattice

V_{0}\subset V

associated with the choice
\tau
.
It induces the good Deligne-Malgrange lattice of
the complexification
(V_{{\mathbb{C}}},\nabla)

on
X\times{\mathbb{C}}
.
We also note that

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V_{0})

are good Deligne-Malgrange lattices
of
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(V,\nabla)
.
Hence,

V_{0}
satisfies the desired conditions.

6
Boundedness as meromorphic objects

6.1
Families of formal meromorphic flat bundles
and formal
\mathcal{D}
-modules

6.1.1
Families of good formal meromorphic flat bundles

Let
R
be a regular Henselian local ring over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}
be the maximal ideal.
We have
\mathbb{K}\simeq R/\mathfrak{m}
.

We set
R_{1}=R[\![x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]\!]

and
\widetilde{R}_{1}=R_{1}(\ast x_{1}\ldots x_{\ell})
.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a finitely generated reflexive
\widetilde{R}_{1}
-module
with an integrable connection

\nabla:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{R_{1}/R}
.
Such
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

is called a formal meromorphic integrable connection
over
\widetilde{R}_{1}
relative to
R
.
A lattice
E
of
\mathcal{E}
means
a finitely generated
R_{1}
-submodule
such that
E\otimes\widetilde{R}_{1}=\mathcal{E}
.

Definition 6.1

A lattice
E
of
\mathcal{E}
is called logarithmic with respect to
\nabla

if

\nabla E\subset\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell}E\otimes\bigl(dx_{i}/x_{i}\bigr)\oplus\bigoplus_{i=\ell+1}^{n}E\otimes dx_{i}.

We say that
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is regular
if there exists a logarithmic lattice.

For any
f\in\widetilde{R}_{1}
,
we have
df\in\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{R}_{1}/R}
.

Definition 6.2

A lattice
E
of
\mathcal{E}
is called unramifiedly good
if there exist a good set
\mathcal{I}\subset\widetilde{R}_{1}/R_{1}

and a decomposition

(E,\nabla)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}(E_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}})

such that

(E_{\mathfrak{a}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}})

are logarithmic.
Here,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\in\widetilde{R}_{1}
denote lifts of
\mathfrak{a}
.
We say that
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is unramifiedly good
if there exists an unramifiedly good lattice.

For any positive integer
e
,
we set
\widetilde{R}_{1}^{(e)}=\widetilde{R}_{1}[x_{1}^{1/e},\ldots,x_{\ell}^{1/e}]
.
We say

\mathcal{E}^{(e)}=\mathcal{E}\otimes\widetilde{R}_{1}^{(e)}
.
It is naturally equipped with an integrable connection

\nabla^{(e)}
relative to
R
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}
denote the Galois group of
the extension
\widetilde{R}_{1}^{(e)}/\widetilde{R}_{1}
.
We have the natural
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}
-action on

\mathcal{E}^{(e)}
.

Definition 6.3

We say that

(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is good
if
(\mathcal{E}^{(e)},\nabla^{(e)})
is unramifiedly good
for some
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
.

Definition 6.4

When
\mathcal{E}
is good,
we say that a lattice
E
of
\mathcal{E}
is good if
there exists a
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}
-invariant unramifiedly good lattice

E^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{E}^{(e)}

such that
E
is the descent of
E^{\prime}
.

6.1.2
The
V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}
-submodules and

\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}
-submodules associated with lattices

We set
\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}=R_{1}\langle\partial_{1},\ldots,\partial_{n}\rangle

and

V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}=R_{1}\langle x_{1}\partial_{1},\ldots,x_{\ell}\partial_{\ell},\partial_{\ell+1},\ldots,\partial_{n}\rangle
.
Any meromorphic integrable connection
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

over
R_{1}
relative to
R

is naturally regarded as a
V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}
-module
or a
\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}
-module.

Let
E
be a lattice of
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
.
We obtain the
V_{\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}}
-submodule

V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}\cdot E\subset\mathcal{E}
.
We set

\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}(E):=\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}\otimes_{V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}}\bigl(V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}\cdot E\bigr).

For
E\subset E^{\prime}
,
we obtain
\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}
-homomorphism

\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}(E)\to\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}(E^{\prime})
.

Proposition 6.5

Suppose that
E
is good.

•

V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}\cdot E

and
\mathcal{E}/(V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}\cdot E)
are flat over
R
.
There exists a natural isomorphism

(V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}\cdot E)\otimes_{R}\mathbb{K}\simeq V\mathcal{D}_{\mathbb{K}[\![x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]\!]/\mathbb{K}}\cdot(E\otimes_{R}\mathbb{K}).

(42)

•

\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}(E)
is flat over
R
.
There exists the following natural isomorphism

\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}(E)\otimes_{R}\mathbb{K}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\mathbb{K}[\![x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]\!]/\mathbb{K}}(E\otimes\mathbb{K}).

Proof
The second and third claims follow from the first.
Let us study the first.
Let us consider the case where
E
is unramifiedly good.
There exist a good set
\mathcal{I}
and a decomposition

E=\bigoplus E_{\mathfrak{a}}

as in Definition
6.2
.
For each
\mathfrak{a}
,
let
I(\mathfrak{a})

denote the set of
1\leq i\leq\ell

such that
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a})_{i}\neq 0
.
It is easy to see that

V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}\cdot E_{\mathfrak{a}}=E_{\mathfrak{a}}(\ast x^{I(\mathfrak{a})})
.
We may assume that

I(\mathfrak{a})=\{1,\ldots,k\}
for some
k
.
Let
\mathcal{I}(k)
denote the set of
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}

such that

I(\mathfrak{a})=\{1,\ldots,k\}
.
We set

E_{k}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(k)}E_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
We obtain the decomposition

E=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{\ell}E_{k}.

(43)

Then, we have

V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}E=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{\ell}E_{k}(\ast x_{1}\cdots x_{k}).

(44)

From this description,
we obtain that

V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}E
and

\mathcal{E}/V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}E
are flat over
R
.
We also obtain the isomorphism (
42
).

Suppose that
E
is good.
There exists a
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}
-invariant unramifiedly good lattice

E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}^{(e)}

such that
E
is the Galois descent of
E^{\prime}
.
We have the decomposition

E^{\prime}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{\ell}E^{\prime}_{k}

(45)

as in (
43
).
The decomposition (
45
)
is
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits_{e}
-equivariant,
Let
E_{k}
denote the Galois descent of
E_{k}^{\prime}
.
We obtain the decomposition

E=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{\ell}E_{k}.

(46)

It is easy to see that

V\mathcal{D}_{R_{1}/R}\cdot E=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{\ell}E_{k}(\ast x_{1}\cdots x_{k})
.
As a result,
we obtain Proposition
6.5
.

6.1.3
Complement

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a free
\widetilde{R}_{1}
-module.
Let
E
be an
R_{1}
-lattice of
\mathcal{E}
.
Let
\mathcal{I}\subset\widetilde{R}_{1}/R_{1}
be a good set of irregular values.
Let
\mathbb{K}_{1}
be an algebraic closure of the fractional field of
R
.
We set
\mathfrak{R}_{1}=\mathbb{K}_{1}[\![x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]\!]

and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}=\mathfrak{R}_{1}(\ast x_{1}\cdots x_{\ell})
.
By the extensions
R_{1}\to\mathfrak{R}_{1}

and
\widetilde{R}_{1}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}
,
we obtain

\mathfrak{E}=\mathfrak{R}_{1}\otimes E

and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{E}
.
It is standard to obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 6.6

Suppose that there exists the decomposition

\mathfrak{E}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\mathfrak{E}_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that

(\nabla-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits)\mathfrak{E}_{\mathfrak{a}}\subset\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathfrak{E}_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes(dx_{i}/x_{i})\oplus\bigoplus_{i=\ell+1}^{n}\mathfrak{E}_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes dx_{i}.

Then, there exists a decomposition

E=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}E_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that

(\nabla-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits)E_{\mathfrak{a}}\subset\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell}E_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes(dx_{i}/x_{i})\oplus\bigoplus_{i=\ell+1}^{n}E_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes dx_{i}.

6.2
Good families of meromorphic flat connections

6.2.1
Families of meromorphic flat connections

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
U=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(S)
be a smooth affine
\mathbb{K}
-scheme.
Let
X
be a normal
\mathbb{K}
-variety with a morphism

X\to U
such that
(i)
X
is projective and flat over
U
,
(ii) each geometric fiber of
X\to U
is irreducible and normal.
Let
H\subset X
be a hypersurface flat over
S
.
We assume that
X^{\circ}=X\setminus H
is smooth over
U
.
For any geometric point
u:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}_{1})\to U
,
we set
(X_{u},H_{u})=(X,H)\times_{U}\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}_{1})
.

Let
j:X^{\circ}\to X
denote the inclusion.
We have the relative exterior derivative

d:j_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\circ}}\to j_{\ast}(\Omega^{1}_{X^{\circ}/U})
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a reflexive coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module
with an integrable relative connection

\nabla:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}\otimes j_{\ast}\Omega^{1}_{X^{\circ}/U}

such that
\mathcal{E}
is flat over
S
.
Such
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is called
a meromorphic flat connection on
(X,H)
relative to
U
.
If
\mathcal{E}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module,

(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is called a meromorphic flat bundle
on
(X,H)
relative to
U
.

For each geometric point
u:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}_{1})\to U
,
we obtain the coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X_{u}}(\ast H_{u})
-module
\mathcal{E}_{u}

equipped with an integrable connection
\nabla_{u}
.

Lemma 6.7

\mathcal{E}_{|X^{\circ}}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\circ}}
-module.

Proof
Because
\mathcal{E}_{u|X_{u}\setminus H_{u}}
are flat

\mathcal{O}_{X_{u}\setminus H_{u}}
-modules for any closed point,
we obtain that
\mathcal{E}_{|X^{\circ}}
is flat,
i.e., locally free.

6.2.2
Families of good meromorphic flat bundles

Suppose that
X
is smooth
and that
X\to U
is smooth.
We also assume that
(i)
H
is normal crossing relative to
S
,
(ii) the monodromy is trivial.

Let
x\in H
be a closed point.
Let
n=\dim X
.
There exists a parameter system

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
such that
the ideal of
H
at
x
is generated by

\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}
for some
\ell
.

Let
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
be a meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)

relative to
U
.
We set

\mathcal{V}_{|\widehat{x}}=\mathcal{V}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
.
We obtain the induced integrable relative connection

\nabla:\mathcal{V}_{|\widehat{x}}\to\mathcal{V}_{|\widehat{x}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X/U}
.

Definition 6.8

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is called regular
(resp. good, unramifiedly good) at
a closed point
x\in H

if

(\mathcal{V}_{|\widehat{x}},\nabla)
is
regular
(resp. good, unramifiedly good).
We say that
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is
good (resp. regular, unramifiedly good) on
(X,H)

if
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)_{|\widehat{x}}
is
good (resp. regular, unramifiedly good) at any closed point
x\in H
.

6.2.3
Etale coordinate systems

An etale coordinate neighbourhood
of
x\in X
relative to
U

is a tuple

(\mathcal{U},x^{\prime},\varphi,\psi)

of a smooth affine
\mathbb{K}
-variety
\mathcal{U}
,
a closed point
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
,
an etale morphism
\varphi:(\mathcal{U},x^{\prime})\to(X,x)
,
and an etale morphism

\psi:\mathcal{U}\to U\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}[x_{1}\ldots,x_{n}])

satisfying the following conditions.

•

H_{\mathcal{U}}:=\varphi^{-1}(H)
equals

\psi^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{z_{i}=0\}\bigr)

for some
1\leq\ell\leq n
.

•

\psi(x^{\prime})\in U\times(0,\ldots,0)
.

•

\psi^{-1}(U\times\{z_{i}=0\})

are connected.

For
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
there exists the ramified covering

U\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}[x_{1}^{1/e},\ldots,x_{\ell}^{1/e},x_{\ell+1},\ldots,x_{n}])\to U\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]).

Let
\mathcal{U}^{(e)}
denote the fiber product of

\mathcal{U}
and

U\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}[x_{1}^{1/e},\ldots,x_{\ell}^{1/e},x_{\ell+1},\ldots,x_{n}])

over
U\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}])
.
Let
\varphi_{\mathcal{U},e}:\mathcal{U}^{(e)}\to X
denote the induced morphism.
We set

H_{\mathcal{U}}^{(e)}=\varphi_{\mathcal{U},e}^{-1}(H_{\mathcal{U}})
.

Lemma 6.9

Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle
on
(X,H)
relative to
U
.
Let
\{(\mathcal{U}_{i},\varphi_{i},\psi_{i})\}_{i=1,\ldots,m}

be an etale covering
of
X
by etale coordinate systems.
Then,
there exists
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

such that

\varphi_{\mathcal{U}_{i},e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)_{|\mathcal{U}_{i}}

is unramifiedly good
on
(\mathcal{U}_{i}^{(e)},H_{\mathcal{U}_{i}}^{(e)})
.

6.2.4
Good lattices

Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle
on
(X,H)
relative to
U
.
Let
E\subset\mathcal{E}
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule
such that
\mathcal{E}=E(\ast H)
.

Definition 6.10

Suppose that
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is unramifiedly good.
Then,
E
is called unramifiedly good if
the following holds for any
x\in H
.

•

We have the decomposition

E_{|\widehat{x}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{E},\nabla,x)}\widehat{E}_{x,\mathfrak{a}}

such that

\widehat{E}_{x,\mathfrak{a}}=(\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{x,\mathfrak{a}}\cap E_{|\widehat{x}})
,
and

(\nabla-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}})\widehat{E}_{x,\mathfrak{a}}\subset\widehat{E}_{x,\mathfrak{a}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X/U}(\log H)
.

Definition 6.11

Let
\{(\mathcal{U}_{i},\varphi_{i},\psi_{i})\}_{i=1,\ldots,m}

and
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

be as above.
We say
E
is a good lattice of
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

if there exist unramifiedly good lattices

E_{e,i}
of
\varphi_{e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

such that
(i)
E_{e,i}
is invariant under the Galois group of

\mathcal{U}_{i}^{(e)}/\mathcal{U}_{i}
,
(ii) the Galois descent of
E_{e,i}
equals
\varphi_{i}^{\ast}E
.

6.2.5
Decompositions into the regular part and the irregular part

Let
(V,\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)

relative to
U
.
Let
E
be a good lattice.
Let
x\in H_{k}
.
We have the natural map

\rho_{k}:\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,e)}_{X}(\ast H)_{x}\big/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H,e)}_{X}(\ast H_{\neq k})_{x}

Let
\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)_{k,0}=\rho_{k}^{-1}(0)
.
We set

(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits,k}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)_{k,0}}(E_{|\widehat{x}}\cap V^{(H,e)}_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}}),\quad\quad(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\not\in\mathcal{I}(V,\nabla,x)_{k,0}}(E_{|\widehat{x}}\cap V^{(H,e)}_{\widehat{x},\mathfrak{a}}).

We obtain the decomposition

E_{|\widehat{x}}=(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits,k}\oplus(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}.

(47)

It is easy to check the following

\nabla(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits,k}\subset(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits,k}\otimes\Bigl(\Omega^{1}_{X/U}(\log H_{k})+\Omega^{1}_{X/U}(\ast H_{\neq k})\Bigr),\quad\quad\nabla(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}\subset(E_{|\widehat{x}})^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits,k}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X/U}(\ast H).

(48)

The following proposition is similar to
Proposition
5.6
.

Proposition 6.12

There exists the decomposition

E_{|\widehat{H}_{k}}=E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\oplus E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}

(49)

which induces
(
47
)

for any
x\in H_{k}
.
Moreover, we have

\nabla(E_{\widehat{H}}^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits})\subset E_{\widehat{H}}^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\otimes\Bigl(\Omega^{1}_{X/U}(\log H_{k})+\Omega^{1}_{X/U}(\ast H_{\neq k})\Bigr)

and

\nabla(E_{\widehat{H}}^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits})\subset E_{\widehat{H}}^{\mathop{\rm irreg}\nolimits}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X/U}(\ast H)\Bigr)
.

By (
48
),
we obtain the endomorphism

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

of
(E^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}_{\widehat{H}_{k}|H_{k}})_{|\widehat{x}}
.

Corollary 6.13

Let
E
be good lattice.
We obtain the residue endomorphism

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

on
(E_{\widehat{H}_{k}}^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits})_{|H_{k}}

which induces
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{H_{k}}(\nabla)

on
(E^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}_{\widehat{H}_{k}|H_{k}})_{|\widehat{x}}

for any
x\in H_{k}
.

6.2.6
Condition for lattices to be unramifiedly good

Let
(V,\nabla)
be a meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)

relative to
U
with a lattice
E
.
Let
\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}((X\setminus H)/U)
be
a unramifiedly good meromorphic Lagrangian cover.
Assume that
U
is irreducible.
Let
u:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}_{1})\to U
be
a geometric point obtained as an algebraic closure
of the generic point.

Lemma 6.14

Assume the following conditions.

•

(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla_{u})
is unramifiedly good,
and that
E_{u}
is unramifiedly good.

•

\Sigma_{u}
is a representative of

{\boldsymbol{I}}(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla_{u})
.

Then, the following holds.

•

(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is unramifiedly good, and

E
is unramifiedly good.

Proof
Let
x\in H
be any closed point.
Let us prove that
E
is unramifiedly good at
x
.
It is enough to consider the case where
there exists a section
U\to X

whose image
U_{1}
contains
x
.
We may assume that
X
is affine,
X=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(R)
.
Let
R_{U_{1}}
denote the completion of
R
along
U_{1}
.
We have

\mathcal{I}\subset R_{U_{1}}(\ast H)/R_{U_{1}}

and a decomposition

\Sigma=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

such that

\kappa_{d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\ast}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

are logarithmic.
By using Lemma
6.6
,
we can prove that there exists a decomposition

E_{|\widehat{x}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\widehat{E}_{x,\mathfrak{a}}

such that

(\nabla-d\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}})E_{x,\mathfrak{a}}\subset E_{x,\mathfrak{a}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X/U}(\log H)
.

6.2.7
Refinements of parameter spaces

Let
U,X,H
be as in §
6.2.1
.
Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be a meromorphic integrable connection
on
(X,H)
relative to
U
.

Proposition 6.15

There exist
a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
\widetilde{U}

with a surjection
\widetilde{U}\to U
,
and a projective birational morphism

\varphi:\widehat{X}_{\widetilde{U}}\to X_{\widetilde{U}}
,
such that
the following holds.

•

\widehat{X}_{\widetilde{U}}
is smooth over
\widetilde{U}
,
and
\varphi^{-1}(H_{\widetilde{U}})
is normal crossing
relative to
\widetilde{U}

with the trivial monodromy.

•

\varphi^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is good.

•

There exists a good lattice
E
of

\varphi^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
.

Proof
It is enough to consider the case where

\mathbb{K}
is an algebraic closure of
a field finitely generated over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Let
T\subset U
be any locally closed irreducible smooth subset.
Let
u:\mathbb{K}_{1}\to T

be an algebraic closure of the generic point of
T
.
We obtain a meromorphic flat connection

(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla_{u})
on
(X_{u},H_{u})
.
There exists a projective birational morphism

\rho_{u}:\widehat{X}_{u}\to X_{u}

such that
(i)
\widehat{H}_{u}=\rho_{u}^{-1}(H_{u})
is simple normal crossing,
(ii)
\widehat{X}_{u}\setminus\widehat{H}_{u}\simeq X_{u}\setminus H_{u}
,
(iii)
\rho_{u}^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla_{u})
is good on

(\widehat{X}_{u},\widehat{H}_{u})
.
Let
