\Sigma(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla_{u})

be a representative of
the Lagrangian irregularity
{\boldsymbol{I}}(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla_{u})
.
We fix an embedding
\mathbb{K}_{1}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
.
There exists a
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange lattice

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})

of

(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla_{u})
.
There exist an etale covering of
\widehat{X}_{u}
by
etale coordinate systems

(\mathcal{U}_{u,i},\varphi_{u,i},\psi_{u,i})
.
There exist unramifiedly good lattices

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}\bigl(\varphi_{u,i,e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{u})\bigr)

of
\varphi_{u,i,e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla)

on
\mathcal{U}_{u,i}^{(e)}
.

There exist a Zariski open subset
T^{\prime}
of
T
,
a finite etale covering
\widetilde{T}^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}
,
a model
(\widehat{X}_{T^{\prime}},\rho_{T^{\prime}})
of
(\widehat{X}_{u},\rho_{u})
,
models
(\mathcal{U}_{T^{\prime},i},\varphi_{T^{\prime},i},\psi_{T^{\prime},i})

of
(\mathcal{U}_{u,i},\varphi_{u,i},\psi_{u,i})
,
a model
\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}(\mathcal{E}_{T^{\prime}})
of

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})
,
a model
\Sigma_{T^{\prime}}
of
\Sigma(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla_{u})
,
and models

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}\bigl(\varphi_{T^{\prime},i,e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{T^{\prime}})\bigr)
.
By Lemma
6.14
,
we obtain that

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}\bigl(\varphi_{T^{\prime},i,e}^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{T^{\prime}})\bigr)

are unramifiedly good.
Hence, we obtain that

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{0}(\mathcal{E}_{T^{\prime}})
is good.

By using the previous argument
and a Noetherian induction,
we obtain the claim of Proposition
6.15
.

6.3
Boundedness of good meromorphic flat bundles
with bounded irregularity

6.3.1
Meromorphic good systems of ramified irregular values

Let
X
be a smooth projective
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
H
be a normal crossing hypersurface of
X
.

Definition 6.16

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}=(\mathcal{I}_{x}\,|\,x\in H)

be a tuple of
good sets of ramified irregular values

\mathcal{I}_{x}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H)}_{X,x}(\ast H)/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(H)}_{X,x}
.
It is called meromorphic
if there exists a meromorphic Lagrangian cover

\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

such that
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma)
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
be a meromorphic
tuple of good sets of irregular values.
Let
\Sigma
be a meromorphic Lagrangian cover
such that
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma)={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{\Lambda}
,
where
\Lambda
denotes the index set of
the irreducible decomposition

H=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda}H_{j}
.
We say

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\geq{\boldsymbol{m}}

if the closure of

\Sigma
in
T^{\ast}X(\log H)\bigl(\sum m_{i}H_{i}\bigr)

is proper over
X
.

Lemma 6.17

Suppose that
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\geq{\boldsymbol{m}}
.
Let
(V,\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)

such that
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(V)\subset{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}
.
Let
V_{0}
be any good lattice of
(V,\nabla)
.
Then, we obtain

\nabla(V_{0})\subset V_{0}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log H)\bigl(\sum m_{i}H_{i}\bigr)
.

Proof
In the unramified case,
we can check it by direct computation.
The general case is reduced to the unramified case.

6.3.2
Boundedness of good meromorphic flat bundles
as meromorphic objects

Let
X_{S}\to S
be a smooth projective morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties
such that each closed fiber is connected.
Let
H_{S}\subset X_{S}
be a hypersurface
which is normal crossing relative to
S

(see §
2.5.2
).
For any
\mathbb{K}
-variety
S^{\prime}
over
S
,
we set
X_{S^{\prime}}=X_{S}\times_{S}S^{\prime}

and
H_{S^{\prime}}=H_{S}\times_{S}S^{\prime}
.
For any closed point
s\in S
,
let
\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm good}\nolimits}(X_{s},H_{s},r,{\boldsymbol{m}})

be a family of good meromorphic flat bundles

(V,\nabla)
of rank
r
on
(X_{s},H_{s})

such that
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}})\geq{\boldsymbol{m}}
.

Theorem 6.18

There exist a
\mathbb{K}
-variety
\mathcal{S}
over
S
,
a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathcal{S}}}
-module
E_{\mathcal{S}}
,
an integrable connection

\nabla_{\mathcal{S}}:E_{\mathcal{S}}\to E_{\mathcal{S}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X_{\mathcal{S}}/\mathcal{S}}(\ast H_{\mathcal{S}})

relative to
\mathcal{S}
such that the following holds.

•

For any
s\in S
,

(V,\nabla)\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm good}\nolimits}(X_{s},H_{s},r,{\boldsymbol{m}})

and a
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange lattice
V_{0}
,
there exist
\widetilde{s}\in\mathcal{S}
over
s

such that

(V_{0},\nabla)\simeq(E_{\mathcal{S}},\nabla_{\mathcal{S}})_{\widetilde{s}}
.

•

(E_{\mathcal{S}}(\ast H_{\mathcal{S}}),\nabla_{\mathcal{S}})

is a good meromorphic flat bundle
on
(X_{\mathcal{S}},H_{\mathcal{S}})

relative to
\mathcal{S}
.

In this sense,
the family
(\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm good}\nolimits}(X_{s},H_{s},r,{\boldsymbol{m}})\,|\,s\in S(\mathbb{K}))

is bounded as meromorphic objects.

Proof
Let
N\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

such that

\Omega_{X_{s}}^{1}(\log H_{s})(\sum m_{i}H_{s,i})\subset\Omega_{X_{s}}^{1}(NH_{s})
.
Let
M_{2}>0
be as in §
5.6

for some
s\in S(\mathbb{K})
.
For
s\in S(\mathbb{K})
,
let
\mathcal{G}_{s}(r)

be the family of locally free sheaves
E
of rank
r
on
X_{s}

satisfying the following conditions.

•

|a_{1}(E)|+|a_{2}(E)|\leq C(r)
,
where

a_{i}(E)
denote the constants
as in §
5.7.1
,
and
C(r)
denotes a constant
as in Proposition
5.32
.

•

\mu_{\max}(E)\leq\mu(E)+r(M_{2}+N\deg_{\omega}(H_{s}))+A
,
where
A
denotes the constant as in Proposition
5.32
.
(See §
5.6
for

\mu_{\max}(E)
,
\mu(E)
and
M_{2}
.)

By the boundedness theorem of Maruyama
[
20
]

(see Proposition
5.30
),
there exist
a morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

\mathcal{S}_{1}\to S

and
a locally free sheaf

\mathcal{E}

of rank
r

on
X_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}

such that
for any
s\in S(\mathbb{K})

and any
E\in\mathcal{G}_{s}(r)
,
there exists
s_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}(\mathbb{K})
over
s

such that
\mathcal{E}_{s}\simeq E
.
By Proposition
5.21
,
there exists a morphism of

\mathbb{K}
-varieties
\mathcal{S}_{2}\to\mathcal{S}_{1}

and a universal integrable meromorphic connection

\nabla^{u}:\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}}\to\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X_{\mathcal{S}_{2}/\mathcal{S}_{2}}}\bigl(NH_{\mathcal{S}_{2}}\bigr)

relative to
\mathcal{S}_{2}

such that the following holds.

•

For any
s_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}(\mathbb{K})

and an integrable meromorphic connection

\nabla:\mathcal{E}_{s_{1}}\to\mathcal{E}_{s_{1}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X_{s_{1}}}(NH_{s_{1}})
,
there exists

s_{2}\in\mathcal{S}_{2}
over
s_{1}

such that

(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}},\nabla^{u})_{s_{2}}\simeq(\mathcal{E}_{s_{1}},\nabla)
.

By refining
\mathcal{S}_{2}
,
we may assume that

(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}}(\ast H_{\mathcal{S}_{2}}),\nabla^{u})

is good relative to
\mathcal{S}_{2}
.

For any
s\in S(\mathbb{K})
and

(V,\nabla)\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm good}\nolimits}(X_{s},H_{s},r,{\boldsymbol{m}})
,
there exists
a
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange lattice
V_{0}
of
V

as in Proposition
5.32
.
By Corollary
5.29
,

V_{0}
is contained in
\mathcal{G}_{s}(r)
.
By the construction,
there exists

s_{2}\in\mathcal{S}_{2}

such that

(V_{0},\nabla)\simeq(\mathcal{E},\nabla^{u})_{s_{2}}
.

Remark 6.19

Later, we shall study
the boundedness of the family

(\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm good}\nolimits}(X_{s},H_{s},r,{\boldsymbol{m}})\,|\,s\in S(\mathbb{K}))

as
\mathcal{D}
-modules
(Theorem
8.2
).

6.4
Boundedness of meromorphic flat bundles with bounded irregularity

Let
X
be a normal projective
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a hypersurface
H

such that
X\setminus H
is smooth.
There exists a closed subset
Z\subset H

with
\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits_{X}(Z)\geq 2

such that
(i)
X^{\circ}=X\setminus Z
is smooth,
(ii)
H^{\circ}=H\setminus Z
is a normal crossing hypersurface of
X^{\circ}
.
We also set
H_{i}^{\circ}=H_{i}\setminus Z
.

For
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{\Lambda}
,
let
\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,{\boldsymbol{m}})
be the family of
meromorphic flat bundles
(V,\nabla)
of rank
r

on
(X,H)

such that the following holds.
(See Proposition
5.20
for the equivalent conditions.)

•

There exists a reflexive lattice
V_{0}\subset V

such that

\nabla(V_{0|X^{\circ}})\subset V_{0}\otimes\Omega_{X^{\circ}}^{1}(\log H^{\circ})(\sum m_{i}H^{\circ}_{i})
.

Theorem 6.20

There exist a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
\mathcal{S}
,
a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}
over
\mathcal{S}

with a morphism
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}:\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}\to X\times\mathcal{S}
over
\mathcal{S}
,
a hypersurface
\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}}\subset\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}

normal crossing relative to
\mathcal{S}
,
a good meromorphic flat bundle
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
on

(\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}},\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}})
relative to
\mathcal{S}
,
and a lattice
\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}}\subset\mathcal{V}

such that
the following holds.

•

\rho^{-1}_{\mathcal{S}}(H\times\mathcal{S})=\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}}
,
and
\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}\setminus\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}}\simeq(X\setminus H)\times\mathcal{S}
.

•

For any
(V,\nabla)\in\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,{\boldsymbol{m}})
,
there exists
s\in\mathcal{S}

such that

\rho_{s}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)\simeq(\mathcal{V},\nabla)_{s}

and that

\widehat{E}_{s}

is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange lattice of

\rho_{s}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
.

Proof
Let
N>m_{i}+1
for any
i
.
By Proposition
2.66
,
there exist a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
S
,
a Zariski closed subset

\Sigma_{S}\subset S\times T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

and a projective morphism

\rho_{S}:\widehat{X}_{S}\to S\times X
,
such that the following holds.

•

\Sigma_{S}\to S\times(X\setminus H)

is a meromorphic Lagrangian cover over
S\times(X,H)
,
and
\Sigma_{S}
is flat over
S
.
The number of any fiber is less than
r
.

•

For any any meromorphic Lagrangian cover

\Sigma\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

over
(X^{\circ},H^{\circ})

such that
the closure of
\Sigma

in
T^{\ast}X^{\circ}(NH^{\circ})
is proper over
X^{\circ}
,
there exists
s\in S

such that

\Sigma=\Sigma_{S}\times_{S}s=:\Sigma_{s}
.

•

\widehat{X}_{S}
is smooth projective over
S
,
and

\widehat{H}_{S}=\rho_{S}^{-1}(S\times H)

is normal crossing relative to
S
.

•

For each
s\in S
,

\rho_{s}^{\ast}(\Sigma_{s})

is good on

(\widehat{X}_{s},\widehat{H}_{s})=(\widehat{X}_{S},\widehat{H}_{S})\times_{S}s
,
where
\rho_{s}:\widehat{X}_{s}\to X

denotes the morphism induced by
\rho_{S}
.

For any smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
T
over
S
,
we set

(\widehat{X}_{T},\widehat{H}_{T})=(\widehat{X}_{S},\widehat{H}_{S})\times_{S}T
.

Let
S^{\prime}
be a connected component of
S
.
Let

\widehat{H}_{S^{\prime}}=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda(S^{\prime})}\widehat{H}_{S^{\prime},i}

denote the irreducible decomposition.
For each
s\in S^{\prime}
,
we obtain the tuple
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma_{s})

on
(\widehat{X}_{s},\widehat{H}_{s})
.
There exists
{\boldsymbol{m}}(S^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{\Lambda(S^{\prime})}

such that

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma_{s}))\geq{\boldsymbol{m}}(S^{\prime})

for any
s\in S^{\prime}
.

By Theorem
6.18
,
there exist
a morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

S_{1}\to S
,
a locally free

\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{X}_{S_{1}}}(\ast\widehat{H}_{S_{1}})
-module

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{u}_{S_{1}}

with an integrable meromorphic connection

\nabla:\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{u}_{S_{1}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{u}_{S_{1}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\widehat{X}_{S_{1}}/S_{1}}

relative to
S_{1}
,
and a lattice
\widehat{E}_{S_{1}}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{u}_{S_{1}}

such that the following holds.

•

(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{u}_{S_{1}},\nabla)

is good relative to
S_{1}
.

•

Let
s^{\prime}\in S^{\prime}\subset S
be any closed point.
Let
(V,\nabla)

be a good meromorphic flat bundle
on
(\widehat{X}_{s},\widehat{H}_{s})

such that

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(V)\geq{\boldsymbol{m}}(S^{\prime})
.
Then,
there exists
s_{1}\in S_{1}
over
s

such that

(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{u}_{S_{1}},\nabla)_{|s_{1}}\simeq\rho_{s_{1}}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
,
and that

\widehat{E}_{s_{1}}
is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-DM lattice of

\rho_{s_{1}}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
.

By refining
S_{1}
,
we may also assume the following.

•

The meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity
of
(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{u}_{S_{1}},\nabla)

equals

{\boldsymbol{I}}(\Sigma_{S_{1}})
.

Let
(V,\nabla)\in\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,{\boldsymbol{m}})
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{I}}(V,\nabla)
be the associated meromorphic Lagrangian irregularity.
Take any
\Sigma(V,\nabla)\in{\boldsymbol{I}}(V,\nabla)
.
By Proposition
5.20
,
the closure of
\Sigma(V,\nabla)

in
T^{\ast}(X^{\circ})(\log H^{\circ})(\sum m_{i}H^{\circ}_{i})

is proper over
X^{\circ}
.
There exists
s\in S

such that
\Sigma(V,\nabla)=\Sigma_{s}
,
and
\rho_{s}^{\ast}\Sigma(V,\nabla)
is good on

(\widehat{X}_{s},\widehat{H}_{s})
.
By Theorem
5.19
,

\rho_{s}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
is good on
(\widehat{X}_{s},\widehat{H}_{s})
,
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\rho_{s}^{\ast}(V,\nabla))={\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}}(\Sigma(V))
.
Hence,
there exists
s_{1}\in S_{1}
over
s

such that

(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{u}_{S_{1}},\nabla)_{|s_{1}}\simeq\rho_{s_{1}}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
,
and that

\widehat{E}_{s_{1}}
is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange lattice of

\rho_{s_{1}}^{\ast}(V,\nabla)
.

Corollary 6.21

There exists a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
\mathcal{S}_{1}

and a coherent torsion-free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\times X}
-module

E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}

with a meromorphic integrable connection

\nabla_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}:E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}\to E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\times X/\mathcal{S}_{1}}(\ast(H\times\mathcal{S}_{1}))

relative to
\mathcal{S}_{1}
such that the following holds.

•

E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}
is a reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\times X}
-module
flat over
\mathcal{S}_{1}
.

•

For any
(V,\nabla)\in\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,{\boldsymbol{m}})
,
there exist
s\in\mathcal{S}_{1}

and a
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange lattice
V_{0}

of
(V,\nabla)

such that

(E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},\nabla_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})_{s_{1}}\simeq(V_{0},\nabla)
.

In this sense, the family
\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,{\boldsymbol{m}})
is bounded.

Proof
By refining
\mathcal{S}
,
we may assume that

\rho_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}}

and

(\rho_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\lor}

are flat over
\mathcal{S}
.
We set

E_{\mathcal{S}}=\bigl((\rho_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\lor}\bigr)^{\lor}
.
By refining
\mathcal{S}
,
we may assume that

(E_{\mathcal{S}})\simeq\bigl((\rho_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\lor}\bigr)^{\lor}
.
Moreover, we may assume

(E_{\mathcal{S}})_{s}\simeq\bigl((\rho_{\mathcal{S}\ast}\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\lor}_{s}\bigr)^{\lor}

for any
s\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{K})
.
In particular,

(E_{\mathcal{S}})_{s}
are reflexive.

We have the meromorphic integrable relative connection

\nabla_{\mathcal{S}}
of
E_{\mathcal{S}}

induced by
(\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}},\nabla)
.
If
(\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}})_{s}
is a good
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange lattice
of
((\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}})_{s}(\ast\widehat{H}_{s}),\nabla)

for some
s\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{K})
,
there exists
Z\subset H
such that

\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits_{X}(Z)\geq 2

such that

(E_{\mathcal{S}})_{s|X\setminus Z}
is
a
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange lattice
of
((E_{\mathcal{S}})_{s}(\ast H),\nabla)_{|X\setminus Z}
.
Because
(E_{\mathcal{S}})_{s}
is reflexive,

(E_{\mathcal{S}})_{s}
is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-DM lattice
of
((E_{\mathcal{S}})_{s}(\ast H),\nabla)
.

7
Families of
\mathcal{D}
-modules

7.1
Families of
\mathcal{D}
-modules

7.1.1
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
S
be a
\mathbb{K}
-scheme.
Let
X_{S}
be a quasi-projective smooth scheme over
S

of relative dimension
n
,
i.e.,
n=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\Omega^{1}_{X_{S}/S}
.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
denote
the sheaf of differential operators
on
X_{S}
relative to
S
.
The sheaf of algebras
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}

is a coherent sheaf of rings
in the sense of
[
10
, Definition 1.4.8]
.

We set
\Omega_{X_{S}/S}=\bigwedge^{n}\Omega_{X_{S}/S}^{1}
.
For any left
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module
M
,
we obtain a right
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module

\Omega_{X_{S}/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{S}}}M
.
Conversely,
for any right
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module
N
,
we obtain a left
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module

N\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{S}}}(\Omega_{X_{S}/S})^{-1}
.
These procedures induce an equivalence
between left
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules
and right
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules.
In this paper,
we shall consider left
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules.
The following lemma is clear.

Lemma 7.1

The category
of
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules

\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
is an abelian category.
The full subcategory of
coherent

\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules

\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
is
an abelian subcategory,
and closed under extensions.
Similarly, the full subcategory of
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules

\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
is
an abelian subcategory,
and closed under extensions.

Let
D(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
denote
the derived category of
complexes of
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules.
Let
D_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})

(resp.
D_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
)
denote
the derived category of
cohomologically coherent
(resp. quasi-coherent)
complexes of
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules.
Let
D^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\subset D(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
,

D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\subset D_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})

and
D^{b}_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\subset D_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})

denote the full subcategories of
cohomologically bounded objects.

Definition 7.2

A
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module is called

S
-flat if it is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{S}
.
An object of
D(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})

is called
S
-flat if
it is cohomologically
S
-flat.

Remark 7.3

The analytic theory for
family of
\mathcal{D}
-modules
has been developed in

[
1
,
2
]
.

7.1.2
Functoriality in the parameter direction

Let
g:S^{\prime}\to S
be any morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-schemes.
We set
X_{S^{\prime}}=X_{S}\times_{S}S^{\prime}
.
The induced morphism

X_{S^{\prime}}\to X_{S}
is also denoted by
g
.
We have the natural morphism of sheaves of algebras

g^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\to\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}
.

Inverse image

We assume that
S
is a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
For any
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module
M
,

g^{\ast}(M):=\mathcal{O}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}\otimes_{g^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X_{S}}}g^{-1}(M)\simeq\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}\otimes_{g^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})}g^{-1}(M)

is naturally a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}
-module.
We obtain

Lg^{\ast}:D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\to D_{c}^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}})

by setting

Lg^{\ast}(M^{\bullet})=\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{g^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})}g^{-1}(M^{\bullet}).

It also induces

Lg^{\ast}:D^{b}_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\to D^{b}_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}})
.

Lemma 7.4

If
M
is
S
-flat,
we have
Lg^{\ast}(M)\simeq g^{\ast}(M)
.

Proof
Let
\mathbb{K}_{X_{S^{\prime}}}

denote the sheaf of locally constant

\mathbb{K}
-valued functions on
X_{S^{\prime}}
.
It is naturally a sheaf of algebras.
Let
D(\mathbb{K}_{X_{S^{\prime}}})

denote the derived category of

\mathbb{K}_{X_{S^{\prime}}}
-complexes.
There exists the natural forgetful functor

a:D(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}})\to D(\mathbb{K}_{X_{S^{\prime}}})
.
It is enough to prove the vanishing of
the
j
-th cohomology sheaves
of
a(Lg^{\ast}(M))
for any
j<0
.

Let
\pi:X_{S^{\prime}}\to S^{\prime}
denote the projection.
There exist the natural isomorphisms

\mathcal{O}_{X_{S^{\prime}}}\simeq\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S^{\prime}})\otimes_{\pi^{-1}g^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}g^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X_{S}})
,
and

\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}\simeq\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S^{\prime}})\otimes_{\pi^{-1}g^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}g^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}).

Let
\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{O}_{S^{\prime}}

be a
g^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-flat resolution.
We obtain the quasi-isomorphism of
the complex of right
g^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules

\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{P})\otimes_{\pi^{-1}g^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}g^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\to\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}.

It is a
g^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
-flat resolution of

\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}
.
Then,
a(Lg^{\ast}(M))

is represented by

\Bigl(\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{P})\otimes_{\pi^{-1}g^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}g^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\Bigr)\otimes_{g^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}g^{-1}(M)\simeq\pi^{-1}\mathcal{P}\otimes_{\pi^{-1}g^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}g^{-1}(M).

Because
M
is
S
-flat,
we obtain
the vanishing of
the
j
-th cohomology sheaves
of
a(Lg^{\ast}(M))
for any
j<0
.

Remark 7.5

If
M
is
S
-flat,

g^{\ast}(M)
is often denoted by
M_{S^{\prime}}
.

Direct image

We assume that both
S
and
S^{\prime}
are smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties.
For any
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}
-module
N
,
we obtain
the
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module
g_{X\ast}(N)
.
For any
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}
-complex
N^{\bullet}
,
by taking a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}
-injective resolution

N^{\bullet}\to\mathcal{J}
,
we obtain a complex of
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules

g_{X\ast}(\mathcal{J})
.
In this way,
we obtain

Rg_{\ast}:D^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}})\to D^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
.
We assume that both
S
and
S^{\prime}
are
smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties.
It also induces

Rg_{\ast}:D^{b}_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}})\to D^{b}_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
.
If
g
is proper,
we also obtain

D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}})\to D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
.

7.1.3
Good filtrations

Assume that
S
is a Noetherian
\mathbb{K}
-scheme.
We have the natural filtration

F(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})

by the order of differential operators
with which
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}

is a sheaf of filtered rings
in the sense of

[
12
, A.1(c)]
.
Let
M
be a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module.
The notion of good filtration for
coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module is defined
in the standard way.
Namely, it is an increasing filtration
F(M)

by coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X_{S}}
-submodules
such that the following holds.

•

\bigcup F_{j}(M)=M
.
There exist
j_{0}(x)
such that

F_{j_{0}}(M)=0
.

•

F_{j}\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}\cdot F_{k}(M)\subset F_{j+k}(M)
.

•

\bigoplus F_{j}(M)
is coherent over

\bigoplus F_{j}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
.

As in the case of
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules,
such a good filtration always exists.
Moreover, if
F
and
F^{\prime}
are two such filtrations,
there exists
i_{0}>0
such that

F^{\prime}_{j-i_{0}}(M)\subset F_{j}(M)\subset F^{\prime}_{j+i_{0}}(M)

for any
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}

(See
[
10
, Theorem 2.1.3]
.)
By taking the graduation with respect to the filtration,
we obtain
the coherent
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
-module
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)
.
Let
T^{\ast}(X_{S}/S)
denote the relative cotangent bundle
of
X_{S}
over
S
.
We obtain
a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{T^{\ast}(X_{S}/S)}
-module
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)^{\sim}

from
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)
.
The support is independent of
the choice of a good filtration
as in the case of
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules
(see
[
10
, Theorem 2.2.1]
).
The support is denoted by
\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M)
, called
the characteristic variety of
M
.

Definition 7.6

A good filtration
F(M)
is called
S
-flat if

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)
is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{S}
.

Lemma 7.7

Suppose that
M
has an
S
-flat good filtration
F
.

•

The
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module

M
and
the
\mathcal{O}_{X_{S}}
-modules
F_{j}(M)
are flat over
S
.

•

Let
\iota:S^{\prime}\to S
be an immersion
of a locally closed subset.
The induced morphism

X_{S^{\prime}}\to X_{S}
is also denoted by
\iota
.
Then,

\iota^{\ast}F(M)=(\iota^{\ast}F_{j}(M))

is an
S^{\prime}
-flat good filtration of
the
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}
-module

\iota^{\ast}(M)
.
We also have

\iota^{\ast}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)=\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\iota^{\ast}F}(\iota^{\ast}M)
.

Lemma 7.8

Let
M
be any coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module.
For any good filtration
F
of
M
,
there exists a non-empty open subset
S^{\prime}
of
S

such that
the induced good filtration
F
of
M_{|X_{S^{\prime}}}

is
S^{\prime}
-flat.
In particular,
M_{|X_{S^{\prime}}}
is
S^{\prime}
-flat.

Proof
By
[
6
, Theorem 6.9.1]
,
there exists a non-empty open subset
S^{\prime}
of
S

such that
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)_{|X_{S^{\prime}}}
is flat over
S^{\prime}
.

Corollary 7.9

For any morphism
\varphi:M_{1}\to M_{2}
of
coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules,
There exists a non-empty Zariski open subset
S^{\prime}

such that

\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits\varphi_{|X_{S^{\prime}}}
,
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\varphi_{|X_{S^{\prime}}}

and
\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits\varphi_{|X_{S^{\prime}}}

have
S^{\prime}
-flat good filtrations.
Here,

\varphi_{|X_{S^{\prime}}}
denotes
the induced morphism

M_{1|X_{S^{\prime}}}\to M_{2|X_{S^{\prime}}}
.

Corollary 7.10

For any
S
-flat coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module
M
,
there exists a decomposition
S=\bigsqcup_{j\in\Gamma}S_{j}

such that
each
M_{S_{j}}
has an
S_{j}
-flat good filtration.
For any morphism of
S
-flat coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules

\varphi:M_{1}\to M_{2}
,
there exists a decomposition

S=\bigsqcup S_{j}
such that

M_{1,S_{j}}
,

M_{2,S_{j}}

\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits\varphi_{S_{j}}
,

\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\varphi_{S_{j}}

and
\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits\varphi_{S_{j}}

have
S
-flat good filtrations.
Here,
\varphi_{S_{j}}:M_{1,S_{j}}\to M_{2,S_{j}}

denotes the induced morphism.

Recall that a geometric point of
S

is a morphism
\eta:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}_{1})\to S
,
where
\mathbb{K}_{1}
is an algebraically closed field.
The induced
\mathbb{K}_{1}
-variety is denoted by
X_{\eta}
.
The induced
\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta}}
-module
is denoted by
M_{\eta}
.

Corollary 7.11

Suppose that
S
is irreducible.
Let
\eta
be a geometric point
obtained as an algebraic closure of
the generic point of
S
.
Let
M
be a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module
such that
M_{\eta}=0
.
Then, there exists a dominant quasi-finite etale morphism
of
\mathbb{K}
-schemes
f:S^{\prime}\to S

such that
(i)
\eta
factors through
f
,
(ii)
f^{\ast}M=0
.

Proof
It is enough to consider the case
where
S
,
X_{S}
and
T^{\ast}(X_{S}/S)
are affine.
We may assume that
M
has an
S
-flat good filtration
F
.
It induces a good filtration
F_{\eta}
of
M_{\eta}
.
Because
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F_{\eta}}(M_{\eta})=0
,
we obtain
\eta^{\ast}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)=0
.
Because
T^{\ast}(X_{S}/S)
is affine,
there exist sections
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}

generating
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)

over
\mathcal{O}_{T^{\ast}(X_{S}/S)}
.
There exists
a dominant quasi-finite etale morphism

f:S^{\prime}\to S
such that

f^{\ast}(m_{i})
are
0

in
f^{\ast}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)
.
It implies

f^{\ast}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M)=0

and
f^{\ast}(M)=0
.

7.1.4
Holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules

Let
M
be an
S
-flat coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module.

Definition 7.12

M
is called holonomic if there exists a
decomposition
\bigsqcup_{j\in\Gamma}S_{j}

by locally closed irreducible subsets
such that
(i) each
M_{S_{j}}
has an
S_{j}
-flat good filtration,
(ii)
\dim\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M_{S_{j}})=\dim X_{S_{j}}
.

Lemma 7.13

A coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module

M
is holonomic if and only if
the following holds.

(A)

For any geometric point
\eta
of
S
,

M_{\eta}
is a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta}}
-module.

Proof
There exists a decomposition
S=\bigsqcup S_{j}

such that each
M_{S_{j}}
has an
S_{j}
-flat good filtration
F
.
Let
\eta:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}_{1})\to S
be any geometric point of
S
.
There exists
S_{j}
such that

\eta
factors through
S_{j}
.
The
S_{j}
-flat good filtration
F
of
M_{S_{j}}
induces
a good filtration
F_{\eta}
of
M_{\eta}

for which we have

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F_{\eta}}(M_{\eta})=\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(M_{S_{j}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S_{j}}}\mathbb{K}_{1}
.
Then, the claim is clear.

7.1.5
Model of morphisms

Suppose that
S
is irreducible.
Let
M_{i}

(i=1,2)
be coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-modules.

Lemma 7.14

Let
\eta:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{K}_{1}\to S
be a geometric point
obtained as an algebraic closure of
the generic point of
S
.
For any morphism
\varphi:M_{1,\eta}\to M_{2,\eta}
,
there exist
a dominant quasi-finite etale morphism of
\mathbb{K}
-varieties
f:S^{\prime}\to S

and a morphism of
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}
-modules

\varphi_{S^{\prime}}:f^{\ast}(M_{1})\to f^{\ast}(M_{2})

such that
(i)
\eta
factors through
f
,
(ii)
\varphi
is induced by
\varphi_{S^{\prime}}
,
(iii)
f(S^{\prime})
contains a Zariski open subset of
S
.

7.2
Basic functoriality

7.2.1
Pull back in the fiber direction

Let
S
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
f:X_{S}\to Y_{S}
be a morphism
\mathbb{K}
-varieties over
S
.
We set

\mathcal{D}_{(X_{S}\to Y_{S})/S}:=f^{\ast}\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}
,
which is naturally
a left
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module
and a right
f^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}
-module.
For any left
\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}
-module
M
,
we set

Lf^{\ast}(M)=\mathcal{D}_{(X_{S}\to Y_{S})/S}\otimes^{L}_{f^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}}f^{-1}(M).

It induces

Lf^{\ast}:D^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})\to D^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
.
It also induces

Lf^{\ast}:D^{b}_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})\to D^{b}_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})

as in the case of
\mathcal{D}
-modules.
(See
[
10
, Proposition 1.5.8]
.
See also
[
10
, Remark 1.5.10]
.)

7.2.2
Direct images in the fiber direction

Let
f:X_{S}\to Y_{S}
be a projective morphism.
We set

\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{S}\leftarrow X_{S})/S}=\Omega_{X_{S}/S}^{-1}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{S}}}f^{\ast}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}\otimes\Omega_{Y_{S}/S}).

For any
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-complex
M^{\bullet}
,
we obtain

f_{\dagger}(M^{\bullet}):=Rf_{\ast}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{S}\leftarrow X_{S})/S}\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}M^{\bullet}\Bigr)\in D^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}).

The following is an analogue of

[
10
, Theorem 2.5.1]
.

Proposition 7.15

We obtain the functors

f_{\dagger}:D^{b}_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\to D^{b}_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})

(\sharp=c,qc)
.

For any locally closed smooth subset
T\subset S
,
let
f_{T}:X_{T}\to Y_{T}
denote the induced morphism.
Let
\iota_{X}:X_{T}\to X_{S}

and
\iota_{Y}:Y_{T}\to Y_{S}
denote the inclusions.

Lemma 7.16

For
M\in D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})
,
there exists the natural isomorphism

f_{S\dagger}\iota_{X\ast}(M)\simeq\iota_{Y\ast}f_{T\dagger}(M).

Proof
There exists the following natural isomorphism

\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{S}\leftarrow X_{S})/S}\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\iota_{X\ast}M\longrightarrow\iota_{X\ast}\bigl(\iota_{X}^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{S}\leftarrow X_{S})/S}\otimes^{L}_{\iota_{X}^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}M\bigr)\longrightarrow\iota_{X\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{T}\leftarrow X_{T})/T}\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}M\bigr).

It is easy to check that
the composition is an isomorphism
in
D^{b}(f_{S}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}))
.
We obtain the following isomorphism
in
D^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})
:

f_{S\dagger}(\iota_{X\ast}M)\simeq f_{S\ast}\circ\iota_{X\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{T}\leftarrow X_{T})/T}\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}M\bigr)\simeq\iota_{Y\ast}f_{T\dagger}(M).

Proposition 7.17

Let
M
be an
S
-flat coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module.
Then,
there is a decomposition
S=\bigsqcup_{i\in\Lambda}S_{i}

by locally closed smooth subsets
such that

f^{k}_{S_{i}\dagger}(M_{S_{i}})

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

are
S_{i}
-flat coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S_{i}}/S_{i}}
-modules.
For any locally closed smooth subset
T\subset S_{i}
,
there exist the natural isomorphisms

f^{k}_{S_{i}\dagger}(M_{S_{i}})_{T}\simeq f^{k}_{T\dagger}(M_{T})
.

Proof
Let
S^{\prime}\subset S
be any locally closed smooth subset.
By Lemma
7.8
,
there exists a non-empty open subset
S^{\prime\prime}\subset S^{\prime}

such that

f^{k}_{S^{\prime\prime}\dagger}(M_{S^{\prime\prime}})
are
S^{\prime\prime}
-flat.

Let
T
be a locally closed smooth subset of
S
.
Let
\iota_{X}:X_{T}\to X_{S}
and
\iota_{Y}:Y_{T}\to Y_{S}

denote the induced inclusions.
Let

\mathcal{J}
be a cohomologically bounded

\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-injective complex.
Let

\iota_{X}^{-1}\mathcal{J}^{\bullet}\to\mathcal{J}_{1}

be an
\iota_{X}^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-injective complex.
There exist the following natural morphisms:

\iota_{Y}^{-1}f_{S\ast}(\mathcal{J})\to f_{T\ast}\iota_{X}^{-1}(\mathcal{J})\to f_{T\ast}\mathcal{J}_{1}.

Hence, there exists a natural morphism

\iota_{Y}^{-1}\Bigl(Rf_{S\ast}\bigl(\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{S}\leftarrow X_{S})/S}\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}M\bigr)\Bigr)\longrightarrow Rf_{T\ast}\iota_{X}^{-1}\bigl(\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{S}\leftarrow X_{S})/S}\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}M\bigr)\\
\simeq Rf_{T\ast}\bigl(\iota_{X}^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{S}\leftarrow X_{S})/S}\otimes^{L}_{\iota_{X}^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\iota_{X}^{-1}M\bigr).

(50)

Let
g:\iota_{Y}\circ f_{T}=f_{S}\circ\iota_{X}
.
There exists the natural isomorphism

f_{T}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{T}/T})\otimes_{g^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}}\iota_{X}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{S}\leftarrow X_{S})/S})\simeq f_{T}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{T}/T})\otimes^{L}_{g^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}}\iota_{X}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{S}\leftarrow X_{S})/S})\simeq\mathcal{D}_{(Y_{T}\leftarrow X_{T})/T}.

Hence, there exists the following natural morphism
in
D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{T}/T})
:

L\iota_{Y}^{\ast}\bigl(f_{S\dagger}(M)\bigr)\longrightarrow f_{T\dagger}L\iota_{X}^{\ast}(M)

(51)

Let us check that (
51
) is a quasi-isomorphism.
It is enough to check the case
where

X=Y\times\mathbb{P}^{m}
for a positive integer
m
,

Y
is affine,
and
f
is the projection.
It is enough to prove that

\iota_{Y\ast}L\iota_{Y}^{\ast}\bigl(f_{S\dagger}(M)\bigr)\to\iota_{Y\ast}f_{T\dagger}L\iota_{X}^{\ast}(M)

is a quasi-isomorphism.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{Y}\to\iota_{Y\ast}\mathcal{O}_{Y}

be a finite resolution by locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
of finite ranks.
We have

\iota_{Y\ast}L\iota_{Y}^{\ast}\bigl(f_{S\dagger}(M)\bigr)\simeq f_{S\dagger}(M\otimes f_{S}^{\ast}\mathcal{P}_{Y})\simeq f_{S\dagger}\bigl(\iota_{X\ast}L\iota_{X}^{\ast}(M)\bigr)\simeq\iota_{Y\ast}f_{T\dagger}(L\iota_{X}^{\ast}M).

By using a Noetherian induction,
we obtain the claims of Proposition
7.17
.

Corollary 7.18

Suppose that
M
is
S
-flat,
and that
Y=S
.
Then, there is a decomposition
S=\bigsqcup S_{i}

by locally closed smooth subsets
such that

f^{j}_{S_{i}\dagger}(M_{S_{i}})

are locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S_{i}}
-modules.
Moreover,
for any closed point
s\in S_{i}
,
there exist the natural isomorphisms

f^{j}_{s\dagger}(M_{S_{i}})_{|s}\simeq H^{\dim X+j}(X_{s},M_{s}\otimes\Omega^{\bullet})
.

7.2.3
Duality

Assume that
S
is smooth quasi-projective over
\mathbb{K}
.
Let
n
denote the relative dimension of
X_{S}
over
S
.
For any
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-complex
M^{\bullet}
,
we set

\mathbb{D}(M^{\bullet})=R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M^{\bullet},\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}\otimes\Omega_{X_{S}/S}^{-1})[n]\in D(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}).

It induces
a contravariant functor

\mathbb{D}:D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\to D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
.

Proposition 7.19

Let
M
be a holonomic
S
-flat

\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module.
Then,
there exists a decomposition

S=\bigsqcup_{j\in\Gamma}S_{j}

by locally closed smooth subsets
such that

\mathbb{D}(M_{S_{j}})
are
holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S_{j}}/S_{j}}
-modules
flat over
S_{j}
.
For any locally closed smooth subset
T\subset S_{j}
,
there exists the natural isomorphism

\mathbb{D}(M_{S_{j}})_{T}\simeq\mathbb{D}(M_{T})
.

Proof
Let
S^{\prime}\subset S
be any smooth irreducible locally closed subset.
Let
\mathcal{P}\to M_{S^{\prime}}

be a finite
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}
-projective resolution.
Then,

\mathbb{D}(M_{S^{\prime}})
is represented by

{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}}\bigl(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}\otimes\Omega_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}^{-1}\bigr)[n].

Let
\eta:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}_{1})\to S^{\prime}
be a geometric point
obtained as an algebraic closure of the generic point of
S^{\prime}
.
The induced morphism

X_{\eta}\to X_{S^{\prime}}
is also denoted by
\eta
.
We obtain the induced locally projective
\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta}}
-resolution

\eta^{\ast}\mathcal{P}\to M_{\eta}
.
There exists the following natural isomorphism:

\eta^{\ast}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}}}\bigl(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}^{-1}\bigr)[n]\simeq{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta}}}\bigl(\eta^{\ast}\mathcal{P},\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta}}\otimes\Omega_{X_{\eta}}^{-1}\bigr)[n].

(52)

We obtain
(\mathbb{D}(M_{S^{\prime}}))_{\eta}=\mathbb{D}(M_{\eta})
,
which is a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta}}
-module.
Hence, there exists
a Zariski open subset
S^{\prime\prime}\subset S^{\prime}

such that

\mathbb{D}(M_{S^{\prime\prime}})
is a holonomic

S^{\prime\prime}
-flat
\mathcal{D}_{X\times S^{\prime\prime}/S^{\prime\prime}}
-module.

Let
T
be a locally closed subset of
S
.
Let
\iota:X_{T}\to X_{S}
denote the inclusion.
We obtain the induced locally projective

\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}
-resolution

\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{P}\to M_{T}
.
There exists the isomorphism

\iota^{\ast}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}\otimes\Omega_{X_{S}/S}^{-1}\bigr)[n]\simeq{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}\bigl(\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{P},\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}\otimes\Omega_{X_{T}/T}^{-1}\bigr)[n].

We obtain

L\iota^{\ast}\mathbb{D}(M)\simeq\mathbb{D}(M_{T})
.

Then, we obtain the first claim of the proposition
by a Noetherian induction.

7.2.4
Kashiwara’s equivalence

Let
\iota:X_{S}\to Y_{S}
be a closed embedding
of smooth quasi-projective
\mathbb{K}
-varieties over
S
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits_{\sharp}^{X_{S}}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})

(\sharp=c,qc)

denote the full subcategory of

\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})

consisting of objects whose supports are contained in
\iota(X_{S})
.
Let
D^{b,X_{S}}_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})

(\sharp=c,qc)

denote the full subcategory of

D^{b}_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})

consisting of objects whose cohomological supports
are contained in
\iota(X_{S})
.
The following proposition
is similar to

[
10
, Theorem 1.6.1, Corollary 1.6.2]
.

Proposition 7.20

The direct image functors induce the equivalences

i_{\dagger}:\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\simeq\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits_{\sharp}^{X_{S}}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S}),\quad i_{\dagger}:D^{b}_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})\simeq D_{\sharp}^{b,X_{S}}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})\quad\quad(\sharp=c,qc).

It preserves the
S
-flat condition and the holonomicity condition.
The quasi-inverse are induced by

Li^{\ast}[\dim X-\dim Y]:D_{\sharp}^{b,X_{S}}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})\to D^{b}_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}).

7.2.5
Pull back of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{D}_{Y/S}
-modules by
closed immersions

Let
\iota:X_{S}\to Y_{S}
be a closed embedding
of smooth quasi-projective
\mathbb{K}
-varieties over
S
.
We regard
X_{S}
as a closed subvariety of
Y_{S}
.
Let
H_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,m)
be closed hypersurfaces of
Y_{S}

such that
X_{S}=\bigcap_{j=1}^{m}H_{j}
.

We set
U=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{K}e_{j}
.
For an ordered subset
J=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k})\subset\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
we set

e_{J}=e_{j_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{j_{k}}\in\bigwedge^{k}U
.
We also set

H(J)=\bigcup_{j\in J}H_{j}
.
We formally set
e_{\emptyset}=1

and
H(\emptyset)=\emptyset
.

Let
M
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module.
We set

\mathcal{C}^{k}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}})=\bigoplus_{|J|=k}M(\ast H(J))\otimes e_{J}.

For any
J\subset\{1,\ldots,m\}

and
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}\setminus J
,
there exists the morphism

M(\ast H(J))\otimes e_{J}\to M(\ast H(J\cup\{i\}))e_{i}\wedge e_{J}

induced by

M(\ast H(J))\to M(\ast H(J\cup\{i\}))

and the multiplication of
e_{i}
.
The morphisms induce

d:\mathcal{C}^{k}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}})\to\mathcal{C}^{k+1}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}})

such that
d\circ d=0
.
Because
(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}}),d)_{|Y_{S}\setminus X_{S}}

is acyclic,

(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}}),d)\in D^{b,X_{S}}_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})
.

Lemma 7.21

There exists the natural morphism

C^{\bullet}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}})\simeq\iota_{\dagger}L\iota^{\ast}(M)[\dim X-\dim Y]

in
D^{b,X_{S}}_{qc}(\mathcal{D}_{Y_{S}/S})
.

Proof
There exists the natural morphism of complexes

C^{\bullet}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}})\to M
.
It induces

L\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}})[\dim X-\dim Y]\simeq L\iota^{\ast}(M)[\dim X-\dim Y].

By Proposition
7.20
,
there exists a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}})[\dim X-\dim Y]\simeq\iota_{\dagger}\bigl(L\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(M,{\boldsymbol{H}})[\dim X-\dim Y]\bigr)\simeq\iota_{\dagger}\bigl(L\iota^{\ast}(M)[\dim X-\dim Y]\bigr).

7.3
The restriction of extensions

7.3.1
Adjunction

Let
S
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
X_{S}
be a
\mathbb{K}
-variety which is smooth projective over
S
.
Let
M
be any coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module.
Let
T\subset S
be a closed complex submanifold.
Let
\iota:T\to S
denote the inclusion.
Let
N_{T}
be any
\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}
-module.

Lemma 7.22

There exists the following natural isomorphism
in
D^{b}(p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S})
:

\iota_{\ast}R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}(L\iota^{\ast}M,N_{T})\simeq R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T})

(53)

Proof
Let
\mathcal{J}_{0}
be a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}
-injective resolution of

N_{T}
.
Let
\mathcal{J}_{1}
be a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-injective resolution of

\iota_{\ast}N_{T}
.
There exists a quasi-isomorphism of

\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-complexes

\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{J}_{0}\to\mathcal{J}_{1}

extending the identity of
\iota_{\ast}N_{T}
.
Let
P\to M
be a locally
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-projective resolution
of
M
.
Then,
R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T})

is represented by

{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(P,\mathcal{J}_{1})
,
and

\iota_{\ast}R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}(L\iota^{\ast}M,N_{T})

is represented by

\iota_{\ast}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}\bigl(\iota^{\ast}P,\mathcal{J}_{0}\bigr)
.
There exists the following natural isomorphism and quasi-isomorphism

\iota_{\ast}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}\bigl(\iota^{\ast}P,\mathcal{J}_{0}\bigr)\simeq{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(P,\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{J}_{0})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(P,\mathcal{J}_{1}).

(54)

We obtain (
53
).

Let
p:X_{S}\to S
denote the projection.

Lemma 7.23

There exists the following morphisms
in
D(p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S})

such that
b\circ a
equals the morphism

(
53
)
:

\begin{CD}\iota_{\ast}R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}\bigl(L\iota^{\ast}M,N_{T}\bigr)@>{a}>{}>R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(\iota_{\ast}L\iota^{\ast}M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T}\bigr)@>{b}>{}>R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T}\bigr)\end{CD}

(55)

Proof
We use the notation in the proof of
Lemma
7.22
.
We have the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\ast}P,\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{J}_{0}\bigr)@>{c_{1}}>{}>{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\ast}P,\mathcal{J}_{1}\bigr)\\
@V{c_{2}}V{\simeq}V@V{c_{3}}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(P,\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{J}_{0}\bigr)@>{c_{4}}>{\rm qis}>{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(P,\mathcal{J}_{1}\bigr).\end{CD}

Here,
c_{2}
is an isomorphism,
and
c_{4}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
We have the following natural isomorphism

\iota_{\ast}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}\bigl(\iota^{\ast}P,\mathcal{J}_{0}\bigr)\simeq{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\ast}P,\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{J}_{0}\bigr).

(56)

The composition of (
56
),
c_{2}
and
c_{4}

is the quasi-isomorphism (
54
).
Note that

R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(\iota_{\ast}L\iota^{\ast}M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T}\bigr)

is represented by

{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\ast}P,\mathcal{J}_{1}\bigr)
.
Hence,
we obtain the morphisms in (
55
).

7.3.2
Tensor product with sheaves on the parameter space

Let
M
be any coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module.
Let
N
be any
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-module.

Lemma 7.24

For any
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module
F
,
there exists the natural isomorphism

R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,N)\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}F\simeq R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,N\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}F).

(57)

Proof
Let
P\to M
be a finite locally projective
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-resolution.
Let
N\to\mathcal{J}_{2}
be a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-injective resolution.
Let
L\to F
be any
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-flat resolution.
Then,

R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,N)\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}F

is represented by

{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(P,\mathcal{J}_{2})\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L\simeq{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(P,\mathcal{J}_{2}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L).

(58)

Let
N\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L\to\mathcal{J}_{3}
be
a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-injective resolution.
Then,

R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,N\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}F)

is represented by

{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(P,\mathcal{J}_{3})
.
There exists a quasi-isomorphism of

\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-complexes

\mathcal{J}_{2}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}_{3}
.
It induces a quasi-isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(P,\mathcal{J}_{2}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(P,\mathcal{J}_{3}).

(59)

We obtain (
57
)
from (
58
) and (
59
).

7.3.3
Change of parameter spaces

By Lemma
7.22

and Lemma
7.24
,
we obtain

\iota_{\ast}R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}\bigl(L\iota^{\ast}M,L\iota^{\ast}N\bigr)\simeq R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,\iota_{\ast}L\iota^{\ast}N)\simeq R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(M,N\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{T}\bigr)\\
\simeq R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(M,N\bigr)\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{T}.

(60)

As a result,
we obtain

\iota_{\ast}Rp_{\ast}R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}\bigl(L\iota^{\ast}M,L\iota^{\ast}N\bigr)\simeq Rp_{\ast}R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(M,N\bigr)\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{T}.

In particular, we obtain the following.

Corollary 7.25

We assume that

M
and
N
are flat over
S
.
Then, there exist the natural isomorphisms

\iota_{\ast}R^{k}p_{\ast}R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}\bigl(\iota^{\ast}M,\iota^{\ast}N\bigr)\simeq R^{k}p_{\ast}R{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}\bigl(M,N\bigr)\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{T}.

7.3.4
Commutative diagrams

We impose the following.

Condition 7.26

We assume that
(i)
S
is affine,
(ii)
M
and
N
are flat over
S
.

We set
N_{T}=\iota^{\ast}N
.
Let
\iota_{\ast}N_{T}\to\mathcal{J}_{1}

be a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-injective resolution.
Let
N\to\mathcal{J}_{2}

be a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-injective resolution.
There exists a morphism of

\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-complexes

\mathcal{J}_{2}\to\mathcal{J}_{1}

extending
N\to\iota_{\ast}N_{T}
.

Let
L(S)\to\mathcal{O}_{S}
and
L(T)\to\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{T}

be a finite resolution by locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules
of finite rank
with the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}L(S)@>{}>{}>L(T)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\mathcal{O}_{S}@>{}>{}>\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{T}.\end{CD}

Note that

N\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(T)\to\mathcal{J}_{2}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(T)

is a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-injective resolution.
There exists the following commutative diagram of
quasi-isomorphisms:

\begin{CD}N\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(T)@>{}>{}>\mathcal{J}_{2}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(T)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\iota_{\ast}N_{T}@>{}>{}>\mathcal{J}_{1}.\end{CD}

Lemma 7.27

The following diagram is commutative
in
D(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
:

\begin{CD}\mathcal{J}_{2}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(S)@>{}>{}>\mathcal{J}_{2}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(T)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\mathcal{J}_{2}@>{}>{}>\mathcal{J}_{1}.\end{CD}

(61)

Proof
Because the following diagram is commutative,

\begin{CD}N\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(S)@>{}>{}>N\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(T)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
N@>{}>{}>\iota_{\ast}N_{T},\end{CD}

we obtain the commutativity of (
61
).

Let
\alpha:M\to\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]
be a morphism of

\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-complexes.
We obtain

\alpha_{1}:M\to\mathcal{J}_{1}[k]

as the composition of

M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}_{1}[k].

We also obtain

\alpha_{2}:M\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(S)\to\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{T}

as the composition of
the following morphisms:

M\otimes p^{-1}L(S)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha\otimes\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\otimes p^{-1}L(S)\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\otimes p^{-1}L(T).

Lemma 7.28

We have
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}

in
\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T})
.

Proof
We have the following commutative diagram
in
D^{b}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
:

\begin{CD}M\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(S)@>{\alpha\otimes\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits}>{}>\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(S)@>{}>{}>\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(T)\\
@V{}V{qis}V@V{}V{qis}V@V{}V{qis}V\\
M@>{\alpha}>{}>\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]@>{}>{}>\mathcal{J}_{1}[k].\end{CD}

Hence, we obtain the claim of the lemma.

There exists the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,\mathcal{J}_{2}[k])@>{}>{}>{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M\otimes p^{-1}L(S),\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\otimes p^{-1}L(S))\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M\otimes p^{-1}L(T),\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\otimes p^{-1}L(T))@>{}>{}>{\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M\otimes p^{-1}L(S),\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\otimes p^{-1}L(T)).\end{CD}

From
\alpha
,
we obtain

\alpha_{3}=\alpha\otimes\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits:M\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(T)\to\mathcal{J}_{2}[k]\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}p^{-1}L(T).

The following lemma is clear.

Lemma 7.29

\alpha_{2}
equals
the image of
\alpha_{3}

via

\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(\iota_{\ast}M_{T},\iota_{\ast}N_{T})\to\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T})
.

Corollary 7.30

The morphism

\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,N)\to\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T})

factors through as follows:

\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,N)\to\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(\iota_{\ast}M_{T},\iota_{\ast}N_{T})\to\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{k}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T}).

7.3.5
The restriction of extensions

We assume Condition
7.26
.
Let
T\subset S
be any smooth closed subset of
S
.
The inclusion maps
T\to S
and
X_{T}\to X_{S}

are denoted by
\iota
.
For any
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}S/S}
-module
G
flat over
S
,
we set
G_{T}=\iota^{\ast}G
.
By Corollary
7.25
,
we obtain

\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,N)\to\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,\iota_{\ast}N_{T})\simeq\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}(M_{T},N_{T}).

For any
\alpha\in\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,N)
,
the image is denoted by

\alpha_{T}\in\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}(M_{T},N_{T})
.

Proposition 7.31

If
\alpha
corresponds to
an extension

0\to N\to\widetilde{M}\to M\to 0
,
then

\alpha_{T}
corresponds to
the induced extension

0\to N_{T}\to\widetilde{M}_{T}\to M_{T}\to 0
.
Note that
\widetilde{M}
is flat over
S
.

Proof
We have the following factorization
as in Corollary
7.30
:

\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(M,N)\to\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(\iota_{\ast}M_{T},\iota_{\ast}N_{T})\to\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{T}/T}}(M_{T},N_{T}).

We have
\alpha^{\prime}_{T}\in\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}}(\iota_{\ast}M_{T},\iota_{\ast}N_{T})

as the image of
\alpha
.
By Lemma
7.29
,
it is enough to prove that

\alpha^{\prime}_{T}
corresponds to
the extension induced by

0\to\iota_{\ast}M_{T}\to\iota_{\ast}(\widetilde{M}_{T})\to\iota_{\ast}(N_{T})\to 0
.

Let
\widetilde{\alpha}:M\to\mathcal{J}_{2}[1]

be a morphism representing
\alpha
.
We also have the natural morphism of
\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S}
-complexes

M\to\mathop{\rm Cone}\nolimits(\widetilde{M}\to M)
,
which also represents
\alpha
.
There exists the following commutative diagram
in
D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
:

\begin{CD}M@>{\simeq}>{}>M\\
@V{\widetilde{\alpha}}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\mathcal{J}_{2}[1]@>{\simeq}>{}>\mathop{\rm Cone}\nolimits(\widetilde{M}\to M).\end{CD}

Let

\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}_{T}:M\otimes_{\pi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}\pi^{-1}L(T)\to\mathcal{J}_{2}[1]\otimes_{\pi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}}\pi^{-1}L(T)

be the morphism induced by
\widetilde{\alpha}
,
which represents
\alpha^{\prime}_{T}
.
We obtain the following commutative diagram
in
D^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{X_{S}/S})
:

\begin{CD}M\otimes\pi^{-1}L(T)@>{\simeq}>{}>M\otimes\pi^{-1}L(T)@>{\simeq}>{}>\iota_{\ast}(M_{s})\\
@V{\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime}_{T}}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\mathcal{J}_{2}[1]\otimes\pi^{-1}L(T)@>{\simeq}>{}>\mathop{\rm Cone}\nolimits(\widetilde{M}\to M)\otimes\pi^{-1}L(T)@>{\simeq}>{}>\iota_{\ast}\mathop{\rm Cone}\nolimits(\widetilde{M}_{s}\to M_{s}).\end{CD}

Note that

\iota_{\ast}(M_{s})\to\iota_{\ast}\mathop{\rm Cone}\nolimits(\widetilde{M}_{s}\to M_{s})

corresponds to
the extension

0\to\iota_{\ast}M_{T}\to\iota_{\ast}(\widetilde{M}_{T})\to\iota_{\ast}(N_{T})\to 0
.
Then, we obtain the claim of Proposition

7.31
.

7.4
\mathcal{D}
-modules associated with good lattices
in the absolute case

7.4.1
The
\mathcal{D}
-modules associated with lattices

Let
X
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a hypersurface
H
.
Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be a meromorphic flat connection
on
(X,H)
.

Let
E
be a lattice of a meromorphic integrable connection

(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
on
(X,H)
.
We obtain the
V\mathcal{D}_{X}
-submodule

V\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot E\subset\mathcal{E}
.
We set

\mathcal{D}_{X}(E):=\mathcal{D}_{X}\otimes_{V\mathcal{D}_{X}}(V\mathcal{D}_{X}\cdot E).

There exists a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{D}_{X}(E)(\ast H)=\mathcal{E}
.

For a morphism of meromorphic integrable connections

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla)\to(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
.
Let
E^{\prime}\to E
be a morphism of lattices
which induces the morphism
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{E}
.
Then,
we obtain
\mathcal{D}_{X}(E^{\prime})\to\mathcal{D}_{X}(E)
.

7.4.2
The
\mathcal{D}
-modules associated with good lattices

Suppose that
H
is simple normal crossing,
and that

(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
is a good meromorphic flat bundle
on
(X,H)
.
We set

\mathcal{E}^{\lor}={\mathcal{H}om}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H))

which is equipped with the induced integrable connection
\nabla
.
We obtain the
\mathcal{D}
-module

\mathcal{E}(!H)=\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{E}^{\lor})
.

Let
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{j}
be the irreducible decomposition.
For
I\subset\Lambda
,
we set
H(I)=\bigcup_{j\in I}H_{j}
.
We set

\mathcal{E}(!H(I)\ast H(I^{c}))=\mathcal{E}(!H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H(I^{c})),

where
I^{c}=\Lambda\setminus I
.
For
I\supset J
,
there exists the natural morphism

\mathcal{E}(!H(I)\ast H(I^{c}))\to\mathcal{E}(!H(J)\ast H(J^{c}))
.

Proposition 7.32

Let
E
be a good lattice of
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
.
Assume that for each
i\in\Lambda

one of the following holds.

(a)

{\mathcal{S}p}(E,\nabla,H_{i})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}=\emptyset
.

(b)

{\mathcal{S}p}(E,\nabla,H_{i})^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq 0}=\emptyset
.

Let
\Lambda(a)
(resp.
\Lambda(b)
)
denote the set of
i\in\Lambda

satisfying (a) (resp. (b)).
We set
H(a)=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda(a)}H_{i}

and
H(b)=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda(b)}H_{i}
.
Then, there exists the natural isomorphism

\mathcal{D}_{X}(E)\simeq\mathcal{E}(!H(b)\ast H(a))
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(\mathcal{E})

be a good
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange filtered bundle
as in §
5.3.2
.

Proposition 7.33

For
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{<0}^{I}\times{\mathbb{Q}}_{\geq 0}^{I^{c}}
,
there exists
a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{D}_{X}\bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}(\mathcal{E})\bigr)\simeq\mathcal{E}\bigl(!H(I)\ast H(I^{c})\bigr).

Let
I\supset J
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{a}}(I)\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{<0}^{I}\times{\mathbb{Q}}_{\geq 0}^{I^{c}}

and
{\boldsymbol{a}}(J)\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{<0}^{J}\times{\mathbb{Q}}_{\geq 0}^{J^{c}}

such that
{\boldsymbol{a}}(I)_{i}={\boldsymbol{a}}(J)_{i}
for
J\sqcup I^{c}
.
Then, there exists the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}\mathcal{D}_{X}\bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(I)}(\mathcal{E})\bigr)@>{\simeq}>{}>\mathcal{E}\bigl(!H(I)\ast H(I^{c})\bigr)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\mathcal{D}_{X}\bigl(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(J)}(\mathcal{E})\bigr)@>{\simeq}>{}>\mathcal{E}(!H(J)\ast H(J^{c})).\end{CD}

Proposition 7.34

For any good lattice
E_{1}
of
\mathcal{E}

such that

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{(0,\ldots,0)}(\mathcal{E})\subset E_{1}
,
we have

\mathcal{D}_{X}(E_{1})\simeq\mathcal{E}
.
For any good lattice
E_{2}
of
\mathcal{E}

such that

\mathcal{P}_{<(0,\ldots,0)}(\mathcal{E})\supset E_{2}
,
we have

\mathcal{D}_{X}(E_{2})\simeq\mathcal{E}(!H)
.

7.5
Families of
\mathcal{D}
-modules associated with good lattices

7.5.1
Families of
\mathcal{D}
-modules associated with lattices

Let
S
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
X
be a
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a smooth projective morphism
X\to S

such that each geometric fiber is irreducible.
Let
H\subset X
be a hypersurface of
X

which is normal crossing relative to
S

with trivial monodromy.
Let
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}

denote the irreducible decomposition.

Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be
a meromorphic integrable connection on
(X,H)
relative to
S
.
For any lattice
E
of
\mathcal{E}
,
we obtain the
V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}
-submodule

V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E\subset\mathcal{E}
.
We obtain
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}
-module

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}(E)=\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\otimes_{V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}}\bigl(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E\bigr).

7.5.2
Families of
\mathcal{D}
-modules associated with good lattices

Suppose that
E
is good.
Let
u
be any closed point of
S
.
Let
x\in H
be a closed point over
u
.
We set

\widehat{R}_{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{U,u}

and

\widehat{R}_{x}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}
.

Lemma 7.35

There exist the natural isomorphisms

(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{|\widehat{x}}\simeq V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}

and

\mathcal{D}_{X}(E)_{|\widehat{x}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}(E_{|\widehat{x}})
.

Proof
The image of the natural morphism

\widehat{R}_{x}\otimes_{R_{x}}(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{|\widehat{x}}

(62)

equals

V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}
.
Because
\widehat{R}_{x}
is flat over
R_{x}=\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
,
the morphism (
62
) is injective.
Hence, the natural morphism

\widehat{R}_{x}\otimes_{R_{x}}(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}\to V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}

is an isomorphism.
We obtain the second isomorphism from the first.

Lemma 7.36

V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E

is flat over
S
.
For any closed point
u\in S
,
the natural morphism

(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{u}\to V\mathcal{D}_{X_{u}}\cdot E_{u}

is an isomorphism.

Proof
Let
x\in H
be a closed point over
u
.
Let us prove that

(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}
is flat over

R_{u}=\mathcal{O}_{U,u}
.
Let
I
be any ideal of
R_{u}
.
It is enough to prove that

I\otimes(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}\to(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}

is injective
for the
S
-flatness.
We set
\widehat{I}=I\cdot\widehat{R}_{u}\simeq I\otimes\widehat{R}_{u}
.
Because

V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}\subset\mathcal{E}_{|\widehat{x}}

is flat over
\widehat{R}_{u}

by Proposition
6.5
,

\widehat{I}\otimes_{\widehat{R}_{u}}\bigl(V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}\bigr)\longrightarrow\bigl(V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}\bigr)

is injective.

By Lemma
7.35
,
there exists the isomorphism

\widehat{R}_{x}\otimes_{R_{x}}(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}\simeq V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}
.
We also have

\widehat{R}_{x}\otimes_{R_{x}}\bigl(I\otimes_{R_{u}}(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}\bigr)\simeq\widehat{R}_{x}\otimes_{R_{x}}\bigl(IR_{x}\otimes_{R_{x}}(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}\bigr)\simeq I\widehat{R}_{x}\otimes_{\widehat{R}_{x}}(V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}})\simeq\widehat{I}\otimes_{\widehat{R}_{u}}V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}.

There exists the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}\widehat{R}_{x}\otimes_{R_{x}}\bigl(I\otimes_{R_{u}}(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}\bigr)@>{}>{}>\widehat{R}_{x}\otimes_{R_{x}}(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{\simeq}V{}V\\
\widehat{I}\otimes_{\widehat{R}_{u}}\bigl(V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}\bigr)@>{}>{}>V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}.\end{CD}

Because
\widehat{R}_{x}
is faithfully flat over
R_{x}
,
we obtain that

I\otimes_{R_{u}}(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}\longrightarrow(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{x}

is injective.
Thus, we obtain that

V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E
is
S
-flat.

Let
\widehat{R}_{1,x}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{u},x}
.
We have the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}\widehat{R}_{1,x}\otimes(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{u}@>{}>{}>\widehat{R}_{1,x}\otimes V\mathcal{D}_{X_{u}}E_{u}\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{\simeq}V{}V\\
\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\widehat{R}_{u}}\bigl(V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{x}/\widehat{R}_{u}}\cdot E_{|\widehat{x}}\bigr)@>{\simeq}>{}>V\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{R}_{1,x}/\mathbb{K}}\cdot E_{u|\widehat{x}}.\end{CD}

We obtain that

(V\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\cdot E)_{u}\simeq V\mathcal{D}_{X_{u}}E_{u}
.

Corollary 7.37

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}(E)
is
S
-flat,
and there exists the natural morphism

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}(E)_{u}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{X_{u}}(E_{u})
.

Let
\varphi:S^{\prime}\to S
be a morphism of smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties.
We obtain

(X_{S^{\prime}},H_{S^{\prime}})=(X,H)\times_{S}S^{\prime}

with the induced morphism

\varphi:(X_{S^{\prime}},H_{S^{\prime}})\to(X,H)
.
We obtain

(\mathcal{E}_{S^{\prime}},\nabla)=\varphi^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

on
(X_{S^{\prime}},H_{S^{\prime}})
.
For any lattice
E
of
\mathcal{E}
,
we obtain the induced lattice
E_{S^{\prime}}
.

Proposition 7.38

Suppose that
E
is a good lattice of
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
.
Then, there exists a natural isomorphism

\varphi^{\ast}\mathcal{D}_{X/S}(E)\simeq\mathcal{D}_{X_{S^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}(E_{S^{\prime}}).

Let
\eta:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}_{1})\to S

be a geometric point.

Proposition 7.39

Suppose that
E
is a good lattice of
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
.
Then, there exists a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}(E)_{\eta}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta}}(E_{\eta}).

7.5.3
Exceptional subsets

Suppose that
S
is irreducible.
Let
\eta(0):\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits(\mathbb{K}_{1})\to S

be the geometric point obtained as
an algebraic closure of the generic point of
S
.
Let
\Lambda=I\sqcup I^{c}
be a decomposition.
Let
E
be a good lattice such that

\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta(0)}}(E_{\eta(0)})\simeq\mathcal{E}_{\eta(0)}(!H_{\eta(0)}(I)\ast H_{\eta(0)}(I^{c}))
.
By Proposition
7.32
,
we have

{\mathcal{S}p}(E_{\eta(0)},\nabla,i)^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}=\emptyset
for
i\in I^{c}
,
and

{\mathcal{S}p}(E_{\eta(0)},\nabla,i)^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq 0}=\emptyset
for
i\in I
.
By Corollary
6.13
,
for
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
i\in\Lambda
,
we obtain the Zariski closed subset

W(E,i,m)=\{u\,|\,m\in{\mathcal{S}p}(E_{u},\nabla)\}\subset S.

We set

A=\bigl\{(i,m)\in I^{c}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}\bigr\}\cup\bigl\{(i,m)\in I\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq 0}\bigr\}
.
We obtain the following countable union of Zariski closed subsets:

W(E)=\bigcup_{(i,m)\in A}W(E,i,m)\subsetneq S.

By Proposition
7.32
,
we obtain the following.

Proposition 7.40

Let
\eta^{\prime}
be any geometric point of
S

such that the image of
\eta

is not contained in
W(E)
.
Then,

\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta^{\prime}}}(E_{\eta^{\prime}})\simeq\mathcal{E}_{\eta^{\prime}}(!H_{\eta^{\prime}}(I)\ast H_{\eta^{\prime}}(I^{c}))
.

Lemma 7.41

Let
E^{\prime}
be another good lattice such that

\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta(0)}}(E^{\prime}_{\eta(0)})\simeq\mathcal{E}_{\eta(0)}\bigl(!H_{\eta(0)}(I)\ast H_{\eta(0)}(I^{c})\bigr)
.
Then, there exists a Zariski closed subset

W_{1}\subset W(E)\cap W(E^{\prime})

such that

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}(E)_{S\setminus W_{1}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{X/S}(E^{\prime})_{S\setminus W_{1}}
.

Proof
There exists a positive integer
N

such that the following holds.

{\mathcal{S}p}(E_{\eta(0)},\nabla,i)^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\subset\Bigl\{\alpha+n\,\Big|\,\alpha\in{\mathcal{S}p}(E^{\prime}_{\eta(0)},\nabla,i)^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits},\,\,n\in{\mathbb{Z}},\,\,|n|\leq N\Bigr\},

{\mathcal{S}p}(E^{\prime}_{\eta(0)},\nabla,i)^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}\subset\Bigl\{\alpha+n\,\Big|\,\alpha\in{\mathcal{S}p}(E_{\eta(0)},\nabla,i)^{\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits},\,\,n\in{\mathbb{Z}},\,\,|n|\leq N\Bigr\}.

Then, the claim is clear.

7.5.4
The associated
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}
-modules and morphisms

Suppose that
S
is irreducible.
Let
H=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda}H_{j}
be the irreducible decomposition.
For any
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Lambda}
,
we set
{\boldsymbol{m}}H=\sum m_{i}H_{i}
.

Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle
on
(X,H)
relative to
S
.
Let
E
be a good lattice of
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
.
We assume the following.

Condition 7.42

For any geometric point
\eta(0)

obtained as an algebraic closure of
the generic point of
S
,
we have

E_{\eta(0)}\subset\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{<0}(\mathcal{E}_{\eta(0)})

for a good
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{\overline{\eta}(0)})

Take
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{\Lambda}

satisfying the following.

Condition 7.43

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{(1,\ldots,1)}(\mathcal{E}_{\eta(0)})\subset E_{\eta(0)}({\boldsymbol{m}}H_{\eta(0)})

for a good
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{\overline{\eta}(0)})
.

We set

W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})=W(E)\cup W(E({\boldsymbol{m}}H))
.

Let
\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\subset S(\mathbb{K})

denote the set of closed points
u

such that the following holds.

•

E_{u}\subset\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{<0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})

and

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{(1,\ldots,1)}(\mathcal{E}_{u})\subset E_{u}({\boldsymbol{m}}H_{u})

for a good
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{u})

of
(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla)
.

The following lemma is clear.

Lemma 7.44

W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cap\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})=\emptyset
.

For any
I\subset\Lambda
,
we set
{\boldsymbol{m}}(I)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{\Lambda}

such that

{\boldsymbol{m}}(I)_{i}=m_{i}

(i\in I)
and
{\boldsymbol{m}}(I)_{i}=0

(i\not\in I)
.
We obtain the
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}
-module

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)\cdot H))
.

Lemma 7.45

Let
\eta
be a geometric point such that
the image of
\eta
is not contained in
W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})
.
Then, there exists a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)\cdot H)\bigr)_{\eta}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{\eta}\bigl(!H_{\eta}(I^{c})\ast H_{\eta}(I)\bigr)
.

Proof
By the construction,
the following holds
for any
u\not\in W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})
.

•

{\mathcal{S}p}\bigl(E_{u}({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)H_{u}),\nabla,i\bigr)\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}={\mathcal{S}p}\bigl(E_{u}({\boldsymbol{m}}H_{u}),\nabla,i\bigr)\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}=\emptyset

for any
i\in I
.

•

{\mathcal{S}p}\bigl(E_{u}({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)H_{u}),\nabla,i\bigr)\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq 0}={\mathcal{S}p}\bigl(E_{u},\nabla,i\bigr)\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leq 0}=\emptyset

for any
i\in I^{c}
.

Then, the claim of the lemma follows.

For
I\subset J
,
there exists the natural morphism

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)\cdot H)\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(J)\cdot H)\bigr).

(63)

By Corollary
7.9
,
we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 7.46

There exists a non-empty open subset

S^{\prime}\subset S

such that
the cokernel of the morphism

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)\cdot H)\bigr)_{|X_{S^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(J)\cdot H)\bigr)_{|X_{S^{\prime}}}

on
X_{S^{\prime}}
is flat over
S^{\prime}

for any pair
I\subset J
.

Let
E^{\prime}
be another good lattice of
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

satisfying Condition
7.42
.
Take
{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Lambda}

satisfying Condition
7.43
for
E^{\prime}
.

Proposition 7.47

There exists a Zariski closed subset

W_{1}\subset W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cup W(E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})

such that

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)\cdot H)\bigr)_{S\setminus W_{1}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I)\cdot H)\bigr)_{S\setminus W_{1}}.

In particular,

W_{1}\cap\bigl(\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cap\mathcal{B}(E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})\bigr)=\emptyset
.

Proof
It follows from Lemma
7.41
.

We also have the following lemma for exceptional subsets.

Lemma 7.48

If
E^{\prime}\subset E
and
E({\boldsymbol{m}}H)\subset E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}H)
are satisfied,
then
we have
W(E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})\subset W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})

and
\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\subset\mathcal{B}(E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})
.

Let us give a complement.

Lemma 7.49

Let
E_{0}
be a lattice of
\mathcal{E}

which is not necessarily good.
Assume that
E\subset E_{0}(-H)

and
E_{0}(H)\subset E({\boldsymbol{m}}H)
.
Let
\mathcal{A}(E_{0})

denote the set of closed points
u
of
S

such that
E_{0,u}
is
a good
{\mathbb{C}}
-Deligne-Malgrange lattice
of
(\mathcal{E}_{u},\nabla_{u})
.
Then,
we have
\mathcal{A}(E_{0})\subset\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})
.
In particular,

W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cap\mathcal{A}(E_{0})=\emptyset
.

7.5.5
Push-forward

We continue to assume that
S
is irreducible.
Let
Y
be a
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a smooth projective morphism

.

be a hypersurface of

H_{Y}=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda_{Y}}H_{Y,j}

Let

\mathbb{K}
-varieties over
S

such that
H=f^{-1}(H_{Y})

and that
f:X\setminus H\to Y\setminus H_{Y}

We have the induced map

.

(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

on

relative to
S
.
Let

and
{\boldsymbol{m}}

Lemma 7.50

There exists an open subset
S^{\prime}\subset S

f_{\dagger}^{j}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\Bigl(E\bigl({\boldsymbol{m}}(f_{\ast}^{-1}(I_{Y}))H\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr)_{|Y_{S^{\prime}}}=0

\eta

(64)

Proof

\overline{\eta}
denote an algebraic closure of
a generic point of

.

for

,
where
I_{1}=f_{\ast}^{-1}(I_{Y})
.

The second claim follows from Proposition

.

7.5.6
Refinements

Let
K\subset X
be a hypersurface
such that
K
and
H\cup K
are flat over
S

and that
\mathop{\rm codim}\nolimits(H\cap K)\geq 2
.
We do not assume that
K\cup H
is normal crossing
relative to
S
.

Let
X^{\prime}
be a
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a smooth projective morphism
X^{\prime}\to S
.
Let
H^{\prime}
be a hypersurface of
X

normal crossing relative to
S
.
Let
H^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda^{\prime}}H^{\prime}_{j}

be the irreducible decomposition.
Let
f:X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism over
S

such that
f^{-1}(H\cup K)=H^{\prime}

and that
X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus(H\cup K)
.
Note that
f^{-1}(H)
is also normal crossing relative to
S
.

Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be
a good meromorphic flat bundle on
(X,H)

relative to
S
.
Let
E
be a good lattice of
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

satisfying Condition
7.42
.
Take
{\boldsymbol{m}}
satisfying Condition
7.43
.

We obtain a good meromorphic flat bundle

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})=f^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
relative to
S
.
We also obtain the good lattice
E^{\prime}=f^{\ast}(E)(-H^{\prime})

satisfying Condition
7.42

for
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
.
Take
{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{\Lambda^{\prime}}

such that

f^{\ast}(E({\boldsymbol{m}}))(H^{\prime})\subset E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}H^{\prime})
.
It satisfies Condition
7.43
for
E^{\prime}
.

For any decomposition
I\sqcup I^{c}
,
we obtain
the
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}
-module

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)H)\bigr)
.
There exists
I_{1}\subset\Lambda^{\prime}

such that

H^{\prime}(I_{1})=f^{-1}(H(I)\cup K)
.
There exists
I_{2}\subset\Lambda^{\prime}

such that

H^{\prime}(I_{2})=f^{-1}(H(I))
.
We obtain

\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}/S}\bigl(E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{2})H^{\prime})\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}/S}\bigl(E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{1})H^{\prime})\bigr).

There exist the following natural morphisms:

\begin{CD}f^{0}_{\eta(0)\dagger}\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}}(E^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{2})H^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}))@>{}>{}>\mathcal{D}_{X_{\eta(0)}}(E_{\eta(0)}({\boldsymbol{m}}H_{\eta(0)}))@>{}>{}>f^{0}_{\eta(0)\dagger}\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}}(E^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{1})H^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}))\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{\simeq}V{}V\\
f^{0}_{\eta(0)\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}\bigl(!H^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}\bigr)\Bigr)\bigl(\ast H_{\eta(0)}(I)\bigr)@>{}>{}>\mathcal{E}_{\eta(0)}\bigl(!H_{\eta(0)}(I^{c})\ast H_{\eta(0)}(I)\bigr)@>{}>{}>f^{0}_{\eta(0)\dagger}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}\Bigl(\bigl(!H^{\prime}_{\eta(0)}\bigr)\Bigr)\bigl(\ast(H_{\eta(0)}(I)\cup K_{\eta(0)})\bigr).\end{CD}

(65)

Proposition 7.52

There exist a Zariski open subset
S^{\prime}\subset S

and morphisms

)

\begin{CD}f_{\dagger}^{0}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}/S}\bigl(E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{2})H^{\prime})\bigr)\Bigr)_{\eta}@>{}>{}>\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)H)\bigr)_{\eta}@>{}>{}>f_{\dagger}^{0}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}/S}\bigl(E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{1})H^{\prime})\bigr)\Bigr)_{\eta}\\

such that the following holds.

The cokernel of the natural morphism

Corollary 7.53

There exist a Zariski closed subset

W\subset W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cup W(E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})
,
a decomposition

,
and morphisms

f_{S_{j}\dagger}^{0}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}/S}\bigl(E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{2})H^{\prime})\bigr)_{S_{j}}\Bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a_{j}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)H)\bigr)_{S_{j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle b_{j}}}{{\longrightarrow}}f_{S_{j}\dagger}^{0}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}/S}\bigl(E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{1})H^{\prime})\bigr)_{S_{j}}\Bigr)

\eta
