Then,
there exist the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}f_{S_{j}\dagger}^{0}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}/S}\bigl(E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{2})H^{\prime})\bigr)_{S_{j}}\Bigr)_{\eta}@>{}>{}>\mathcal{D}_{X/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(I)H)\bigr)_{\eta}@>{}>{}>f_{S_{j}\dagger}^{0}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{X^{\prime}/S}\bigl(E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}(I_{1})H^{\prime})\bigr)\Bigr)_{\eta}\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{\simeq}V{}V\\
f_{S_{j}\dagger}^{0}\Bigl(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\eta}(!H^{\prime}_{\eta})\Bigr)\bigl(\ast H_{\eta}(I)\bigr)@>{}>{}>\mathcal{E}_{\eta}\bigl(!H_{\eta}(I^{c})\ast H_{\eta}(I)\bigr)@>{}>{}>f_{S_{j}\dagger}^{0}\Bigl(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\eta}(!H_{\eta})\Bigr)\bigl(\ast(H_{\eta}(I)\cup K)\bigr).\end{CD}

Let us give a complement.

Lemma 7.54

\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\subset\mathcal{B}(E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})
.
As a result,
we have

\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cap(W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cup W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}))=\emptyset
.

Proof
We have

f^{\ast}(E)(-H)_{u}\subset f^{\ast}(\mathcal{P}_{<0}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}\mathcal{E}_{u})(-H)\subset\mathcal{P}_{<0}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}(f^{\ast}\mathcal{E}_{u})
.
We also have

\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{(1,\ldots,1)}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{u})(H)\subset f^{\ast}(\mathcal{P}^{\mathop{\rm DM}\nolimits}_{(1,\ldots,1)}\mathcal{E}_{u})(H)\subset f^{\ast}(E({\boldsymbol{m}}H))(H)\subset E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}H^{\prime}).

Then, we obtain the claim of the lemma.

7.6
Almost minimal extensions associated with cells

7.6.1
Cells and almost minimal extensions

Let
X
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
Z
be a closed subset of
X
.
Let
H
be a hypersurface of
X
.

Let
S
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
which is not necessarily irreducible.
Let
Z_{S}
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety projective over
S

with a normal crossing hypersurface

H_{Z_{S}}
relative to
S
.
Let
\rho:Z_{S}\to S\times X
be a projective morphism over
S

such that
(i)
H_{Z_{S}}=\rho^{-1}(H)
,
(ii)
Z_{S}\setminus H_{Z_{S}}\simeq S\times(Z\setminus H)
.
Let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be a good meromorphic flat bundle
on
(Z_{S},H_{Z_{S}})
relative to
S

with a lattice
E_{0}
.

Let
H_{Z_{S}}=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda}H_{Z_{S},j}

denote the irreducible decomposition.
Let
E
be a good lattice of
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)

satisfying Condition
7.42
.
Take
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{\Lambda}

satisfying Condition
7.43
.

Let
H=\bigcup_{j\in\Gamma}H_{j}
be the irreducible decomposition.
For any
I\subset\Gamma
,
we obtain
\rho^{\ast}(I)\subset\Lambda

such that

\rho^{-1}(S\times H(I))=\bigcup_{j\in\rho^{\ast}(I)}H_{Z_{S},j}
.

Lemma 7.55

There exist a Zariski closed subset

W\subset W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})
,
a morphism of smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

\widetilde{S}\to S

such that the following holds:

•

The image of
\widetilde{S}\to S
is
S\setminus W
.

•

For any
I\subset\Gamma
,

\rho_{\widetilde{S}\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{\widetilde{S}}/\widetilde{S}}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)_{\widetilde{S}}\Bigr)

are
\widetilde{S}
-flat,
and

\rho^{k}_{\widetilde{S}\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)_{\widetilde{S}}\Bigr)=0

(k\neq 0)
.

•

Let
\eta
be any geometric point of
\widetilde{S}

whose image is not contained in

\widetilde{S}\times_{S}W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})
.
Then, there exist the following natural isomorphisms

\rho_{\widetilde{S}\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)_{\widetilde{S}}\Bigr)_{\eta}\simeq\rho_{\eta\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\eta}\bigl(!H_{Z_{S},\eta}\bigr)\Bigr)(\ast H(I)_{\eta}).

Moreover,
the following diagrams are commutative
for
I\subset J
:

\begin{CD}\rho_{\widetilde{S}\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}}))_{\widetilde{S}}\Bigr)_{\eta}@>{\simeq}>{}>\rho_{\eta\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\eta}\bigl(!H_{Z_{S},\eta}\bigr)\Bigr)(\ast H(I)_{\eta})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\rho_{\widetilde{S}\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}J)H_{Z_{S}}))_{\widetilde{S}}\Bigr)_{\eta}@>{\simeq}>{}>\rho_{\eta\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\eta}\bigl(!H_{Z_{S},\eta}\bigr)\Bigr)(\ast H(J)_{\eta}).\end{CD}

•

The cokernel of

\rho_{\widetilde{S}\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)_{\widetilde{S}}\Bigr)\to\rho_{\widetilde{S}\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}J)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)_{\widetilde{S}}\Bigr)

are flat over
\widetilde{S}
.

By replacing
S
with
\widetilde{S}
,
we may assume that the claims of Lemma
7.55

even in the case
\widetilde{S}=S
.
As a consequence,
there exist the following natural isomorphisms:

\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits\Bigl(\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\to\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}J)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr)\simeq\\
\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits\Bigl(\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\to\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}J)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr),

(67)

\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits\Bigl(\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\to\rho_{\widetilde{S}\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}J)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr)\simeq\\
\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits\Bigl(\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\to\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}J)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr),

(68)

\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Bigl(\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\to\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}J)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr)\simeq\\
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Bigl(\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}I)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\to\rho_{\dagger}\Bigl(\mathcal{D}_{Z_{S}/S}\bigl(E({\boldsymbol{m}}(\rho^{\ast}J)H_{Z_{S}})\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr).

Definition 7.56

Definition 7.57

A tuple
\mathcal{U}=(S,Z_{S},\rho,\mathcal{E},\nabla,E,{\boldsymbol{m}})

is called a cell if the following holds.

•

The claim of Lemma
7.55

holds
\mathcal{U}=(S,Z_{S},\rho,\mathcal{E},\nabla,E,{\boldsymbol{m}})

S
-flat.

\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{U})=\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})

\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{U})=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{U}^{\lor})

whose image is not contained in

Lemma 7.59

and a natural isomorphism

7.6.2
Change of hypersurfaces

Let
H^{\prime}
be a hypersurface of
X

such that
H\subset H^{\prime}
.
By refining
S
,
we assume that there exists
a projective morphism
f:Z_{S}^{\prime}\to Z_{S}
over
S

such that

H^{\prime}_{Z_{S}^{\prime}}=(\rho\circ f)^{-1}(H^{\prime})

is simply normal crossing relative to
S
.
We set
\rho^{\prime}:=\rho\circ f
.

We set

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla)=f^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
on
(Z^{\prime}_{S},H^{\prime}_{Z_{S}^{\prime}})
.
We obtain a good lattice

E^{\prime}=f^{\ast}(E)(-H^{\prime}_{Z_{S}^{\prime}})

of
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla)

satisfying Condition
7.42

for
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla)
.
Take
{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}

such that

f^{\ast}(E({\boldsymbol{m}}H_{Z_{S}}))(H^{\prime}_{Z_{S}^{\prime}})\subset E^{\prime}({\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}H^{\prime}_{Z_{S}^{\prime}})
,
which satisfies
Condition
7.43

for
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla)
and
E^{\prime}
.
By refining
S
if necessary,
we obtain a cell

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=(S,Z_{S}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime},E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})
.

Lemma 7.60

There exist a Zariski closed subset

W\subset W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cup W(E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})

and a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{S\setminus W}\simeq\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})_{S\setminus W}
.
We also have

\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\subset\mathcal{B}(E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})

and hence

\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cap\bigl(W(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})\cup W(E^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime})\bigr)=\emptyset
.
In particular,

W\cap\mathcal{B}(E,{\boldsymbol{m}})=\emptyset
.

Proof
It follows from Proposition
7.52
.

7.7
Approximation of universal extensions

7.7.1
Localizability over subsets

Let
S
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
with a subset
A\subset S(\mathbb{K})
.
Let
M_{S}
be an
S
-flat holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{S\times X/S}
-module.

Definition 7.61

We say that
M_{S}
is localizable over
A

if for any hypersurface
H

there exist a quasi-finite morphism of smooth
\mathbb{K}
-varieties

\widetilde{S}\to S
,
a subset
\widetilde{A}\subset\widetilde{S}(\mathbb{K})
,
a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{S}\times X/\widetilde{S}}
-module

M_{\widetilde{S},1}
,

\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{S}\times X/\widetilde{S}}
-homomorphisms

M_{\widetilde{S}}\to M_{\widetilde{S},1}\to M_{\widetilde{S}}(\ast(\widetilde{S}\times H)),

and a countable union of Zariski closed subsets

W(M_{\widetilde{S},1})\subset\widetilde{S}

such that the following holds.

•

\widetilde{A}
is mapped onto
A
.

•

W(M_{\widetilde{S},1})\cap\widetilde{A}=\emptyset
.

•

Let
\eta
be any geometric point of
\widetilde{S}

whose image is not contained in

W(M_{\widetilde{S},1})
.
Then, the induced morphism

(M_{\widetilde{S},1})_{\eta}\to M_{\widetilde{S}}(\ast(\widetilde{S}\times H))_{\eta}

is an isomorphism.

Remark 7.62

Such
\widetilde{S}
and
M_{\widetilde{S},1}
are not unique.

The following lemma is clear.

Lemma 7.63

Suppose that
M_{S}
is localizable over
A
.
Let
T\to S
be any locally closed irreducible subset.
We set
A_{T}=A\cap T(\mathbb{K})
.
Then,
M_{T}
is localizable over
A_{T}
.

Lemma 7.64

Let
0\to M^{\prime}_{S}\to M_{S}\to M^{\prime\prime}_{S}\to 0

be an exact sequence of

S
-flat holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{S\times X/S}
-modules.
If two of
M^{\prime}_{S}
,
M_{S}
and
M^{\prime\prime}_{S}
are localizable over
A
,
the remaining one is also localizable over
A
.

Proof
Let
T\subset S
be any locally closed irreducible subset.
We set
A_{T}=A\cap T(\mathbb{K})
.
It is enough to consider the case
A_{T}\neq\emptyset
.
Let
\eta
be a geometric point
obtained as an algebraic closure of the generic point of
T
.
There exist a quasi-finite morphism

\widetilde{T}\to T

and
an exact sequence
of
\widetilde{T}
-flat holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{T}\times X/\widetilde{T}}
-modules

0\to N^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to N_{\widetilde{T}}\to N^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to 0

which induces

0\to M^{\prime}_{\eta}(\ast H_{\eta})\to M_{\eta}(\ast H_{\eta})\to M^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}(\ast H_{\eta})\to 0
.
We may assume that
there exist morphisms of exact sequences

(0\to M^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to M_{\widetilde{T}}\to M^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to 0)\to(0\to N^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to N_{\widetilde{T}}\to N^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to 0)\to(0\to M^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to M_{\widetilde{T}}\to M^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to 0)(\ast\widetilde{T}\times H)
.
Let
A_{\widetilde{T}}\subset\widetilde{T}(\mathbb{K})

be the inverse image of
A_{T}
.

Let us show that
M_{S}
is localizable over
A

by assuming that
M^{\prime}_{S}
and
M^{\prime\prime}_{S}
are localizable over
A
.
The other claims can be proved similarly.
We may assume that there exist

M^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to M^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T},j}\to M^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}(\ast(\widetilde{T}\times H))

and

M^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}\to M^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T},j}\to M^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}(\ast(\widetilde{T}\times H))

as in the definition.
We may also assume that
there exist isomorphisms

M^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T},j}\simeq N^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}

and

M^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T},j}\simeq N^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T}}
.
We set
W(M_{\widetilde{T},j})_{\widetilde{T}}=\bigl(W(M^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T},j})\cup W(M^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T},j})\bigr)\times_{S}\widetilde{T}
.
Let
\eta
be a geometric point of
\widetilde{T}

whose image is not contained in
W_{\widetilde{T}}
.
Because

(M^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T},j})_{\eta}\simeq(M^{\prime}_{\widetilde{T}})_{\eta}(\ast H_{\eta})

and

(M^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T},j})_{\eta}\simeq(M^{\prime\prime}_{\widetilde{T}})_{\eta}(\ast H_{\eta})
,
we obtain that

(M_{\widetilde{T},j})_{\eta}\simeq(M_{\widetilde{T}})_{\eta}(\ast H_{\eta})
.
Hence, we obtain that
M
is also localizable over
A

by using a Noetherian induction.

Lemma 7.65

Let
\kappa:M\to M^{\prime}
be a morphism of
S
-flat holonomic

\mathcal{D}_{S\times X/S}
-modules.
Assume that
M
and
M^{\prime}
are localizable over
A
,
and that
\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits\kappa
is
S
-flat.
Then,
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\kappa
,
\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits\kappa
and
\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits(\kappa)

are also localizable over
A
.

Proof
There exist

(\widetilde{S}_{i},\widetilde{A}_{i},(M_{i})_{\widetilde{S}_{i},1},W(M_{\widetilde{S}_{i},1}))

as in Definition
7.61
.
By refining
\widetilde{S}_{i}
,
we may assume that
\widetilde{S}_{1}=\widetilde{S}_{2}=:\widetilde{S}

and
\widetilde{A}_{1}=\widetilde{A}_{2}=:\widetilde{A}
.
We may also assume that
there exists the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}(M_{1})_{\widetilde{S}}@>{}>{}>(M_{1})_{\widetilde{S},1}@>{}>{}>(M_{1})_{\widetilde{S}}(\ast(\widetilde{S}\times H))\\
@V{}V{}V@V{\kappa_{\widetilde{S},1}}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(M_{2})_{\widetilde{S}}@>{}>{}>(M_{2})_{\widetilde{S},1}@>{}>{}>(M_{2})_{\widetilde{S}}(\ast(\widetilde{S}\times H)).\end{CD}

Moreover, we may assume that the cokernel of

\kappa_{\widetilde{S},1}

is
\widetilde{S}
-flat.
Then, the claim is clear.

Lemma 7.66

For
\mathcal{U}=(S,Z_{S},\rho,\mathcal{E},\nabla,E,{\boldsymbol{m}})

be any cell,

\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})
and
\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})
are localizable over

\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{U})
.

Proof
It follows from
Lemma
7.59
and
Lemma
7.60
.

7.7.2
Strong localizability

Let
M
be an
S
-flat holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{S\times X/S}
-module
with
A\subset S(\mathbb{K})
.

Definition 7.67

We say that
M
is strongly localizable
if the following holds
for any smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety
Y

any cell

\mathcal{U}_{Y}=(S_{1},\rho_{1},\mathcal{E},\nabla,E,{\boldsymbol{m}})

on
Y
.

•

The
\mathcal{D}_{(S_{1}\times S)\times(Y\times X)/(S_{1}\times S)}
-modules

\mathcal{L}\bigl(\mathcal{U}_{Y}\bigr)\boxtimes M

and
\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}\bigl(\mathcal{U}_{Y}\bigr)\boxtimes M

are localizable over
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{U}_{Y})\times A
.

Lemma 7.68

Let
0\to M^{\prime}\to M\to M^{\prime\prime}\to 0

be an exact sequence of
S
-flat holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{S\times X/S}
-modules.
If two of them are strongly localizable,
then the rest is also strongly localizable.

Lemma 7.69

Let
\varphi:M\to M^{\prime}
be a morphism of

S
-flat holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{S\times X/S}
-modules.
Suppose that
\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits\varphi
is
S
-flat,
and that
M
and
M^{\prime}
are strongly localizable.
Then,
\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits(\varphi)
,
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits(\varphi)
and
\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits(\varphi)

are strongly localizable.

7.7.3
Approximation of the space of extensions

Let
S
be a smooth
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
A\subset S(\mathbb{K})
be a subset.
Let
M
be an
S
-flat holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{S\times X/S}
-module
which is strongly localizable over
A
.

Let
\mathcal{U}=(T,Z,H,\rho,\mathcal{E},E)
be a cell on
X
.
We obtain
the
(T\times S)
-flat holonomic

\mathcal{D}_{(T\times S)\times(X\times X)/T\times S}
-module

\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M
.
It is localizable over
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{U})\times A
.

Let
\Delta:X\to X\times X
denote the diagonal embedding.
It induces

(T\times S)\times X\to(T\times S)\times X\times X
.
Let
H_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,m)

be a tuple of hypersurfaces
such that
\Delta(X)=\bigcap_{i=1}^{\ell}H_{i}
.

There exist
U\to T\times S

and
W\subset U

such that the following holds.

•

W
is a countable union of
closed Zariski subsets of
U

such that

W\cap\bigl(U\times_{T\times S}(\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{U})\times A)\bigr)=\emptyset
.

•

For any
J\subset\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
there exist
coherent
\mathcal{D}_{T\times S\times X\times X/T\times S}
-modules

(\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M)_{U,J}

with morphisms

(\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M)_{U,J}\to(\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M)_{U,J^{\prime}}

for
J\subset J^{\prime}

such that

\begin{CD}\bigl((\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M)_{U,J}\bigr)_{\eta}@>{}>{}>\bigl((\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M)_{U,J^{\prime}}\bigr)_{\eta}\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V@V{\simeq}V{}V\\
(\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M)_{\eta}(\ast H(J)_{\eta})@>{}>{}>(\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M)_{\eta}(\ast H(J^{\prime})_{\eta})\end{CD}

for any geometric point
\eta
of
U

whose image is not contained in
W
.

Let
U
denote the
\mathbb{K}
-vector space
with the base
e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell}
.
For an ordered subset
J=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})
,
we set
e_{J}=e_{j_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{j_{m}}
.
We set

\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits,k}\bigl(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M\bigr)=\bigoplus_{|J|=k}\bigl(\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M\bigr)_{U,J}\otimes e_{J}.

We obtain the complex

\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits,\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)
.
We also set

\mathcal{C}^{k}\bigl(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M\bigr)=\bigoplus_{|J|=k}(\mathbb{D}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})\boxtimes M)(\ast H(J))\otimes e_{J}.

We obtain the complex

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)
.
There exists the natural morphism of
complexes.

\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits,\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)\to\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M).

For any geometric point
\eta

whose image is not contained in
W
,
we have

\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits,\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)_{\eta}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)_{\eta}.

Let
\pi:U\times X\to U
denote the projection.
We obtain the following morphisms

R^{k}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits,\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)\longrightarrow R^{k}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M).

By refining
U
,
we may assume that

R^{k}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)

are locally free
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-modules of finite rank.

For any geometric point
\eta

whose image is not contained in
W
,
we have

\Bigl(R^{k}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)\Bigr)_{\eta}\simeq\Bigl(R^{k}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)_{\eta}\Bigr)\simeq\Bigl(R^{k}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{\eta},M_{\eta})\Bigr)\simeq\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{k}\Bigl(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{\eta},M_{\eta}\Bigr).

Let
E^{1}
be the vector bundle over
U

corresponding to

R^{1}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits,\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)
.
Let
p:E^{1}\to U
denote the projection.
Let
\pi_{1}:E^{1}\times X\to E^{1}
denote the projection.
We obtain

p^{\ast}R^{1}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits,\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)\to p^{\ast}R^{1}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)\simeq R^{1}\pi_{1\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{E^{1}},M_{E^{1}})

on
E^{1}
.
The universal section of

p^{\ast}R^{1}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm app}\nolimits,\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U}),M)

induces the section
s_{1}

of

R^{1}\pi_{1\ast}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{E^{1}},M_{E^{1}})

on
E^{1}
.
Because
E^{1}
is an affine scheme,
it induces an element

\alpha(s_{1})\in\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{E^{1}\times X/E^{1}}}\Bigl(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{E^{1}},M_{E^{1}}\Bigr).

We obtain the extension of

\mathcal{D}_{E^{1}\times X/E^{1}}
-modules
corresponding to
\alpha(s_{1})
:

0\longrightarrow M_{E^{1}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{M}_{E^{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{E^{1}}\longrightarrow 0.

Let
a\in A\subset S(\mathbb{K})

and
b\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{U})\subset T(\mathbb{K})
.
Let
u\in\widetilde{U}(\mathbb{K})
be over
(a,b)
.
There exists an isomorphism

E^{1}_{|u}\simeq\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{b},M_{a}).

We obtain the following proposition from
Proposition
7.31
.

Proposition 7.70

For
x\in E^{1}_{|u}
,
the extension

0\longrightarrow M_{E^{1}|x\times X}\longrightarrow\widetilde{M}_{E^{1}|x\times X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{E^{1}|x\times X}\longrightarrow 0

corresponds to

x\in\mathop{\rm Ext}\nolimits^{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{b},M_{a})
.

8
Boundedness for some families of
\mathcal{D}
-modules

8.1
Boundedness condition

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.
Let
S
be a
\mathbb{K}
-variety.
Let
S(\mathbb{K})
denote the set of closed points.
Let
\pi:X_{S}\to S
be a smooth projective morphism
of relative dimension
n
.
For each
s\in S(\mathbb{K})
,
we set
X_{s}=\{s\}\times_{S}X_{S}
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits(\mathcal{D}_{X_{s}})
denote the category of

\mathcal{D}_{X_{s}}
-modules.

Definition 8.1

Let
\mathcal{C}_{s}

(s\in S(\mathbb{K}))

be full subcategories of
\mathop{\rm Mod}\nolimits(\mathcal{D}_{X_{s}})
.
We say that the family
(\mathcal{C}_{s}\,|\,s\in S(\mathbb{K}))
is bounded
if there exist the following objects.

•

A
\mathbb{K}
-variety
\mathcal{S}
over
S
.
We set

X_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{S}\times_{S}X_{S}
.

•

A coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{\mathcal{S}}/\mathcal{S}}
-module

\mathcal{M}

which is flat over
\mathcal{S}
.

•

For any
s\in S(\mathbb{K})
and
any object
M
in
\mathcal{C}_{s}
,
there exists a closed point

\widetilde{s}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{K})
over
s

such that

\mathcal{M}_{s}\simeq M
.

8.1.1
Boundedness of meromorphic flat connections

We have the following boundedness.

Theorem 8.2

Let
S
,
X_{S}
,
H_{S}
be as in

§
6.3.2
.
Then, the family
(\mathcal{C}^{\mathop{\rm good}\nolimits}(X_{s},H_{s},r,{\boldsymbol{m}})\,|\,s\in S(\mathbb{K}))

is bounded in the sense of Definition
8.1
.

Proof
Let
\mathcal{S}
and
(E_{\mathcal{S}},\nabla_{\mathcal{S}})

be as in Theorem
6.18
.
We set

\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}}=E_{\mathcal{S}}(\ast H_{\mathcal{S}})
.
We obtain the good meromorphic flat bundle

(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}},\nabla_{\mathcal{S}})
relative to
\mathcal{S}
.
By refining
\mathcal{S}
,
we may assume that there exists a good lattice
of
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}},\nabla_{\mathcal{S}})
.
Let
\mathcal{S}_{1}
be an irreducible component of
\mathcal{S}
.
We obtain

(X_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},H_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})=(X_{\mathcal{S}},H_{\mathcal{S}})\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}_{1}
.
As the restriction,
we obtain

(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},\nabla_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})

and the induced lattice
E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}
.
Let
H_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}=\bigcup_{j\in\Lambda_{1}}H_{\mathcal{S}_{1},j}

be the irreducible decomposition.
Let
E^{(1)}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}

be a good lattice of

(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},\nabla_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})

satisfying Condition
7.42
.
We take
{\boldsymbol{m}}_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{\Lambda_{1}}

satisfying Condition
7.43

for
E^{(1)}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}
.
We also assume the following condition.

•

E^{(1)}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}\subset E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}(-H_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})

and

E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}(H_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})\subset E^{(1)}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}({\boldsymbol{m}}_{1}H_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})
.
It implies

\mathcal{A}(E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})\subset\mathcal{B}(E^{(1)}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}},{\boldsymbol{m}}_{1})
.
(See Lemma
7.49
for
\mathcal{A}(E_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})
.)

We obtain the coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}/\mathcal{S}_{1}}
-module

\mathcal{D}_{X_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}/\mathcal{S}_{1}}\bigl(E^{(1)}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}({\boldsymbol{m}}_{1}H_{\mathcal{S}_{1}})\bigr)

which is flat over
\mathcal{S}_{1}
.
Applying this construction to each irreducible component of
\mathcal{S}
,
we obtain the claim of the theorem.

Theorem 8.3

Let
X
,
H
,
r
,
{\boldsymbol{m}}
be as in

§
6.4
.
Then, the family
\mathcal{C}(X,H,r,{\boldsymbol{m}})

is bounded
in the sense of Definition
8.1
.

Proof
Let
\mathcal{S}
,
\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}
,

\rho_{\mathcal{S}}:\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{S}\times X
,

H_{\mathcal{S}}
,
a good meromorphic flat bundle

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)

on
(\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}},\widehat{H}_{\mathcal{S}})

relative to
\mathcal{S}
,
and a lattice
\widehat{E}_{\mathcal{S}}

as in Theorem
6.20
.
By refining
\mathcal{S}
,
we may assume that

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
has a good lattice.
As in the proof of Theorem
8.2
,
we construct
a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{\widehat{X}_{\mathcal{S}}/\mathcal{S}}
-module
\mathcal{M}

such that the following holds.

•

\mathcal{M}_{s}\simeq\mathcal{V}_{s}

for any
u\in\mathcal{A}(E_{\mathcal{S}})
.

By considering

\rho_{\mathcal{S}\dagger}\mathcal{M}
,
we obtain the claim of the theorem.

8.2
Boundedness of holonomic
\mathcal{D}
-modules with dominated
characteristic cycles

Let
X
be a smooth projective
\mathbb{K}
-varieties.
Let
\Lambda=\sum m_{i}\Lambda_{i}
,
where
m_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
and
\Lambda_{i}
are complex Lagrangian cone in
T^{\ast}X
.
For any holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
M
,
we have the characteristic cycle
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)
.
Let
\mathcal{C}(X,\Lambda)

denote the family of holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules
M

such that
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)\leq\sum m_{i}\Lambda_{i}
,
i.e.,

\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)=\sum n_{i}\Lambda_{i}
with
n_{i}\leq m_{i}
.

Theorem 8.4

The family
\mathcal{C}(X,\Lambda)
is bounded
in the sense of Definition
8.1
.

8.2.1
Boundedness of minimal extensions

Let
Z\subset X
be an irreducible closed subset,
which is not necessarily normal.
Let
H
be a hypersurface of
X

such that
Z\setminus H
is smooth.
We set
H_{Z}=H\cap Z
.
Let
\iota_{Z}:Z\setminus H_{Z}\to X\setminus H
denote the immersion.

A meromorphic connection on
(Z,H_{Z})
means
an algebraic
\mathcal{O}_{Z\setminus H_{Z}}
-module
V

with an integrable connection
\nabla
.
It induces a
\mathcal{D}_{X\setminus H}
-module

\iota_{Z\dagger}(V)
.
The minimal extension is denoted by

(\iota_{Z\dagger}V)_{\min}
.

Let
\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits_{\min}(Z,H_{Z},\Lambda)

denote the category of
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules
obtained as

(\iota_{Z\dagger}V)_{\min}

for a meromorphic flat connection
V
on
(Z,H_{Z})

such that

\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits\bigl((\iota_{Z\dagger}V)_{\min}\bigr)\leq\Lambda.

Proposition 8.5

There exists a cell

\mathcal{U}=(S,Z_{S},\rho,\mathcal{E},\nabla,E,{\boldsymbol{m}})

such that the following holds.

•

For any

(\iota_{Z\dagger}V)_{\min}\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits_{\min}(Z,H_{Z},\Lambda)
,
there exists

a\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{U})
such that

(\iota_{Z\dagger}V)_{\min}\simeq\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{U})_{a}
.

Proof
Let
\varphi:\widetilde{Z}\to Z
denote the normalization.
Note that
\varphi
is a finite map.
Let
\widetilde{Z}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}
denote the smooth part.
We set
H_{\widetilde{Z}}=\varphi^{-1}(H_{Z})
.

Let
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
denote
the irreducible decomposition.
Let
H^{\circ}
denote the smooth part of
H
.
We set
H_{i}^{\circ}=H_{i}\cap H^{\circ}

which is Zariski open in
H_{i}
.
There exists a Zariski open subset

H^{\triangle}_{Z,i}\subset H_{Z,i}^{\circ}

such that

\varphi^{-1}(H^{\triangle}_{Z,i})\to H_{Z,i}^{\triangle}

is etale.

Let
(V,\nabla)
be a meromorphic flat bundle
on
(\widetilde{Z},H_{\widetilde{Z}})
.
Let
r
denote the multiplicity of

T^{\ast}_{Z^{\circ}}X
in
\Lambda
.
We obtain

\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(V)\leq r
.

Let
V_{\min}
denote the minimal extension
on
\widetilde{Z}^{\mathop{\rm sm}\nolimits}
.
We set

V_{!}=\mathbb{D}\bigl(\mathbb{D}(V)(\ast H_{\widetilde{Z}})\bigr)
.
There exists the natural morphism

V_{!}\to V
,
and
V_{\min}
equals the image.
Let
C(V)
and
K(V)
denote the cokernel and the kernel.
Their supports are contained in
H_{\widetilde{Z}}
.
On
H^{\triangle}_{Z,i}
,
we have

\varphi^{j}_{\dagger}(C(V))=\varphi^{j}_{\dagger}(K(V))=0

unless
j=0
.
Hence,
around
H^{\triangle}_{Z,i}
,
we have

\varphi_{\dagger}(V_{\min})=\bigl(\varphi_{\dagger}(V)\bigr)_{\min}
.

Lemma 8.6

Let
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits_{i}(V_{\min})

denote the multiplicity of
T^{\ast}H_{\widetilde{Z}_{i}}

in
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(V_{\min})
.
Let
k_{i}
denote the multiplicity of

T^{\ast}_{H_{i}}X
in
\Lambda
.
We obtain

\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits_{i}(V_{\min})\leq k_{i}
.

Proof
It is enough to study the case
\mathbb{K}={\mathbb{C}}
.
We can work in the complex analytic setting.
By
[
13
, Proposition 9.4.2, Theorem 11.3.3]
,
around
H^{\triangle}_{Z,i}
,
we obtain

\varphi_{\ast}(\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(V_{\min}))=\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits\bigl((\varphi_{\ast}V)_{\min}\bigr).

Here
\varphi_{\ast}
for Lagrangian cone is
defined as in

[
13
, Definition 9.3.3]
.

Let
P
be a general point of
H_{\widetilde{Z},i}
.
There exists a neighbourhood
U
of
\varphi(P)

such that for the irreducible decomposition

Z\cap U=\bigcup Z_{P,j}
,
we have
Z_{P,j}\cap Z_{P,k}=H_{Z,i}

(j\neq k)
.
Also,
\varphi^{-1}(Z_{j})\to Z_{j}
are homeomorphisms.
Note that

\varphi_{\ast}(T^{\ast}_{H_{\widetilde{Z},i}}\widetilde{Z})=T^{\ast}_{H_{Z,i}}X

because
\varphi_{\ast}{\mathbb{C}}_{H_{\widetilde{Z},i}}={\mathbb{C}}_{H_{Z,i}}
.
Hence, we obtain the claim of Lemma
8.6
.

The following lemma is easy to see.

Lemma 8.7

There exist
N_{i}
depending only on
k_{i}
and
r

such that the following holds.

•

Let
\widetilde{Z}^{\circ}
denote the smooth part of
\widetilde{Z}
.
Let
\iota_{Z\dagger}(V)_{\min}\in\mathop{\rm Mero}\nolimits(Z,H_{Z},\Lambda)
.
For any
\Sigma\in{\boldsymbol{I}}(V)
,
the closure of
\Sigma

in
T^{\ast}\widetilde{Z}^{\circ}(\sum N_{i}H_{i})

is proper over
\widetilde{Z}^{\circ}
.

Then, we obtain
the claim of Proposition
8.5
.

8.2.2
Proof of Theorem
8.4

Definition 8.8

An ordered partition of
\Lambda
is
a decomposition

\Lambda=\sum\Lambda^{(i)}
,
where
\Lambda^{(i)}=\sum m_{j}^{(i)}\Lambda_{j}

for some non-negative integers
m_{j}^{(i)}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}(\Lambda)
denote the set of ordered partition.

We obtain the following proposition
from Proposition
8.5

and Proposition
7.70
.

Proposition 8.9

Let
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}=(\Lambda^{(i)})\in\mathcal{P}(\Lambda)
.
There exists a complex variety
S(\boldsymbol{\Lambda})

and a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{X\times S/S}
-module

\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda})

with a filtration
\mathcal{F}
by
\mathcal{D}
-submodules
such that the following holds.

•

(\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}),\mathcal{F})
is flat over
S
,
and
\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda})
is holonomic.

•

Let
M
be a holonomic
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-module
with a Jordan-Hölder decomposition
\mathcal{F}

such that

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{j}(M)\leq\Lambda^{(j)}
.
Then, there exists

s\in S

such that

(M,\mathcal{F})\simeq(\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda}),\mathcal{F})_{s}
.

Let
M
be a holonomic
\mathcal{D}
-module on
X

such that
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)\leq\Lambda
.
There exists a Jordan-Hölder filtration
\mathcal{F}(M)
.
We have

\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M)=\sum\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}(M))
.
There exists partition
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\in\mathcal{P}(\Lambda)

such that
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}(M))\leq\Lambda^{(i)}
.
There exists
s\in\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda})

such that

M\simeq\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda})_{s}
.
Therefore,

\mathcal{S}=\bigsqcup_{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\in\mathcal{P}(\Lambda)}\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Lambda})

with the induced
\mathcal{D}_{X\times\mathcal{S}/\mathcal{S}}
-module
satisfies the desired property in
Theorem
8.4
.

8.2.3
Appendix: Constraint on the characteristic cycles

Let
M
be a coherent
\mathcal{D}_{S\times X/S}
-module
which is flat over
S
and holonomic.
Let
\Lambda_{i}\subset T^{\ast}X

(i=1,\ldots,\ell)

be complex Lagrangian cones.
Let
m_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,\ell)
be positive integers.
We set
\Lambda=\sum m_{i}\Lambda_{i}
.

Lemma 8.10

There exists a constructible subset
S^{\prime}\subset S

such that

\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M_{s})\leq\Lambda
if and only if
s\in S^{\prime}
.

Proof
Let
T\subset S
be a locally closed smooth subvariety.
There exists a Zariski open subset
T^{\prime}\subset T

such that

M_{T^{\prime}}
has a good filtration flat over
T^{\prime}
.
We obtain the characteristic variety
\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M_{T^{\prime}})

and the characteristic cycle
\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M_{T^{\prime}})

by using the good filtration.

Let
\mathbb{K}_{1}
be an algebraic closure of
the generic point of
T
.
Let
\eta:\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{K}_{1}\to T

be the induced morphism.
We obtain
M_{\eta}
on
X_{\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.
We obtain a good filtration of
M_{\eta}

from the good filtration of
M_{T^{\prime}}
.
We have

\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M_{\eta})=\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M_{T^{\prime}})_{\eta}
.
If
\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M_{\eta})\not\subset|\Lambda|\times_{\mathbb{K}}\mathbb{K}_{1}
,
there exists a Zariski open subset
T^{\prime}\subset T

such that

\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M_{s})\not\subset\bigcup\Lambda_{i,\mathbb{K}_{1}}

for any
s\in T^{\prime}
.
If
\mathop{\rm Ch}\nolimits(M_{\eta})=\bigcup\Lambda_{i,\mathbb{K}_{1}}
,
we have

\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M_{T^{\prime}})=\sum n_{i}(T^{\prime}\times\Lambda_{i})

for some
n_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
.
We have
n_{i}\leq m_{i}
if and only if

\mathop{\rm CC}\nolimits(M_{\eta})\leq\sum m_{i}\Lambda_{i,\mathbb{K}_{1}}
.
Then, we obtain the claim of the lemma
by using a Noetherian induction.
